# Vectorworks feature parity

A working checklist of what Vectorworks offers and where openvwx stands,
grouped as Vectorworks groups it. Ticked items work in the web editor today;
a note after an unticked item says what part is there. Add rows as you find
features missing, and tick them as they land. The format details behind each
item are in [FORMAT.md](FORMAT.md).

## Workspace

- [x] Menu bar: File, Edit, View, Modify, Tools (Organization, Custom Selection, Find and Modify, Records, Reports, Utilities, Origin, Snap Settings), Text, Window, Help
- [x] Text menu: size, justification and vertical alignment of the selected text
- [x] Text > Convert Text to Polylines: each selected text becomes a group of closed polylines, one for each piece of ink in its characters (a character's holes joined into it, so it fills as the character does), on the text's layer and in its class, filled solid in the colour the text is drawn in, with no pen; rotation, font, bold, italic and underline as the drawing shows them; one undo step. The characters are traced from the text drawn at about 256 pixels to the em, so the polylines are runs of straight segments within a fraction of a percent of the text's size, not the font's own curves; the group goes on top of the layer rather than where the text was; texts inside groups aren't converted
- [x] Text > Text Along Path…: a single-line text and a path selected (a line, polyline, polygon, rectangle, arc or circle), the text's letters become polylines (traced as Convert Text to Polylines traces them, filled in the text's colour) set along the path, each letter turned to the path where its middle falls; Vectorworks' Text Size choices (Preserve Height and Width, which needs a path as long as the text and says so otherwise; Scale Width Only; Scale Height and Width) and Above Path (the letters on the path's left as it was drawn, or hanging below it), with Reverse Direction (Object Info's button in Vectorworks) in the dialog; one undo step. The path and the letters are grouped where Vectorworks makes a Text Along Path object, so it isn't edited afterwards as one (Modify > Edit Text Along Path, Rot About Path); letters come as curves only (Vectorworks' Surfaces and Extrusions are 3D objects); text and path must be on one layer outside groups
- [x] Text > Size > Set Size…: the selected texts' size typed in points (any value above 0, "pt" optional), the size they share shown (else Mixed), each text's layer scale applied, one undo step
- [x] Text > Format Text…: font, size (any, in points), bold, italic, underline, justification and vertical alignment of the selected texts in one dialog, Mixed where they differ, only what changes set, one undo step (line and character spacing aren't decoded yet)
- [x] Text > Font and Style: the file's fonts or common ones (added to its font list), Plain, Bold, Italic, Underline; Object Info's Text section too; drawn, and exported to PDF and SVG, in that face
- [x] Tools > Utilities > Purge…: unused symbol definitions (with those placed only inside them), unused classes (with those only the purged definitions used) and coincident duplicate objects, each with its count, in one undo step
- [x] Tools > Utilities > Batch Rename…: classes, design layers, sheet layers (number or title), stories and resources; find and replace, case, prefix, suffix and numbering, previewed with clashes marked, on the ticked rows in one undo step
- [x] Tools > Check File Health…, Vectorworks' File Health Check: Bounding Box Far from Origin, Large Bounding Box (the objects outside a square of the size set around the middle of the rest, when the design layers span more), Polygons with High Vertex Count (polygons, polylines and 3D polygons), Transparent 2D Symbols, 3D Polygons in Groups, Hatch Complexity (levels), Texture Size (pixels), Too Many Document Colors and Classes Set to Show Others, each ticked to run (Run Selected, Run All) at a limit kept in this browser, with a result bubble (not run, failed with the count, partly resolved, passed). The issues found, by test, ticked and then located (selected on their layer and zoomed to; a hatch opened in its editor), deleted (Delete Objects) or auto-resolved (Simplify Polys opened on the polygons; Class Options set to Show/Snap/Modify Others); kept while the drawing is open and checked again on reopening, the ones put right shown as resolved or deleted (Hide resolved issues). Its default limits are Rodeo's own, since Vectorworks doesn't publish its own. A dialog rather than a web palette. Not offered: 3D Geometry Created from Connected Lines, Small Sweep Increment Angle, Corrupted Plug-in Objects, Use of Cropped Perspective, Use of Legacy Options and Overly Large/Complex Symbols, which need what Rodeo doesn't read; distances are measured in plan only
- [x] Tools > Compress Images…: Apply JPEG Compression to the Selected Bitmaps (with those in selected groups) or All Bitmaps on the drawing's layers; each image's own record keeps its place, size, class and attributes and its picture becomes a JPEG (quality 0.8), one undo step, reporting the size before and after. Rodeo's reading: images that are JPEGs already, have clear parts (a JPEG has none: an 8-bit plant picture's clear white, say) or wouldn't get smaller stay as they are, with the count of each; image resources (image fills and textures) and images inside symbol definitions aren't compressed
- [x] Tools > Origin > Locate Internal Origin (the window centred on 0, 0 in Top/Plan, the drawing kept in sight, the origin marked) and Center Drawing on Internal Origin… (the farthest object's distance before and after, then every design-layer object moved, hidden ones too, the sheets' plan viewports following, one undo step; refused while elevation, section, 3D or design-layer viewports or objects that can't move are there; no user origin kept, saved views stay)
- [x] Tools > Utilities > Change Symbol Attrs…: the objects of the symbol definitions in a folder (and its subfolders), in none or in all put in one class, taking the class's fill, pen colour, line style, line weight and opacity as chosen, the attributes not taken kept or set to the Attributes palette's; a confirmation naming the count; one undo step. A mesh's faces (which share one id) take its class and attributes with it, here and in Set Class, the Attributes palette and By Class
- [x] Tools > Utilities > Create Color Chart…: the drawing's colour palette on the active layer as a grouped chart, sixteen swatches to a row in palette order, the palette index numbers under them as chosen; swatches and numbers sized on paper; one undo step
- [x] Tools > Utilities > List Symbols…: a text file of the symbol folders, with their definitions or folders only, a tab per level
- [x] Tools > Utilities > Replace Font…: one font for another in every text object on the drawing's layers and every text style, each font listed with what uses it, in one undo step (text inside symbol definitions keeps its font)
- [x] Tools > Utilities > Reset All Plug-Ins: after a confirmation naming how many objects of which plug-ins, every plug-in object on the drawing's layers whose plug-in Rodeo can draw (an installed Vectorworks plug-in, or Hyperlink) is drawn again from its parameters by its plug-in's current script, one after another; a status window then says how many were reset, which failed and why (the first reason), and which were left as they were last drawn because Rodeo can't draw their plug-ins (Vectorworks' own, or not installed). Each object is its own undo step rather than one for the lot; plug-in objects inside symbol definitions aren't reached
- [x] Tools > Reports > Create Report…: a new worksheet of the selection's, the active layer's (the edited group's), the visible or all design layers' top-level objects, optionally only those meeting Custom Selection's criteria; columns Type, Name, Class, Layer, Area, Perimeter and Length and the objects' record and plug-in fields; sorted by the columns in order, and optionally summarized (a row per set of objects alike, with a count and their numbers added up); lengths and areas as dimension and area cells in the document's units; a name already used refused; one undo step, then open in the worksheet editor. Its settings and font come from a worksheet the drawing has (chosen when there are several)
- [ ] Menu bar: the rest of the AEC menu, Spotlight/Landmark menus and the rest of the Model menu; the other Utilities; Create Report as Vectorworks makes it (a database row that recalculates: formulas can't be written yet), and in a drawing with no worksheet to make it from
- [x] View bar: undo and redo, zoom field, fit, zoom to selection, Top/Plan or 3D, active layer, active class
- [x] View bar: standard views, render mode, saved views (open, save current), layer and class options pop-ups
- [x] View > Save View…: the current view saved under a name ("View N" offered, a name already saved refused) with Restore layer and class visibility, joining Navigation's and the View bar's saved views; kept on this computer for the drawing's file name, not in the file; a view saved while the 3D view shows keeps its camera and projection and opens in 3D through it (marked 3D in the lists), and one saved in 2D opens in 2D, as Vectorworks' saved views keep the view's projection and orientation (Rodeo's reading: render mode and lighting aren't kept with it)
- [x] View bar: layer and class visibility lists (visible, grayed, hidden per row; a column head sets every listed row; search)
- [x] View bar: projection (2D Plan, Orthogonal, Narrow, Normal or Wide Perspective, a custom one, Two-Point Perspective or an Oblique one; a 3D one chosen in 2D Plan shows the 3D view)
- [x] View bar: working plane (the active plane, Layer Plane or a saved one, picked from a pop-up that also sets one on a face, looks at it and opens the palette; lit while a working plane is active)
- [x] Mode bar: active tool, its modes (Dimension, Arc, Move by Points, Clip), its instructions
- [x] Mode bar: several mode groups per tool, and a Preferences button (Double Line, Double Line Polygon, Rounded Rectangle, Offset, Fillet, Chamfer, Regular Polygon)
- [x] Mode bar modes: Mirror and Rotate (the selection or a copy), Zoom (In, Out), Eyedropper (Pick Up, Put Down), Offset (by distance or point; duplicate or offset the original), Fillet and Chamfer (alone, and split, and trim), Regular Polygon (inscribed, circumscribed, by edge), Split (Point Split, Line Split, Line Trim), Connect/Combine (Single Object Connect, Dual Object Connect, Dual Object Combine), Polyline (Corner, Bezier, Cubic, Arc, Point on Arc vertices), Tape Measure (Single, Multiple Segment), Freehand (smoothing None, Low, Medium, High), Polygon (Vertices, Inner Boundary, Outer Boundary); number keys choose them
- [x] Mode bar modes: Selection's Rectangle, Polygon and Lasso marquees (inside, or touching with Option)
- [x] Mode bar modes: Selection's interactive scaling (No Scaling, Single Object, Multiple Objects: eight handles on the selection's bounds, Shift keeps the proportions)
- [x] Mode bar on a narrow canvas: the tool's icon alone and each mode group a drop-down, so every mode stays in reach; menus near the bottom of the window open upwards
- [x] Mode bar modes: Text's justification (Left, Centre, Right; keys 1–3) and vertical alignment (Top, Top baseline, Centre, Bottom baseline, Bottom), anchored on the click
- [x] Mode bar modes: Symbol Insertion's Click and Click and Rotate (a second click sets the rotation, Shift in 15° steps)
- [x] Mode bar: Polyline's Cubic vertex mode (a point the curve passes through, its control placed as Vectorworks places it; keys 1–5)
- [x] Palettes dock left or right, float, roll up, close and resize; docks resize and split their height
- [x] Window > Palettes > Activate Object Info Palette (also in the drawing's right-click menu, whose Palettes submenu lists the palettes): Object Info shown, unrolled or brought to the front, its first field taking the keyboard
- [x] Window > Palettes lists the palettes as Vectorworks 2025 to 2027 do, by name: besides Object Info, Navigation and Visualization, each of their tabs as a palette of its own (Shape, Data and Render; Design Layers, Classes, Stories, Sheet Layers, Viewports and Saved Views; Lights and Cameras), with Basic, Resource Manager, Tool Sets, Attributes, Snapping, Working Planes and the palettes features add (a tab named as a palette is, e.g. Render, is listed with its palette's name). Each is checked while in view; choosing one shows it, a tab still in its palette coming to the front of it; choosing one in view closes it, a tab out of its palette going back into it and one in it closing with its palette. Hide Enabled Palettes (⇧⌘P) puts every palette away and brings them back. The command search finds them by the same names. References isn't listed: Rodeo has no referencing
- [x] Window > Home Screen: the start screen (recent drawings, New, Open) over the open drawing, with Back to drawing and Escape to return to it as it was
- [x] Window menu: each palette, Hide Enabled Palettes (⇧⌘P, in Window > Palettes), roll up or unroll all, View, Mode and Snapping bars, Reset Bars, Top Bar, auto-hide either dock, reset layout
- [x] A menu bar across the top as Vectorworks has it (Window > Top Bar > Menu Bar; the Dense and Classic workspaces show it): every menu's title in a row, including those features add, in the order Menus… sets; a press drops a menu and moving over another title while one is down switches to it; Escape or a second press closes it
- [x] The bars over the drawing move and roll up like palettes: the View bar, Mode bar and Snapping bar each have a grip; drag it to put the bar anywhere over the drawing (kept inside it as the window narrows, back where it was as it widens), click it to roll the bar up to the grip and the bar's name (the Mode bar: the tool's icon), the grip staying put, double-click to put the bar back; arrow keys on the grip nudge it (Shift: 50 px); kept per browser and with workspaces; a phone's bars stay fixed
- [x] Notification Center, as Vectorworks 2027 has it on the status bar: a bell on the Snapping bar listing every alert of the session (notes and errors, each with its time), newest first, with a count of those not yet seen (red when one is an error) and Clear All; the alerts still show at the foot of the window and go by themselves; Window > Notification Center opens it too; a reload starts an empty list
- [x] Screen tips over tools and modes, as Vectorworks shows them: after the pointer rests, a tool's name and key, its tool set and how to use it (the Mode bar's help and the keys it takes), a mode's name and key with its tool and group; the next along a row at once; a press, a key or the wheel puts it away; not on touch. Not yet the expanded tips Vectorworks has for some items
- [x] Smart Options display (` or a middle click that doesn't pan): Tools (Basic and every tool set), Snaps, View (zoom, previous and next views, 3D, standard views, rendering, grid) and Layers (active layer and class) in a ring at the cursor
- [x] Palette layout kept per browser
- [x] Palette sections fold (Object Info, Attributes, Visualization): a click on a section's heading, or Enter on it, folds it to the heading, Option-click folds or opens every section of the palette; kept per section as the selection changes, per browser
- [x] Quick Preferences (the View bar): the chosen preferences as rows checked while on, a click turning one on or off; Customize… picks which of 17 it lists (kept per browser and with workspaces), Preferences… opens the dialog
- [x] Tools > Quick Search… (F, as Vectorworks keys it; Fillet moves to ⇧F): Search Everything (also ⇧⌘K and the top bar's magnifier) finds the menus' commands (those in submenus built as they open, such as Working Plane, Workspaces and Data Bar Options, too), the tools and every Quick Preference, with palettes, layers, classes, saved views, symbols and objects; a Quick Preference is a check box set in place, the search staying open. Commands that don't apply now are left out rather than greyed, and there are no help links, filters or remembered window position
- [x] Palettes docked as tabs of one another: drop a title bar on another, drag a tab out, or use the title bar's menu
- [x] Palette tabs that come out, as Vectorworks 2027 detaches them: a tab of Object Info (Shape, Data, Render), Navigation (Layers, Classes, Stories, Sheets, Viewports, Views) or Visualization (Lights, Cameras) dragged off its strip becomes a palette of its own under the pointer (or, by the palette's title bar menu's Undock, floating beside the palette; out, it takes its Vectorworks name, e.g. Design Layers), which floats, docks, rolls up and follows the selection like any other; the last tab stays. Tabs out of the same palette connect (dropped on one another's title, or the docking guide's middle square), and nothing else joins them; dropped back on their palette, closed, or its title bar menu's "Dock [tab] here" or "Dock all tabs here", they go back at the end of the strip. A tab dragged along the strip moves along it; "Lock tabs" stops moving, coming out and going back. Kept with the layout and workspaces; a phone's sheet shows the palette whole
- [x] Tab Style in a tabbed palette's title bar menu, as Vectorworks 2027 has it: Icon Only, Text Only (the default) or Icon and Text for that palette's tabs, both the palettes tabbed together in its title bar and its own strip (Object Info's Shape, Data and Render; Navigation's Layers, Classes, Stories, Sheets, Viewports and Views), each with an icon; Icon Only makes them square, Navigation's strip half as wide, the name in the tooltip; per palette, kept with the layout and workspaces; a palette without tabs doesn't offer it
- [x] Vectorworks 2027's four docking bars: besides the left and right docks, a top and a bottom bar between them, along the drawing's edges, where palettes stand side by side across the drawing (each its share of the width, a handle between each two moving the share; the bar's inner edge sets its height, up to 60 per cent of the window; rolled up, a palette in a bar is its title, and a bar whose palettes are all rolled up is as tall as their titles, its edge's double-click or the title bar menu's Roll Up Docking Bar rolling them all). A palette joins one dragged near the drawing's top or bottom edge, over the bar, or onto the docking guide's square at that edge (a line shows where it will stand, or a band where the bar will be), or by the title bar menu's Send to (Top, Bottom, Left or Right Docking Bar); the drawing gives the bars their height; kept with the layout and workspaces; Hide Palettes hides them too
- [x] Wide palettes in columns: a palette wider than two columns of fields (in a top or bottom docking bar, floating, or in a dock pulled wide) lays its sections side by side, as many columns as fit with a hairline between, and scrolls down rather than across; Object Info, Attributes, Visualization and Render do so, the lists (Navigation, Resources) stay as they are. Visualization alone in a 1440 px window's bottom bar fits its Lights tab in 151 px instead of 250, three columns across
- [x] Floating palettes dock to each other, as Vectorworks does: dragged over a floating palette, the docking guide stands over it too, its top and bottom squares docking the palette above or below it (a line shows where) and its middle one as a tab; the palettes stand in one floating card, a hairline between each two, resized from its corner (the width the card's, each filling palette's height its own). The top palette's title bar moves the card, and docks or tabs it with every palette in it, in order; another palette's title bar pulls that one out; the top one closed or docked, the next takes its place. Window > Palette Options > Floating Palettes Can Dock to Each Other (on at first) turns the guide over floating palettes, and their titles taking tabs, off; kept with the layout and workspaces
- [x] Docking guide and docked palettes side by side, as Vectorworks docks them: while a palette is dragged, squares over the docked palette under the pointer put it above, below, as a tab or in a new column to its left or right (up to four a side; the dock widens by the palette's width, previewed as a blue band, while the drawing keeps 240 px), and a square at each side of the drawing docks it at the end of that dock; Option or Ctrl held keeps it floating; a handle between columns resizes them, the dock's edge the column beside the drawing, and a column left empty goes, the dock narrowing by its width; kept with the layout and workspaces
- [x] Docks fold to a rail of palette icons (the rail's fold button, Window > Fold Left Dock and Fold Right Dock, a double-click on a dock's inner edge, or a title bar's menu), giving the drawing all but 36 px of the dock's width (1352 × 844 px of a 1440 × 900 window with both folded); an icon opens its palette beside the rail, over the drawing, until a click elsewhere or Escape; kept with the layout and workspaces
- [x] Auto-hidden docks, as Vectorworks' Auto Hide: the rail's pin, Window > Auto-Hide Left Dock and Auto-Hide Right Dock, or a title bar's menu fold a dock to its rail; a palette slides out over the drawing while the pointer rests on its icon (a tab's icon brings that tab to the front) and back once the pointer leaves it, staying out while a field in it is being typed in or a menu is open; the drawing gets 1352 px of a 1440 px window with the right dock auto-hidden; kept per browser and with workspaces
- [x] Dock rails: every palette of a side, closed ones too, an icon on the dock's window edge; a click opens a palette, brings a rolled-up one or a tab behind another into view, or closes it; a right-click gives its title bar's menu; a dock whose palettes are all closed is just its rail
- [x] Named workspaces: save the palette arrangement, switch, delete (Window > Workspaces)
- [x] Window > View Bar Options: Display View Bar in Regular, Compact (28 px, tighter, without the layer's scale) or Auto-Hide Mode (a short mark where it is till the pointer comes within 24 px; kept out while a field in it has focus or a menu is open) and Show View Bar Group Labels (Layer, Class, Views); kept with the layout and workspaces
- [x] Window > Palette Options: Auto-Hide Docked Palettes (both docks folded to their rails, a palette sliding out from its icon) and Auto-Hide Floating Palettes (each floating palette rolled up to its title bar while the pointer is elsewhere, unrolled as it comes back); Window > Palettes > Save Palette Positions (into the saved workspace in use; with a built-in one, Save Workspace As… names one)
- [x] Interface density: Window > Compact Interface packs the top bar (36 px), palette title bars (26 px), rows and fields (20 px), tab strips (wrapping instead of hiding tabs) and the drawing's margins as tightly as Vectorworks does; kept with the layout and workspaces
- [x] Window > Use Full Screen: the window fills the screen without the browser's bars (checked while it does), and back
- [x] Multiple view panes (Window > View Panes, and the View bar): Single, Two Panes Side by Side and Two Panes Stacked, the plan and the 3D view drawn at once (the 3D one rebuilt as the plan is edited); the active pane ringed, taking View > 3D View, Top/Plan, the standard views, render mode, zoom and Fit, a click in the other making it active; a draggable divider (double-click: half and half); kept in this browser
- [x] Multiple view panes: Three Panes (the plan down the left, two 3D views stacked beside it) and Four Panes (two by two), each 3D pane with a view of its own (Front, Right and an isometric view to start with) kept as it was left; the live 3D view in the active 3D pane, the others drawn as pictures again as the model or the look changes; both dividers drag
- [x] Multiple view panes: the plan's pane large on any side (Three Panes and Four Panes, Plan on the Left, on the Right, on Top or Below, the small panes sharing their side evenly), each arrangement pictured in the menu; the arrangement kept with saved and exported workspaces
- [x] Multiple view panes: each 3D pane its own render mode and projection (the View bar and the Visualization palette set the live pane's; the pane the live view moves to takes its own back; a new arrangement starts every pane in the one shown)
- [x] Multiple view panes: a plan or a 3D view in any pane (Top/Plan, 3D View and the standard views turn the active pane into either), two plans at different zooms, the live plan moving to the plan pane clicked at that pane's zoom; kept with the arrangement and workspaces
- [x] Multiple view panes: Realistic in the panes without the live 3D view, the render feature's still picture through each pane's own camera (Shaded until it has loaded and built its scene, then drawn again; again as the Render palette changes), the live view's render and path tracer left as they are
- [x] View > Multiple View Panes (also at the head of Window > View Panes and the View bar's pop-up): Enable Multiple View Panes (one pane, or back to the last arrangement of several), Split Horizontally and Split Vertically (the active pane cut in two, the new half showing what it showed), Close Active View Pane (its room to its neighbours, the live plan and 3D view staying where they were); as far as the twelve arrangements reach: four panes at most, the large pane beside three small ones not closing
- [x] Multiple view panes: Path Traced in the panes without the live 3D view, each refining a path-traced picture through its own camera to the preview samples (512 at most), one pane at a time on a renderer of their own (the live view's renderer, camera and path tracer left as they are), a Realistic picture until its turn; let go of when no pane shows Path Traced
- [x] View > Multiple View Panes > Create Floating View Pane: another view of the drawing in a window floating over the drawing area, starting as the active view (the plan, or the live 3D view's camera); Top/Plan or any standard view from its view button or right-click menu; drag pans the plan or orbits the model (Shift- or middle-drag pans it), the wheel zooms, Fit to Window, Show in Main View (or a double-click) hands its view to the live plan or 3D view; moved by its title, resized from its corner, rolled up and closed; the 3D ones drawn in View > Rendering's mode with the model kept up to date while the 3D view is hidden, Realistic redrawn once the render scene is ready. Not in Vectorworks' way: it floats inside the browser window rather than as a separate OS window, nothing is drawn, picked or snapped to in it, Path Traced shows as a still Realistic picture, and the panes close with their drawing and aren't saved with it
- [x] Open Camera View in New Pane (a Renderworks camera's right-click menu, on a design layer): a floating view pane titled with the camera's name, looking through it (its eye, look-to point and view angle across the picture, as the Visualization palette's Cameras look through it), its picture the camera's shape and in the camera's projection, drawn again as the camera is moved or re-aimed until the pane's own view is orbited, panned, zoomed or turned to a standard view. Not as Vectorworks links it: turning the pane's view doesn't move the camera (Activate Camera View's link), and the pane isn't a docked one of the split panes. Checked with a stand-in camera (no test drawing holds one): the pane's picture matches the 3D view's own look through the same camera
- [x] Built-in workspaces (Window > Workspaces): Standard, Two Sidebars, Dense (compact rows, a 256 px sidebar, Attributes, Snapping and Visualization tabs of Object Info) and Classic (the tools in the Tools and Tool Sets palettes down a 200 px left dock, as Vectorworks keeps them, the top bar without them)
- [x] The top bar's groups shown and hidden (a right-click on the top bar, or Window > Top Bar): 2D, 3D and Views, Tools, Undo and Redo, Zoom, and Show All; hiding the tools brings the Tools and Tool Sets palettes out when neither is shown; kept per browser and with workspaces; a phone keeps its tool row
- [x] One sidebar as the default layout: Object Info with Attributes as its tab above Navigation with Resources and Agents as tabs, all on the right, the left side down to its rail; the drawing gets 1108 × 844 px of a 1440 × 900 window (Two Sidebars, the layout before, 864 × 844; Dense 1146 × 860); a layout kept in the browser stays as it was
- [x] Tool shortcut editor (Window > Workspaces > Tool Shortcuts…: a letter or Shift+letter per tool, clashes refused, defaults restorable)
- [x] Workspace files to share (Window > Workspaces > Export Workspace… and Import Workspace…: the palette arrangement and tool and menu shortcuts as one file)
- [x] Menu command shortcut editing (Window > Workspaces > Menu Shortcuts…: a ⌘ combination per command, filterable, clashes and the browser's own keys refused, defaults restorable; menus, tooltips and Help follow it)
- [x] Workspace editor, tool sets: new sets, renaming, reordering, deleting, and which tools each set holds and in what order (Window > Workspaces > Tool Sets…; kept with saved and exported workspaces)
- [x] Tool palettes' display: Small Icons, Large Icons, Icons and Names (their title bar's menu; kept with workspaces)
- [x] Workspace editor, menus: new menus, renaming, reordering, deleting, and commands or whole submenus moved between menus, up and down, or taken out (they still run from their shortcut) (Window > Workspaces > Menus…; kept with saved and exported workspaces)
- [x] Data bar: live coordinates, length and angle beside the cursor; Tab to type values
- [x] Window > Data Bar Options: Use Floating Data Bar, or Only When Tab Key Is Pressed (gone again once the object is placed or Esc pressed); Show Floating Data Bar Below or Above SmartCursor Cues (the other side where there's no room); Allow Numeric Keypad Entry for Instant Data Bar Activation, Do Not Allow Numeric Keypad Entry (main keys only) or Do Not Allow Instant Data Bar Activation (Tab only, the default, so the 3 key still shows the 3D view); kept in this browser
- [x] Window > Data Bar Options: Show Only Primary Fields, Primary and Secondary Fields, or Primary, Secondary, and Cursor Location Fields on Data Bar; Auto Cycle (or Do Not Auto Cycle) to Non-Displayed Fields When Tabbing Past Last Field. The Line and Polyline/Polygon bars take L and A, with ΔX and ΔY secondary; the Rectangle and Oval take ΔX and ΔY; the Circle R; each second point's X and Y are its location fields. A point's typed X or Y puts it there, else its typed ΔX or ΔY, else the tool's own fields; kept in this browser
- [x] Window > Data Bar Options: Show (or Do Not Show, the default) Floating Data Bar After Datum Creation: a datum set brings the bar out with its first field ready, as Tab does; kept in this browser
- [x] Status bar: cursor position, zoom, active layer, selection count, snap and grid toggles
- [x] View > Rulers: rulers along the drawing's top and left, marked in the document's units at the zoom (feet and inches in feet or inches), a mark on each following the pointer; the bars over the drawing move clear of them; kept per browser
- [x] Palettes and bars styled after Spline's editor, light and dark (solid, no glass: the chosen look)
- [x] Density: every palette fits a narrow dock (163 px) without clipping, whatever is selected (checked for rectangles, text, symbols, several objects, the Data tab and the Visualization palette opened out); the drawing keeps all the height under the top bar, and all the width with Hide Palettes
- [x] Context (right-click) menus: the drawing (selection or empty space), layer, class and viewport rows, palette title bars
- [x] Organization dialog (⇧⌘O): classes, design layers and sheet layers; visibility, names, scales, titles, stacking order, new and delete; applied on OK as one undo step
- [x] Duplicate on the Navigation palette's right-click menu and in the Organization dialog (applied on OK, a copy keeping what was on a layer deleted in the same OK) for a layer (a copy just after it with its settings and a copy of everything on it, on no story at the same elevation; one undo step; refused, changing nothing, when it holds an object Rodeo can't copy yet) and for a class (all its settings: attributes, texture, text style, visibility); the copy is made active (in the dialog, selected) with its name to edit
- [x] Organization dialog: Viewports tab (every viewport by sheet, its kind and scale to edit)
- [x] Organization dialog: Saved Views tab (rename, delete, reorder, the Visibility box)
- [x] Organization dialog: renaming a viewport; New on the Saved Views tab (the current view); Object Info names any object
- [x] Organization dialog: editing a class's attributes (Edit… or a double-click on the Classes tab: line weight and style, pen and fill colour, fill style, opacities; objects using the class style follow; applied with the rest on OK)
- [x] Organization dialog: Stories tab (name, suffix and elevation to edit, the top story first, how many design layers each has; new and delete), and each design layer's elevation and story on the Design Layers tab; a story's new elevation moves its layers with it; applied with the rest on OK
- [x] Organization dialog: a class's RenderWorks texture (Edit…: None or any of the drawing's textures; objects textured by class follow in 3D; applied with the rest on OK)
- [x] Story levels: Navigation > Stories' Edit… and the Organization dialog's Stories tab (Levels…) set a story's levels (level type, elevation above the story and the design layer on the story at it, which then sits at the story's elevation plus the level's), add and remove them, in one undo step
- [ ] Organization dialog: references tab; a class's markers and "use at creation"; story layer naming
- [x] Autosave: a copy of a drawing with unsaved changes kept in the browser every few minutes, offered back after a crash (drawings up to 150 MB)
- [x] Preferences (Edit > Preferences…, ⌘,) in Vectorworks 2026's panes beside a list: General (interface theme, black background), Edit (offset duplicates, mouse wheel zooms or scrolls), Display (zoom line thickness; whether other objects show while a group is edited: grayed, or neither drawn nor snapped to), Session (tool help in the Mode bar), 3D (3D conversion resolution, remembering the last render mode and projection), Autosave (on or off and its interval), Interactive (full-screen cursor, the selection and snap boxes and their sizes, pre-selection highlighting and its delay, animated view transitions with their duration and motion)
- [x] Document units: File > Document Settings > Units… sets the length unit (millimetres, centimetres, metres, kilometres, inches, feet, feet and inches, yards, miles) and the area and volume units with it; nothing moves, every length then shows and is typed in the new unit; one undo step, shared live with collaborators
- [x] Preferences > Appearance (Vectorworks' Interactive Appearance Settings): the selection, pre-selection, snap indicator and cursor colours, with Restore Defaults
- [ ] Document preferences (display; units' rounding and dual dimensions)

## Palettes

- [x] Basic: selection, navigation, drawing and editing tools in a two-column grid
- [x] Tool Sets: Dims/Notes and Insertion
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Extruded Rectangle, Extruded Polygon, Sphere, Hemisphere and Cone, drawn in Top/Plan as true extrudes and sweeps (the cone's and extrudes' heights in their Preferences; one undo step each)
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Chamfer Edge (Setback), Fillet Edge (Radius, constant only), Shell Solid (Thickness; Inside or Outside; Picked Face or Tangent Faces) and Taper Face (Angle), picked from the set: each shows the 3D view, a click chooses on an extrude, multiple extrude, sweep, solid or mesh, Shift-click adds or takes back, a drag still orbits, and a bar over the view holds the value with Apply (or Enter) and Clear (or Esc); the chosen edges and faces show in orange. Chamfer and Fillet select by Tangent Entities, Single Edge, Faces or All Edges; Taper Face takes the face or plane to turn about, then the face, then the angle, positive leaning it in. Rodeo has no solid-modeling kernel, so the result is a **mesh** in the solid's place (faces worked out from its triangles, by the BSP booleans for chamfer, fillet and shell), and it can be edited again by these tools. **Not as in Vectorworks:** the setback, radius or thickness can't be changed afterward in Object Info and nothing restores the original but Undo; no Variable Radius; two filleted edges meeting at a corner mitre rather than blend into a ball; only outside corners can be cut (an inside edge is refused, as adding material isn't done); no highlight on hover, only on the click; a click anywhere on a face takes that face's nearest edge; Taper Face has no drag preview and no Tangent Faces mode; Shell Solid works on solids only, not NURBS surfaces or planar objects; a group is refused; and the booleans' limit of 4,000 triangles applies, which a chamfered round object reaches sooner.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Loft Surface (No Rail, One Rail, Birail Sweep): picked, it shows the 3D view and draws the curves it can take in grey; a click near one chooses it (the point nearest the click starting a closed section, the nearer end an open one), a second click takes it back, orange lines show the proposed alignment, and Enter or Loft opens the Loft Creation dialog: each curve's Reverse and Start (a share of its length, for Select Alignment by Percentage), Ruled, Closed, Create Solid and Keep Curves, the view showing the loft as they change. Sections needn't match: each is measured at the shares where any has a vertex, so none loses a corner. **Not as in Vectorworks:** the curves are 3D polygons, or 2D shapes on the active layer at its plane, not NURBS curves (Rodeo has none); the result is a mesh of flat pieces, not a NURBS surface or generic solid, so it can't be reshaped as one; a curved loft is a Catmull-Rom curve through the sections, not a NURBS fit; 3D loci can't be its ends; Select Alignment by Point is covered by the Start share, not stepped through; the rail doesn't have to touch a section, but with One Rail each section is placed by the point of the rail nearest its middle, and with Birail Sweep the section is taken as it stands at the rails' starts, with the click order and click location rules in the help not followed. Create Solid leaves a flat cap only on the first and last closed section.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Align Plane (`app/tools/alignPlane.ts`, sums in `app/alignPlaneShapes.ts`): works in the 3D view and lays the selected 3D objects on the active working plane, or the layer plane when none is active. Surface mode takes a click on a face of one of them; Three Points mode takes three clicks on them, the first going to the plane's origin, the second along its x axis and the third to its y side, as Vectorworks has it. A click within 12 pixels of a solid's corner takes the corner. With nothing selected, the object clicked is taken. One undo step. Limits: the help doesn't say where a surface lands along the plane or which side the object ends up on, so Rodeo keeps the clicked point's place along the plane and stands the object on the plane's upper side (Mirror flips it, as the help says for Three Points). Help pairs the tool with a polygon render mode for Surface; Rodeo takes face clicks in any mode. 2D objects, plug-in objects, walls, doors and windows don't turn out of plan, as with Rotate 3D, so they stay where they were and a note says how many. Corner snapping reads extrudes, sweeps, solids and meshes only; on symbols and 3D polygons the point clicked is taken as it is.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Push/Pull and Offset Edge (`app/tools/pushPull.ts`, sums in `app/solidOps.ts`): both work in the 3D view; a click chooses, the pointer previews and sets the distance, a click or Enter makes the change, and the distance can be typed (below 0 goes in). Push/Pull's Extrude Face adds or cuts a slab with square sides on a flat or curved face, and raises a flat closed curve, a 2D shape lying flat becoming a real extrude and anything else a mesh. Move Face moves a flat face with its neighbours running on to meet it, a boss or pocket on the face going along and a through hole stretching. Sub-Face works from closed curves, nested ones making holes, or one open curve from edge to edge with the click choosing the side; a cut can run past the far side. Offset Edge offsets an edge, a run of edges (Shift-click) or a whole loop of the clicked flat face, into it or off it, as a 3D polygon; an open one's ends run on to the face's edge; its Push/Pull and Combine modes go straight on to Sub-Face. Limits: a solid's result is a mesh with no history to edit afterward, and the curve is laid on the face's plane; Sub-Face and Offset Edge take flat faces only (Vectorworks offsets on curved ones and shells them); Edge Loop means the loop round the clicked face, not one running round several faces such as a box's sides; there's no Select Face dialog or Option-click for back faces, so orbit to reach one; Push/Pull mode isn't on the 2D drawing tools (Rectangle and so on), only on Offset Edge; the Shift+R key isn't assigned.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Deform (`app/tools/deform.ts`, `app/deformShapes.ts`): works in the 3D view with Vectorworks' clicks (the solid, Shift-click for more; the centre; the reference line or the spine's end; the value), previewing as the pointer moves, the value typed if you prefer. Twist Solid can go past 360°, turning the two ends opposite ways when the centre is partway. Twist Face turns the face, with the faces next to it and what runs smoothly on from them following down to their far rim. Taper Solid and Bulge Solid take a ratio, Symmetric or along the reference line only. Bend Solid takes an angle over the spine, with Symmetric and Finite Length as shown in the help, and refuses a bend too tight for the solid's thickness. Limits: Rodeo has no generic solid, so the result is a mesh cut into small flat faces (up to about 16,000) and curved only to that fineness, and a later Add or Subtract Solids is held to its 4,000-face limit; the centre sits on the working plane if one is active, otherwise on the face clicked or the layer's plane, with no screen plane; the Data bar's Tab shortcut is the box on the tool's bar instead.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Analysis (the 3D view; Proximity: a solid, surface, curve or 3D locus, then another: a 3D locus on each where they come nearest, a locus where a curve passes through a solid or crosses another curve, a 3D polygon along where two solids cross, and the distance told; Interrogation: a curve or surface, then moving along it shows the curvature circle or circles in red and the tangent or normal in blue, and a click opens Curve Properties or Surface Properties with Create Curvature Circle(s), Locus at center(s) of curvature, Create Tangent or Normal Curve and Length, several made as a group. Rodeo's curves are polylines and its surfaces triangles, so curvature is worked out from the neighbouring points, not a NURBS formula: a faceted shape reads close to the true figure (within about 3% on a 48-sided sphere band), a corner in a polyline reads as a tight circle and a straight run as none. The results are 3D polygons and 3D loci, not NURBS curves. The help doesn't list what the Properties dialogs show, so they give the point, radius, curvature and tangent or normal. Proximity takes anything with faces, walls and symbols too; Rodeo has no subdivision objects. Two things whose faces multiply past 16 million aren't compared. Shift+' isn't assigned, as Rodeo's tool keys are letters.)
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Shell Solid on open surfaces and flat objects: a click on an open mesh, or on the line of a closed flat 2D shape or 3D polygon (the tool draws those lines over the 3D view, since 2D shapes don't draw there), chooses it, Shift-click adds or takes back, the shell shows before it's made, and Apply or Enter thickens each: Inside against the surface's normal, Outside along it. A 2D shape looks up; an upright polygon toward +y or +x. Faces stay the thickness apart over folds, a thickness that would cross the surface's faces is refused, and a side shared by three faces can't be thickened. Rodeo has no NURBS or shell object, so each result is a mesh in the object's place and its thickness can't be changed afterward; a shape's holes aren't kept, since the tool reads its outline.
- [x] Tool Sets: Building Shell's Chain Extrude: select the profile (a closed polygon or polyline, up to two more in front of it, or two and a locus) and click a path, open or closed. The back shape runs along the path with mitred corners, and each shape in front is a rib given a thickness: along each side at the Spacing, clear of the mitre zones and centred on what's left, and at the corners on the bisector, elongated. The path sits at the top or bottom right of the back shape's box, or at the locus. A plug-in object: Object Info has Draw 3D, Flip, Center Ribs, Corner Ribs, Vertical Datum, Thickness, Spacing and Rodeo's own Elevation, and redraws it. Its plan shows the back shape's inner and outer edges and the ribs in outline, dashed if the drawing has a dashed line. Reading the help's picture, the shape's left goes to the left of the way the path is walked, with Flip to the right. Merge with Structural Objects isn't there, since section viewports don't merge. Draw New Path replaces the path; it can't be reshaped with Reshape, and the profile can't be edited afterward. The profile shapes stay in the drawing. Not checked in Vectorworks, where its own plug-in may redraw it differently.
- [x] AEC > Existing Trees > Create Existing Trees from Loci: each selected 2D or 3D locus or symbol becomes an Existing Tree at its place (a symbol's at its insertion point, not at the locus inside it; this path hasn't been run on a real symbol). Use Attached Record picks a record, and a table maps each field to Tree No, Botanical or Common Name, Action, Significance, Canopy Max or Min Diam, Canopy Rotate, DBH, Critical or Primary Root Diam, Ball Diameter or TPZ Radius, guessed from its name, with a unit (None/Automatic, mm, cm, m, in, ft). A field with nothing in it leaves the tool's setting, as does an Action or Significance it can't read. Preserve Record attaches the record to the new tree. A tree here is the plain group the Existing Tree tool draws, so fields such as Height and Condition have no parameter and are only kept by Preserve Record. A 3D locus's height isn't kept. The mapping is remembered while the page is open; there are no saved sets. Also fixes new groups inheriting a record attached to the group they were modeled on.
- [x] Symbols in a new drawing: Create Symbol, Symbol Insertion, Replace with Symbol, editing a symbol, importing a symbol and bringing a model in as a symbol now work in a blank drawing, or any file with no symbol to copy from, which used to refuse. Its first symbol makes the resource library before the class list. A definition made this way has none of the seven aux records Vectorworks' hold, whose use isn't known, and Vectorworks has not opened one yet. Checked here on Create Symbol, a second instance, selecting one, a mesh inside it in 3D, double-click editing, undo and reopening; both instances follow an edit.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Project (Split, Trim, Add with Add Upward, Add Downward, Add Both Directions): picked, it shows the 3D view and draws in grey the curves it can take, 3D polygons on any layer and 2D shapes on the active one. Click the curve, then the solid or surface; it's projected along the normal of the curve's plane. Split leaves a group of the pieces; a closed curve cuts out what's inside it, and an open curve needs both ends outside the object and cuts it in two. Trim then asks for a click on the side to take away. Add needs a closed curve on one plane and stands a rib on a solid, swept until it meets the first face, trimmed to what it meets, with the way it will go drawn from the curve's middle; it says so if the projection misses. One undo step; the object keeps its class and attributes. Add Upward is up the layer's Z, or +y then +x on an upright plane, whichever way the curve was drawn. Rodeo's limits: the results are meshes with no history, not generic solids or NURBS, and Add, Split and Trim on solids stop at 4,000 triangles between the object and the prism (Add at 150 faces facing the curve); Add takes closed solids only, not open surfaces, and Rodeo keeps the curve where it was as the help doesn't say either way; a trimmed surface isn't a group when one piece is left; a straight curve has no plane, so it's projected straight down, or along x when it stands upright; a non-planar closed curve is flattened on to its best plane for Split and Trim; a subdivision object can't be projected on because Rodeo has none; Shift+; isn't assigned.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's Extract: picked, it shows the 3D view. A click chooses an edge or a face of a solid, mesh or open surface, Shift-click adds to or takes from the choice, a drag still orbits, and Apply or Enter extracts, leaving the object as it was; several results are grouped. Extract Point: 3D loci at the start, end and middle of each edge, and at the centre of a circular one. Extract Curve: 3D polygons along the edges, closed round a loop. Extract Iso-parametric Curves: a 3D polygon through the click, Along (U) or Across (V) the face. Extract Surface: the face as an open mesh, or with Create Planar Objects a closed 3D polygon round each of a flat face's loops. Preferences: Select Tangent Entities, Select Faces, Select All Entities, Create Planar Objects. Rodeo's limits, as there's no NURBS kernel: an edge is a crease or an open rim on the triangles, with a faceted curve's pieces joined back into one by how evenly they turn; curves are 3D polygons, not NURBS; a curve runs through a point just two chosen edges share, so a whole box gives twelve lines and a top face one closed loop, which may not be how Vectorworks divides them; a mesh has no real U and V, so Along runs a flat face's longest side, a curved face's steadiest direction, or up a ball, and an iso-curve is a slice through the face at the click rather than a line of the surface's own; Create Planar Objects makes 3D polygons, not 2D objects on a plane; the curve isn't shown before the click; back faces can't be picked with Option or Alt; Shift+L isn't assigned.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's 3D Locus: each click places a 3D locus, a point in space that doesn't print, and selects it. In Top/Plan it lies at the snapped cursor at the Elevation in its Preferences above the layer's plane; in the 3D view a click on a face puts it on that face, and a click on nothing puts it on the active working plane, or else at that Elevation. Object Info gives its X, Y and Z (Z above the layer), and it moves, turns, lifts with its layer, saves and opens again. It shows as a cross in plan, the same size as a 2D locus's, and as a cross about 200 mm across in 3D. Format: a record of type 9 and 126 bytes, read off the ten in the demos, all at the origin: its three numbers are taken as x, y, z as a 3D polygon's are, by analogy, so a file from Vectorworks with loci off the origin could show them in the wrong place until that's checked against one (FORMAT.md). Rodeo's limits: it can't be clicked in the 3D view, only in Top/Plan (the 3D view picks faces); 3D snapping to a locus isn't there; it has no attributes; Shift+0 isn't assigned because tool keys are letters.
- [x] AEC > Survey Input > 3D Polygons to 3D Loci…: a 3D locus at each vertex of the 3D polygons selected on the active layer, at the vertex's height, with Delete original 3D polygons as asked; one undo step. Vectorworks' site model isn't in Rodeo, so they don't feed one.
- [x] Modify > Convert > Stake Object from 3D Locus…: Convert to Stake Object takes Style, Label Reference (with the Stake tool's list), ID Prefix, Auto ID Numbering, (Initial) ID Number, Description, Display Leader Line, Scale Factor and Delete Source Loci, and puts a stake at each selected 3D locus, its Z-value the locus's height above the layer. Rodeo's limits: a stake is plain objects in plan with its height only in its label; Mode and Site Model aren't asked as there's no site model to modify; Coordinate Units are the document's; no Station Point Data.
- [x] Tool Sets: 3D Modeling's 3D Polygon: corners clicked one by one, double-click or Enter ending it open and a click on the first corner closing it. In Top/Plan they lie at the Elevation in its Preferences above the layer's plane; in the 3D view a click on a face puts a corner on that face, and a click on nothing puts one at that Elevation, with a bar over the view to Finish or Clear. **Not as in Vectorworks:** no Push/Pull or Push/Pull Combine modes; in the 3D view corners don't snap to points on the model, only onto the face clicked, and there's no rubber band from the last corner; working planes don't set where a click on nothing lands.
- [x] Tool Sets: Visualization's Light tool (point, spot or directional light objects, click to place or drag to aim)
- [x] Tool Sets: Insertion's Plug-in Insertion tool: places a copy of any kind of plug-in object the drawing has on its design layers (columns and beams, plants, cabinets, stairs, grid axes, railings, spaces, free-standing doors and windows...), its parameters and drawing the copy's, at a click or turned by a drag; and new objects of installed Vectorworks plug-ins, drawn by their script from their default parameters
- [x] Tool Sets: Visualization's Camera tool (press at the eye and drag to the look-to point, or click to look north, at its Preferences' eye height)
- [x] Tool Sets: Visualization's Video Camera (Spotlight's), after Camera: a click where it stands and a click (or drag) the way it faces places a video camera, a plug-in object Object Info edits: Position, Name, Activate Camera (or a double-click: the 3D view in perspective from the lens's front, the angle across its picture that the lens's focal length and the body's sensor give in the format, a toast naming them), Show Field of View (None, Lines or Filled, reaching Field of View Length), Format (Camera Default, 16:9, 4:3, 1.85:1, 2.39:1, 1:1, 9:16 or Custom Aspect with Custom Format), Fine Tune Camera's Aim Camera (pans toward the next click) and Pan Left/Right (±90°), Tilt Up/Down (±90°) and Rotate Camera L/R (±20°), Lens with Focal Length and Diagonal Angle of View kept in step and within the lens's range, Camera Body with its CCD/Sensor Format, and Stand Type (Tripod, Pedestal or None) with Use symbol for stand and Height (the lens's). It draws its stand, its body and lens as the head turns and tilts them, and its field of view in plan, and the camera on its stand as solids in 3D. Tool Preferences set the lens, focal length, body, format, stand, height and field of view for the next. Rodeo's reading: a plug-in of Rodeo's own ('Rodeo Video Camera'), not Vectorworks' Video Camera; its lenses and bodies are seven generic lenses and four sensor sizes, not Vectorworks' library symbols and records (so no Minimum Focus Distance, Aperture, Maximum Resolution, wheels, gimbal or articulation points), and its plan and 3D are Rodeo's simple drawing; Activate Camera looks through it without rolling or a viewfinder frame, the 3D window showing the picture's height and as much to the sides as its own shape allows, and isn't linked to the camera afterwards; Aim Camera pans only, refusing a point behind it; no Render Mode, Auto update 3D view, Focus Point, Crop Frame Scale, camera effects, classes, equipment parts or inventory, automatic numbering, or linking to a sheet viewport
- [x] Tool Sets: Visualization's Heliodon tool (click to place a copy of a Heliodon the drawing has, set to its Preferences' latitude, date and solar time, its lights turned to the sun; one undo step)
- [x] Tool Sets: Visualization's Render Bitmap, after Heliodon: a marquee dragged round part of the drawing, in Top/Plan or over the 3D view, is drawn as a bitmap and placed on the active layer where the area was, selected, in one undo step; a click without dragging takes the whole window. Its Preferences are the Render Bitmap Options: Mode (the 3D view's render modes, Wireframe to Path Traced), Resolution (DPI, 10 to 2400, dots per inch on paper at the active layer's scale) and Enable Alpha Transparency (the background left clear). Path Traced shows a progress window whose Finish now keeps the picture so far and Cancel gives up; the photographic modes are kept as JPEG unless alpha is on, the line modes as PNG. Rodeo's reading: in the 3D view the area is drawn from the view's camera and the image placed where the same part of the screen lies in Top/Plan, which is then shown, as Rodeo has no screen plane; in Top/Plan the plan is drawn as it shows, whatever the mode; alpha comes from the picture drawn on black and on white; there's no background rendering or Cancel Rendering in Object Info, the progress window standing in; a bitmap is kept to 8000 pixels a side (the dpi lowered, with a note); a drawing must already hold an image for one to be added, as for Import Image
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Break Line (a line with a zigzag break at its middle, its height on paper in Preferences) and Insulation (batt loops as one polyline, or rigid insulation's outline crossed corner to corner, grouped; to the left of the line drawn, centred on it or to its right; the thickness in Preferences), each a drag or two clicks, Shift in 15° steps, drawn as plain objects where Vectorworks makes plug-in objects
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Repetitive Unit (bricks or blocks of a size in Preferences laid in courses along a line, stretcher bonded and cut at its ends, grouped), Steel Section (wide flange, channel, angle, tee, I-beam or Z-section outlines of a size in Preferences, without root fillets, the I-beam's flanges sloping 1 in 6 inside from their mean thickness; rectangular, square or round tubing as one outline with its inside joined in as a hole across the wall at the right side's middle, so a short seam line crosses the wall there, rectangular corners rounded to twice the wall outside and the wall inside, round tubing 64-sided) and Lumber (a nominal size's dressed section, or a custom one, crossed for a continuous member or with one diagonal for blocking); the sections take a click, or a press dragged to turn them (Shift in 15° steps); plain objects where Vectorworks makes plug-in objects
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Steel Section - 3D, as Vectorworks' Angle - 3D, Channel - 3D, I-Beam - 3D, Rectangular, Round and Square Tubing - 3D, Tee - 3D, Wide Flange - 3D and Z-Section - 3D, in one tool with the shape on keys 1 to 9: a click places the section (a press dragged turns it, Shift in 15° steps) as an extrude of the Length in Preferences, standing on the layer, beside the same sizes as Steel Section. Rodeo's reading: sizes are typed, not picked from a series; no root fillets, as in 2D; a plain extrude, so it doesn't redraw from its size afterwards; laid along the plan, turn it with Rotate 3D
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Bulb Flat, Shaft Break and Shaft Break 2. Bulb Flat: Size from EN 10067's hot-rolled bulb flats as Corus Special Profiles lists them (HP 120 x 6 to HP 430 x 20, 59 sizes), or Custom Size with Width, Thickness, Bulb Height, Bulb Width, Bulb Radius and Fillet Radius; Place Locus at Centroid; Lock Centroid Position (the centroid on the click instead of the web's bottom middle); placed by a click, a press dragged turns it; the section's bulb angle, area, centroid, moments of inertia, section moduli and radii of gyration about X-X and Y-Y, in mm, listed in the Preferences note for the size as it opens (every listed size's area, centroid and moments come within 2.3% of Corus' table). Shaft Break: Shaft Diameter, Width of Break, Configuration Solid or Hollow and Wall Thickness; an S from the shaft's top into a drop-shaped loop to its bottom, a hollow shaft's bore a smaller drop inside it, as VW's help pictures draw it; a click on the axis, a press dragged turns it. Shaft Break 2: the same Preferences, a click where the shaft breaks off and one where it carries on (or a drag), a break at each end facing the gap. Rodeo's reading: the bulb's underside is the line that meets the web the bulb width down and touches the tip's circle, so the bulb angle follows from the other figures (30° for every listed size) and can't be typed; the square corners take EN 10067's largest radius for the thickness; section moduli are to the farthest edge, as Engineering Properties gives them (Corus' Y-Y modulus is to the web's back); VW's other Series aren't known, so only EN 10067 is listed; VW's Shaft Break 2 is one object whose second break is set by a control point, and the second break's turn (half round from the first) is Rodeo's; plain objects where VW makes plug-in objects, so nothing is editable from Object Info afterwards; no Bulb Flat - 3D
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Bolt (a hex-head bolt from the side along a line, its diameter in Preferences, threaded for twice the diameter and 6 mm) and Weld (an AWS welding symbol: the arrow from the joint to the reference line, a fillet or V-groove on the arrow side, the other side or both, a circle for all around; its height on paper in Preferences; no size or length text), each a drag or two clicks; grouped plain objects where Vectorworks makes plug-in objects
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Plant (a plant in plan, a scalloped or round canopy of the spread in Preferences round a crossed trunk; a click places one, or in Row a line places them at the spacing set, each plant its own group and all selected), plain objects where Vectorworks makes plant objects with a plant record
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Screw (countersunk, threaded from two fifths of the way down), Nail (flat head) and Anchor (a wedge anchor: nut, washer, threaded rod, split sleeve and flared tip), from the side along a drag from head to tip, the diameter in Preferences; plain objects where Vectorworks makes plug-in objects
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Carriage Bolt and Lag Screw, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) head up on the bearing face under its head, in Front, Side, Top or Bottom on keys 1 to 4, with Show Center Line. Carriage Bolt: Series Inch or Metric; Bolt Type Round Head (ASME B18.5 Table 1, #10 to 7/8), Round Head Short Square Neck (Table 3, 1/4 to 3/4) and Round Head Square Neck (Table 2, #10 to 1) in inch, Round Head Square Neck (ASME B18.5.2.2M, M5 to M24) and Round Head Square Neck with Large Head (DIN 603, M5 to M20) in metric; Size; Length under the head; Use Standard Length Increments; Thread Style Simplified (ASME), Simplified (ISO), Schematic or Detailed; threads 2D + 1/4 in (2D + 1/2 in past 6 in) or 2d + 6, 12 or 25 mm. Lag Screw: Series; Head Type Hex or Square (inch only; ASME B18.2.1, #10 to 1-1/4) and metric Hex (B18.2.3.8M, M5 to M24) with their threads' pitch and root; Nominal Size; Length under the head; Show Threads (crests and roots, or the root hidden); threaded half the length and 1/2 in, at most 6 in, leaving the standard's shoulder plain. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it or the tables leave it out: a type or size the series lacks moves to Round Head Square Neck or the nearest size (VW's lists follow the series), heads are the tables' largest, the carriage head a circular dome on a rim (B18.5.2.2M's edge thickness C, a quarter of the head's height where the standard gives none), the square neck seen across its corners from the front and side, standard lengths the inch steps of 1/4 in to 4 in, 1/2 in to 6 in and inches past or ISO 888's (B18.5 lists none and DIN 603's per-size lists aren't used), a gimlet point tapering over 1.25 diameters as VW pictures it (no standard dimensions it), and a metric lag screw's plain shoulder half a diameter with the inch thread rule in mm (B18.2.3.8M's isn't published freely); no 3D versions
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Wood Screw and Sheet Metal Screw, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) head up on the bearing face, in Front, Side, Top or Bottom on keys 1 to 4, with Show Threads and Show Center Line; Slot Type Regular, Cross or Square on every head that takes one. Wood Screw: Series Inch (ASME B18.6.1, #0 to #24, round heads #2 to #14) or Metric (DIN 97, 95 and 96 slotted and DIN 7997, 7995 and 7996 cross recessed, 1.6 to 10 mm); Head Type Flat Countersunk, Oval Countersunk, Pan or Round; Nominal Size; Length under the head (a flat or oval head's overall); Use Standard Length Increments; threaded two thirds of the length (0.6 in metric) to a gimlet point. Sheet Metal Screw: Series Inch (ASME B18.6.4, #0 to #14 and 1/4 to 1/2) or Metric (ISO 1478 threads, ST2.2 to ST9.5); Head Type Flat, Oval, Truss, Pan, Hex or Hex Washer (ISO 1482/7050, 1483/7051, 1481/7049, 1479 and 7053); Point Type A, AB or B; Nominal Size; Length; threaded to the head. A head or size a type doesn't come in is refused with the range it does. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it or the tables leave it out: a size of the other series moves to its nearest; heads are the tables' largest; DIN gives no pan head or wood thread pitch, so a metric wood pan head and thread take the inch ones' proportions at the nearest diameter, as a metric truss head does; a square recess is ASME B18.6.3's for pan heads, scaled to the size, a metric cross recess 0.6 of its width deep, and a recessed inch pan head taller by B18.6.3's ratio of recessed to slotted height; Type A is inch only, so metric takes AB, drawn as ISO's pointed type C, and B as its flat-ended type F; hex heads drawn plain with any slot type (ASME gives them no recess, ISO no slot); A and AB points taper over 1.25 diameters as VW pictures them (no standard dimensions it); standard wood lengths are inch steps or DIN 95 to 97's list. The hex washer's bottom view (and the Fastener tool's flanged heads') now hides the hex behind the washer; no 3D versions
- [x] Tool Sets: Furniture/Fixtures' Table and Chairs (a rectangular table seating a chair per 600 mm along both long sides and optionally at its ends, or a round one with chairs evenly round it; a click places it, a press dragged turns it) and Space Planning's Space (a rectangle or a clicked polygon with its name and its area in the drawing's units at its centroid); grouped plain objects where Vectorworks makes plug-in and Space objects
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Hardscape (an outline with a border inside it and square paving joints laid from the drawing's origin, so neighbouring areas line up), Landscape Area (a cloud boundary, or as drawn, with its name and area), Property Line (each side's surveyor's bearing and length beside it, read the right way up) and Parking Space (a row of stalls to the left of a dragged curb at 90°, 60° or 45°, their width and depth in Preferences), the outline ones a rectangle or clicked polygon as Space draws them; Building Shell's Column (rectangular with its diagonals or round with crossed diameters; a click places it, a press dragged turns it) and Stair (a straight stair dragged foot to head: its treads to the nearest whole number at the going set, the walk line and arrow, the break line and "UP"). Grouped plain objects in plan where Vectorworks makes plug-in objects with 3D
- [x] Tool Sets: Furniture/Fixtures' Desk (straight, or with a left or right return running forward from the front, and its chair), Shelving Unit (the sides, back and shelf as cut in plan, the panels' thickness and the back set in Preferences), Clothes Rod (the rod's centre line under an optional shelf), Bath-Shower (a bathtub with a rounded basin and its drain at an offset from the end, or a shower tray with falls to a middle drain) Grab Bars (a bar standing off the wall at a clearance, its ends turning back to flange plates), Fireplace (a unit narrowing from its front width to its rear width against the wall, its firebox opening and depth, and an optional top flue at an offset from the back) and Toilet Stall (a row of stalls with left, dividing, right and front partitions as set, a door in each front at an offset from the left partition swinging in or out, a toilet, and an optional 60 in turning circle) and Cabinet (Base, Utility and Wall modes: a base cabinet with an optional counter overhanging its front and a back splash, a utility cabinet crossed, a wall cabinet with one diagonal, since a blank drawing has no dashed line type); a click puts the middle of the fixture's back on the point and a press dragged turns it along the wall. Grouped plain 2D objects in the active class, where Vectorworks makes plug-in objects with 3D, styles and Resource Manager configurations; the Vectorworks help gives these objects' parameters but not what they draw in Top/Plan, so the plan drawings are Rodeo's own. Ceiling Grid: a rectangle or clicked polygon with the lines between tiles of the width and length set, at an angle, a tile centred in it or the grid laid from the drawing's origin (Vectorworks draws it by clicked vertices; its own tile start isn't documented). Seating Section: a rectangle or clicked polygon filled with rows of chairs facing a set direction, the first row at the section's front, seats a set spacing apart centred on its middle (Align Rows) or every other row shifted half a seat (Offset Rows), placed where they fit inside; Vectorworks' seat symbols, numbering, curved sections, rise and Shift/Distribute Rows aren't offered. Not drawn: the toilet stall's accessible icon and grab bars, the cabinets' doors, drawers, kicks and handles, the fireplace's two- and three-sided configurations and back flue
- [x] Tool Sets: Building Shell's Simple Stair, Ramp, Elevator and Escalator, and Furniture/Fixtures' Compartment Sink, in plan (a click, or a press dragged to turn them; Shift in 15° steps). Simple Stair: Width, Flr-Flr Height, Max Riser (the fewest risers no taller), Tread Depth, Config (Straight, Straight with Landing, Left/Right Landing, Left/Right Double-back Even and Irregular), Landing Tread, Separation, Offset 1/2, Curved Landing, Stringer Width/Offset, Draw Lower Stair Break, Show Data ("UP 16 R at 175" per flight on the arrow), Draw Top Tread, Left/Right Rail, Rail Width, Arrows Up/Down/None. Ramp: Width, Height, Rise over Run (a fraction or a number), the same configurations, Landing Height, Offset 1/2, Curved landing, 2D Stair Break, Left/Right Rail, Rail W, Arrows. Elevator: Hoistway Width/Depth, Front Door (Cab and Wall Doors, Cab Door Only), Rear Door (None too), Door Type (Single Slide, Side Bi-pass, Bi-part, Two-Speed Bi-part), Door Width, Door Offset, Cab Display, Internal Cab Width/Depth, Cab Wall Thickness, Cab Offset X, Front Cab Clearance; the doors drawn open beside their openings, the cab crossed. Escalator: Height, Slope angle (27.3°, 30°, 35°), Step width (600, 800, 1000 mm), Horizontal lead in (2–4 steps), Balustrade type (Glass, Solid), Section cut (None, Up, Down), Floor depth above, Show pit, Show headroom. Compartment Sink: Ctr Length/Width, Ctr Edge Dia (the rolled edge's inner line), Splash Thk, Show Bowls, Num Bowls, Bowl Length/Width, Bowl Corner Radii, Bowl Offset Length/Width, Dim Between Bowls, a drain in each bowl. Rodeo's reading: VW's help lists these parameters without their plan graphics, so the drawings are Rodeo's own: the cut for breaks is 1200 mm above the floor, slanted at 60°, ending what's drawn there; Offset 1 lengthens a landing along the first flight and Offset 2 reaches a turning one further toward the turn; an escalator's 400 mm steps, 900 mm landing plates, 300/350 mm balustrades, the pit (bottom landing plus 1000 mm of truss) and the headroom opening (where the steps come within 2300 mm of the upper floor's underside) are Rodeo's sizes, its Type and the span and height overrides aren't offered; heights, thicknesses, styles, rails' heights, legs and braces and all the 3D aren't drawn (blank drawings can't make 3D objects); each is grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Furniture/Fixtures' Railing/Fence, Site Planning's Stake and Building Shell's Pilaster, in plan. Railing/Fence: click each corner of the path, a double-click or Enter ending it open and a click on the first corner closing it; Offset from Path (positive to the left of the way it's drawn), Top Rail with its width or diameter and Top rail ends at post, Post or No Post, Display posts in 2D view, the post's profile (Round, Rectangular, Octagon) and size, Place post at starting point and at end point with their distances, Place post at corner or Place two posts at corner with the distance, the Arrangement (Approximate Distance, Fixed Distance, Number of Posts), and Frame/Panel (vertical frame bars, round or rectangular, at no more than the maximum distance; horizontal bars; a panel of its thickness), drawn in plan under the rail with the posts over it. Stake: Standard Insertion (a click, or a press dragged to where the label goes, with the leader line) and Poly-Vertex Placement (a stake at each corner clicked); Z-value, the eight Styles (None, Triangle, Filled Triangle, Circle, Dot, Cross, Circle Cross, Tick Mark), Scale Factor, Mark Offset X/Y, Mark Rotation, Label Reference (ID, Elevation, Description, ID/Elevation, ID/Description, Description/Elevation, Coordinate Point NE, EN and XY, No Label), Label Position (Auto, Right, Left, Center), the six Bubble Styles (None, Box, Round Rect, Cloud, Hexagonal, Circle), RR Corner Radius, Text Margin, ID Prefix/Suffix, Auto ID Numbering from the Initial ID Number, Show Elevation, Show Unit Mark, Display Leader Line. Pilaster: a click puts the middle of the shaft on the wall's face, a press dragged turns it along the wall with the shaft to the drag's right; Use Component (Architectural Only, Structural Only, both), Shaft Type (Rectangular, Oval: a half oval off the wall), Width, Depth, Corner Radius, Use Base and Use Capital with their width and depth (outlined round the shaft), Struct Type (Concrete, Wood, Steel), Struct Shape, Width, Depth, Offset X/Y, Rotation, Clearance (cut round the structural part), Center Marks with their size, Column ID with Display Column ID. Rodeo's reading: plan only and Rodeo's own graphics where VW's help shows none; a railing's path is straight segments (no arcs or 3D Line mode), wall brackets, fascia mount posts, per-post position edits, custom profiles and symbols, miters and the rail and post heights aren't offered; a stake's coordinates are from the drawing's origin (Rodeo keeps no user origin), it isn't site model data, a site modifier or 3D, and Stationing Data, latitude and longitude, its own coordinate and elevation units and precision aren't offered; a pilaster isn't inserted in a wall (it stands on the face as placed), a steel part is a wide flange of its width and depth rather than one chosen from VW's shape library, the column ID goes in front of the pilaster instead of on its insertion point with a control point, and its heights, bounds, base divisions, top and base plates and finishes aren't drawn; each is grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Fence (also in Building Shell and Event), in Top/Plan. Click each corner of its path, a double-click or Enter ending it open, a click on the first corner closing it. General: Draw 2D Simple Line, Line with Square Posts, Line with Round Posts or Full Detail; Offset from Path (positive to the path's right); Fence Face, the side the infill goes. Posts: Use post at start and at end; Corner Post Alignment with the previous segment, the next, or the angle between; Post Spacing Fixed Distance (stepped from each segment's start, its last bay shorter) or Equal Spacing (bays near the distance); a Square or Round post's Width and Depth; Add footer Square or Round with its Drainage Layer, drawn below grade. Rails: Number of Rails Vertically (0 draws none), Rail Width, Continuous rail, Rail Offset to Post (Centered, Flush with Front or Rear, Placed on Front, Custom Offset from Center), Switch side for the offset every other segment, Rail extends beyond post side. Infill: Rails Only, Panel, Pre-Constructed (its width spacing the posts, centred on them), Vertical Boards (at the Optimal Spacing, turned by Rotate vertical infill diagonally) or Horizontal Boards, on the rails' front at the Infill Offset, between posts or with Continuous coverage; Add gravel board. Refused with a message: a spacing or size of 0, more than 2000 posts or 10000 boards, or an offset that folds the path. Rodeo's reading: plan only; posts' Width runs along the fence, Depth front to back; rails one over another fall in one place in plan, so their heights aren't asked; footers and drainage are drawn in the drawing's first dashed line type and left out in a drawing without one; styles, profile, rail, infill and gate symbols and gate configurations, heights, raked or stepped configuration, sending to the surface, run and corner posts set apart, classes and 3D aren't drawn or kept; grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Curb, in Top/Plan, its Vertex mode: click each corner of its front edge, a double-click or Enter ending it open, a click on the first corner closing it. Draw 2D Simple Line (the front edge), Front and Back Lines (the back edge in the drawing's first dashed line type, solid without one) or Full Detail: the curb its Width to the side it lies (left or right of the path), a piece between each pair of corners mitred where they meet, cut into units the Unit Length long from each piece's start (0 for one extruded piece) with a Paver Joint Width between, joints that would cross a mitre left out, and the foundation under it, dashed, reaching in front and behind. Refused with a message: a width of 0, a joint as wide as the units, more than 5000 joints, or a curb too wide for its corners. Rodeo's reading: plan only; one curb configuration of straight units stands for the style's extrudes and symbols; Associated and Freestanding Pick, curb styles, path, corner and insertion symbols, radius statements, border rows, corner treatments other than mitres, elevations and the vertical insertion offset, site modifiers, slope annotations, specifications and 3D aren't drawn or kept; grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Hedgerow, in Top/Plan, its Vertex mode: click each corner of its path, a double-click or Enter ending it open, a click on the first corner closing it into a ring (the band filled between its two edges, the edges drawn over the fill). Align (Center, or Left or Right of the path), Width and Smooth corners (the outside of each corner rounded about the path's corner; otherwise mitred, cut square past four times the offset); Border Style Standard (a single line), Plant Cloud (billows about the Billow Size long rising the Billow Height, Convex out of the hedge or Concave into it, up to the Billow Variability bigger than each other, starting afresh at each sharp corner) or Plant Line (segments the Segment Length long straying up to half the Segment Thickness either side); Density by plants per metre or square metre (per foot or square foot in imperial drawings) and up to seven plants typed with their percentages (100% at the most), reported after drawing as Hedge Length, area and each plant's count.
