Refused with a message: a width or density of 0, plants over 100%, a path turning too tightly for the width, or more than 50000 edge points. Rodeo's reading: plant styles from the Resource Selector, Height and 3D, site model draping, worksheets and plant lists, Edit Path, Create Objects from Shapes and Polyline modes other than Vertex aren't offered; the cloud's and plant line's randomness is Rodeo's own; grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Existing Tree, in Top/Plan: a click places a tree (a press dragged turns it) with its Preferences' Botanical and Common Name, Action (Retain, Remove with a cross, Transplant Original with its canopy dashed, Transplant Proposed with an inner ring), Significance (Low, Moderate, High, Hazardous, as a fill), numbering (Auto Number with a prefix, leading zeros and next number, or a typed Tree No), Canopy Max and Min Diameter and Rotate, DBH, Root Display (Critical and Primary Root Zones, 2.5 and 5 x DBH unless set, or a Root Ball), and Tree Protection (a circular zone or 12 x DBH), grouped in the active class, named by the number, and tagged with it and the names. Not Vectorworks' plug-in object: plain objects, so changing a tree's Action later means placing it again. Top/Plan only with no 3D tree (Height and First Branch aren't offered), no species list or Condition/arborist fields, no irregular canopy or protection zone, no custom symbols; the fill colours are Rodeo's own; numbering isn't carried to duplicates or kept per layer; the same number twice goes without a name since a drawing's names are all different; zones and a transplant's outline are dashed only if the drawing has a dashed line type, like Rodeo's other dashed pieces; nothing reaches a worksheet.
- [x] Tool Sets: MEP's Piping Run, and AEC > Electrical > Create Piping Runs turning selected polygons and polylines into runs in their place: Conduit Size, Material, Remarks, Show Tag, Show Remarks, Section Length, Num of Runs, and Turns as Use Elbows (corners, counted as 45s and 90s; 135° is one of each) or Use Bends (corners rounded to Radius, or as much as their segments hold, the arcs in the length), reporting Length, Num of Sections and Couplings. Not Vectorworks' plug-in object: a group of plain objects named Piping Run N with the figures in a toast and a Piping Run record. They're worked out once, so reshaping the path doesn't update them. Turns at a junction box aren't left out of the count, as Vectorworks does; the material list is Conduit Sizing's, as the help doesn't name one.
- [x] Tool Sets: MEP's HVAC Straight Duct (dragged end to end), Elbow Duct (Inner Radius, Angle, Turn), Flex Duct (clicked points it curves smoothly through, with its wire's hoops across it), Vertical Duct (a plan symbol, a cross or one diagonal, rising from Bottom Elevation by Rise), Transition (rectangular or round at each end, Length, centered or flat on one side), Splitter (Width, Length to Fork, a width for each branch, Branch Length, Branch Angle, a tee at 90°), Damper (the duct's section with a blade and handle), Diffuser (square or round, Face Size, Neck Size, Height, hanging from Face Elevation) and Outlet (a grille's plate and louvres at Center Elevation, with the air's arrow): each the plan and a solid in a group, a straight duct, elbow, riser, transition, splitter and damper taking Shape, Width and Height (a round one's diameter), Center Elevation, Show Label (a Label string with {W} and {H} filled in the drawing's units, a round one's saying its diameter) and Show Elevation Marker (its top and bottom above the layer). The 2025 help has only a general HVAC page and points to a Duct_Object.pdf that isn't online, so all of their parameters beyond width, height, label and marker are Rodeo's reading, and the shapes are too; Vectorworks' come from its libraries as many items. Not modelled: flanges and joints, duct insulation, joining fittings automatically, dragging a label or marker by a control point (they're text in the group), Show 3D Detail, and the Diffuser, Register and Grille report (Rodeo keeps no record on them). Rectangular splitters only. Not opened in Vectorworks.
- [x] Tool Sets: Furniture/Fixtures' Custom Cabinet and Site Planning's Roadway (Straight), Roadway (Curved) and Roadway (Tee), in plan. Custom Cabinet: a click puts the middle of its back on the point with its front down, a press dragged turns it (Shift in 15° steps); Height, Width, Depth and Show Diagonals in Top/Plan view. Roadway (Straight): a drag, or a click at each end of its centre line; Paving Width, Curb Width (0 for none: the paving's edges drawn instead) and Show Joints. Roadway (Curved): a click puts the start of its centre line on the point, a press dragged sets the way it leaves; Radius (to the centre line), Width, Curb Width, Sweep (positive turning left, negative right) and Show Joints, with a locus at the arc's centre. Roadway (Tee): a click puts the middle of the throat's open end on the point, a press dragged points it into the tee; Radius of the fillets either side, Throat Width, Gutter Width, Curb Width, Draw Gutter Curb and Show Joints, with loci at the fillets' centres. Each refuses settings that can't draw (a curved road's inner curb past its centre, a tee's curb wider than its radius). The paving has no pen and takes the drawing's fill; joints are in the drawing's first dashed line type (solid in a drawing without one). Rodeo's reading: plan only; a custom cabinet's columns, boxes, doors, drawers, plinth and scribe (its front and 3D views) aren't drawn, its height only kept for the next one; roadways aren't site modifiers and draw no 3D, slopes, cross slopes, curb heights or elevations; each is grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Parking Along Path, Parking Area and Roadway (Poly), in plan. Each is drawn as a path of straight runs: click each corner, a double-click or Enter ending it open, a click on its first corner closing it, Escape starting over. Parking Along Path: square stalls either side of each run (Spaces on Left of Poly and Spaces on Right of Poly, the left being the way it's drawn), Space Width and Space Length, each run's stalls centred in its room and stopping short where a corner turns towards their side, with dividers, the line along their far ends and the path itself; Space Numbering from Start Num At, the left side first. Parking Area: a closed outline (an area ended open closes anyway) filled with double rows of stalls back to back about header stripes at the Row Angle, each stall at the Space Angle (up to 90°) to its stripe, a Lane Width between rows and along the sides they face, as many rows as fit centred across it, kept where a stall lies wholly inside; Show Stripe, Show Perimeter and Space Numbering (top row first, left to right). Roadway (Poly): the paving mitred along the centre line clicked, Paving Width, Curb Width (0 for none) outside each edge and Show Joints at an open road's ends; a closed path makes a ring road. Each refuses what can't draw (no side chosen, nothing fits, an area too shallow for a double row, a path turning too tightly for the road's width), and stops at 2000 stalls. Rodeo's reading: straight runs only (no Bezier or arc vertices); no control point, origin or reshaping afterwards; no 3D, site model, stations, pads or grade limits; parking islands, end islands, accessible spaces and the stall count Vectorworks reports aren't offered; each is grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Parking Spaces: a click places a row (a press dragged turns it, or Rotation in Preferences), its header stripe running from the click and its stalls below it. Space Style Single Bay, Double Bay (a mirrored row above) or Parallel (Length along the curb, Width out from it, no bumpers); No. of Standard Spaces, Space Width, Space Length, Space Angle; Header Style Straight or Angled (square to each stall, a sawtooth, a double bay's rows moved apart so their teeth just meet); No. of Accessible Spaces, Accessible Width, Accessible Location, Show Accessible Symbol (Rodeo's own drawing of the international symbol, in place of the library's symbol that can be edited), Add Accessible Aisle Spaces, Aisle Width, Aisle Location, Aisle Fill; Show Header, Start and End Stripe; Space Numbering from Start Numbering At, left to right and the upper row first; Show Bumpers; Show 3D Stripes (a mesh of the stripes painted on the layer in the drawing's pen colour, and bumpers standing on it). Plain objects in a group, not an object you can edit afterwards in Object Info, so a change means placing it again. Rodeo's readings, since the help shows no pictures or lists of these: stalls below the header, the counts for each row in a double bay, an aisle or two for each accessible space, the Aisle Location choices (Left, Right, Both Sides) and Aisle Fill's (None, Solid, Stripes, where Vectorworks offers the drawing's fills), bumpers' size and place, and a Text size in place of Text Style. Space Height, which Vectorworks keeps for IFC only, isn't asked for. Not opened in Vectorworks.
- [x] Tool Sets: Event Design's Stage Deck, Stage Plug, Stage Steps, Stage Ramp and Rope and Stanchion, in Top/Plan, in a new Event Design tool set. Stage Deck: a click puts its middle on the point, a press dragged turns its front toward the pointer (a click faces it down the screen); Deck Shape Rectangular (Width, Depth) or Round (Radius, a Sweep Angle centred on its front, 360° a whole circle); Structure Simple, Border, Deck Only, Legs-Basic or Legs-Braced, with Hide Deck showing a braced deck's legs and braces alone; Leg Diameter, Leg Profile (round, square, octagonal) and Reference Spacing; a railing on each side (front, right, back, left; a round deck's start radius, arc and end radius): None, Straight, Toe or Straight + Toe. Stage Plug: a clicked outline, closed even when ended open, with the deck's structures but Legs-Braced. Stage Steps: a click puts the middle of their top on the point, a drag turning the way they come down; Structure Simple, Solid, Open or Frame Free-Standing or Self-Adjusting (its steps spread over the floor its Stringer Length reaches from the Stage Height), Number of Steps, Add Top Step at Stage Height, Step Unit Width and Depth, stringers beside or under the treads with Add'l Stringers and Side Stringer Indent, left and right railings with Add'l Railings, and an Up or Down arrow with UP or DN. Stage Ramp: placed as the steps; Top and Bottom Height, Width, and its floor length Created Based On a Ramp Length, Floor Length, pitch Angle, Ratio or Percent; Simple or Legs-Basic; railings and arrow as the steps. Rope and Stanchion: a clicked path (a click on its first corner closes it); stanchions at each corner and evenly between, no further apart than the Ideal Spacing or the belt's, rope's or chain's length, with End Stanchions Both, Start, End or None; round or square bases of Base Width round each Post Diameter; a belt as a line, a rope as a band its diameter wide, a chain as links flat and on edge by turns. Legs, braces and inside stringers out of sight under a deck are drawn in the drawing's first dashed line type and left out in a drawing without one; deck-only legs are loci. Each refuses what can't draw (a deck too small for its legs, self-adjusting stringers shorter than the stage is high, a pitch of 90° or more, a ramp no higher at its top) and stops at 2000 legs or stanchions. Rodeo's reading: plan only; heights, thicknesses, trims, casters, colours and textures, 3D options, labels (Text Options, Purpose/Set Cart ID, Note), the class options and Leg Total / Total Stanchions readouts aren't drawn or kept; the Top and Bottom Insertion Point modes aren't offered (they only differ in height); railings are a 40 mm top rail or a 25 mm toe board seen from above, their uprights under them; ramps and steps run straight (no curved ramps); each is grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit. Aisle, Stage Lift, Stage Plug's reshaping and the rest of Event Design aren't here
- [x] Event Design's Create Lectern (Spotlight > Event in Vectorworks; Stage > Event here): a dialog chooses one of five lecterns (Classic Wood, Modern Panel, Acrylic, Column, Tabletop), previewed from the front, the side and above with its size; a click sets where it stands and a second the way its front faces the audience (Shift 15°), Esc giving up. It goes in as an instance of a hybrid symbol of the lectern's name, its outlines in Top/Plan and solids in 3D (a reading desk sloped 15° with a book stop), made the first time and placed again after that. Custom Attributes makes a new symbol under the name typed, its parts in a class chosen or made there (the class then sets fill, pen and texture) or in None with the Fill Color, Pen Color and Texture set in the dialog. Over a stage built in 3D (an extrude, a mesh, a solid, or a group or symbol holding them) it rises to the top face under the first click, up to 3 m above the layer. Limits: Rodeo's lecterns are generic shapes, not Vectorworks' lectern symbols or their names; Rodeo's Stage Deck is plain 2D with no height, so a lectern placed on one stays on the layer; the instance goes in the active class; the symbols aren't checked in Vectorworks.
- [x] Event Design's Create Stage, Create Stair and Create Screen (Spotlight > Event in Vectorworks; Stage > Event here). Create Stage: with closed shapes selected (rectangle, polygon, closed polyline, circle), a dialog sets the Stage Height, Standard Stage Deck Width and Depth (8' x 4' and 2' high in feet and inches, else 2 m x 1 m and 600), Structure (Simple, Border, Deck Only, Legs-Basic), Top Thickness, Leg Diameter, Leg Profile, Leg Reference Spacing, Minimize Leg Count, Class, Fill and Pen Color and Texture (for the None class) and whether to delete the shape; standard decks lie on a grid square to the shape's longest side from a corner of it, a cell the edge cuts keeping what is inside as a smaller deck when it's still a rectangle, else as a stage plug (or several where the edge cuts it in pieces), so a rectangle takes decks alone; each deck or plug is drawn as the Stage Deck and Stage Plug tools draw it (legs out of sight dashed, a deck-only structure's legs as loci) with its 3D: a Simple or Border piece a block to the Stage Height, Deck Only its top alone, Legs-Basic its top on its legs; Minimize Leg Count drops a leg standing within one and a half diameters of a leg already under a neighbouring piece; one undo step, the pieces selected. Create Stair: a dialog sets the Width, Style (Simple, Self-Adjusting, Frame, Solid or Open Free-Standing), a Stage Height for where no stage is found, Class, Fill and Pen Color and Texture; a click where the steps meet the stage's edge and a click the way they come down (Shift 15 degrees); they rise to the top of the 3D stage found just behind the first click (as Create Stage builds one) in risers up to 180 mm, each tread 11" (280 mm) deep, else to the height given; drawn as the Stage Steps tool draws them with an UP arrow, with a 3D flight of blocks. Create Screen: a dialog picks a Front Projection Screen (goalpost or tripod), Rear Projection Screen, LED Video Screen, LED Screen (tile array) or Flat Panel Television and its width (a television's diagonal); a click where the middle of its face goes and a click the way it faces; drawn as the Video Screen, LED Screen and Television tools draw them, grouped. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks inserts Stage Deck, Stage Plug, Stage Steps and Video Screen plug-in objects that Object Info edits; here they're plain groups (a 2D plan with a mesh, shown as a hybrid shows) that Object Info can't re-edit; the grid's start and the sliver size left out (under 100 mm2) are Rodeo's choices; Trim Height, Add Casters, Purpose/Set Cart ID/Note and the 3D Options' per-part colours aren't offered, and Legs-Braced isn't (plugs have no braces); Minimize Leg Count is Rodeo's reading of 'supported by adjacent stages'; a stair's riser limit and tread depth are Rodeo's, and its 3D is solid blocks whatever its style, without stringers or railings; a screen's 3D, aspect choice and projector model aren't offered; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Event Design's Create Room and Create Views (Spotlight > Event in Vectorworks; Stage > Event here, in Vectorworks' order: Create Room, Create Stage, Create Stair, Create Lectern, Create Screen, Create Views). Create Room: with closed shapes selected (each the room's inside), a dialog sets the walls' Height, Style (Plain at the Thickness given, or any of the Wall tool's styles: copies of the drawing's walls, or its plain sizes in a drawing without walls), Thickness, Class, Fill and Pen Color (None class), the floor's Thickness, Class, Fill and Pen Color and Texture, and whether to delete the shape; real walls stand round each shape, their inner faces on its edge (a style's control line placed by its faces), each mitred to the one before and the last to the first, at the Height (10' or 3 m as it comes), with a floor under them all, out to the walls' outer faces, its top at the layer's elevation; one undo step, the walls and floor selected. Create Views: two sheet layers, Plan View with a plan viewport of the visible design layers (with their visibilities and the classes' as they are) at the active layer's scale, and Rendered View with a 1600 x 1000 picture of the model from Right Isometric in Shaded, framed, 400 mm across; Plan View is made active with the viewport selected. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' floor is a slab object; here it's a plain group (its outline in plan and its 3D, shown as a hybrid shows), as Rodeo lays slabs only on rectangles copied from one the drawing has; a wall style's texture and hatch aren't set from the dialog (Fill Hatch and wall Texture aren't offered), nor are its colours when it's a styled copy in a class; Vectorworks' rendered view is a viewport Update redraws, Rodeo's a picture taken when the command runs (run it again after changes), and a drawing that holds no picture yet (a new blank one) can't take one, so it gets Plan View alone, saying why; a name already taken gets a number after it; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: Event Design's Soft Goods, Television, Video Screen and LED Screen, in Top/Plan. Soft Goods: a clicked path (a double-click or Enter ends it, a click on its first corner closes it) with Function Curtain, Border or Pipe-and-Drape; pleats a Pleat Width long and Pleat Depth deep about the path (a border's to one side, Reverse side swapping it; Flip pleats), or Simple 2D as a line; Open From Left, Right or Center with the Opening Width and Center Overlap, the open stretch as a ghost; Add pipe and Add track (its Width); Show Markers at the start, end or both; Stock Drape Width, Minimum Overlap and Vertical Tied Fullness % counting the drapes, with Show drape breaks and Show overlap marking them; a border's Adjust Starting and Ending; pipe-and-drape's uprights on square base plates or round boom bases at each corner and evenly between at about the slider Target Length, End Hardware, Show slider(s) and Double wall. Television: a click puts the middle of its back on the point, facing the drag; TV Type Flat Panel, CRT or CRT Curved, Screen Aspect 4:3 or 16:9 (refused for a curved CRT), its Size across the diagonal, Add Adjustable Stand. Video Screen: the middle of the image's face on the point, facing the drag; Screen Type Front or Rear Projection or LED Screen, Aspect (4:3, 16:10, 16:9, 1.85:1, 2.39:1 or Custom) and Width (with Height when Custom), Frame (No Frame, Frame with its Depth, Border Only) with left and right border widths, Screen Support None, Goalpost Legs (Show Feet front, back, both or none), Tripod, Roll-Down or Roll-Up Case (Case Depth, a motor at one end), Show Centerline, Show Projector and Show Projection Cone at a Throw Ratio in front or behind. Television and Video Screen: Show Coverage Zone between the Near and Far Multipliers of the Zone Reference (width, height or diagonal) within the Max Horiz. Angle, drawn unfilled. LED Screen: Module Shape Tile Flat or Cap, Disk Flat or Cap, Tube (vertical or horizontal, its Length) or Dome, Module Width and Cap Depth; Build Array Based On Module Counts and Spacing, Overall Dimensions and Spacing, or Overall Dimensions and Module Counts; Base Structure Blocks, Frame, Curtain, Web, Vert. Strips, Horiz. Strips or None. Each refuses what can't draw (a stock drape narrower than the overlap at its fullness, pleats too fine for the run, a far zone not beyond the near, modules that don't fit the array). Rodeo's reading: plan only; a ghost curtain and centerline are drawn in the drawing's first dashed line type and left out in a drawing without one; drape, upright and module totals, running length and solenoids aren't shown; heights, trims, materials and finishes, weights, the traveler pull, slider option ranges and base pin location and shift, casing and projector models from libraries, stock screen sizes, dress kits, screen images, tilt, vertical viewing angles and viewing height, pixels, vertical module counts, labels and 3D aren't drawn or kept; module and casing depths (an LED tile 80 mm, a disk 40 mm, a flat panel 60 mm, a CRT about as deep as it's wide, a projector 500 by 400 mm) are Rodeo's; each is grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: MEP's Receptacle, Switch, Comm Device and Incandescent Fixture, and Event Design's Speaker, in Top/Plan. The MEP indicators take a click, or a press dragged to turn them, and stay the same size on paper (the 2D Scale Factor times the layer's scale): on a wall the end of the stem goes on the point, the symbol standing off toward the drag; on the ceiling or floor the circle is centred there. Receptacle: Config Simplex, Duplex, Triplex or Quad; Type Regular, Split-Wired, Special Purpose, Electric Range or Junction Box; Location Floor (boxed), Wall or Ceiling. Switch: Regular ($), Ceiling Pull, w/ Single Recep or w/ Double Recep. Comm Device: Telephone, Data, Data/Voice, TV/Cable, Watchman, Sound, Staff, Paging, Fire Alarm, Clock, Nurse Call, Pushbutton, Bell/Buzzer, Bell Transformer, Chime and Electric Door Opener. Incandescent Fixture: Mounting Surface, Recessed or Pendant, Location Wall or Ceiling, Type Down Light, Wall Wash or Standard Light, Emergency. Each has its Config Label and Rotate Label with Object (a turned label kept reading away from the symbol). Speaker: the middle on the point, its front facing the drag; a box with Front and Back Width and Height and Depth (the back no larger than the front), Flip orientation, or a Ceiling speaker's Grill and Cutout Diameter; Show dispersion with Horiz. Dispersion Full or Asymmetrical Left and Right, Vert. Dispersion, Throw Distance, Elevation, Listening Height and Actual Tilt Angle: left, right and centre lines from the front and the coverage shadow on the listening plane, drawn unfilled (a ceiling speaker's as a circle). Rodeo's reading: plan only; the indicators' sizes on paper (a 4 mm circle on a 4 mm stem) are Rodeo's, matched to Vectorworks' pictures; a wall washer's and an emergency light's halves are filled in the pen colour at half strength; a ceiling speaker's cutout is drawn in the drawing's first dashed line type and left out in a drawing without one; mounting heights, text styles, plug-in styles, circuiting records, 3D plates, catalog and symbol speakers, supports (arrays are Speaker Array's), expanded dispersion ranges, hatches, power, rigging load data, labels and 3D aren't drawn or kept; each is grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: Event Design's Speaker Array, in Top/Plan. A click puts the bumper's middle on the point, a press dragged turns its front toward the pointer (a click faces it down the screen). Column ID, drawn behind the bumper at 2.5 mm on paper; Bumper Width, Depth and Height, Trim Height (the bumper's underside above the floor) and Bumper Tilt; Speakers A, B and C, hung in that order, each with a Count (0 leaves it out, 48 boxes at most in all), Width, Height, Depth, Horiz. and Vert. Dispersion and Angle Relative to Preceding (the first from the bumper); Listening Height and Throw Distance; Override dispersion with array angles (a box's vertical spread becomes its angle from the one above); Show dispersion: left, right and centre lines from the front for the widest spread, the throw tilted by the shallowest box, a tick across the centre line where each box aims at the listening plane, and the coverage shadow, drawn unfilled, where the boxes' vertical spreads together meet that plane within the throw; Hanging Support One or Two hoists, with Show rigging guides crossing the bumper where they pick it up. Refused with a message: no boxes, more than 48, a box or bumper without a size, dispersion angles outside 0–180°, or the listening plane not below the bottom box. Rodeo's reading: plan only; each kind's outline is drawn once, in the drawing's first dashed line type where the bumper covers it (left out in a drawing without one); catalog speakers and bumpers, 3D and the array's elevation view, weights, loads and Braceworks, hatches, text styles, classes and the Speaker Array's report aren't drawn or kept; grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Layout's Rack Ruler (in Event Design, after Speaker Array), in Top/Plan. Click where the ruler's foot goes beside a rack, then click (or drag) toward its top to turn it (Shift in 15° steps; two clicks together stand it up the screen). Height (RU) rack units of 1.75 in run up from Base Offset above the first click: one column numbers them with a tick at each unit and a longer one every fifth, the column outside it gives their height in the drawing's units every five, each headed (U, and the unit), the labels turned back to read when the ruler is turned past level. Rodeo's reading: the labels' size on paper (Text height, 2 mm) is Rodeo's, and where they'd crowd at a small scale the units are numbered every 2, 5, 10…; the ruler is grouped plain objects, not a rack ruler object Object Info can edit, and isn't tied to an equipment rack
- [x] Tool Sets: Rigging's Straight Truss, Curved Truss, Lighting Pipe, Lighting Pipe Ladder, House Rigging Point and Hoist, in Top/Plan. Trusses: Profile Box, Triangle or Ladder, Width, Chord Width, Top Ladder Bar and Top Lacing Diameters (0 draws none), Folding and Highlight; from above the top chords (a triangle's apex chord too), ladder bars across and lacing corner to corner in each bay, refusing what Vectorworks' configuration rules forbid (narrower than three chord widths, bars or lacing thicker than the chords, a laced folding truss, bars spaced under three diameters, an interval no longer than the bar spacing). Straight Truss: a drag or two clicks along it, in sections a Connection Interval long, bays at the Ladder Bar Spacing, the short last section red with Highlight, Show Direction's arrowhead. Curved Truss: a click with the arc's middle up the screen (a drag turns it) round the point, Diameter outside to outside, Total Arc, Segment Arc and Ladder Spacing Arc. Lighting Pipe: a clicked path (Polyline Insertion, covering Line Insertion), Line Type single or double at its Diameter, End Marker at the start, end or both, Show Centerline Guide, Show Direction, ticks (Display ticks as Line or Dot, Centers, Origin from the centre or split, Size, Offset). Lighting Pipe Ladder: Rectangle Insertion lying flat (Guides centre, inside or outside) or Point Insertion standing up along a drag, Width, Number of Rungs, Line Type, Rung and Side Diameter, Verticals both, left, right or none. House Rigging Point: the circular schematic shown without a symbol, its Name and ID label. Hoist: the motor's quartered circle (Hoist Up or Down, or Not Shown as a cross), Hoist Symbol Scale, its Hoist ID label and the Measurement Label's X and Y in the document's units, from the hoist origin its Origin names (see Hoist Origin) or from the drawing's origin when that's blank or none has the name (said in a message), measured when it's placed. Rodeo's reading: plan only; a truss's bays are as near the spacing as divides each section evenly; a curved truss is centred on its click; an end marker is a bar four diameters across; the rigging point's square (Size) and the hoist's 500 mm circle are Rodeo's sizes; the centre line guide is drawn in the drawing's first dashed line type and left out in a drawing without one; roll and hanging angles, heights and trims, connections, pre-rigging, cross sections, weights and materials, smooth corners, arc radii, tick gaps and snap points, curved ladder tops and custom rung spacing, library symbols and pick-ups, baskets, forces, chains and power, automatic numbering, Braceworks calculations and 3D aren't drawn or kept; each is grouped plain objects, not a rigging object loads attach to, Object Info can edit, or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: Rigging's Hoist Origin, in Top/Plan. A click centres it on the point, a press dragged turns it and its label; the schematic it shows without a symbol: a square round a circle, crossed through its middle on the origin. Name, shown in the drawing (Show label in drawing) at its Label Offset X/Y from the point; Size. The placed group takes the Name as its Object Info Name, so a Hoist's Origin finds it by that name and measures its X/Y from the circle's middle; a name a layer or another object already has, or an empty one, is refused with a message, and the number at the end of the Name counts on after each placing, as Automatic Numbering does. Rodeo's reading: plan only; a hoist's X/Y is measured from the origin when the hoist is placed and doesn't follow it after; along the drawing's axes, not the origin's turn; symbols from libraries, notes, label classes and hoist reports aren't kept
- [x] Tool Sets: Irrigation's Outlet, Pipe, Valve, Point of Connection and Controller, in Top/Plan. Outlet: Single Outlet Placement (a click sprays up the screen, a press dragged sprays toward the pointer), Poly-Vertex (one at each clicked corner), Poly-Edge (spaced along a clicked path, spraying to its left) and Rectangular and Triangular Array (filling a clicked outline in rows from its lower left, triangular rows offset by half a spacing, keeping those inside it); Outlet Type Spray (a square), Rotor (a circle) or Emitter (a dot), Radius, Arc (a coverage circle or a wedge centred on the spray), Mode Overlap (by the percentage of the radius, 100% head to head), Fixed, Best Fit by distance or by overlap (from the start to the end, evenly, at least that far apart) or By Count, Offset Along Spray Direction, Symbol Scale, Symbol Rotation Toward Coverage (a part-circle spray's square turned a corner toward its spray) and Show Coverage; each outlet its own group. Pipe: Main, Lateral or Drip Tubing along a clicked path, a main heavier than a lateral, drip tubing finer and dashed in a drawing with a dashed line type, Show Flow Direction's arrowhead at its middle, its Nominal Diameter labelled beside it. Valve (a quartered circle on a pipe stub, two quarters filled), Point of Connection (a union's flanges) and Controller (two solid triangles point to point): a click or a press dragged to turn them, Symbol Scale, their Zone ID, POC Name or Controller Name above. Rodeo's reading: plan only; symbols and labels are sized on paper (an outlet 2 mm, a valve 3 mm, a point of connection and a controller 4 mm, a pipe's label 2 mm) times the Symbol Scale; Overlap and Fixed step from a path's start (the end may be left uncovered), arrays from the outline's lower left; catalogs and parts, manual radii, strip and side-strip sprays, drip outlets, system components, design zones and hydrozones, connections and autoconnect, pipe sizing, elevations, sleeves and trenches, pressures, flows and hydraulic calculations, tag and label formats and 3D aren't drawn or kept; each is grouped plain objects, not an irrigation object Object Info can edit, or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: Irrigation's Drip Outlet, System Component, Design Zone and Hydrozone, in Top/Plan. Drip Outlet: Drip Line along a clicked path, or Drip Area, its outline clicked and filled, as Show Drip Lines draws it, with parallel rows of drip line the Row Spacing apart, each cut where it crosses the outline (without Show Drip Lines the outline takes the fill from Attributes); too many rows for the area refused. System Component: the default symbol (two solid triangles point to point), a click or a press dragged to turn it, Symbol Scale, its Component Name above. Design Zone: the zone's outline, Rectangle or Polygon, unfilled, labelled at its middle with its Zone ID (Show Zone ID) and Zone Name, the Zone ID counted on numerically or alphabetically with each zone placed. Hydrozone: the outline filled from Attributes, its Name, Method, Water Use (Very Low, Low, Moderate, High or Custom) and Water Use Value, and Plant Type, labelled with them and its area in the drawing's units. Rodeo's reading: plan only; drip rows run along the area's first edge (Vectorworks turns them with a control point) and are centred across it; the system component is 3 mm on paper times its Symbol Scale; Very Low, Low and Moderate's suggested values (0.1, 0.3, 0.6) are the top of their WUCOLS ranges (High's 0.9 is Vectorworks'); a zone's label (3.5 mm and 2.5 mm text) stands in for an attached tag; snaking and branching configurations, component types and their library symbols, catalog data, connections, pressures, flows and lengths, coverage analysis, POCs, classes by water use, site areas, method lists and water budget worksheets aren't drawn or kept; each is grouped plain objects, not an irrigation object Object Info can edit, or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: Event Design's Blended Screen, in Top/Plan: Place Screen or Place Screen and Projector, a click (or a press dragged to turn it) with the image's face on the point, facing the drag. The screen as Video Screen draws it (Screen Type Front or Rear, Width and Height (image), Frame, Border Widths, Frame Depth, Screen Support None, Goalpost Legs or Roll-Down Case with Show Feet, case depth and motor, Show Centerline, the coverage zone with Zone Reference, Max Horiz. Angle and Near and Far Multipliers); the projectors sharing its image in areas as high as the image plus the Overshoot % and as wide as their Aspect (4:3, 16:10, 16:9) makes that, Area Horiz. Count of them (raised, with a note, to the fewest that cover the width; refused where one image covers it all), overlapping evenly to fill the width, each the Throw Ratio times its area's width in front of the screen (behind it, rear projected), Multiple Projector Single, Stacked or Side-by-Side (the Horiz Space apart), Show Projection Cones to its area's edges and Show Centerline (Area). Rodeo's reading: plan only; the Throw Ratio stands in for a projector's lens and Place Based On; a projector is Video Screen's 500 by 400 mm box; stacked pairs are drawn as one from above; curved screens, tilt, dress kits, roll-down tab tensioning, projector models, resolutions and pixel overlaps, lenses and zoom ranges, vertical positions, stands, shifts and heights, blend references (the areas fill the width evenly), names, notes and labels, screen images, classes and loads aren't drawn or kept; it's grouped plain objects, not a blended screen Object Info can edit, or a style
- [x] Tool Sets: Rigging's Bridle and Mother Grid, in Top/Plan (Click Insertion). Bridle: Two Leg, Three Leg or Reversed Two Leg; a click for its connection on the truss under its apex, then one for each leg's rigging point (a reversed bridle's two leg points on the truss, then the rigging point over its apex); each leg drawn from the apex to its point with a dot at its end, a square at the connection (with Use default coloring, magenta, green and red legs and a blue connection), Show Leg Labels giving each leg's length and angle from vertical and the down leg's length, measured from the Rigging Point, Apex and Trim Heights; heights out of order refused. Mother Grid: clicks for its start and end, a hoist at each, and its connection point, where the third hoist hangs (put on the truss's line; one past its ends refused); the truss drawn as Straight Truss draws it (Profile, Width, Chord Width, Top Ladder Bar and Lacing Diameters, Folding, Highlight, Connection Interval, Ladder Bar Spacing), the hoists as the Hoist tool's schematic turned with it. Rodeo's reading: plan only; the heights are the tool's preferences rather than read from the rigging points, trusses and Tool bar; dots, squares and labels are sized on paper (1.5 mm, 2 mm, 2 mm text); Auto Insertion, bridle parts and baskets, configurations and parts lengths, hoists as down legs, names, IDs, locations, times and costs, allowable forces, reports and assembly diagrams, the mother grid's truss and hoist symbols and trim, and 3D aren't drawn or kept; each is grouped plain objects, not a bridle, truss or hoist Object Info can edit or Braceworks can load
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Cable Path and Cable (Single Connection), in Top/Plan. Cable Path: click its corners, a double-click or Enter ending it (a click on its first corner closes it); Corner Vertex keeps them sharp, Arc Vertex rounds each with the Fillet Radius as a polyline's arc vertices, each kept to what fits halfway along the edges beside it; it is one polyline. Cable: click its corners the same way, in either vertex mode; with Automatically follow paths on, a click on a cable path (any polyline or line) joins it there, the cable running along it and leaving at the point nearest the next click (or a second click on the path); its Cable Type (Feeder, Multi, Jumper, Data) sets its line weight; it is sized into the fewest stock Cable Parts (lengths set in Preferences, the least spare among those, longest first) with a Connector Marker (Chevron, Tick, Dot or None) where each part meets the next, the parts listed when it's placed; Line Start and End Markers (Arrow or Dot); a middle label of the Cable Run ID and its length, over a white box (Middle Text Fill) or above the cable; too many parts refused. Rodeo's reading: plan only; the markers' and label's sizes on paper (1.5 mm, 2 mm text), the parts' order and the line weights are Rodeo's; any polyline or line can be followed, not only cable paths, and a cable follows a path's arcs as the points they're drawn with; Distributor, Schematic Distributor and Daisy Chain modes, distributors and outputs, cable styles, inventories and connectors by name, Automatic Numbering, IDs, locations, heights and vertical drops, trusses followed, reshaping cables with their path, loads, data tags and 3D aren't drawn or kept; a cable is grouped plain objects, not a cable object Object Info can edit or a report can count
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' and Cable/Power Planning's HopOver (in ConnectCAD Schematics, after ReRoute Circuits). Click a line where another crosses it and it hops over the other in a half circle at the crossing nearest the click: three vertices put into its straight piece there (a corner a radius before the crossing, a point the arc passes through a radius to the side, a corner a radius after), so the hop is a true arc in a polyline; a line or open polygon becomes a polyline, keeping its markers, class and attributes. The line under the cursor, the crossing (ringed) and the hop show before the click; one undo step. Preferences: Hop radius on paper (1.5 mm) and Hop Side, Up (left on an upright line) or Down (right). A crossing nearer the straight piece's end than the radius, a straight piece nothing crosses, and closed shapes are refused. A ConnectCAD circuit (a polyline circuit) hops the circuit crossing it where the click is nearest: the hop is kept with the circuit, so it follows when either moves and is redrawn at every place the two cross; a click on a hop already there takes it off; the circuit under the cursor is the one that hops, unless one circuit was selected first and crosses within a few pixels of the click, which then hops instead, as a selected circuit keeps priority in Vectorworks. Arrow circuits are refused (no run to hop), as is a crossing nearer the circuit's corner than the radius when the hop is made; a crossing that later moves that near a corner is drawn straight until it moves clear. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks hops circuits only; Rodeo also hops any plain line, open polygon or open polyline on the active layer over any other visible outline, and a plain line's hop doesn't follow when the lines move. Circuits hop only circuits on their own layer; a hop over a circuit that's deleted is kept but draws nothing; how Vectorworks stores a circuit's hops is unknown, so they live in Rodeo's own circuit (as ["hop", circuit, radius, side] in its Corners) and aren't read from Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Device, Socket, Terminator and Connect (a ConnectCAD Schematics set, after Event Design), and Stage > ConnectCAD > Number Cables and Clear Cable Numbers, in Top/Plan. Device (Rectangle mode): click the box's corners, or click twice in one place for the usual box (8 rows of 5 mm on paper across, 4 down); a name already in the drawing takes the next number (DEV1, DEV2…; Preferences: Device Name, Description, Manufacturer). Object Info: Device Name, Display Tag (follows the name until it's changed), Description (in the box's first row), Manufacturer, Box Width and Height, the physical Height, Width and Depth, Power (W), Auto BTU calculation (W × 3.41), BTU, Weight (kg), Height RU, Rack Width, Room, Rack and Rack U (written under the box), and Edit Sockets… (or a double-click): a table of its sockets' Name, Display Tag, Type, Side, Row, Signal, Connector on Cable (the signal's own), Terminated and Delete, with Add Socket; names must be unique on the device. Socket: Input, Output or Bi-Directional (keys 1–3), on the Left, Right, Top or Bottom side or by Automatic Type (the side nearest the click), on the row clicked, named with the next free number on its device (IN1, IN2…, or the Name in Preferences), with the Signal and Connector from Preferences; an output shows a red triangle pointing out, an input a green one pointing in, IO both, and the box grows to hold them; clicking a socket opens Edit Sockets on it. Terminator: click a socket to terminate it (a filled block outside the edge), again to take it off; a socket with a circuit can't be terminated, nor a terminated one connected. Connect: Single Connect clicks a socket, any corners (Shift squares one to the last, Backspace takes one off), then the socket to connect it to; Multi Connect joins the first device's free sockets of the clicked signal, from the one clicked, to the other device's, in their order; Polyline or Arrow (keys 1, 2). Drawn from an input, a circuit runs from the output; two inputs, two outputs, the same socket and devices on two layers are refused. A circuit leaves each socket straight out and runs square between (halfway across, between the rows when it runs backward, under them when they're level), or through the corners it was drawn with; it's coloured by its signal's kind, writes its Number by its ends (Number Display Auto, Source, Destination, Both, Mid or None; Auto writes the middle on a short run) and its Cable under its middle; Arrow draws an arrow out of the source and one back into the destination, each naming the other end's device and socket. Circuits keep to their sockets when devices move, turn or change, in the same undo step, their corners staying where they are; one whose device or socket is deleted keeps its last run with a warning triangle at the loose end. Object Info: Flip (an IO-to-IO circuit only), Number, Cable, Signal, Circuit Type, Number Display, Cable Length, Calculated Cable Length (from Calculate Cable Lengths, read only), Source and Destination; Reshape moves its corners and its ends stay on their sockets. Number Cables: the unnumbered circuits on the active layer, or only the selected ones, by Signal and Sequence (the signal's letter and a count: V001, A001…), Source Device and Sequence (CAM1-01), Source Device and Socket (CAM1-SDI1) or Sequence, Left to Right then Down, Top to Bottom then Right, or Drawing Order, with Number Format's digits; numbers in use anywhere in the drawing are skipped. Clear Cable Numbers: the selected circuits' numbers, or every one's on the active layer, after a yes. Rodeo's reading: the signals, their connectors, letters and colours are Rodeo's own short lists, not Vectorworks' settings files; the four numbering systems are fixed, not Vectorworks' rule-based ones (ConnectCAD Settings picks the one Number Cables starts on, but can't make new ones); sockets are kept in their device, not objects of their own, and devices aren't symbols, so there's no Standard Insertion, Linear Array, Pick Up/Apply, Save as Symbol or Save to Database (the Device Builder tool makes a device from a description instead); arrow circuits stay on one layer, with no Navigate to Other End; HopOver works on plain lines, not circuits; a circuit copied without its devices still joins the devices it was drawn between; Cable Type and Number of Circuits aren't kept; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD: Connect Selected, Check Drawing, Clear Markup and Renumber ConnectCAD Objects. Connect Selected: with devices side by side selected, each free output on a device's right side is joined at once to the free input of a matching signal directly opposite (on its row) on the nearest selected device to its right; with one device selected, or none facing, the next click picks the device to connect to, and each selected device's free sockets are joined to that device's free ones of the same signal in their order (its outputs to the device's inputs when that's to its right, the device's outputs to its inputs when it's to the left). Check Drawing: Sockets (a socket name twice on a device: error), Devices (a device name twice or none: warning; a display tag twice: note), Circuits (a number twice: warning; a circuit whose device or socket has gone: error) and Signals (a circuit joining different signals, passive and unknown going with anything: note), on the active layer or all layers; each problem is marked with an outline (a ring round a socket or a circuit's middle, a box round a device) in its severity's colour, chosen in the dialog, as a markup object in the class ConnectCAD Markup, and a count follows. A markup's Object Info gives its Status Category, Status Type, Problem and Information, and Previous Markup and Next Markup select and zoom to the other markup of the same finding. A new check takes away an earlier check's markup on the layers it checks; Clear Markup takes away all of it. Renumber ConnectCAD Objects: the selected devices (only devices) labelled Numeric (Start, Increment by, Format's digits) or Alpha (Start letters, Increment by, Case), after a Delimiter (None, -, _, ., Space), with Prefix Name (else the name's own stem) and Prefix Display Tag (else the tag's), in Drawing Order, Left to Right then Down, or Top to Bottom then Right, with an Example; circuits read the new names; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: layout objects, cable paths and jackfield holes aren't checked (Rodeo has none of them yet); a loose circuit being an error is Rodeo's choice; Connect Selected doesn't join devices on different layers; Renumber renames devices, not sockets, racks or rooms; two circuits run backward between the same rows overlap until reshaped; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD: Current Layer Circuit Report, All Layers Circuit Report, Current Layer Device Report, Device Connections Report and ConnectCAD Parts Report. Each makes a new worksheet (named after the command, with -2, -3… when that name is taken, so an earlier report and its edits are kept) and opens it in the worksheet editor; one undo step. Circuit reports: Number, Signal, Cable, Source Device and Socket, Destination Device and Socket, Cable Length, Calculated Length and Circuit Type, a row per circuit by number (counting order: V2 before V10, unnumbered last), on the active layer or, with a Layer column first, on all layers. Device Report: the active layer's devices by name (Device Name, Display Tag, Description, Manufacturer, Sockets, Power, BTU, Weight, Room, Rack), then a summary by Description and Manufacturer (Count, Power, BTU, Weight) and a Total row. Device Connections Report: every device with each socket's Type, Signal, Connector, the circuit joined to it and the device and socket at its other end, or Not connected. Parts Report: each device name once (a device drawn on two layers is one part) with Description, Manufacturer, Height RU, Weight and Power, then the Quantity of each Description and Manufacturer. A layer or drawing without circuits or devices says so. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' reports are database worksheets that recalculate, while Rodeo's hold the values as they are now (Rodeo can't write worksheet formulas yet); the columns are Rodeo's choice, not those of Vectorworks' ConnectCAD Reports.vwx; a worksheet is made from one the drawing has, so a drawing without one (a new blank drawing) shows the report in a dialog instead; Cable Pull Report (cable routes), equipment, adapters, racks and rack frames in the Parts Report, Find in Report and two-way editing aren't done; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' External (after Device). Modes Single Click and Linear Array; Input, Output (the default) or Bi-Directional (1–3); Left External or Right External; Preferences Name (EXT1), Signal and Connector (the signal's first when it isn't one of the signal's). Single Click places one external at the click (its top left there), a name already in the drawing taking the next number; Linear Array takes a click where the row starts and one where it ends, Shift keeping it level or plumb, the externals a box and a pitch apart along it with the Count in the hint, named on from the name's number (EXT2, EXT3… skipping names taken). An external is a box six pitches wide pointed at its far end (a left external points left, its sockets on its right facing the drawing; a right one the other way) with its name in its top row; it is a plug-in object 'Rodeo External' that reads as a device everywhere else, so the Connect tool, Connect Selected, the Socket and Terminator tools, Edit Sockets, Check Drawing, Renumber and the circuit and connections reports take it (the device and parts reports leave it out). Object Info: Name, Facing, Socket Type, Signal, Connector, External Circuits (1–64: more add sockets numbered on, fewer cut the last; the box as tall as they need) and Box Width; a change of facing, type, signal (bringing its first connector) or connector goes to every socket, each keeping its id and name so circuits stay joined; Edit Sockets… One undo step each. Rodeo's reading: the outline, its size and the array's spacing (a box and a pitch) are Rodeo's, since the help doesn't give them; drop points (the Type pop-up listing them) aren't done; Connector is a text field in the Object Info; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Socket Loop (after Socket), and Linear Array for Socket and Socket Loop. Socket Loop: modes Single Click and Linear Array, Input or Bi-Directional (1–2; loops are never outputs), and the Socket tool's sides (Left, Right, Top, Bottom, Automatic Type); Preferences Name, Signal and Connector (the Socket tool's). A click on a device's box loops the socket there with the one below it (or the one above, when only that one is there), adding any socket missing (taking the signal of one there already, named on from the name asked); two free rows get two new looped sockets; a click on a looped socket takes its loop off; an output, or a socket looped already, is refused. A loop is drawn as a half circle outside the box joining the two sockets' tips. A looped input connects either way, so the second socket of a loop-thru feeds the next device with the Connect tool, and a circuit that would run back across its own device (both ends leaving the same side) runs round it instead. Linear Array (Socket and Socket Loop): click a device's box where the row starts and again where it ends (on or past the box, which grows to hold them), the Count in the hint: each free row gets a socket, or each two free rows side by side a looped pair. Loops are kept through Edit Sockets and the Terminator tool, and dropped when either socket goes, moves away or becomes an output; one undo step each. Rodeo's reading: a loop is a link between the two sockets kept on the device (Vectorworks' is an object of its own); its look is Rodeo's; Connect Selected and Multi Connect don't treat a looped input as a source; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Legend (after Connect), in Top/Plan. A click places the legend's top left corner and a second click sets its rotation (a click level to the right leaves it unturned; Shift isn't needed). The legend lists the signals of the circuits on its layer, each once in the signal list's order (others after it, by name), with a sample of the circuit line in its colour, the signal's name and, with Show Count, how many circuits carry it, under its Title (Signal Legend); a layer with no circuits shows No circuits. Sizes are on paper (2.5 mm writing, a 3 mm title) and scale with the layer. It is a plug-in object 'Rodeo ConnectCAD Legend' with Title, Show Count and Refresh Legend in the Object Info palette; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: line colours are Rodeo's by signal category, not taken from the circuits' classes as Vectorworks does, so there's no double-click to edit a signal's class from the legend; the legend keeps the rows it was drawn with until Refresh Legend, rather than following the drawing as it changes; a sample is drawn as Rodeo draws circuits (solid, 7 mils, the signal's colour), not in a line type of the signal's own.
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Distribution Amplifier (after External), in Top/Plan. Modes Single Click and Linear Array, and Video or Audio Distribution Amplifier (1–2); Preferences: Video and Audio DA Name (VDA1, ADA1), Video and Audio Signal (SDI, Analog Audio), Number of Outputs (1–32, 4), Loop-thru and Manufacturer. A DA is a device box with its input on its left (looped to a second input below it with Loop-thru) and its outputs down its right, all of the signal, with '1×4 Video DA' in its first row; a name already in the drawing takes the next number. Single Click places one at the click (its top left there); a click within half a pitch of a circuit with both ends joined puts the DA in the circuit instead, its input on the circuit where it was clicked: the circuit now runs to the DA's input and a new one runs from its first output to where the circuit went, the DA taking the circuit's signal (a video DA refuses an audio circuit and the other way round, saying why); one undo step, with the circuit and its preview ringed before the click. Linear Array takes a click where the row starts and one where it ends, Shift keeping it level or plumb, the DAs a box and a pitch apart with the Count in the hint, numbered on. It is a plug-in object 'Rodeo Distribution Amplifier' that reads as a device everywhere (Connect, Check Drawing, reports, Renumber, Edit Sockets), its Object Info a device's with a read-only Device Type (Video DA or Audio DA), Number of Outputs and Loop-thru: changing them adds or takes off outputs and the looped input, keeping the sockets that stay (and their circuits), the description following while it's the one it was given, the box as tall as its sockets need; Edit Sockets' changes show back in them. Rodeo's reading: there is no list of makes and models to pick (Manufacturer is typed, and any number of outputs up to 32 is allowed), and the Tool bar's Make/Model, number and outputs lists are in Preferences; Edit Device Array (the DA Settings dialog for several DAs, with Renumber) isn't there, so DAs are edited one at a time and renumbered with Renumber ConnectCAD Objects; a DA's Symbol (where its tag and description go) isn't offered; only Single Click splits a circuit, an Arrow circuit is never split, the split circuit's corners are dropped so it routes afresh, and the new circuit has no number until Number Cables; circuits route round only their own two devices, so one already drawn may pass over a DA placed in its way.
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Term Panel, Jackfield and Linked Jackfield (after Distribution Amplifier), in Top/Plan. Modes Single Click and Linear Array, and each tool's types: Term Panel's Video, Audio, Data, Optical and DIN Rail Term Panel; Jackfield's Video, Audio, Data and Optical Jack; Linked Jackfield's U-Linked Video, Full-Normal and Half-Normal Audio, Normal Data and Linked Optical Jack (1–5). Each device is one port of a panel: devices of one panel share its name (the prefix, group and panel: TPA1, JA1) and display tag (A1), and each has its port (01, 02…) written in its first row; a term panel's socket takes a circuit from either side (a socket each side, both bi-directional), a jack takes the output it's fed on its left and feeds an input from its right, and a term panel's or linked jack's two sides are joined by a line through it. Ports count on as they're placed (and along a Linear Array), past ports the drawing has, to the panel's size and then port 1 of the next panel, passing over a panel that holds another type; with one device of the tool's kind selected, the next follows it. Preferences: Prefix (TP, J), the next Group, Panel and Port, the Connector (the type's own: BNC, XLR3 or TRS, RJ45, LC, Phoenix) and Manufacturer. A click within half a pitch of a circuit puts the device in the circuit as the Distribution Amplifier does, taking its signal (a type of another signal category refuses, saying why; a DIN rail takes any). They are plug-in objects 'Rodeo Panel' that read as devices everywhere, their Object Info a device's with a read-only Device Type and Group, Panel and Port: changing them renames its sockets, and its name and tag while they're its own. Check Drawing leaves panel devices out of its duplicate name and tag checks and warns of one panel's port used twice instead. Rodeo's reading: the prefixes are in each tool's Preferences (ConnectCAD Settings sets them for a drawing), panels hold 32 video ports, 48 audio jacks or 32 audio terminals, 24 data or optical and 32 on a DIN rail unless ConnectCAD Settings changes the sizes, ports are numbered 01, 02… (no alphabetical labels or delimiters), and a term panel's name has its panel number as a jack's does; the Tool bar's Group, Panel and Port lists are in Preferences; the three linked jack types are drawn alike, and a jack has one connector for both sides; Edit Device Array (several devices' Group, Panel and Port, with Renumber ports) isn't there; the Symbol (where the tag and description go) isn't offered; the tool's port count isn't put back by Undo (ports the drawing has are passed over anyway); splitting is as the Distribution Amplifier's.
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD: Combine Panels and Split Panels. Combine Panels makes the selected panel devices of one name and type on a layer one device, a row for each port in their order (a term panel's or jack's sockets either side of each row), in the place of the one of the lowest port, the others deleted; every circuit stays joined to its port, and is drawn again to where the port now is. Split Panels makes each selected panel device of more than one port a device for each, of its name and type, the first port's in its place and the rest one below another under it, turned as it is; circuits again stay joined. Each is one undo step, the devices made selected; a selection with nothing to combine or split says so. A combined device's Port is its first; changing it in the Object Info numbers its rows on from it. Check Drawing reads a combined device's ports from its sockets. Rodeo's reading: a combined device keeps the box width of the device it was made from, and its rows close up from the top.
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Adapter. A click on a device's left or right socket puts an adapter on it, just outside the device, its first row level with the socket, turned as the device is: a box with its Make/Model in its top row and its own sockets down its far side, one for each name in Sockets (L, R for a breakout; none for one socket of no name), each of the device socket's way and signal, of the adapter's Connector. Hovering shows the socket it goes on, or why not (a top or bottom socket, a looped or terminated one, one with an adapter already, an adapter's own socket). The socket's circuits move to the adapter's first socket, and a circuit at an adapter is named by the device's socket and the adapter's (OUT1-L) in its Object Info, its arrows, Number Cables, Check Drawing and the reports, which list it at the device's socket; the device's socket under an adapter takes no circuit of its own. Moving or turning the device brings its adapters with it; an adapter dragged off its socket shows a warning triangle (Check Drawing warns Adapter is not on a socket), and dropped with its near edge within half a pitch of another free left or right socket goes on that one, snapped to it, that socket's circuits moving to its first socket while that's free; deleting the device or its socket leaves its adapter off, with the warning. Object Info: Make/Model, Connector and Sockets, the sockets made again with circuits kept by place. Each is one undo step. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' adapters are symbols chosen in the Resource Manager (Use Symbol, Save as Symbol, Edit Adapter's object editing mode), Rodeo's a box of its own with no symbol; an adapter's sockets keep its device socket's signal (an adapter can't change the signal yet); adapters aren't in the Parts Report; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' ReRoute Circuits. Select the circuits to re-route (with the Selection tool, or in the tool: a click selects a circuit, Shift-click adds or takes one off), then click the circuit they're to follow, highlighted under the pointer with the re-routed runs previewed. A click busses them on its run: each leaves its source's stub square onto the run where it's nearest, runs along it and leaves it square to its destination's stub where that's nearest; Display Breakout Nodes mode puts a dot where each joins and leaves the run (none at the run's own ends), No Breakout Nodes none. A drag off the circuit sets a distance between (shown as Distance between, in quarter pitches): those whose sources are on one side of the run take one step, two… on that side, nearest first, the run's corners mitred, so outputs a pitch apart run parallel on their own rows. Re-routed circuits become polyline circuits, stay selected, and keep their new corners (and nodes) when their devices move; one undo step. An arrow circuit (no run) or one loose at an end isn't followed; circuits on another layer or loose at an end are left. Rodeo's reading: the bus is the circuit's whole run, sockets' stubs included; the distance can't be typed in the Data bar; rounded polyline circuits and fan-out styles aren't drawn (Rodeo's circuits are square polylines or arrows); not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Device tool eyedropper (Transferring device properties): modes Device, Pick Up and Apply (1–3), in Top/Plan. Pick Up: a click on a device takes its sockets, Manufacturer, Description, box size, physical data (Height, Width, Depth, Power, Auto BTU calculation, BTU, Weight, Height RU, Rack Width) and location data (Room, Rack, Rack U), its class, attributes and data records, and the tool goes on to Apply; the device a click would take is outlined. Apply: a click on a device, or on one of the devices selected (all of them then), gives it what was picked up: a socket of the same name takes the picked-up socket's settings and place, keeping its own number so its circuits stay and are drawn again to its new place; a socket only the picked-up device has is added, moved down a row at a time past any socket in its way; the device's name and tag stay its own; the devices a click would change are outlined. Preferences, in Pick Up or Apply (Device Eyedropper Transfer Preferences): Only apply to devices with the same Manufacturer and/or Description (model); Missing Socket Action for the device's own sockets: Do not delete any sockets, Delete if not connected (a socket with a circuit or an adapter on it counts as connected), or Delete even if connected (its circuits deleted with it); Transfer attached records (a record attached where it's missing, its values copied). One undo step a click; a device refused says why. Rodeo's reading: what's picked up is a copy taken at the click, kept while the tool changes, so later edits to that device aren't carried; a device's own socket where a same-named socket now goes moves down a row (Vectorworks doesn't say); devices only: distribution amplifiers, panels, externals and adapters are neither picked up nor changed; Rodeo's devices have no equipment items or graphics of their own, so make, model, physical and location data always transfer and the box is the graphics transferred; Vectorworks' Make and Model are Rodeo's Manufacturer and Description; the preferences last for the visit
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD: Convert Report to List, Compare Cable List and Drawing and Make Connections from List. Convert Report to List: the Select Worksheet dialog (the drawing's worksheets, the one made last chosen), then a new worksheet named after it with List (a number after when the name's taken) holding its rows as they are, which opens; Rodeo's reports are already plain rows, so this freezes a copy. Compare Cable List and Drawing: a list from the drawing's worksheets or, in Rodeo, a spreadsheet file (CSV, tab- or semicolon-delimited text, or Excel .xlsx); its columns are found by their headings in the first row that has them (Source Device, Source Socket, Destination Device and Destination Socket, and Number and Cable when there; From, Src, To, Dest and Dst forms too), devices and sockets matching whatever their case and either way round, an adapter's socket by its host's names (IN1-L) as the reports write it. The active layer's circuits are compared with it: a circuit whose number differs is marked (Warning), a circuit in the list whose sockets the layer has but no circuit joins is marked at both sockets (Error), and a circuit on the layer the list hasn't is marked (Warning), as markup in the class ConnectCAD Markup that Clear Markup takes away and a new compare replaces; a count says what was found. The list with a Check column after its last (OK, Number differs: the drawing has …, Not on the drawing, or Not on the drawing: DEV : SOCKET isn't on the layer) is a new worksheet named after it with Check, or a dialog in a drawing with no worksheet. Make Connections from List: from the same choice, a circuit for each row whose sockets the layer has but no circuit joins, numbered and cabled from the list, a polyline when its sockets are within 40 grid spaces of each other and an arrow circuit when further; the circuits made are selected; one undo step; rows naming what the layer hasn't, and circuits that can't be made (a terminated socket, two inputs), are counted. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks inserts the Check column into the list worksheet itself, but Rodeo can't add a column to a worksheet yet, so it makes a copy with one; worksheets' database rows (Vectorworks' own reports) can't be read yet, so only plain rows are listed and compared; how near is fairly close for a polyline is Rodeo's choice; a list from a file is Rodeo's own, so drawings with no worksheet (every new one) can use these; Convert Report to List needs a worksheet in the drawing
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD: Create Devices from Worksheet. The Select Worksheet dialog (the drawing's worksheets or, in Rodeo, a CSV, tab- or semicolon-delimited text or Excel .xlsx file), then the Worksheet Columns Assignment dialog: Header Row (the first row with a Make or Model heading, chosen from the first 40 rows), and for each worksheet column (Field and its number for one with no heading) what it's assigned to: Name, Display Tag, Make, Model, Quantity, Room, Rack, Rack U, Power (W), Weight (kg) or Height RU (found by their headings and alike ones such as Manufacturer, Description, Qty and Watts), a field of the Imported Device Data record (any other heading), or not used; a column must go to Make or Model. Each row below the header with a make or model asks for its Quantity of devices (one when there's none); the drawing's devices of that make and model, matched whatever their case, count first and have the row's data put in, and the rest are made, named from the Name column (numbered from 1 past the names taken when there's more than one: CAM 1, CAM 2) or else from the model, tags alike. A device made of a make and model the drawing has is made like the first of them: its sockets, box, physical data, class and look, but not its room or rack. A row's columns fill each device's fields (Power works out its BTU); the others go in the Imported Device Data record, made or given the fields it lacks and attached to each device. Devices are made eight to a row below the active layer's devices and selected; one undo step; a count says how many were made and brought up to date and which rows were passed by (no make or model, a quantity that isn't a number). Running it again on the same list makes none. Rodeo's reading: Rodeo has no ConnectCAD device database, so a make and model the drawing hasn't is made as a plain device with no sockets, with no Device Mapping dialog, and the devices the Device Builder keeps aren't looked in; Vectorworks adds an error column to the worksheet and highlights the rows it couldn't use, which Rodeo names in the count since it can't add a column to a worksheet yet; Vectorworks places devices round the outer drawing area, Rodeo below what the layer has; worksheets' database rows can't be read yet, so only plain rows are listed
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Layout's Layout Room (Rectangle and Polygon modes, Vectorworks' Vertex), in a new ConnectCAD Layout tool set with the Rack Ruler, which moved there from Event Design. A rectangle (a drag or two clicks) or a polygon (a double-click, Enter or a click on the first corner closes it) becomes a layout room: its outline with its Room ID at the top left in plan, and unless 2D only, its walls up to its Height as a wireframe in 3D. Object Info: Room ID, Height (greyed while 2D only), 2D only, and Area (read-only, in the drawing's units). Reshape moves its corners; Preferences set the Room ID, Height and 2D only a new room takes, the ID numbered on when another room has it (AV1, AV2). AEC > Create Objects from Shapes makes one on each closed shape (Object type Layout Room), with Delete source shapes. Rooms can be nested or overlap: what stands inside takes the IDs of the rooms round it joined by dots, the outermost first (MAIN.AV1). One undo step each. Rodeo's reading: Use Layer Wall Height is left out, since Rodeo doesn't read a design layer's wall height yet; Rodeo has no equipment racks yet, so nothing takes a Room ID from a room until they come, and a space's Name doesn't act as a room yet; rooms stacked on the same outline both give their IDs; where the Room ID sits and the wireframe in 3D are Rodeo's choice
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Layout's Equipment Rack 3D. A click for the middle of the rack's front, a click toward where it faces; the first time in a drawing its Preferences open (Rack ID, Make, Model, Rack Opening, Height in rack units, Depth, Rack Style, Weight of Rack and the parts to draw). Rack Styles: Rack (4-post), Rack (2-post), Console (its mounting face sloped back by Console Slope, Panel Thickness its cladding) and Enclosure (its height set overall, walls the posts' width thick). In plan its outline, posts and a heavier front with its Rack ID; in 3D its base, top and posts, with rails, side panels and front and rear doors (see-through) as asked (covers and doors not on a 2-post rack, rails not on a console or enclosure). Object Info follows Vectorworks' sections: General, Configuration (Rack Opening 10", 19", 23", 24" or Custom with its width), Physical (Height, Width and Depth overall, Post Width and Depth, Top Thickness, Base Height, Power Consumption, Weight of Rack, Total Weight), Options and Location (Room ID). Editing an overall size works as Vectorworks says: a new overall height keeps the mounting area in whole rack units and the base makes up the rest; a new overall width changes the posts, and when they'd be thinner than 10 mm the opening drops to the largest standard one that leaves room, else to a custom one; a new overall depth changes Depth. The Room ID is the layout room it stands in (nested rooms joined by dots), and Power Consumption and Total Weight count the devices whose Rack is its Rack ID; both follow as rooms, racks and devices change. One undo step each; a new rack's ID is numbered on when another rack has it. Rodeo's reading: the totals count its equipment items and the devices assigned to it that no linked equipment item stands for; Make/Model is two fields; a custom opening is a field rather than the Set Custom Opening dialog; half rack units (a ConnectCAD Settings option) aren't offered; the Room ID can't be edited to move a rack, and a space's Name doesn't count as a room; rulers, per-part classes, the drop point for cable networks, Create Rack Elevation and Edit Front/Rear of Rack aren't there yet; the parts' sizes (rails, doors, panels) and a console's profile are Rodeo's choice
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Layout's Equipment Item. A click over an equipment rack puts equipment in it at the first free unit from the top (on the rack's front or rear, as Mounting says); anywhere else it stands alone, its front's middle at the click. The first time in a drawing its Preferences open. Object Info follows Vectorworks' sections: General (Name, Link device name), Manufacturer (Make, Model), Physical (Height, Width, Depth, Power, Auto BTU calculation at W × 3.41, BTU, Weight, Height RU, Rack Width Full/Half/Non-rack, Mounting Front/Rear, Mounting Depth), Display (Show text, Text Align), Location (Room, Rack, Rack U) and Show Schematic Device(s), which selects the devices of its name and goes to their layer. In a rack it turns to face the rack's front or rear, sits at its Rack U (on a console's slope, leaning with it), is as wide as the opening (Full) or half of it (two Half pieces share a unit side by side), is set back by its Mounting Depth, and moves and turns with the rack; past the rack's units it sits on top, and at Rack U 0 or less below it, a unit lower for each below 0. Dragged off a rack it stands alone where it's dropped; dragged onto one it goes in at the first free unit (back to its own unit when it's the same rack and that's free); typing a Rack ID moves it into that rack's first free unit, and editing Rack U moves it within. A rack's Rack ID renamed is followed. With Link device name, named after a device on the schematic it takes the device's make and model (the device's Manufacturer and Description) and, when the device has a size, its physical data; from then the devices of its name take its make, model, physical and location data (greyed in their Object Info), and renaming it renames them. A rack's Power Consumption and Total Weight count its equipment and the devices assigned to it that no linked equipment stands for. In plan stand-alone equipment is a white outline with its name and racked equipment an outline over its rack's; in 3D a box, racked equipment a light front panel with ears and a narrower body. One undo step each; a new item's name is numbered on. Rodeo's reading: stand-alone equipment stands on its layer (no X/Y/Z rotation or Z); for rack equipment Height RU sets Height to whole units and Height sets Height RU; racked equipment's name doesn't show in plan, where it would stack on the others; Room is read-only (the layout room it stands in, or its rack's); a device with no size isn't treated as virtual; half rack units, a symbol for the item, Auto-size text, cable path handles and drop points, attaching to cameras and other Event Design objects, 3D drag snapping, Edit Front/Rear of Rack and the Data bar's location entry aren't there yet; colours and the panel and ear sizes are Rodeo's choice
- [x] Commands: ConnectCAD Layout > Create Equipment (Stage > ConnectCAD Layout). Its dialog lists the layers with devices (Use, with Select All Layers and Deselect All Layers) and Selected devices only. It makes an equipment item on the active layer for each device name on those layers that has no equipment yet (devices sharing a name get one), linked to them and taking their make, model, rack width and units, and physical data when they have a size. Each goes where its device's Location says: in its rack at its Rack U, or stacked below the rack (a unit lower each) when that unit is taken or it has none; round the inside of its layout room's walls, backs to the walls and facing in, starting round again when the walls are full; or, with no location, round the drawing, rack equipment stacked down the left and the rest in a row along the top. A room, rack or rack unit the drawing hasn't opens Missing Locations with those devices and the missing parts in red, to create them anyway round the drawing (a missing rack's name kept) or leave them out. After it, the devices' make, model, physical and location rows are greyed in Object Info, kept by their equipment. One undo step; the made equipment selected. Rodeo's reading: only plain devices count (not distribution amplifiers, panels or externals); a space's Name doesn't count as a room; lighting devices and distributors of the same name aren't checked for; Get 3D symbols isn't there (equipment is drawn as a box); round the drawing is in plan, from what's drawn on the design layers; Create Anyway and leaving them out are a choice in the dialog rather than two buttons
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Layout's Rack Frame, and modular equipment in its slots. A click over an equipment rack puts a frame in it at the first free unit from the top (on the rack's front or rear, as Mounting says); anywhere else it stands alone, its front's middle at the click. The first time in a drawing its Preferences open. Object Info: General (Name, Link device name), Manufacturer (Make, Model), Physical (Type Frame or Tray, Mounting, Height RU, Depth, Power, Auto BTU calculation, BTU, Weight), Configuration (Slots H, Slots V, Slot Direction), Options (Power Supplies, PSU Location, PSU Size, Front Door), Location (Room, Rack, Rack U) and Show Schematic Device(s). Its slots are numbered left to right from the top row, in what its ears, walls and power supplies leave; power supplies stand side by side at the left or right, each PSU Size percent of a slot's width.
