Equipment items gain Configuration (Modular, Number of Slots) and Location's Slot: modular equipment clicked or dragged into a rack, or given a Rack ID, goes into the first frame from the top with that many slots free along a row (Horizontal) or down a column (Vertical), as wide and tall as those slots; dragged back into its own rack it keeps its slots when they're still free. A frame moved by Rack U, Mounting, Rack ID or its rack takes its modules with it; dragged off its rack, its modules stay where they are, out of it. Turning Modular off moves a module into a free unit of its own. A Slot or Number of Slots that runs off the frame or onto another module, or Slots H, Slots V or Slot Direction that would leave a module without its slots, is refused. The rack's power and weight count the frame and its modules. In 3D a frame is two sides, a top, bottom and back (a tray a bottom and a low back lip) between ears, with grey power supply boxes and a see-through door; modules are front panels and bodies without ears, a little inside their slots so neighbours show apart. One undo step each; a new frame's name is numbered on. Rodeo's reading: a frame is a plug-in of its own ('Rodeo Rack Frame') sharing the equipment item's machinery; Edit Slots (named slots, slots of different sizes) isn't there, so slots are an even grid; modules in a stand-alone frame, half-unit frames and frames narrower than the rack aren't there; a module is drawn as tall as its slots while its Height field is left as set; editing a frame's Rack U doesn't check for overlap, as with equipment; equipment items made before this keep their old fields and can't be made modular; colours and the wall, door and power supply sizes are Rodeo's choice
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Layout's Make Rack Equipment Worksheet. A click opens Rack Equipment Columns: Select Rack (the rack clicked over, or the drawing's first), then the columns with #, Use and Column, a row dragged to change their order, and Select All Columns / Deselect All Columns (none used is refused). OK makes a worksheet named after the rack's Rack ID listing the equipment in it at each rack position from the top, the front's and then the rear's: each piece where its units start (a range for more than one), a rack frame before its modules and them by slot, each run of free units between as a Free row, and equipment below the rack after; then Total for power, BTU and weight when those columns are used, and the free units on each side. The columns: Rack U, Mounting, Slot, Name, Make, Model, Height RU, Rack Width, Depth, Power (W), BTU and Weight (kg). The worksheet opens in the worksheet editor; the columns chosen are kept for the next one. Rodeo's reading: the help doesn't list Vectorworks' columns, so these are Rodeo's, taken from the equipment's fields; the worksheet isn't placed on the drawing at the click (Rodeo can't place a worksheet on a drawing yet), so it opens instead; a worksheet is made from one the drawing has, so in a drawing with none (a new one) the contents show in a dialog; the worksheet is a snapshot, not a live database worksheet that updates; schematic devices assigned to the rack without equipment aren't listed
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD > Calculate Cable Lengths…. The Cable Length Options dialog: a rack diagram numbering the allowances, Calculate Cable Lengths for All circuits or Selected circuits, and Equipment Rack Estimates: Jackfield Swan-Neck, Equipment Tail and False Floor Depth (lengths in the drawing's units, 0 or more, kept for the next run). Each circuit whose Cable Length is empty or Auto gets a Calculated Cable Length (read-only in Object Info, in the drawing's units, rounded up to the millimetre) from the equipment items named as the devices at its ends (for an adapter's socket, the device it's on): in one rack, the height between their middles; in racks side by side (outlines within 100 mm of each other, through a row or group of them), the height of each above the floor, twice the False Floor Depth and the way along the racks, middle to middle; plus at each end the Equipment Tail, or the Jackfield Swan-Neck at a patch panel (a term panel or jackfield). A Cable Length typed in is kept and the circuit passed by. Circuits it can't work out are left without one and marked in the class ConnectCAD Markup, as Check Drawing marks: disconnected, a device with no name or no equipment item of the device's name (errors), more than one equipment item of the name, equipment not in a rack or racks not side by side when the drawing has no cable paths (warnings; with cable paths, see Cable Route); an earlier run's marks on those circuits go first. A message counts the circuits worked out, kept and marked. The circuit reports gain a Calculated Length column after Cable Length. One undo step. A drawing made before the field existed gains it as it's written (Rodeo's own plug-in formats now gain new fields, their objects keeping their values). Rodeo's reading: the help gives no formula, so the sums (the equipment's middles, a tail at each end, the floor depth down and up) are Rodeo's reading of it; Spotlight cables and Cable Run IDs aren't used, and circuits aren't assigned to cable path segments; Cable Length is a text field, not Vectorworks' pop-up of Auto and standard lengths; the lengths aren't totalled for the project; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Cable Route's Cable Route and Drop Point (a new ConnectCAD Cable Route tool set, after ConnectCAD Layout), in Top/Plan. Cable Route: click each corner of a cable path (a 'Rodeo Cable Path' plug-in object), a double-click or Enter ending it; modes Join to Existing Routes or Separate Path (1–2) and Cable Height (a list of heights, 0, 2400 and 3000 mm to start; 3 steps through them, Shift+3 back); its Preferences are Edit Cable Route Heights (each height edited, Add, Delete). Each corner is at the Cable Height chosen when it was clicked; where the height changes the path rises or drops at the corner before. With Join to Existing Routes, a click on a drop point or a cable path (within 8 px, the one under the pointer highlighted) starts the path there or ends it there; a path met part way along is split there in two, and the new path meets it at its height, rising or dropping there to the Cable Height. In plan a cable path is a blue line with a ring where it rises or drops; in 3D a line at its heights above its layer. Object Info: ID, Location, Length (read-only, along it in 3D, in the drawing's units). Reshape moves its corners in plan, each keeping its heights. Drop Point: modes Standard Insertion and Insert in Cable Path (1–2); click where it goes (in Insert in Cable Path, on a cable path, which is split there), then click toward where it turns; Preferences Category and Cable Slack. A 'Rodeo Drop Point': a box with a cross (round for a floor pocket or ceiling box) and its name upright beside it. Object Info: Name (A, B… Z, then AA…, I and O passed by; another drop point's name is refused, and the equipment naming it follows a rename), Category (Wall Box, Floor Pocket, Wall Socket, Ceiling Box, Cable Exit), Connected Equipment (read-only: the equipment items naming it), Cable Slack, Location (read-only: the layout room it's in), kept up to date as rooms and equipment change. Equipment items gain Drop Point under Location (a pop-up of None and the drawing's drop points; greyed for equipment in a rack, which is cabled through its rack; a name no drop point has is refused); equipment placed before the field existed gains it when a drop point is placed. Calculate Cable Lengths now finds equipment that isn't in one rack or in racks side by side along the shortest way through the cable paths: paths whose corners meet (within 10 mm in 3D) make one network; a rack joins it where a path's corner is inside its outline or within 50 mm of it, as does equipment standing on its own; equipment naming a drop point joins at the path corners within 50 mm of the drop point, adding the straight way from the drop point to the equipment and the drop point's Cable Slack; from a corner to the equipment's middle the rise or drop counts, and the tails and swan-necks are added as before. New marks: no drop point of the name (error); more than one; a drop point or a rack not on a cable path; equipment in no rack, on no path and with no drop point; no route between the two ends (warnings). With no cable paths in the drawing the earlier marks stand. Rodeo's reading: Z Insertion and Working Plane modes, the Cable tool and Set Working Plane aren't there; ends don't snap to equipment items (a path ending inside one's outline joins it for Calculate Cable Lengths); drop points are plain 2D, with no symbol, style, leader line or 3D; equipment is joined to a drop point by its Drop Point field, not by dragging its handle; dragging a path doesn't move the network with it, and Reshape's Auto Heal Paths and constraint modes aren't there; Detach from Cable Route Network and Cable Area aren't there (Cable Count, Analyze Cable Routes and Edit Cabling came after); the heights are above each layer, so a network across layers at different elevations doesn't line up; how near a corner must be to join, and the way from a drop point to its equipment, are Rodeo's reading; Spotlight's Cable Path tool still draws plain polylines, outside the network; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD > Analyze Cable Routes, and ConnectCAD Cable Route's Edit Cabling tool, in Top/Plan. Analyze Cable Routes finds each circuit's way as Calculate Cable Lengths does (within one rack or racks side by side, else the shortest way through the cable paths from the rack, equipment or drop point at each end) and writes each cable path's Cable Count (a new read-only field in its Object Info: the circuits running along it). Circuits within racks need no cable path, and circuits to an external (or a device with <EXT> in its name) are left out. With every circuit routed a message counts them; otherwise a dialog lists the circuits it couldn't route with the reason, and Mark up Circuits marks them in the class ConnectCAD Markup (an earlier Analyze's marks go first; one undo step). Edit Cabling: click a cable path (the one under the pointer highlighted) to list the cables along it, or click a drop point and then another to list those between them. The Edit Cabling dialog shows the Cable Count and a table of the cables: a select box, a colour box, Route Index (the same number for cables taking the same way, numbered as they're found, then the route's letter between its ends: A the shortest, B the next, up to F), Number, Signal, Cable, Source, Destination, Drop Points (where each end joins: drop point, rack ID or the equipment's name) and Length (as Calculate Cable Lengths would work it out). Cables assigned to circuits are grey. A double-click on a row shows its route on the drawing in its letter's colour (orange the shortest, blue the next, then green, purple, red and teal), the view fitted to it, the dialog left open; the colour boxes change a letter's colour for the session. Spare cables (pre-cabling): Cable Type (the ConnectCAD Settings' cables, or typed when it has none), Quantity and Route Index (between two drop points clicked: each of their routes, A to F, with its length; along a path: each pair of drop points whose shortest route runs along it) and Add list new ones; their cable type can be changed in the list; Delete takes the selected spare ones out (cables assigned to circuits can't be). Select Alternative Route opens Select Route for the cables selected (all between the same two places): the routes between them, shortest first, with Use, the route's letter and colour, its length and how many cable path pieces it takes; the one picked is kept on each of them (a Cable Route field on the circuit), so Analyze Cable Routes, Calculate Cable Lengths, the riser and cabling sheets and Edit Cabling take it from then on. OK makes the new spare cables on a Spare Cabling design layer (made when there's none, at the scale of the circuits' layer): each an external at each end named for its drop point and number, joined by a circuit numbered for the two drop points and a count (A-B-1, A-B-2…) of the cable type, which runs between those drop points; it deletes those taken out with their externals, keeps the routes and cable types, and writes the paths' Cable Counts, in one undo step, with a message. New Worksheet does the same and then makes a worksheet of the list (in a drawing with no worksheet to make one from, it shows in a dialog). A spare cable joined to a device since is a cable like any other. Rodeo's reading: a kept route is kept by its letter, the second shortest way and so on as the cable paths are when worked out, not as the paths it took when picked, so new or moved paths can change which way that is (a letter no longer there falls back to the shortest, noted); the Cable Count is written at Analyze Cable Routes and Edit Cabling's OK, so a later change shows then, and a path split since keeps its old count until then; Cable Cross-Section Area isn't worked out (cables have no diameters yet); Append to Worksheet only makes a new worksheet (Rodeo can't add rows to one yet); a spare cable's signal is the Socket tool's, and its externals and circuit are Rodeo's reading of Vectorworks' spare cable labels; Connect's Redraw Circuit mode isn't there, so a spare cable is assigned by connecting the sockets and deleting it; Cable tool cables and Spotlight cables aren't listed; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD > Create Riser Sheet… and Create Cabling Sheet…, as Top/Plan viewports. Both run Analyze Cable Routes' search first, so each cable path's Cable Count is current. Create Riser Sheet: Sheet Layer (one of the sheet layers or New Sheet Layer), Viewport Name (Riser-1, Riser-2…) and Cable Path Annotation; it makes a plan viewport of the design layers holding cable paths, at the first one's scale, framing what they draw with a 15 mm margin on paper. Auto-annotate tags each path in the viewport's annotations: a leader from the middle of its plan, up at 45° and then level, to its ID, Location and Cable Count (each that's set on a line of its own, 2 mm text); Annotate manually opens the viewport's annotations with the Text tool chosen; Do not annotate leaves them empty. Create Cabling Sheet: Design Layer (Source) (the layers holding cable paths), Sheet Layer, Viewport Name (Cabling-1…) and Cable Annotation Style (0–100 %: the share of the pairs of places drawn as arcs, the nearest first, the rest as home runs). In the viewport's annotations, the cables between each two places where they join the cable paths (a drop point, a rack, equipment on its own) are drawn in the cable paths' blue, either as an arc between the places labelled with how many of each cable type (the signal when a circuit has no cable type) or as a home run from each end: an arrow toward the other end, labelled like "To B: 2 × SDI". A place on another design layer gets no mark, and the home run from the other end points toward it. Each tag or mark is grouped, a message counts what was drawn, and one undo takes it all away. Rodeo's reading: the viewport is a plan only (Vectorworks' Viewport View offers others); the tags are plain text, not data tags that follow the paths, and the Data Tag tool doesn't reach through a viewport, so Annotate manually means the Text tool; tags and home runs can overlap one another; an arc bows by 0.2 of its length, and the sizes on paper (5 mm leaders, 14 mm home runs) are Rodeo's; circuits to externals and circuits within racks aren't drawn; container sizes aren't worked out; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Stage > ConnectCAD > Create Panel View…, the Layout set's Panel Connector tool, and a panel layout object (Rodeo Panel Layout) with Update From Schematic, Create Schematic Devices, Panel Builder and Edit Connectors. Create Panel View lists the schematic's panel devices (Rodeo Panel devices; the selected ones, those on the active layer or all), by Device, Type, Ports and Layer, with Select All, Deselect All and Select Type, a panel choice (19 in, 10 in or none) and a design layer to draw on (one of the design layers, or New Design Layer, which makes Panel Views 1 at 1:5). Each panel device becomes one layout per run of one connector type, its connectors tagged by port, its rack units as many as the connectors need, placed one under another 50 mm apart. A panel layout draws a rack-mount panel (19 in or 10 in opening, ears with mounting holes at the EIA spacing), a custom plate (Width, Height, Mounting Holes) or no panel, with each connector's footprint (XLR D flange, BNC, RJ45, speakON, HDMI, USB, D-Sub, fibre, terminal block, TRS, or a ring with a cross for others) and writing above it: the Display Tag, and as set, the Connected Device and Connected Socket it's linked to through the schematic (Text Vertical Offset spaces it). Its Device Name links it to the schematic device of that name; Device Type and Connected To follow the schematic as it changes. Update From Schematic adds a connector for each of the device's sockets the layout lacks. Create Schematic Devices asks for a layer (the Select Layer dialog) and makes one device named after the layout, with an IO socket for each connector, and links the layout to it. Panel Builder (after a warning that it replaces the connectors) takes rows of Qty, Panel Connector, Name Prefix and Layout (Horizontal, Vertical or Square), with Duplicate, Delete and Add, and the panel's type, rack units, opening, width, height and holes, and lays the connectors out across the panel, adding rack units when they don't fit. Edit Connectors (also a double-click) lists each connector's Display Tag, Linked to Socket, Connector, X and Y, with Delete. The Panel Connector tool's Single Click, Linear Array and Grid Array modes put one connector at a click, a line Spacing apart from one click toward the next, or a grid across two clicks; its preferences are Link-to Socket (the first connector links to it and the next ones to the sockets after it), Display Tag (counting on), Connector and Spacing. A click in a layout adds to it; a click elsewhere (more than Spacing from a layout with no panel) starts a new one with no panel. Rodeo's reading: links are by socket name; footprints are Rodeo's drawings, not Vectorworks' connector resources or styles, and there's no Layout resource column; there's no object editing mode, Edit Connectors and the Panel Builder take its place; the show-device and show-socket choices are per layout, not per connector; the writing is 2.5 mm high in the model, whatever the layer's scale;writing added later can run past the panel's top; Create Schematic Devices' sockets take the connectors' types where Vectorworks leaves them unknown, and there's no Split Panels; there's no schematic Connector Panel tool and no adapter slots; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Connector Panel tool, and a connector panel's Edit Device Array. The tool places Connector Panel Input or Output devices in Single Click or Linear Array. Its Preferences are Custom Panel Prefix (CTP_), Panel (the connector panels in the drawing, or New… and a name), Port Code, Number (0 for ---, no number), Connector (any connector) and Manufacturer. Each device is one connector of a panel. It is named by the prefix and panel (CTP_BOX1, tagged BOX1), and its port by the code and number (MIC01). Numbers count on past the ports the panel already has; with one connector panel selected, the next follows it. Clicking on a circuit puts the device in the circuit, and the device takes the circuit's signal. Object Info adds Port Code (the sockets follow it) and Edit Device Array…, which sets the connector panels selected: Connector Type, Panel, Port Code, Connector and # Circuits, plus Number for one device or Renumber ports from a number for several (top to bottom, then left to right). Each circuit is a row of sockets (MIC01.1, MIC01.2…), and taking away rows that hold circuits is refused. Create Panel View lists connector panels with the other panels. Rodeo's reading: both types are drawn alike, an input on the left and an output on the right wired through, and only Device Type tells them apart; a panel's name is kept as its letters and last number, so FOH-B2 becomes FOHB2; the CTP_ default comes from the help's example; each row of a multi-circuit connector becomes its own connector on a panel layout; there are no label symbols or Number Format setting (ports have two digits); not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Commands: ConnectCAD/Spotlight Create Labels, the Label Format object, and the Color-Coded Linked Text and Linked QR Code tools. Create Labels (Stage > ConnectCAD, and Stage > Lighting Paperwork) opens Cable Labels: a worksheet to read, a table of label formats with Use checks (the one that suits the worksheet's header ticked), a preview of the first row's label, the count of labels and pages, a sheet layer (or New Sheet Layer) and Save as PDF too. Each format used becomes a Label Format object on the sheet layer, a label for each worksheet row below the header, the pages drawn side by side with grey outlines in the Label Outlines class. Object Info has Worksheet, Skip Top Rows, Type, Format, Description, Page Size (Letter, Legal, Tabloid, A4, A3 or Custom), Page Width/Height, Labels Across/Down, Offset X/Y, Left/Top Margin, Center on Page, Label Width/Height, Horizontal/Vertical Pitch and Number of Copies, and the buttons Update, Export to PDF… (a page each, outlines left out) and Edit Label…. Edit Label lists what a label shows: text with {n} for column n, QR codes and colour codes of a column, each with its place and size, and adds or deletes them. Color-Coded Linked Text and Linked QR Code add a colour code or a QR code of the Selected Column where you click in a label, to every label of that format. Colour codes show a number's digits in the resistor colour code. Rodeo's reading: the formats are six Avery sheets of Rodeo's (two for cables, two for equipment, one for devices, one for lighting devices), not Vectorworks's library, and there's no Import Label Formats or Type New; there's no edit mode for a label: Edit Label and the two tools stand in for it, and there are no barcodes, graphics or plain linked text (a text's {n} links it); database rows of a worksheet aren't read; a colour code shows the last digits, 0 in front when there are fewer; text can run past its label; other objects on the sheet layer within a page print too; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Commands: ConnectCAD Settings (Stage > ConnectCAD), kept in the drawing. General: Number Format (1, 01, 001 or 0001: the least digits in the numbers a Linear Array gives sockets, socket loops, externals and distribution amplifiers) and Enable automatic hopover (each polyline circuit hops the circuits before it in the drawing wherever it crosses them, as it's drawn and again when it moves; turned on, the circuits already drawn hop; the HopOver tool then refuses circuits). Panel Settings: Jackfield, Term Panel and Custom Panel Prefix (the tools take them, and do again whenever the drawing opens) and the panel sizes (jack and terminal panels by video, audio, data and optical, a DIN rail panel, a custom panel), which the panel tools fill before starting the next panel. Numbering: the cable numbering system Number Cables starts on. Signals: the built-in list, with Edit to make one the drawing's own (shown in bold, Reset to take it back) and Add for new ones: name, category (its circuits' colour), letter (in cable numbers) and connectors; every signal list and the signal colours and letters follow. Connectors: the drawing's own, offered for any socket, panel or adapter. Cables: with any listed, a circuit's Cable is chosen from them. Cable Lengths: a circuit's Cable Length is chosen from them; the defaults (0.5 m to 100 m, or 3' to 200' in a drawing in feet or inches) come back with Restore Defaults. Rodeo's reading: the schematic grid, power and weight units, circuit label offsets, Device Builder data source, default circuit graphics and half rack unit mounting aren't offered, since Rodeo's schematics keep their own; there are no group or panel series, alphabetical port labels or delimiters, and no Cable Types pane; numbering systems can't be added or edited; signals have no line types or class attributes; automatic hopover goes by drawing order (the later circuit hops), and turning it off leaves the hops as they are; the settings are kept per drawing as show data and undo with it, not in user or workgroup folders; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Schematics' Device Builder (after Device), in Top/Plan. Click where the device's top left goes and the Device Builder dialog describes it: Make/Model (New Make/Model or one of your devices, which fills the dialog from it) with Delete from My Devices; Make, Model, Device Name (one already in the drawing takes the next number, and a tag that was the name follows it), Display Tag, Display Description, Device Type (Physical or Virtual: a virtual device has no sizes or rack units); Height, Width and Depth in mm, Height in rack units, Half-rack width, Power (W, with the BTU worked out) and Weight (kg); a preview of the device as it'll be drawn; and the Sockets Definition, rows of so many sockets of one type (IN, OUT, IO), signal, connector (the signal's, from ConnectCAD Settings too), side and name, numbered from the name (IN1, IN2…, the type's name when empty; one socket keeps its name as typed), with Use, Duplicate, Delete and Add. A row's sockets go down its side together, a row's gap apart. OK places the device and keeps it in your devices by make and model; Create Temporary Device only places it. Preferences: Minimum Width, Space at Top of Device, Space at Bottom of Device and Socket Group Spacing (in grid spaces, a socket row each), Truncate long display tags (a tag of more than 13 characters becomes its first and last five), Enable database (off, there's no device list and nothing is kept). The device is an ordinary Rodeo Device, so its Object Info, sockets, circuits and reports work as any other. Rodeo's reading: there's no manufacturer device database or downloaded library, so your devices are kept in this browser, not a user or workgroup folder, and a device with no make or model isn't kept; there are no device categories, search, label symbols or modular devices (module widths, slots); the device is placed as a device, not a symbol definition, so there's no Save as Symbol; power and weight are in W and kg only; rows are reordered by Duplicate and Delete, not dragged; Truncate long display tags cuts the tag only; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Commands: Cable Pull Report (Stage > ConnectCAD). A worksheet of the cables to install along each cable route, as the drawing is now: Route (numbered in the order listed: the places at its two ends, whichever way round, and the way between them), From and To (where each end joins the cable paths: drop point, rack ID or the equipment's name), Cable (the circuit's cable type), Quantity, Length (the longest of them) and Total Length, a row for each route and cable type; then each cable type's Quantity and Total Length over every route. Each cable's way and length are Analyze Cable Routes' and Calculate Cable Lengths' (a route kept with Select Alternative Route, spare cables between their drop points); nothing is written to the circuits. In a drawing with no worksheet to make it from, it shows in a dialog; with no cable along the cable paths, a message says so. Rodeo's reading: the help says only that it lists the number and type of cables for each cable route, so the columns, the totals and the longest length are Rodeo's; cables within one rack or racks side by side and cables to externals aren't routes, so aren't listed; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Commands: Create Rack Elevation (Stage > ConnectCAD Layout). With one or more equipment racks selected: the Create Rack Elevation dialog's Sheet Layer (Destination) (a sheet layer, or New Sheet Layer), Viewport Name (the rack's ID, or the racks' IDs for one row) and Views to Create (Front, Rear or both). Racks standing side by side (within 100 mm, as Calculate Cable Lengths counts it) and facing the same way go in one view, left to right as seen from that side; racks apart, or facing another way, get views of their own, named with their racks' IDs after the name. Each view is a viewport at the racks' layer's scale named with /F for the front or /R for the rear (numbered when the name's taken), side by side on the sheet, selected and shown: the racks face on, their base, top and posts (and rails when drawn), a console's cladding round its sloping face, the fronts of the equipment items, rack frames (with their slots and power supplies) and modules mounted on that side at their Rack U, each named, unit numbers up the left post of a 2- or 4-post rack, and each rack's ID below; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks makes section viewports of the 3D racks; Rodeo's viewports draw only plans live, so the elevation is drawn into each viewport's annotations as the racks and equipment are when it's made (its design layers hidden), and doesn't follow later changes (make it again), nor can equipment be moved in it (Edit Section In-Place); each view shows only the equipment mounted on its side, not what's on the other side through the gaps, and leaves out doors, side panels and an enclosure's back, as a section just inside the face would; a rack frame's name isn't written over its slots; the unit numbers and Rack IDs are Rodeo's; equipment below a rack (no Rack U) isn't drawn; on a sheet with something on it already, the new views go to its right; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Commands: Create Equipment Detail View (Stage > ConnectCAD Layout). With one or more equipment items or rack frames selected: the Create Equipment Detail View dialog's Sheet Layer (Destination), Viewport Name (Equipment Detail and the piece's name; for several, each piece's name after the name given) and Views to Create (Front, Rear or both). Each view is a viewport named with /F or /R (numbered when the name's taken), side by side on the sheet (right of what's on it already), selected and shown: from the front, its panel and the ears it's racked by, a rack frame's slots, power supplies and the modules in it, each named; from the rear, its body, narrower by its ears; its name below; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks makes section viewports of the 3D equipment; Rodeo's viewports draw only plans live, so the view is drawn into the viewport's annotations as the equipment is when it's made (its design layers hidden) and doesn't follow later changes; each view is at the largest of 1:1, 1:2, 1:5, 1:10, 1:20, 1:25 and 1:50 that keeps the front within 150 × 100 mm on paper (1:5 for 19-inch gear), where Vectorworks' takes the layer's scale; the rear shows no sockets or connectors; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Commands: ConnectCAD Status (Stage > ConnectCAD). A palette that runs Check Drawing's checks over every design layer again whenever the drawing changes while it's open, without marking anything, and lists the issues, errors before warnings before notes: Status (a coloured dot and Error, Warning or Note), Object (Socket, Circuit, Device, Adapter or Panel), Issue and Details; View buttons that make the issue's layer active, zoom to it and flash its outline, with previous and next buttons to step through the others of a duplicate name or number; and Resolve, where Ignore greys the issue at the foot of the list until Restore; a summary line counts what's left and what's ignored, and No issues found when there are none. Rodeo's reading: only Check Drawing's four checks (duplicate names and numbers, loose circuits and signals that differ), not layout objects or cable paths; no alarm icon is drawn on objects that aren't connected; ignored issues are kept by what they say while the drawing is open, not saved with it; it checks again once edits have stopped for 300 ms; Spotlight lighting devices and distributors aren't checked; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Tool Sets: ConnectCAD Layout's Adapter Slot (after Rack Frame). Adapter slots in an equipment item, for equipment made of a main unit and plug-in modules: modes Single Click, Linear Array and Rectangular Array, and Front or Rear; Socket (a socket of the device of the item's name to pick, or a name to type when there's none; numbered on along an array, left to right and top to bottom), Width and Height. A slot named as a socket in the item's linked device stands for it: it shows the make and model of the adapter on that socket (<Unknown> for none given), and otherwise its Adapter Type, or crossed lines for none; renaming either breaks the link. Slots show in 3D on the item's front or rear, in its Create Equipment Detail View views, and on the equipment's front in Create Rack Elevation views; Slot Name, Adapter Type, Width and Height can be changed. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks places slots on the 3D item in object editing mode, by the automatic working plane; in Rodeo a click on the item in plan, or its Adapter Slots… button in the Object Info palette, opens its front or rear face on in a dialog where slots are placed, picked, changed and deleted, kept on OK in one undo step; the slots are kept in the item rather than as objects of their own, so they're changed in that dialog, not the Object Info palette; an array's slots go side by side with no gap, as many as reach the second click, a row level or plumb; a slot that runs off the face (or onto a racked item's ears) or over another is refused; after a placement the next socket not yet used (or the name numbered on) is offered; Rodeo's adapters aren't symbols, so there's no symbol name to fall back on; slots stand 1 mm proud of the face in 3D and aren't drawn in plan; there's no CC-Equipment-Graphics-FrontDoor class, so slots always show; rack frames take none; not checked against Vectorworks files.
- [x] Commands: Spotlight Numbering (Stage > Lighting, and Stage > ConnectCAD). Numbers the objects of one type in one or more fields at once: Object Type lists the plug-in objects on the drawing's design layers (lighting devices first, Rodeo's own named without 'Rodeo') and the records attached to objects; Available Fields and Fields In Use (Add, Remove, or a double-click), each field in use with its own Start (a number, leading zeros kept as its width, or letters, upper or lower case: A…Z, AA), Prefix and Suffix, Apply same number to all or Increment by (a negative step counts down), Increment by DMX footprint for lighting devices (each device's address counts on by its footprint, and one that won't fit before 512 starts the next universe at address 1, which a Rodeo Instrument's Universe takes), Limit (No Limit, Restart at, Stop at) and Number of Units per Increment, with an example of the first values. With objects of the type selected, Numbering Direction (shown only then): Primary (Left to Right, Right to Left, Top to Bottom, Bottom to Top, or Manual), Secondary across it, Zigzag and Tolerance (objects within it across the primary direction count as one row); they're numbered in that order as one undo step and stay selected, and Stop at numbers those up to the limit, selects the rest and opens the dialog again for them. With nothing of the type selected, or Manual, a click on each object gives it the next values (the object under the pointer outlined with the values it would take); Shift-click gives the last values again without counting on; a double-click on empty space, Enter or Esc ends and selects those numbered; each click is its own undo step. A field that holds a number refuses letters, a prefix or a suffix. Rodeo's reading: only objects directly on design layers are offered (not those inside groups); text, whole-number and number fields are offered, not pop-ups, checkboxes or read-only fields; directions go by the plan as shown (a rotated plan's screen), from each object's insertion point or the middle of its box; a click picks the smallest box of the type under the pointer; Accessory Numbering isn't offered, since a Rodeo accessory is a name in its device's Accessory field rather than an object of its own; there are no multi-cell devices to number cell by cell; the settings are remembered while Rodeo is open, not kept in the drawing; the Cmd+Shift+Option+N shortcut isn't set; a Vectorworks plug-in object Rodeo doesn't draw itself has its fields set but keeps its saved drawing until Vectorworks redraws it; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Commands: Convert To Lighting Device and Replace Lighting Devices… (Stage > Lighting), and a lighting device's Symbol. A Rodeo Instrument's Symbol (Object Info, under Drawing; 'Generic outline' or any of the drawing's symbols) draws that symbol in place of its generic outline, turned so the symbol's front (toward the bottom of the symbol) points where the device aims, Symbol Scale times its size, its 3D part at the device's Lens Height; the label legend (channel bubble, unit number, colour, purpose) is set round the symbol's extent as it is round an outline. Convert To Lighting Device turns the selected symbol instances into lighting devices showing them, where each was and aimed as it was turned (an unturned symbol aims down the page), in its class; each starts with the figures of the generic type its name suggests (Source Four or Leko a 26° profile, Fresnel, PAR 64 by its lamp, LED PAR, movers by wash, spot, beam or profile, cyc floods, battens, followspots; else a 26° profile), and the converted devices are selected; one undo step. Anything else selected is left, with a note to make a symbol first. Replace Lighting Devices: Replace selected lighting devices or Replace All (every lighting device on the visible design layers) with a generic type (which brings its typical wattage, weight, beam, field and lumens and drops a symbol) or one of the drawing's symbols (the device keeps its type and figures); every other field (position, unit, channel, patch, colour, focus) is kept; one undo step, the devices selected after. Object Info's Replace Lighting Device… opens it on the one device. Rodeo's reading: the replacements are Rodeo's 22 generic types and the drawing's own symbols, not the Spotlight libraries through the Resource Selector, and a symbol brings no photometric data (Rodeo has no Light Info Record), so replacing with a symbol keeps the device's figures; the type a symbol's name suggests is Rodeo's guess from its words; Vectorworks' own lighting devices in an opened file keep their saved drawing and aren't replaced; a converted symbol's records aren't carried over and its height isn't read (the device takes the default Lens Height); a mirrored symbol converts unmirrored; the shortcut Cmd+Shift+Option+R isn't set; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Commands: Label Legend Manager…, Assign Legend to Lighting Devices… and Create Label Legend from Lighting Device… (Stage > Lighting), and a lighting device's Use Legend. The manager lists the drawing's label legends with Rodeo default (each device's Channel, Unit, Color and Purpose as its Show boxes say) first: Active (the legend new lighting devices take as they're placed), Add…, Duplicate (a copy under a free name, opening its fields), Edit Fields… and Remove (not the active one). Add New Legend and Edit Label Legend set the Legend Name (needed, and not one already taken), Non-Rotating, and a table of 15 attributes (Channel, Unit Number, Instrument Type, Position, Purpose, Color, Gobo, Dimmer, Circuit, Universe/Address, Universe, Address, Wattage, Accessories, Notes), each with Use, # (the order), Position (Front, Back, Left, Right, the four corners, or On the Body), Container (None, Circle, Oval, Rectangle, Hexagon, Diamond), Right-Reading and Size % (of the device's Label Size). Labels at the same position stack outward from the device in # order, clear of its outline or symbol; positions are as a device aimed down the page shows them and turn with the device, unless the legend is Non-Rotating (then they stay put round the body's reach); a right-reading label stays level and one that isn't turns with the device, never upside down; a container is drawn white round its text; blank fields show nothing. OK writes the legends and draws again the devices using one that changed, as one undo step: renaming a legend renames it on its devices, and devices using a removed one take the active one. Assign Legend to Lighting Devices gives the selected devices a legend (the active one offered); Object Info's Use Legend (under Drawing) gives one device a legend or Rodeo default. Create Label Legend from Lighting Device, with one lighting device selected, makes a legend of its labels (a copy of its legend, Non-Rotating as it is, or Rodeo default's as its Show boxes have them: channel in a circle in front, unit on the body, colour behind, purpose to the right), named after its legend with -1 (or the next number free; a name needed and not taken), and gives it to the device; the active legend stays as it was; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks drags each label into place round a layout symbol in Edit 2D Layout; Rodeo picks one of nine positions instead, and text widths are estimated (labels can sit a little loose or close); labels can't be dragged on one device, so Create Label Legend from Lighting Device copies the labels as its legend lays them, and doesn't make the device's symbol the legend's layout symbol; there are no 3D labels, Edit 3D Layout, single label format, label classes, fonts, custom container symbols, Use Layout Symbol, Assign Legend properties to selected (flipping, 3D view) or Import from another file, and a legend's channel isn't tinted by the colour as Rodeo default's bubble is; accessories and multi-cell parts don't get legends of their own; legends are kept in the drawing as Rodeo show data, not Vectorworks' label legend resources, so legends in an opened Vectorworks file aren't read, and Vectorworks' own lighting devices keep their saved drawing and can't take one; the shortcut Cmd+Shift+Option+L isn't set; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] Commands: a lighting device's right-click menu: Select Lighting Devices by Instrument Type, Select Lighting Devices by Position, Select Rigging Object, Remove from Hanging Position, Swap Lighting Devices… and Refresh Lighting Devices; a hanging position's: Select Lighting Devices on Rigging Object; and Refresh Lighting Devices on the drawing's right-click menu (when it has lighting devices or hanging positions) and Stage > Lighting (⌘/). The selecting ones add the devices of the type right-clicked, at its position, or on the position right-clicked, to the selection; Select Rigging Object selects the hanging position a device's Position names (its layer made active when it's on another). Remove from Hanging Position clears the Position of the device right-clicked (or of the selected ones when it's among them) and weighs the position again, one undo step. Swap Lighting Devices, with two devices selected and nothing else: they trade places, each taking the other's hanging point and lens height, with the Unit Number and Position staying where they hung, and the Circuit, Dimmer and Universe/Address too unless they're ticked to move with the device; everything else (type, colour, channel, accessories, focus, notes) goes with it, one undo step. Its Shift+click choice: Move data immediately using previous settings (skips the dialog) or Display this dialog box; the settings are kept on this machine. Refresh Lighting Devices draws every Rodeo lighting device (labels and legends) and hanging position (its weight and label) again, one undo step. A right-click menu now stays open while a modifier key alone is pressed, so a row can be Shift-clicked. Rodeo's reading: they select where Select Similar looks (the active layer, or the group being edited), not across layers; Rodeo keeps one Circuit field, so Circuit Name and Number move or stay together; a device a drawing brought from Vectorworks is moved and its fields set but its drawing isn't redrawn by Refresh; rigging objects other than hanging positions (trusses, pipes as Braceworks objects) and focus points aren't objects these commands know, so Select Lighting Devices on Rigging Object is on hanging positions only and Select Focused Lighting Devices isn't here; Two-fers aren't kept
- [x] Tool Sets: Furniture/Fixtures' Countertop (Vertex mode) and Spotlight's Focus Point, in Top/Plan. Countertop: click its corners, a double-click, Enter or a click on the first corner closing it; Corner Vertex or Arc Vertex (the Fillet Radius rounding the corners away from the backsplash, as a polyline's arc vertices); a backsplash on the first Backsplash Edges edges from the corner clicked first, drawn as a line its Backsplash Thickness inside them and meeting the edges either side (all the way round when every edge has one); one deeper than the countertop refused; the outline in the drawing's attributes. Focus Point: a click asks for its name in Place Focus Point (offering the next after the last: FP1, FP2…), then places an octagon (Standard Hybrid, Standard 2D) or a 2D locus (Standard 3D, Locus Points Only) with Show Focus Point Name putting its name beside it; a blank name refused. Rodeo's reading: plan only; the octagon's and name's sizes on paper (4 mm, 2.5 mm text) are Rodeo's; the countertop's Marquee, Pick and Bridge modes (from cabinets), elevation, thickness, overhangs, edge and trim profiles, waterfall edges, insertions, materials and styles, and the focus point's height, symbols and the lighting devices assigned to it aren't drawn or kept; each is a polyline or grouped plain objects, not a plug-in object Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Linear Material, in plan and detail drawings. Click the path's corners (Corner Vertex, or Arc Vertex rounding them with the Fillet Radius); a double-click or Enter ends it open, a click on the first corner closes it. The material lies on the path's left, from the path moved by the Offset (its right when negative) out to the Thickness. Types: Board (generic), its two edges with the space between in the drawing's fill; Gyp. Bd. (detail), a facing line near its face, stippled; Plaster/Stucco, a rough face (detail: stippled); Plywood/OSB, four plies (detail: slashes across it in threes); Built-up Roof, a solid band under gravel; Corrugated Deck, its profile at the Pitch and Corrugation Depth (w/fill: a topping the Thickness over the flats filled into the pans; detail: stippled). Closure closes the start, end or both; Density sets the stipple per cm² on paper; too many dots refused, as is a material too thick for the path's corners. Rodeo's reading: the facing's place, the ply count, the slashes' spacing, the gravel, the roughness and the deck's flat-to-web shares are Rodeo's, matched to Vectorworks' pictures; stippled types are drawn with dots as the Stipple tool's; Bezier and cubic path vertices, plug-in symbols of it and editing its path afterward aren't offered; it is grouped plain objects, not a linear material Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Braceworks' Rigging Load, in Top/Plan. Point Load mode: a click where the load hangs draws its generic circle (green) with the Legend Entry over it and its label beside it: the Load Name (Show Name), its weight and #Load ID, its Load Category under them (Show Load Category); a press dragged along the rigging turns the label Non-Rotated, Perpendicular (across the rigging) or Angled (45° to it), kept reading left to right. Distributed Load mode: a clicked path along the rigging (double-click or Enter ends it, a click on the first corner closes it) drawn as a heavy yellow line, labelled beside its middle with its name, category, total weight, weight per length and length (Show Label); Distributed Weight mode reads a weight per foot or metre, or a weight with a slash before the length it's spread over (500/10 is 500 per 10 of the drawing's units), Total Weight mode the whole load. Weights are pounds in an imperial drawing and kilograms otherwise, unless lb or kg follows them, shown to two decimals (lb, lb/ft or kg, kg/m); a weight that isn't one is refused and the last kept. Text Size in points. Rodeo's reading: the load isn't attached to or centred on a rigging object (nothing is highlighted, and a distributed load may run past one), so the drag stands in for the rigging's direction; the circle is 5 mm across on paper and the legend 0.6 of the text size; the label sits where it's drawn, without a control point to move it; load sets (Load Settings), Include in Calculations, Scale Distributed Load, symbols in place of the circle, standard classes, Select Similar Objects, Add Point Loads and Add Distributed Load at Selection, and Braceworks calculations and reports aren't offered; each is grouped plain objects, not a load object Object Info can edit or Braceworks can weigh
- [x] Tool Sets: Detailing's Framing Member, in Top/Plan. A drag or two clicks set its span and turn (Shift in 15° steps); it's drawn the Width across by its 2D Display: Solid (a closed outline in the drawing's attributes, filled with its fill), Centerline (a line down its middle), Width (its outline unfilled) or Width with Centerline (the outline and its centre line in the drawing's first dashed line type, solid in a drawing without one). Beginning and Ending Miter cut each end that many degrees from square in plan (within 80°; positive lengthens the left edge, as drawn, at that end), miters that would cross refused; Bearing Inset starts the member that far before its first click and Overhang runs it that far past its second, as a rafter's do; Show Label puts the Label Text above its middle, turned with it and read left to right. Rodeo's reading: the label is 2.5 mm high on paper and sits where it's drawn, without a control point; the start arrow shown when selected, Height, Pitch, Length (span only), bevels, the types (rafter, solid beam, wood floor truss, open-web steel joist, cold-formed steel channel, steel section, custom profile) and their webs, chords, gauges, notches, hangers and profiles, roll angle, Structural Use, Material, Volume and Quantity Label, vertical reference, eave styles and fascia, and 3D aren't drawn or kept; each is plain objects grouped, not a framing member Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Building Shell's (and Braceworks') Structural Member, in Top/Plan. Column Insertion stands the column's section where it's clicked, a press dragged turning it (Shift in 15° steps): a steel Wide Flange, Channel, Angle, Tee, I-Beam, Rectangular, Square or Round Tubing or Z-Section as Steel Section draws it (Depth, Breadth, Web and Flange Thickness), a concrete rectangle or circle, or a wood rectangle. Linear Insertion's two clicks (Shift in 15° steps) and Poly Insertion's clicked path (double-click or Enter ends it) lay the member the Breadth wide from above (a square or round tube's Depth): Axis Alignment puts the clicks on its Left, Center or Right edge as seen along it, Offset moves it across; Start and End Offset run each end on past its click or cut it back (a member cut to nothing refused); Start and End Miter cut each end up to 70° from square (positive lengthens its left edge there; miters that would cross refused); Start and End Cap draw each end's line; Use Center Mark draws a line the Mark Length on its middle beyond each end, the Mark Gap from it. Steel sizes that don't make a section are refused. Rodeo's reading: one set of sizes serves every shape; the member is a closed outline in the drawing's attributes (its fill) when both caps are on, else its fill unstroked with its edges and the cap that's on drawn as lines; the centre marks' look is Rodeo's; Member ID, Structural Usage, material resources and shape libraries' series and sizes, covers, Profile Rotation, the vertical part of axis alignment, elevations, bounds and Column Height, bevels, Auto Join Members and end conditions by association, attributes by class, line style, material or cut plane, and 3D aren't drawn or kept; each is plain objects, a linear member grouped, not a structural member Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Dims/Notes' Title Block Border. A click places the border centred on it, its sizes on paper times the layer's scale: Sheet Size ISO A0–A4, ARCH A–E1, ANSI A–E or Custom (Width, Height), Landscape or Portrait, Sheet Fit Mode (the sheet is the margins' outside edge or the frame inside them), Left/Right/Top/Bottom Margins, the frame drawn the Border Line Weight, Hide border (the title block alone, where it sits in the frame); Show grids letters the Vertical Zones (A–Z without I and O, then AA…; Top to Bottom or Bottom to Top) and numbers the Horizontal Zones (Left to Right or Right to Left) in the margins at the Grid Label Size, with marker lines between them out from the frame the Grid Marker Line Length (no further than the margin); Show grid line extensions runs the frame's lines out through the margins; Crop Marks None, Crops (crosses on the sheet's corners), Outline (its edge) or both; a title block in the frame's bottom right corner (Title Block Scale Factor) with Project Name, Sheet Title, Drawn By, Date (today's to start), Scale (the layer's when blank) and Sheet Number. Margins that leave no frame, more than 100 zones a side and a title block bigger than the frame are refused. Rodeo's reading: margins start at ISO 5457's 20 mm left and 10 mm elsewhere and 0 zones gives about one every 50 mm, rather than each standard size setting its own; the title block is Rodeo's generic 170 × 56 mm layout; the border is placed where clicked, not locked to the page centre; title block layouts, graphics and styles, project, sheet, revision and issue data shared with Title Block Manager and records, automatic drawing coordination and page numbers, grid lines across the drawing, fold marks, countersignatures, border fills and crop mark line weights aren't kept; it's plain objects grouped, not a title block border Object Info or Title Block Manager can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Lighting Device, in Top/Plan. A click where it hangs and a second click (or a drag) where it focuses places an instrument of one of 22 generic types, at its real size with the type's typical lumens, watts, weight and beam and field angles; Automatic Numbering counts the unit and channel on. It's a Rodeo Instrument, a plug-in object that Object Info edits and draws again from its position, unit, channel, patch, dimmer, circuit, colour, gobo, purpose, accessory, notes and focus. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks inserts a library symbol as a Lighting Device; a device can show one of the drawing's own symbols (Symbol in its Object Info, Convert To Lighting Device, Replace Lighting Devices), and label legends (Label Legend Manager), but library symbols, the Light Info Record, multi-cell devices and fixture modes aren't here, the figures are typical ones rather than a maker's photometric data, and how Vectorworks itself shows a Rodeo Instrument hasn't been checked. Vectorworks' own Lighting Device objects in an opened file are read by the Stage menu's reports and edited where their fields match
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Photometer and Photometric Grid (in Rigging, after Focus Point), in Top/Plan, as real plug-in objects. Photometer: a click where it reads, a second click (or drag) for its rotation. Photometric Grid: Center-line Placement or Edge Placement, two clicks along it and a third for its width. Object Info has Elevation, Show Elevation, Show Zero, Use Threshold Settings, Threshold Settings (the zero, below, between and above colours and the two thresholds), Refresh, and for the grid Grid Spacing X and Y, Grid Width X and Y, Slope Based On, Tilt Angle X and Y, and Height Offset X and Y, each pair worked out from the other. Readings are footcandles in a drawing in feet or inches and lux in a metric one, and a tilted grid takes the cosine of its own plane. Not as Vectorworks: Calculate Using Only Visible Beams (Rodeo's instruments have no Draw Beam, so every instrument counts), Show 3D Labels (the 3D view draws no text), and Text Style (a Text Size instead). The light comes from Rodeo's own beam model, a curve through each instrument's catalogue beam and field angles and its lumens, not a photometric file, at the levels being looked at; it leaves out bounced light, so its numbers are estimates and won't match Vectorworks' to the digit. Elevation is above the same floor as lens heights, and a grid's tilt rises from its corner at -X, -Y, which is Rodeo's reading of the help
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Hanging Position (in Rigging, before Lighting Device) and Stage > Lighting > Hanging Position, Convert to Hanging Position, Auto Number Positions, a real plug-in object holding its geometry and a label. Convert takes the selection (several: a dialog chooses one position from all or one for each), asks for a name and Use Geometry or Create Symbol (`<name>-Sym`), and gives the name to selected devices and to loose ones in its box. The tool places a position showing a symbol from the drawing (Position Symbol in its Preferences, or the one picked in Resources): click, click again for the rotation, name it; Automatic Numbering in its Preferences counts names on without asking. Object Info: Position Name (renames its devices' Position), Location, Show Name, Trim Height (the geometry's lowest point; moves it and its devices), Insert Position Summary (an Equipment Summary Key filtered to it), Starting Number, Increment, page-relative Numbering Direction, Auto Number, Symbol Name (swaps the geometry for a symbol), Edit Position Geometry, Label Location X/Y, Total Hung Weight, Select Lighting Devices, Refresh. The Lighting Device tool outlines the position under the pointer, and a device placed there, with no Position in the tool's Preferences, takes its name and trim. 3D-only geometry gets its mesh rims (or its box) as 2D lines so it shows in Top/Plan. Tested with a blank drawing's synthetic pipes, meshes and symbols, at layer elevations 0 and 1000, through save and reopen; not opened in Vectorworks. Limits: belonging is by the Position name, and which position a click is on is by its box (with the label, plus 150 mm), not the geometry's lines; moving a position doesn't carry its devices (Select Lighting Devices picks them to move together); only lighting devices count as loads, so Total Hung Weight leaves out the structure and other loads, and it reads the devices when the position last drew (Refresh); Edit Position Geometry puts the geometry back on the layer to convert again rather than an edit mode; the symbols are those in the drawing, not the libraries. Left out: Roll and Hanging Angle, Text Style, Multi-Cell Numbering (Rodeo has no multi-cell devices), Classes, Select System Objects, Change Trim Height of System, the truss's Convert button and right-click row (Rodeo's trusses are plain groups).
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Lighting Accessory (in Rigging, after Lighting Device), in Top/Plan. Default Insertion: click a lighting device (a ring shows which) to add the accessory chosen in Preferences; Remove: click to take its last one off; Apply to Selection (key 3) adds it to every selected device, or in Remove mode takes all of theirs off. 12 generic accessories: Barn Doors, Top Hat, Half Hat, Gel Frame, Donut, Color Scroller, Iris, Gobo Holder, Gobo Rotator, Safety Cable, C-Clamp, Side Arm. A Rodeo Instrument draws them where their part goes, as the help has it: lens ones in front, one before another, body ones a third of the way back, yoke and base ones on its hanging point; the channel's bubble moves out past them. They're written to its Accessory field, so anything typed there counts too (unknown names aren't drawn), and each adds its weight to the device's. Tested on a blank drawing's synthetic devices, through save and reopen; not opened in Vectorworks. Limits: Vectorworks' accessories are symbols with 3D and a Parts record, moved in an accessory edit mode; here they're generic outlines in plan only, the weights are generic figures of Rodeo's, a device has one of each, a Vectorworks device only has its field and weight changed, and Remove takes the last one rather than the one under the pointer. Left out: accessory edit mode, cells of a multi-cell device (Rodeo has none), a base accessory moving the insertion point.
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Equipment Summary Key (in Rigging, after Photometric Grid), in Top/Plan, a real plug-in object. A click places it with its insertion point at the corner the mode chooses (Top Left, Bottom Left, Top Right, Bottom Right). It lists the lighting devices by type, with a generic outline, and the plug-in objects in the drawing with their counts; Object Info has Key Title, Position Filter, Column Headers, Total, Color Summary (and Show Swatch), Gobo Summary, Circuit Summary, Position Height ("Varies" unless it's filtered to one height), Show Border, Text Size and Refresh, and Items and Elements… ticks what's listed, symbols included. Not as Vectorworks: it has no inventory or its filters, no symbol thumbnails, no column breaks, subtotals or custom headers and notes, and no Insert Position Summary, as Rodeo has no Hanging Position object yet. Its columns are sized from the text's length, as Rodeo can't measure text here, and it doesn't count things inside groups
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Focus Lighting Devices Along Path (in Rigging, after Focus Point), in Top/Plan. With lighting devices selected: Closest Point, Evenly Between (a beam at each end) or Evenly Inside (half a space in), on a closed path starting at the first vertex or to either side of it, along an existing object clicked or a path drawn in Vertex mode (a double-click or Enter ends it), with each beam and its centre line previewed. Create Focus Points and Prefix mark each spot with a focus point. Each device's focus and aim are set. Not as Vectorworks: Vertex draws straight corners only, not the other polyline modes; a path of separate focus point objects isn't read; the Selection tool's Alt boomerang and the right-click entry to the tool aren't there; and devices that lie crosswise to the path take their spots by the closest point along it, which is Rodeo's reading of what keeps beams from crossing
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Ganging (in Rigging, after Lighting Device), in Top/Plan. Click each lighting device to gang, the last with a double-click (or Enter after it; a single device refused), then where the two-fer's label goes. A line runs from each device straight up or down the screen to the label's height and one line there joins them, its outer corners Square, Arc or Bézier (3 mm on paper, smaller where the lines are shorter); where the label lies past the outer devices the joining line runs on to it, the device's line meeting it in a T. The label shows the Field Value (for Gang By Circuit, and Dimmer and Channel, two values separated by a comma, the second below the first), in a circle with Use Container, the joining line and any device line under it stopping at its edge; Show Label off draws the lines alone; an empty Field Value with Show Label on refused. The two-fer and its label show as it's placed; Esc cancels. Rodeo's reading: Rodeo's lighting devices are grouped plain objects with no records, so the clicks are taken as the devices' points and the shared circuit, dimmer or channel isn't written into them or checked; Rotation, text styles, the label's class, editing the two-fer in Object Info and counting it in reports aren't kept; the lines, circle and text are grouped plain objects
- [x] Stage > venue: New Venue… draws a proscenium, thrust, arena, black box or traverse room with its stage, seats and load-in door and sets the stage origin; Set Stage Origin (the Stage Origin tool) and Show Stage Coordinates measure across from the centre line and up from the plaster line; Linesets… keeps a lineset schedule with its hang plot and draws battens on the plan; Lighting Section… draws the section with each focused instrument's beam and the first and last rows' sightlines, and flags what the front row can see
- [x] Stage > Sightlines: Audience… seat blocks, Show Seats, Seat Coverage Map (how much of the stage each seat sees past the proscenium or chosen masking, as a heat map and a list of the worst seats, a click on one looking from it in 3D), Masking Solver… (legs and borders from one critical seat, drawn on the plan or added to the linesets) and Seating Rake Solver… (risers by the isoptic C-value method, with its section). Rodeo's reading: the coverage map is in plan only, so seat height and rake aren't tested, and the masking solver leaves out cloth fullness and rigging loads
- [x] Stage > Scenes: the Scenes palette holds the set's pieces and where each sits in each scene, Go moving them as one undo step, a scrub through the change with ghosts, and a warning when two pieces hit on the way; Shift Plot… (who moves what, where to, with crew and seconds), Spike Sheet… and the Spike Mark tool. Rodeo's reading: hits are checked with each piece as a rectangle moving in a straight line
- [x] Stage > Scene Shop: New Flat… and New Platform… draw shop drawings, Cut List… nests their stock and sheet parts, and Model Box Cut Sheet… lays out a scale model for a laser cutter as SVG
- [x] Stage > Lighting: Show Beam Pools (off, selected, all), Light Coverage Map… (a lux heat map on the focus plane), Wash Planner… (spacing and pipe for an even wash, with predicted uniformity), the Focus Instrument tool, the Looks palette (levels per channel, cross-fades, a cue list), Show Beams in 3D and Gel and Paint Matrix…. Rodeo's reading: light figures are estimates from typical lumens with no bounce; colour is RGB, not spectral; 3D beams blend normally rather than adding
- [x] Commands: Manage Scenes… and Animate Scenes… (Stage > Visualization; Spotlight > Visualization in Vectorworks). Manage Scenes lists the drawing's scenes in the order they were saved (Number, Scene Name, Up and Down Time, devices kept); Save… keeps every lighting device's color, position, height and focus point and each channel's level under a Scene Name, Scene Number (one past the highest to start), Up Time and Down Time (seconds, 0 to 999; names unique); Edit… changes those four after confirming; Remove takes the scene out after confirming; Go! asks, then puts the scene's devices and levels back. The list is kept in the drawing on OK (or Go!), one undo step with Go!'s changes; devices deleted since a scene was saved are counted, ones added since left as they are. Animate Scenes: a Start Scene and End Scene each with Edit Scene… and a Hold for time, and Video Settings… (frames per second 24, 25, 30 or 60, quality, frame size 640 × 480 to 4K or the screen's), recording the 3D view as it looks now (its render mode, Shaded where it renders realistically) as an .mp4 (else .webm): the start held, then the fade with each channel rising over the End Scene's Up Time and falling over its Down Time, then the end held; the drawing and the levels shown are put back after. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' help doesn't say how a fade runs, so levels move in a straight line, as a console's timed fade does; a scene's levels are Rodeo's look levels by channel (Vectorworks keeps a level on each device), and a scene saved with no look shown has every channel at full; in a movie the devices' colors, positions and focus change at once where the fade starts rather than moving through it; Vectorworks' Choose Path dialog is the browser's download, and its custom frame size isn't offered
- [x] Commands: Set Spotlight Rendering Options… (Stage > Visualization; Spotlight > Visualization in Vectorworks): Lighting Device Options (Cast shadows; Lit fog with how thick the haze is, 1 to 100 %, so the lights' beams show in the air in the 3D view and in Realistic renders), Background (Physical Sky, Gradient, Solid Color or Studio, the renders' sky), Indirect Lighting (None to Interior, 16 Bounces, as View > Set Lighting Options counts them), Ambient Light (On, Brightness 0 to 100 %, 35 % being the usual) and Emitter Brightness (0 to 1000 %). Rodeo's reading: these are the same settings the Render and Visualization palettes and Set Lighting Options show, so they belong to this machine rather than the file and have no undo; Cast shadows is the 3D view's shadows, which come from the sun and the first lights near where the view looks, not every device; not offered: Soft shadows, Distance Falloff (Rodeo's lights fall off as the inverse square, always), the ambient light's Color, White Color Temperature, and Vectorworks' Background list of its own resources
- [x] Commands: DMX Patch… (Stage > Lighting), Spotlight's patch dialog. Every lighting device's Channel, Universe and DMX Address in a list, editable in place, filtered by universe or to <Unpatched>; each device's problems named as Vectorworks names them (Patch overlap: another device takes some of its addresses from a different start; Identical patch: the same start and channel; Patch conflict: the same start, another channel; Out of universe: it runs past 512); Find Next Conflict (by universe, then address, round to the start); Unpatch (the channel, the universe and address, or all, as Patch Settings says); Patch Settings (Channel, Patch Universe and Address, or Patch all fields; the start channel, universe and address, shown from the first selected device or the first free addresses it fits; the channel Increment) and Patch, the address going along by each footprint and a device that won't fit before 512 starting the next universe, asking before it unpatches the devices it lands on; the diagnostic diagram, 32 addresses to a row, free, patched, each problem in its own colour and more than one in red, the selected devices outlined with arrows at their first and last addresses, where pointing names what's on an address and dragging a device moves it (across universes too); OK writes what changed as one undo step. A Vectorworks device's Universe/Address is left blank when unpatched. Rodeo's reading: there are no systems (A to Z) and no universe labels or Spotlight preferences Universes pane, every universe holds 512 and none is limited to 255; multi-cell devices and accessories aren't patched part by part; the list's columns don't sort; a device the Patch run lands on loses its universe and address only
- [x] Commands: Inventory and Equipment List… (Stage > Lighting Paperwork; Spotlight > Inventory and Equipment List in Vectorworks). Symbols and Parts: the equipment in the drawing (lighting devices by type, plug-in objects and symbols by name, on design layers) by category, with Symbol/Object, In Use, Part Type, Category (an item moved by picking another), Source (one of the inventories), Remaining in source inventory, Total in source inventory, Remaining via all in use inventories and Total via all in use inventories, red where there isn't enough; a category filter; New Category…, Edit Category… (renames it, its items following) and Remove Category (only when empty); Create Equipment List Worksheet… (Worksheet Name, Show vendor information when everything listed comes from one inventory in use naming its vendor, Include assigned sources column: the list shown by category with quantities and a total) and Create Equipment List Report… (a row an item, every column). Manage Inventories: Use ticks, the inventory's name, vendor and item count; New Inventory from File (the drawing's equipment, each item's count as its stock, after Inventory Information: name, vendor, contact, notes), Update Inventory File (made again from the drawing, after confirming), Delete Selected Inventory (after confirming), Edit Inventory Settings…, Import… and Export… (.xml), and the highlighted inventory's items with their Stock to type and the Totals in the inventories in use. Rodeo's reading: inventories are kept on this machine (Vectorworks keeps them as .xml files in the user folder), and the drawing keeps which it uses, each item's source and its categories, in one undo step on OK; items are matched by name (a lighting device by its type's name), as Vectorworks' help doesn't say how it matches them; the .xml is Rodeo's own layout, Vectorworks' being unpublished; where the drawing has no worksheet to copy one from, the worksheet or report is saved as a .csv file instead; the worksheets are plain ones, not database worksheets; not offered: subparts, virtual parts and independent virtual parts, Edit Selected's Properties, filters (Manage Filters), Show project name, Assign Sources in Object Info and the Spotlight preferences' Inventory pane; equipment inside groups isn't counted
- [x] Stage > Lighting Paperwork: Paperwork… (instrument schedule, channel and dimmer hookups, colour cuts, gobo pull, shop order, problems; CSV and zip), Patch… (wizard, numbering, universe map), Export to Console (USITT ASCII, CSV), Export MVR… and Import MVR…, Focus Cards… (printable, with QR codes) and Magic Sheet…. Rodeo's reading: MVR hasn't been opened in other software, and its import doesn't bring colour, channel or footprint
- [x] File > Export > Export Lighting Device Data… and File > Import > Import Lighting Device Data… (`features/theatre/patch/deviceData.ts`, dialogs in `deviceDataDialogs.ts`): Spotlight's tab-delimited exchange with Lightwright and spreadsheets, for every lighting instrument (Rodeo Instruments and Vectorworks' Lighting Devices alike). Export ticks the fields to write (All, User for the last set exported, None, named sets saved and loaded), with the field names as the first record when ticked, and always writes the UID first. Import reads tab-delimited text, CSV or an Excel workbook's first sheet; takes a Unique ID Field to match rows on; links each incoming column to a record field (AutoLink by name, by hand, Unlink All, named sets of links); has Import First Record for files with no field names; and sets the matched instruments in one undo step. Rows that match none are placed as new Rodeo Instruments in a row below the drawing's bottom left, as Vectorworks places orphans outside the page. Limits: the UID is Rodeo's id for the object, not Vectorworks' own UID field, so a file exported from Vectorworks matches by another field (the Unique ID Field can match any linked field, Channel say) rather than by UID. Rodeo's fields are those the instruments carry (type, position, unit, channel, patch, dimmer, circuit, colour, gobo, purpose, accessory, notes, photometrics, height, aim; location, layer and class going out only), not Spotlight's full list. Accessories go out in their field, not as rows of their own. Saved field sets and links are kept in this browser rather than as files. Universe/Address and Universe and Address set the same patch, so only one of the two may be linked. On a Vectorworks device only the fields its record has are set, and its drawing stays as saved.
- [x] Stage > Loads and Power: Load Check… (a continuous beam per batten from its supports) and Power Balance… (across one or three phases, and demand per look). Rodeo's reading: a planning check, not an engineer's calculation
- [x] Stage > Show Audit…: patch overlaps and overruns, loads, low lenses, guard rails, a piece that won't go through the load-in door, and anything across the iron line. Rodeo's reading: a flat is taken as 100 mm thick, and a piece is assumed carried lengthwise through the door
- [x] Tool Sets: Basic's Leader Line Simple and Leader Line. Leader Line Simple: click the start of the shoulder, then where the shoulder ends and the leader starts, then the leader's end; the end gets the Attributes palette's end marker, or Vectorworks' default arrow (1/8 inch at 15°) when it has none. Leader Line: click where the leader points, then where it meets the shoulder; the shoulder runs level from there the Shoulder Length on paper toward the side the text goes (Arrows: away from the point, Left or Right), and the point gets its Marker Style (Arrow, Dot, Slash or None) at its Marker Size and Marker Angle. Shift keeps each segment to 15° steps; Esc cancels. Each is an open polygon carrying a real line marker, so the Attributes palette and Object Info edit it like any line. Rodeo's reading: the Design Suite Leader Line's two clicks set where it points and where its shoulder starts rather than placing it and turning it; it isn't a leader line object with marker and shoulder control points, its text isn't kept with it, and Align/Distribute Leader Lines isn't there
- [x] Tool Sets: Machine Components' Torsion Spring - Front and Torsion Spring - End (2D). Front: a click places the spring (a press dragged turns it), its close-wound coils running right along their axis (the Number of Active Coils plus one wire's width long, the Outside Diameter across, the Wire Diameter thick) in the springs' five displays on keys 1–5, with a Left End and a Right End leg each its Length from the axis, Up or Down, and Normal (leaving the coils' side), Facing Forward (crossing in front of them) or Facing Rearward (crossing behind them), Spiral Left Hand or Right Hand, and a centre line. End: the coils' ring round the click, End #1 and End #2 each a leg leaving it on a tangent at its Deflection (End #1's measured clockwise from straight up, End #2's anticlockwise, as the help's picture shows them), its Length from the centre to the bend's outside, then its End Style: Plain, 1/4, 1/2 or 3/4 Bend CW or CCW round its Bend Radius and on its Bend Length, or a Full Loop; a left-hand spiral mirrors it. A wire too thick for the diameter, a leg too short to clear the coils or reach its bend, and a bend radius under half the wire are refused. Rodeo's reading: a front leg is a straight band across the axis rather than following the wire's half-pitch slant, and Normal leaves from the axis; the 3D torsion spring (and End's Number of Active Coils, which only it uses) isn't drawn; it's plain objects grouped, not a torsion spring object Object Info can edit
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Distributor and Electrical Component, in Top/Plan. Distributor: a click places it centred on the click (a press dragged turns it; Shift 15° steps) as the rectangle Vectorworks places when no distributor symbol is chosen, Width by Depth, its Outputs (0–48) numbered in a row along it, its ID with its Name over it (Show Name) level on the page at the Label Size on paper, and with Draw output lengths a circle the Output Length round it; Automatic Numbering counts the last number in the ID on by Increment by after each one (prefixes, suffixes and leading zeros kept). Electrical Component: Power Source, Input, Overcurrent Protector, Output or Consumer on keys 1–5, drawn from above 10 cm square at Scale X/Y 1, a yellow, green, blue or magenta drum on a grey base, or a narrow grey box with an orange cap for an overcurrent protector.
