Rodeo's reading: the socket row, the label's place and the output length circle round the centre are Rodeo's, and the glyphs are read off the help's 3D pictures; distributor symbols, power patching (inputs, outputs, connectors, voltages, payloads, power sources), Braceworks loads and attaching to rigging, data tags, paperwork, Numbering Preferences' start, limit and units per increment, and the components' names, connectors, sockets, ratings and power aren't kept; both are plain objects grouped, not objects power planning can read
- [x] Tool Sets: Dims/Notes' Hyperlink. A click inserts a hyperlink, a Hyperlink plug-in object holding its Label, Function and target (named as Vectorworks' report of hyperlinks shows them: EditableLabel, EditableFunction, EditableTarget, EditableSaveAs), selected, the tool staying for more. Hyperlink Settings: Label; Function: Open Webpage (Webpage), Launch Application, Open Document and Open Folder (the path, Save Path As absolute or relative), Activate Saved View (the drawing's saved views), Activate Sheet Layer (its sheet layers) and QR Code (Text / URL and Image Width on paper); the Tool bar's hint shows the function and target. It's drawn as its label beside a chain link (Label Size on paper), or as the QR code of its text: byte mode, error correction level M, the smallest of versions 1 to 40 that holds it, the best-scoring mask, on a white square with a four-module margin the Image Width across, its dark modules one polygon. Cmd-click (Ctrl-click) on it with the Selection tool, Open Link on its right-click menu or Object Info's Open Link button follows it: a webpage (or a QR code's web address) opens in a new tab, a saved view is restored, a sheet layer (by number or title) is activated. Object Info edits its parameters and draws it again, showing Save Path As only for paths and Image Width only for QR codes. Rodeo's reading: a chain link and label stand in for Vectorworks' hyperlink symbols (#Hyperlinktext#), which aren't chosen, edited or kept; a browser can't launch applications or open documents and folders on this computer, so those links are kept but say so when followed; Rodeo opens http, https, mailto and tel addresses only; saved views are kept on the computer that saved them, so a link to one only works there; Vectorworks' own Hyperlink objects show their parameters as saved, and links aren't made live in PDF export or Create Report
- [x] Tool Sets: Dims/Notes' Section-Elevation Line. Click each corner of the line, any number of segments, a double-click or Enter ending it; Esc cancels. Configuration: Section (a bubble past each end), Partial Section (past its start) or Elevation (a bubble on the middle of the line, the line stopping at its circle); Look Left or Look Right of the way it's drawn, each arrow square to its own end segment (or, for an elevation, the segment it sits on). Drawing Number and Sheet Number in each bubble, kept level; Marker Scale Factor on a 10 mm bubble on paper; Use gapped line with the Gap Line Length of solid line kept at each end of a section. Rodeo's reading: plain 2D objects grouped in the active class rather than a section-elevation line object, so it isn't linked to a viewport (no Create Section Viewport, Navigate to Viewport, auto-coordinated titles or a line defining a viewport), styles, edited marker layouts and a different end marker aren't offered, the Drawing Title and Note aren't shown, the marker always reads level (so Auto-rotate isn't needed), an elevation's marker isn't a control point to drag along the line, and Reverse Direction is choosing the other look before drawing
- [x] Tool Sets: Visualization's Flyover, Translate View and Walkthrough (also beside Pan and Zoom in the top bar). Picking one in Top/Plan shows the 3D view, and while one is active the Mode bar stays in 3D for its modes and preferences; the middle button still zooms, the right button pans, and W A S D, the arrows, Q and E still walk. Flyover: a drag turns the view about the View Center, an Interactive Origin clicked on the model, or the Object Center (the middle of the selection, else of the visible model) (keys 1–3); Flyover Preferences' Rotation Sensitivity (0.1 to 5) scales how far a drag turns it. Translate View: a drag slides the view across the screen, or with Perspective Move In-Out moves it along the sightline, dragging up going in (keys 1–2). Walkthrough: switches to a perspective projection and stands the viewer the Viewer Height (Walkthrough Preferences, 1600 mm to start) above the active layer's plane, looking level; Walk (dragging up goes forward, down back, sideways turns), Look Around (turns and tilts where the viewer stands, up to 85° up or down) and Elevator (rides up and down) (keys 1–3). Not built: Rotate View, Perspective Adjustment, the Data bar's speed and angle fields, and gamer-style mouselook
- [x] Tool Sets: Spotlight's Align and Distribute (in Rigging, beside Focus Point). With lighting devices or any other objects selected, click the guide line's start and end (Shift in 45° steps; Esc starts over) and each moves by its insertion point (symbols and plug-in objects) or the middle of its box. Align (key 1) moves each square onto the line, keeping its place along it; Distribute (key 2) orders them along the line (ties from the top of the screen down) and lays them Evenly between points (key 3: the first at the start, the last at the end), Evenly inside points (key 4: an equal space at each end) or At Interval (key 5: the Interval in Preferences apart from the start). One undo step; the selection and tool stay. Rodeo moves any selected object, where Vectorworks takes lighting devices and other loads; Align places onto the line run on past its ends; 3D snapping to a working plane isn't built
- [ ] Tool Sets: Building Shell's tools past the Wall, Wall Join, Door, Window, Slab, Column, Pilaster, Stair, Simple Stair, Ramp, Elevator and Escalator tools (columns, pilasters, stairs, ramps, elevators and escalators in 3D and inserted in walls, roofs, drilled footings, mullions, wall end caps), Site Planning's tools past Plant, Hardscape, Landscape Area, Hedgerow, Property Line, Parking Space, Parking Spaces, Parking Area, Parking Along Path, Stake, Fence, Curb and Roadway (Straight), (Curved), (Tee) and (Poly) (site models, grades and pads, massing models, stakes as site model data, roadways as site modifiers, Roadway (NURBS)), Furniture/Fixtures past Table and Chairs, Desk, Shelving Unit, Clothes Rod, Bath-Shower, Grab Bars, Fireplace, Toilet Stall, Cabinet, Custom Cabinet, Compartment Sink, Railing/Fence, Ceiling Grid and Seating Section (railings in 3D and along arcs, custom cabinets' front and 3D views, countertops, workstations, aisles), Space Planning past Space (zones, stacking diagrams, the Space object's own data), and 3D Modeling's Push/Pull, Deform, Shell and NURBS tools
- [x] Object Info, Shape tab: type, class, layer, geometry, text, symbol, viewport settings, plug-in parameters
- [x] Object Info, Data tab: attached records, editable
- [x] Object Info, Render tab: each texture part of an object (the whole object, and the further parts walls, slabs, solids and some plug-ins hold) with its texture (none, the class's or one of the drawing's), offset, scale and rotation; a 3D object without parts takes one on the whole object
- [ ] Object Info, Render tab: the mapping method, and the names Vectorworks gives each part (shown by number)
- [x] Texture context menu (right-click a part's heading in the Render tab, or a textured object in the drawing for all its textured parts): Rotate Texture by 90° (the mapping a quarter turn more), Reset to Default Mapping (offset 0, scale 1, rotation 0; greyed when it is already), Remove Texture Override (the part's texture to None; from the drawing, the whole object's only), each one undo step; Expand All / Collapse All on the heading fold or open every part
- [ ] Texture context menu: picking a face in 3D to act on it alone, Locate in View and Add Decal; a component part's Remove Texture Override sets None rather than going back to the overall texture (Rodeo doesn't know how a part says "from overall"); "default mapping" is taken as no offset, scale 1 and no rotation, unchecked against Vectorworks
- [x] Attributes: fill and pen style, colour and opacity, line weight and type, from class, for the selection or new objects
- [x] Attributes: line markers (none, arrow, dot or slash at each end, their size) and drop shadow (on or off, offset, angle, blur, opacity, colour), for the selection, one undo step each
- [x] Attributes: marker angle (arrows and slashes); markers and drop shadow for new objects, given in the same undo step as the object
- [x] Attributes: fill mapping handles (the Attribute Mapping tool)
- [x] Tools > Options > Edit Marker List… and Line Thickness…: the named markers the Attributes palette's Start and End marker menus offer (style, size on paper, angle; reordered, added, deleted, Reset) and the line weights its Line weight menu offers (millimetres, sorted, Reset), kept in this browser rather than in the drawing; a line keeping a marker taken off the list shows its style, size and angle. Styles are the arrow, dot and slash Rodeo draws, angles whole degrees and weights thousandths of an inch, as the file stores them
- [ ] Attributes: custom marker shapes
- [x] Navigation: Classes, Design Layers, Sheet Layers, Viewports (by sheet, filterable, click to go), Saved Views
- [x] Navigation: Stories tab (each story with its suffix and elevation, its design layers under it with the level each is at and its height above the story; click a layer to make it active; new, edit and delete stories; move a layer to another story from its right-click menu, here or on the Layers tab)
- [x] Navigation: a design layer's elevation beside its scale, for the active layer
- [x] Active Layer Scale… on the drawing's right-click menu (Vectorworks' document context menu, off any object): the Layer Scale dialog with the architectural, engineering and metric scales (the drawing's own kind first) or 1:n typed, and All Layers to give every design layer that scale; greyed on a sheet layer; one undo step. Scale Text isn't offered: how Vectorworks keeps a text's size when the scale changes isn't known, so texts stay as they are
- [ ] Navigation: References tab
- [x] Resource Manager: symbols by folder with previews and search; place with the Symbol tool
- [x] Resource Manager views: Small, Medium or Large Thumbnails, or a List with each resource's kind (the title bar's menu)
- [x] Locate [resource] in Resource Manager on the drawing's right-click menu: a row for each resource the object uses (a symbol instance's definition, a worksheet image's worksheet, a fill's hatch, gradient, tile or image fill, its line type, a text's text style, a 3D object's texture), named for its kind as Vectorworks words it (Locate Hatch in Resource Manager); it shows Resources with that tile selected, scrolled to and focused, its search and kind filter cleared and its folder opened, a non-symbol shown in the detail area and a symbol made the one the Symbol tool places without taking up the tool. Not offered for resources Rodeo doesn't tie to objects: wall, slab and roof styles, plug-in styles (Locate Plug-in Style in Resource Manager), materials, a texture's other parts beyond the first that has one, and a text's style through its class
- [x] Resource Manager: hatches, tiles, gradients, textures, line types, materials, record formats, worksheets and styles listed by kind and folder, with the file's previews
- [x] Resource Manager: rename any resource; duplicate and delete symbol definitions; delete worksheets and data record formats (right-click a tile). Placed symbols and worksheets, a plug-in's parameter format, and names other records hold as text are refused
- [x] Resource Manager: create, edit, duplicate and delete hatches (Edit Hatch: name, ground colour, page or world sizing, and levels of lines, each with its angle, spacing, shift, dash, start point, colour and weight, previewed as it changes; the hatch's previews redrawn on OK). A hatch that objects, classes, styles or walls use isn't deleted
- [x] Resource Manager: create, edit, duplicate and delete gradients (stops with position, colour, opacity and midpoint, on a preview bar) and simple line types (dash and gap lengths on the page, in mm or inches); a first gradient can be made in a drawing with none. The panel's + makes a new hatch, gradient, line type or record format. Previews are redrawn on OK (a JPEG preview becomes a PNG one). One in use by objects, classes, components, styles or materials isn't deleted
- [x] Resource Manager: import hatches, gradients and simple line types from another .vwx file (ticked from its list; a taken name comes in as Name-2, replaces the drawing's own, or stays out; hatch colours matched to the palette; one undo step)
- [x] Resource Manager: import symbols from another .vwx file (in the same list as hatches, gradients and line types; the symbols nested in them come along, and the classes, line types, hatches, gradients, text styles and record formats they use that the drawing lacks; fonts added to the font list; colours matched to the drawing's own, else the nearest; a taken name comes in as Name-2, replaces the drawing's symbol of that name, whose instances then draw the new contents, or stays out; a symbol holding 3D objects brings the textures they show)
- [x] Resource Manager: import textures from another .vwx file (in the same list; each with its shaders' images and settings, bringing the shader record formats the drawing lacks; a replaced texture keeps its name, so classes using it follow)
- [x] Resource Manager: import text styles and data record formats from another .vwx file (in the same list; a replaced record format keeps the values of the fields both have, of the same kind, in the records attached; formats with fields a new one can't have yet, such as choice fields, are listed with the reason)
- [ ] Resource Manager: create, edit and delete tiles, textures, complex (geometric) line types and styles; image fills; importing symbols holding plug-in objects, and importing tiles, image fills, object styles and the other kinds from another file
- [x] Snapping palette: each snap kind on its own (end, mid, centre, intersection, on object, grid)
- [x] Snapping palette: snap radius and a fixed grid spacing (Snap Settings…)
- [x] Snapping: smart points (rest on a point to acquire it, then line up with it horizontally or vertically along dashed guides)
- [x] Snapping: extension lines (beyond an acquired end of a slanted straight edge)
- [x] SmartCursor datum (G over the drawing, as Vectorworks keys it; Regular Polygon moves to ⇧G): a temporary origin at the snapped point under the pointer, marked by a small circle; while it is set the data bar's X and Y and the status bar's are measured from it (typed X and Y too, so a point goes a typed distance from it), the X and Y labels in the accent colour, and the cursor lines up with it along the smart points' guides. G elsewhere moves it, G over it releases it; another drawing starts without one
- [x] Smart Point Settings, as Vectorworks' Smart Point Snap Settings (a right-click or double-click on the Snapping palette's Smart points button, or Tools > Smart Point Settings…): Acquire smart point if mouse stops for _ sec; Set datum if mouse stops for _ sec (off at first; resting there again releases it); Use datum offset, whose points that far from the datum along its horizontal and vertical extensions show as diamonds on dashed lines and snap (Datum offset); kept in this browser
- [x] Selection tool moves show the floating data bar: ΔX and ΔY, and the dragged point's X and Y, measured from the datum while one is set (G during the drag too); the bar only shows them
- [x] Snap Loupe (Z over the drawing, as Vectorworks keys it; the Zoom tool moves to ⇧Z): a round window magnifying the drawing 4× at the pointer, Z again doubling it up to 64×; the tools get the point under the pointer there, snapped as finely, a click acting on it and closing the window; Escape, a click outside, a zoom or a pan closes it without acting
- [x] Snapping: tangents and perpendiculars, from the point a line, polyline, double line, dimension or measurement is drawn from
- [x] Working Planes palette: set a plane on a face clicked in the 3D view (its x along the world's, as near as the face allows), its grid drawn there with red and green axes; save, rename and delete planes (kept per file name in this browser, as saved views are); Look At squares the 3D view to it; its origin and facing shown
- [x] Modify > Working Plane: Set Working Plane (a face clicked), Look at Working Plane (also in View), Save Working Plane… (named, a name already saved refused), Previous and Next Working Plane (the last ten made active), Align Working Plane with Current View, with Layer Plane and its X Axis with Layer Plane, Rotate About X', Y' and Z' Left and Right 90° and Flip X', Y' and Z', the origin kept; a plane changed so is unsaved until saved
- [ ] Working planes: drawing 2D objects on the active plane in 3D views (planar objects), the planes a drawing saves read and written (how a 2D object keeps its plane isn't known yet)
- [x] Visualization palette: projection, the sun (following the view, or placed by the Heliodon), shadows, ambient light and the clip cube (a setting of the window, not saved in the file), in two tabs as Vectorworks 2025 splits it: Lights (the sun, shadows, ambient light and the light objects) and Cameras (projection, the Renderworks cameras and the clip cube), each saying when the drawing has none
- [x] Visualization palette: Renderworks cameras listed (on design layers and in sheet viewports), clicking one looks through it in the 3D view (its eye, look-to point, view angle and projection)
- [x] Cameras edited: with the 3D view open, a camera's Match View points it the way the view looks (its matrix, plan box and view parameters, one undo step; its lens stays), and moves (Move, nudges, Move 3D) carry its eye and look-to point
- [x] Visualization palette: light objects listed (point, spot and directional, in symbols and plug-ins too; click one to select what holds it), lighting the 3D view with their colour, brightness and spot cones (the 16 nearest where it looks)
- [x] Light objects edited: Object Info's Light section sets on, kind, brightness, colour and a spot's spread and beam (a light-fixture symbol's in Edit Symbol, for every instance; a plug-in's are its own), and the Visualization palette turns the drawing's own lights on and off
- [x] A light's position and aim: moves (Move, nudges, Move 3D) and Duplicate take light objects, and Object Info sets a light's X, Y and Z, a spot's pan and tilt and a directional light's azimuth and elevation
- [x] A light's right-click menu: Turn On or Turn Off, and for a spot or directional light Set Light to View (the light turned to shine the way the view looks: the 3D view's camera while it shows, straight down in Top/Plan) and Set View to Light (the 3D view, shown if the plan was, turned to look the way the light shines, framing the visible model); Object Info's Light section has Set Light to View and Set View to Light as buttons too, as Vectorworks' has. One undo step each. A light in a symbol (in Edit Symbol) turns on and off but isn't aimed from here; a custom light, which Rodeo doesn't have, isn't offered
- [x] Light objects casting shadows in the 3D view while the sun does: the 2 spots, the point light and the directional light nearest where it looks, a shadow map each, drawn again only when the model, the layers shown, the render mode or the clip cube change
- [x] Light objects drawn in plan: an amber mark at a fixed screen size (rays for a point light, a spot's beam, a directional light's arrow, faint when off), clicked to select and dragged to move; a symbol's or plug-in's lights stay part of its 3D (Edit Symbol shows a fixture's)
- [x] Placing cameras: the Camera tool puts a Renderworks camera on the active layer, pointed, named "Camera n", one undo step
- [ ] A camera's lens and its plan drawing redrawn after an edit; placing a camera in a drawing that has none (a new camera copies one the drawing has); a light's own Cast Shadows setting; placing a light in a drawing that has none (a new light copies one the drawing has)

## File

- [x] Open .vwx (2023–2026 demos tested), drop to open
- [x] Save in place (File System Access), Save As
- [x] File > Save a Copy As…: the drawing as it stands to a new file (suggested as "<name> copy.vwx"; a download where the browser has no save dialog) while the window goes on working on its own file, unsaved changes and all
- [x] File > Save as Template…: the drawing as it stands to a .sta template (suggested as "<name>.sta", the template type first in the save dialog; a download where the browser has no save dialog), which File > New from Template… starts drawings from; the window goes on working on its own file (whether Vectorworks then works on the template instead isn't documented)
- [x] File > File Info…: the active file as a list: its name and kind (drawing, template, or a new drawing not saved yet), its size as opened and when it last changed on disk (when the browser gave a handle to it), whether it has unsaved changes; the Vectorworks versions the file names as creating it and last saving it (VW23.0.2 shown as Vectorworks 2018 (23.0.2)), its build and units; and what it holds: design and sheet layers, classes, objects, symbol definitions, resources and records. A browser isn't told the file's folder, so there is no location; project sharing's project file and server aren't in Rodeo
- [x] File > Save All: each open drawing with unsaved changes saved as Save would (asking where for one never saved), the front one first, then the one that was in front brought back; greyed when every drawing is saved
- [x] Several drawings open at once, each in a tab over the drawing (shown once two are open): a click brings one to the front as it was left (its active layer, class, selection, view and undo), × or a middle-click closes it, + starts a new one; File > Close; Window > Drawings lists them with Next and Previous; opening a file already open goes to its tab, and the same cloud or drive file opened again takes its tab's place. A live cloud drawing joins its session again from where it left, and another tab waits while its changes are still being sent. Autosave keeps a copy of the drawing in front only
- [x] File > Close All: every open drawing closed, those behind first, asking about each with unsaved changes; Cancel keeps that one open and stops there. Window > Show Document Tabs hides the strip of tabs (kept per browser), the drawings still listed in Window > Drawings
- [x] Export PDF (per layer at scale or fitted to paper; images, viewport renders included), Print
- [x] Export DXF (R12), SVG
- [x] File > Export > Export EPSF: the layers the window shows, drawn together on one page at the active layer's scale (the area their objects cover plus a margin), as an EPSF 3.0 file (PostScript Level 2) for page-layout and illustration programs to place: line work, fills, line weights, dashes, clipping and text in Helvetica, bold or oblique as it is (Latin-1 characters; others as ?), grayed layers faded and hidden classes left out as on screen. EPS has no transparency, so a see-through colour is mixed with white by its opacity, as it prints with nothing under it; drop shadows are left out, and so are images (a message counts them). No preview image (TIFF or PICT) is embedded, so a program that only shows the preview shows a box
- [x] File > Export > Export Metafile: the same page as Export EPSF, as a Windows enhanced metafile (.emf) for Office and other Windows programs: paths filled and stroked with the lines' weights, caps, joins and dashes, clipping kept, text in Arial (Arial Narrow for narrow fonts) with each character placed where Helvetica puts it, turned as the text is. The points are worked out in hundredths of a millimetre, so the file needs no world transform. As in EPS, see-through colours are mixed with white by their opacity, and drop shadows and images are left out (a message counts the images). A dash pattern always starts at its beginning (EMF has no dash offset), and the file holds no EMF+ records
- [x] File > Export > Export Image File…: the visible layers (all their objects, or the part the window shows: Export area Current view), at the active layer's scale and a typed resolution (10–2400 dpi, the longer side capped at 8000 px, the pixel size and memory shown), as PNG, JPEG (Maximum, High, Medium or Low quality), TIFF (uncompressed, its dpi written) or BMP (24-bit), in Full color, Grayscale or Black and white, with Save Alpha Channel (a transparent background) for PNG and TIFF in full colour; the last settings kept. The 3D view isn't exported here (Render Image… saves it), and there is no Marquee area, Lock Aspect Ratio or typed pixel size, render preview or other formats (GIF, JPEG 2000, PICT)
- [x] View > Rendering > Create Batch Render Job… and Start Batch Render…, as Vectorworks has them: a job is a name, the view (the 3D view's view when the job is made, or one Renderworks camera or all of them) and Render Image's choices for the render mode (Fast with the Realistic engine, or Photoreal path traced with its samples, time limit and denoise), the size and the format (PNG or JPEG); Start Batch Render's Available Jobs and Chosen Jobs (listed with their extension), Choose All, >>, <<, Duplicate (Assign Name), Edit (Edit Batch Render Job) and Delete (no undo); Start renders the chosen jobs in order with the Render palette's settings under one progress window (Esc drops the job in hand and goes on with the rest, Cancel stops all; Finish now keeps the picture in hand) and downloads the pictures as one zip of a folder named for the date and time, as Vectorworks writes them into a folder so named. Jobs are kept per file name on this machine (localStorage, as saved views are), not in the .vwx; the modes Rodeo renders are its own (Vectorworks' Shaded, Hidden Line and Renderworks modes, and Export Image File's settings, aren't offered); no Parent Folder to choose, the browser's downloads take it
- [x] File > Export > Export High Dynamic Range Image (HDRI) as Export HDRI…: Render Image's camera (the view, a Renderworks camera or all of them), size, engine and quality, made here (no cloud), each picture saved as a Radiance .hdr (RGBE, run-length coded; several cameras' in one zip): linear and unclipped, after the lens's fringes and sharpening, depth of field, bloom, fog, exposure and white balance, before tone mapping, contrast, highlights and shadows, saturation, outlines, vignette and grain. Checked: three.js and Blender read the files; exposure +1 doubles every value (mean ratio 2.003); Fast and Path Traced agree. Up to 16.8 megapixels (the float picture takes 16 bytes a pixel on the graphics card). Not here: Vectorworks' Export Image File dialog (Export Area, Marquee, Resolution and Print Size, Save Alpha Channel) and its Renderworks modes
- [x] File > Export > Export Panorama… (with the 3D view showing): a 360° picture from the 3D view's eye, its heading in the middle, in Shaded (the view's own drawing, as the Shaded Options have it), Realistic or Path Traced (64, 256 or 1024 samples, with the Render palette's settings, no depth of field, two-point shift or vignette, so the sides meet), at 2048 × 1024, 4096 × 2048 or 8192 × 4096: six 90° pictures round the eye unrolled into one twice as wide as high, with progress and Cancel. Saved as a .jpg, or as one .html page with the picture inside it that opens from a folder in any browser (Vectorworks saves .html, .jpg and .js, which Chrome won't open locally) to look around in: drag, wheel or pinch to zoom, the arrow keys. Not here: Renderworks styles, Process on Cloud Services, Save on cloud storage, Get shareable link, Shaded Preview, the device's motion sensors and VR mode
- [x] Model > Create Animation: Create Walkthrough Path from Selection, Create Walkthrough Path from Saved Views…, Create Orbit Path… and Create Spin Path each make an animation path object on the active design layer, selected, its keyframes placed for it (a walkthrough's at its path's corners and an arc's apex, the path replacing the line, arc, polyline or polygon it came from; an orbit's and a spin's every 45°), drawn in plan as its path with a ring on each keyframe (the selected one filled) and the camera, a wedge its view angle wide, where Time puts it, and as its path in 3D. Orbit Options: Orthogonal Projection (an Orthographic Orbit path), Rotation angle and Rotate about center of the Active Layer Plane, the Working Plane or the Selection; the camera goes round at the 3D view's eye height and distance from the centre, looking at it. Spin (needs a selection): once round it, framed, from above it to as far below and back. From saved views: the Create Walkthrough Path dialog's Saved Views Available and Walkthrough Path to Create lists with Add, Add All (a double-click adds too), Remove, Remove All, and Move Up and Move Down for the order (Vectorworks drags the rows), two views or more; a Fly Freely path straight from each view's camera to the next's, a keyframe on each with its look-to point and view angle, 4 seconds a stretch first. Walkthrough: a Walk path the way it was drawn, the camera its Camera Height (1600 mm first) above the active layer, looking 2 m ahead along it (a third of a short path). The commands go back to the 2D view when they were chosen there. Object Info as Vectorworks lays it out: Name, Length, Path Type (Fly Freely, Walk or Orthographic Orbit), Duration, a walk's Camera Height; Preview: Play and Pause (the 3D view through the camera from Time on, at its Duration's pace), Skip Backward and Skip Forward (the first and last keyframes), Time (0%–100%), Activate and Deactivate Camera View (any other move of the 3D view deactivates it); Keyframe: Previous and Next, Add Keyframe (where the camera is; greyed on a keyframe) and Delete Keyframe (not the first or last), the selected keyframe's Speed (−99% to 100%: the camera's pace near it against the others) and View Angle (12°–130°), Display Frames (a tick at each of the movie's frames) and Keyframes; Create Movie… (also on its right-click menu). Reshape moves its vertices and makes it Fly Freely, as Vectorworks does. Create Movie: Duration in seconds (the path's, changed in either place), Frames per second 24, 25, 30 or 60, Rendering in the 3D view's Wireframe, Hidden Line, Dashed Hidden Line, Sketch, Shaded or Textured, Quality Low to Very High, Time Range Full Duration or Limited Duration (Start Time, End Time), Frame Size Screen, 640 × 480, HD, Full HD, 4K UHD or Custom (240–4096 pixels each way), all kept with the path; it records the movie with progress and Cancel and saves it, named after the path, as an .mp4 (H.264, as Vectorworks makes) or, where the browser can't, a .webm. Rodeo's reading: how Vectorworks saves an animation path isn't known, so the path is a Rodeo plug-in object of its own ('Rodeo Animation Path'), which Vectorworks sees as one it doesn't have; Vectorworks doesn't say how its camera eases between keyframes: Rodeo's pace goes evenly from one keyframe's speed to the next's along the path, its look-to point goes straight from one keyframe's to the next's, and a walkthrough's looks ahead; the orbit turns counter-clockwise seen from above; a walk rides flat at one height (not following terrain, slabs or stairs) and comes only from 2D paths (not 3D polygons or NURBS curves); names aren't checked for being unique on the layer; keyframes' look-to points can't be dragged, and there's no Cameras-tab entry or Open Camera View in New Pane; Realistic, Path Traced and Renderworks movies, 360° video and cloud processing aren't offered; only saved views made in the 3D view in perspective are listed (sheet layers, which Vectorworks lists too, have no camera in Rodeo); frames bigger than the browser can capture in a frame's time (4K) come out a little slower than the frame rate
- [x] File > Export > Export Database…: every record of one data record format the drawing's objects hold (on every layer, in groups too), a row per object and a column per field, tab-delimited (.txt) or comma-delimited (.csv, quoted where needed), field names in the first row when ticked; booleans as True and False, lengths in the document's units, areas as Rodeo shows them, volumes in cubic metres; refused when no object holds the format. Merge, DIF and SYLK files aren't written, and records in symbol definitions are left out
- [x] Export Worksheet…: a worksheet as tab-delimited text, comma- or semicolon-delimited CSV (UTF-8), DIF, SYLK (Windows-1252, as Excel reads them) or an Excel workbook (.xlsx: one sheet named after the worksheet, plain numbers as numbers, the rest as text, columns as wide as their values), its values as last calculated, database rows as their objects' rows (recalculating first not yet)
- [x] File > Export > Export Script and File > Import > Import Script…: the drawing written as a Vectorscript (.vs) that draws its design layers' 2D objects again (lines, rectangles turned or not, rounded rectangles, ovals, arcs, polygons, polylines with all their vertex kinds, text with its size, justification and angle, loci, groups) on layers of the same names, in their classes and stacking order, with their names, fills, pens, line weights, opacities and the attributes they take from their class; a header counts what isn't written (symbols, plug-in objects, 3D objects, images, sheet layers) and what is written plainer (hatch, gradient, tile and image fills as solid, dashed lines as solid, radius vertices as CurveThrough); the script checks it runs in a drawing of the same units. Import Script runs a .vs, .px or .py file in the Run Script window, as one undo step
- [x] File > Export > Export Simple VectorScript (3D only)…: Export in Front view or Current view (the 3D view's, or Top/Plan as it shows, turned with Rotate Plan), then the 3D objects on the visible design layers written as a plain Pascal-like script (.vs): each flat face a Poly3D call through its corners' x, y and z in the drawing's units (feet and inches as inches), each object's faces a group, ClosePoly first, in procedures of up to 400 faces with no object split between two; Front view turns the model so the view looks down on it (x stays, z becomes y, the depth toward the viewer z). Rodeo's vs module now has Poly3D, BeginPoly3D, Add3DPt and EndPoly3D, so the script also runs back in Rodeo (File > Import > Import Script…) as 3D polygons. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' own output for this command isn't documented beyond its view choice, so the calls, grouping and comments are Rodeo's; coordinates are absolute (layer elevations included) and turned about the origin; faces with holes come out as their outlines as the 3D view's faces give them; colours, textures, classes, names and layers aren't written; hidden objects and classes are left out as the 3D view leaves them
- [x] Import Worksheet… (File > Import, or a dropped .csv, .tsv, .dif, .slk or .xlsx): tab-delimited text, CSV (commas or semicolons), DIF, SYLK and Excel workbooks (.xlsx) become a new worksheet named after the file, opened in the worksheet editor; Excel Import Options picks a workbook's sheet; numbers as number cells, a workbook's bold, alignment, column widths, dates (as text) and formulas' last calculated values kept; UTF-8 or Windows-1252 text. The worksheet copies its settings from one the drawing has, as Create Report's does (.xls workbooks, keeping formulas and keeping a reference to the file not yet)
- [x] Import DXF (lines, polylines, circles, arcs, ellipses, text, points, blocks)
- [x] Export OBJ and glTF: the 3D model of the visible design layers (metres, y up; named objects grouped by layer, colours and opacity; no textures yet)
- [x] Import images (File > Import Image…: PNG and JPEG as they are, GIF, WebP and BMP as PNG; placed at 72 dpi on the active layer)
- [x] Export IFC (IFC4: a storey per design layer, elements classed by plug-in name, coloured tessellated geometry) and 3DS (a material per colour, meshes split at 65535 vertices)
- [x] Import PDF (File > Import PDF… or a dropped .pdf: a page's line work, fills and text as vector objects, grouped, at a chosen scale)
- [x] Import Metafile… (File > Import, or a dropped .emf): a Windows enhanced metafile's line work, fills and text as vector objects, grouped, centred in the view at the picture's size at a chosen scale (the layer's at first), as Import PDF places a page. Its GDI records are played as Windows draws them: the mapping modes with their window and viewport (and scaling), the world transform, SaveDC and RestoreDC, pens (their widths), solid brushes, fonts (size and escapement) and the stock objects, lines, polylines, polygons and poly-polygons (16- and 32-bit), Bézier curves, PolyDraw, rectangles, rounded rectangles, ellipses, arcs, chords, pies and AngleArc in the arc direction set, path brackets (fill, stroke or both) and ExtTextOut text placed by its alignment and advances. Rodeo's own Export Metafile comes back at its size, text and all. Dash patterns become solid lines; pictures, hatched and pattern fills, regions and drawing only in EMF+ records are left out and counted; clipping is left out (as in Import PDF); WMF (.wmf) isn't read
- [x] Import OBJ, glTF (.glb, or .gltf with its buffers inside) and 3DS: a mesh per part on the active layer, grouped, at the model's coordinates (OBJ and 3DS units and up axis asked for), part colours as fills; lines, points, textures and compressed geometry left out
- [x] Export STL… (File > Export): what Vectorworks' Export STL Options offers: the selected objects on the active layer (all of it when nothing there is selected), the active layer or all visible design layers, binary or ASCII; millimetres, z up, the 3D view's triangles (its Quality setting has no counterpart), no colours
- [x] Export STEP (3D only)… (File > Export): Vectorworks' Export Options (Export Solid as Trimmed Surfaces), then the 3D objects on the design layers the 3D view shows as a STEP file (ISO 10303-21, AP214 automotive_design, .stp): each object's flat faces as planar B-rep faces with straight edges, the faces that join along their edges one body, a solid (manifold solid B-rep) when they close round it, else a surface model, named as the object; with Trimmed Surfaces every face a body of its own (a cube as six). Faces are turned to agree and to face out (the 3D view's caps can come wound either way); bodies take their object's colour (a face of another colour its own), each design layer is a presentation layer holding its bodies; millimetres, z up, all of it one part. Rodeo's reading: Rodeo holds meshes, not Vectorworks' Parasolid bodies, so curved surfaces go flat-faceted as the 3D view draws them, never as cylinders, spheres or NURBS; faces meeting at a T-junction or an edge three faces share leave that body a surface model; 2D objects, 3D loci and NURBS curves aren't written; opacity, textures, classes, groups and symbols (as instances) aren't carried; checked by a STEP reader of Rodeo's own (references, arities, loops, Euler, outward normals), not yet opened in a CAD program
- [x] Export IGES (3D only)… (File > Export): the same Export Options (Export Solid as Trimmed Surfaces) and the same bodies as Export STEP, as IGES 5.3 (.igs, millimetres): a closed body as a manifold solid B-rep object (Type 186: shell 514, faces 510 on planar NURBS patches 128, loops 508, edge list 504 of lines 110, vertex list 502), an open one as trimmed surfaces (Type 144: each face's outline a curve on a parametric surface, 142, as a degree-1 NURBS curve in space and in the patch's parameters, 126, bounding a planar patch, 128), as Vectorworks maps closed solids to 186 and trimmed surfaces to 144; with Trimmed Surfaces every face a 144. Each object named (Name property, 406 form 15), coloured (colour definitions, 314; a face of another colour its own) and on the level numbered as its layer in the drawing's order. Rodeo's reading: as for STEP, curved surfaces go flat-faceted (no 126/128 NURBS of Vectorworks' own curves and surfaces), 2D objects, 3D loci and NURBS curves aren't written; levels are numbers, not named; a non-ASCII name's letters become ?; checked by an IGES reader of Rodeo's own (columns, sections, pointers, parameter counts, B-rep loops, Euler, outward patches, outlines on their planes), not yet opened in a CAD program
- [x] Export USD… (File > Export): Vectorworks' Export USD Options: File Format (.usdz package, .usda text, or .usd holding text; text with textures downloads as a zipped folder of the scene and its images, as Vectorworks writes a folder), Scene Graph Organization (by layer: an Xform per layer, an object a Mesh or an Xform of Meshes by material; by texture: an Xform per material) and Combine objects (by texture: one Mesh per material), and Metadata (each object's name, type, id, class and layer always, as `vw:` attributes; record data adds its records' and plug-in parameters' values, typed). The design layers the 3D view shows, its triangles flat shaded, in metres y up; materials are UsdPreviewSurfaces of the colour and opacity or the texture's image (PNG or JPEG), tiled as the 3D view tiles it. Checked by importing into Blender 4.4 (meshes, faces, materials, the image; the `vw:` attributes show with its All custom attributes import). Not here: binary .usdc, Geometric Detail (the 3D view's faceting), IFC metadata, textures' other shaders (bump, transparency masks)
- [x] Export KML… (File > Export): Vectorworks' KML Export Options: Site Mapping by a Reference Point's X and Y, the Latitude and Longitude it goes to and the Page North, or, in a georeferenced drawing, filled in from the georeferencing and turned off; Z Height Offset; the active layer or all visible ones, each a folder in Google Earth's Places with a placemark for each object. Not done: the reference point from Next Mouse Click, Objects To Export's filter by kind (every 3D object goes), textures (colours only), COLLADA models or KMZ. The file is plain KML polygons, one for each triangle, so it's refused over 250,000 triangles, and it hasn't been opened in Google Earth itself: it was read back and its coordinates checked
- [x] Import STL… (File > Import, or a dropped .stl): binary and ASCII, a mesh per ASCII solid, grouped, at the file's coordinates (units and up axis asked: millimetres, z up at first); Import as symbol, as Vectorworks has it: a symbol named after the file holds the meshes, its instance at the origin; the drawing needs a mesh (and, for a symbol, a symbol) to copy settings from
- [x] File > Document Settings > Georeferencing…: places the drawing on the Earth by the internal origin's latitude and longitude (decimal, 39° 10' 32" or 39d 10m 32s) and the Angle to True North, in a Local Transverse Mercator on the origin's meridian, WGS 84 / UTM or WGS 84 / Pseudo-Mercator, or by the EPSG codes 3857, 326xx and 327xx. Vectorworks takes any system GDAL knows, a .prj file or WKT, and each design layer can have its own; Rodeo has those three and one for the drawing. It's kept with the file's name in this browser, not in the file, and a drawing's own georeferencing isn't read. Checked against the published meridian arc, Pseudo-Mercator's world extent and Vincenty's test line.
- [x] Modify > Move Geographic…: the selection's middle to a Latitude and Longitude, or by an Azimuth (0° the Y axis, 90° the X axis) and Distance over the ellipsoid.
- [x] Tool Sets: GIS's Graticule (Rectangle or Vertex mode; Grid starts at and Grid spacing in Geodesic or Cartesian coordinates, Major lines at count, Label major lines, Label grid lines, Label style, Label precision: true parallels and meridians clipped to the area, major ones in the pen you draw with and the rest grey), Great Circle (Constrained or Unconstrained Line; Show Distance, Distance Unit, Show Angle, Angle Format azimuth or bearing, Reverse: the arc over the Earth, and the shortest distance over the WGS 84 ellipsoid), Survey Point (a click sets that point's geographical or Cartesian coordinates and the Angle to True North, which places the drawing, and marks it with its main and secondary coordinates, the angle and a project elevation; one to a drawing, a new one replacing the last) and GIS Stake (Standard Insertion or Vertex, marking points with Coordinate Point NE, EN or XY in metres, feet, document units, decimal degrees or degrees/minutes/seconds). Each opens Georeferencing first in a drawing that has none. They're plain groups, so their figures stay as placed. Not modelled: pen and line type choices for a graticule's lines, the Survey Point's triangulation reference points, its lock and aligning it with the internal or user origin, a stake's Set Geolocation and background maps. At very small layer scales such as 1:20000 Rodeo's text stops growing, so labels read smaller than 2.5 mm on paper.
- [x] Import Shapefile… (File > Import): an ESRI shapefile's .shp with its .shx, .dbf, .prj and .cpg, or a zip of several; points as loci, lines as open polygons, polygons with their holes (a filled outline with the rings as its edges), multi-part features grouped; a new layer and a class named GIS-<name> per file (or the active ones); the .dbf attributes attached as a record "<name> Data" (text, number, on/off and date fields); the .prj's units, or asked when there is none; data far from the origin centred; one undo step. Longitude and latitude are projected equirectangular about the data's centre (no other projections, no georeferencing kept; MultiPatch features counted, not read)
- [x] Export Shapefile… (File > Export): the selected objects, the active layer or all visible design layers as a zip of polygon, line and point shapefiles (.shp, .shx, .dbf, .cpg); rectangles, circles, arcs (as segments), polygons, lines and loci, a group of one kind as one feature (holes kept), mixed groups by part; Layer and Class columns plus, optionally, a record format's fields; metres, feet or millimetres (no .prj written, so no coordinate system; text, images, symbols, plug-in objects, walls and 3D objects left out and counted)
- [x] Import IFC (IFC2X3, IFC4, IFC4X3, via web-ifc): each element a mesh (split by colour) on the active layer at the model's coordinates in its own units, grouped by building storey with each group named after its storey, colours as fills; spaces left out and counted; one undo step
- [x] Import USD… (File > Import, or a dropped .usda, .usdc, .usd or .usdz): USD, text or binary (the crate format, versions 0.4.0 on: compressed sections, compressed integer and float arrays, inlined values, list ops), alone or as a .usdz's root layer, each Mesh (and Cube, Sphere, Cylinder and Cone) through its xformOps (translate, rotations, orient, scale, matrices, inverted ops, resetXformStack) and the references inside the layer, a GeomSubset bound to its own material a part of its own, coloured by its UsdPreviewSurface diffuse colour (bindings inherited) or its displayColor, grouped by the prims under the default prim; Vectorworks' Import USD Options: Import as symbol, Re-scale units per meter, Offset imported model and Redefine up axis (the file's metersPerUnit and upAxis otherwise). Into the active layer and class (Layer name and Class name aren't offered, as the other imports); colours, not textures (Create textures needs a texture to copy). Checked with what Export USD writes, what Blender 4.4 writes as .usda, .usdc and .usdz (each read the same), and binary USD written by USD 25.02 itself at crate versions 0.4.0, 0.7.0 and 0.9.0 holding every kind of value (read back as written, the scene the same as USD's own text of it)
- [ ] Import USD: references and payloads to other files, curves, points and instancers are left out and counted; an animated transform (time samples) comes in at its default value; what a variant set's selected variant holds isn't brought in
- [x] Import Rhino 3DM… (via rhino3dm): meshes, and polysurfaces and extrusions by the render meshes saved with them (SubDs and bare surfaces counted), blocks expanded; curves, text and points as 2D objects in plan, pen coloured, a text dot sized in points by the layer; grouped by Rhino layer path. Export Rhino 3DM: millimetres, a Rhino layer per design layer and a sublayer per class, objects as coloured meshes, plus the 2D linework with no 3D form at its layer's elevation, text as text dots in points
- [x] Import FBX… (binary and ASCII, the full transform chain, split by material) and Export FBX (binary 7.4, centimetres, y up, a null per layer, a mesh per object, a material per colour)
- [x] Import Collada (SketchUp)… (.dae and .kmz: library components instanced, nested transforms, diffuse colours, grouped by top-level node) and Export Collada (SketchUp) (COLLADA 1.4.1, metres, z up, a node per layer and object), as SketchUp's own .skp is closed
- [x] Import Blender… (.blend as Blender saves it, zstd- or gzip-compressed too: mesh objects in place, split by material slot, grouped by collection; modifiers not applied and counted; curves, text and collection instances counted); Import PLY… and Export PLY (binary, metres, z up, vertex colours)
- [x] Export IFC carries each storey's elevation (placement and Elevation) and each object's records as property sets (IfcLabel, IfcBoolean, IfcReal), as ArchiCAD and Revit read them; a drawing with no mesh of its own takes 3D imports (a built-in mesh); a dropped .skp, .pln, .rvt, .dwg, .max, .c4d or Maya file says what to export from its app instead
- [x] Revit families and templates (.rfa, .rte, .rft; dropped or File > Open): the release and build that saved them, save time, worksharing, Project Information, a family's category, parameters and types, linked files and the thumbnail (placeable as a picture), read from the compound file's own metadata streams; Revit 2015 to 2027 samples
- [x] Revit models (.rvt, dropped or File > Open, read by rvt-rs in WebAssembly): a new drawing with a story and design layer per level, a class per category, walls as walls, floors as extrudes of their outlines, rooms as outline and name, doors, windows and every other element as meshes in their exact colours, all parameters as records; Revit 2015 to 2027 samples
- [x] Revit models: doors and windows cut their walls (the wall's 3D less each opening, its plan broken where it is cut 1.2 m up), as do the holes of a wall's edited profile (sloped tops, gables, cut-outs); a family type placed more than once is a symbol where its instances are alike, matched by their corners whichever way each is turned or flipped
- [ ] Revit models, the rest: doors and windows as Rodeo's own door and window objects in their walls (editing a wall now stretches its cut openings), wall styles from wall types, curved and slanted walls as walls, floors' holes (VoidOpening), roofs as roof faces, rooms as spaces; elements rvt-rs only knows by their bounding box (drawn see-through, counted); writing .rvt
- [ ] Import and export DWG and SketchUp's own .skp (closed; Collada stands in); IFC elements as native walls, slabs, doors and windows, with their property sets on import; PDF pictures and clipping; IFC materials and openings
- [x] Revert; Open Recent (files opened with Open…, dropped where the browser gives a handle, or saved with Save As)
- [x] New document from a template (File > New from Template…: a .sta or .vwx opens as "Untitled n")
- [x] New blank document (File > New, or New drawing on the start screen: one design layer and the class None, in millimetres or feet and inches by the browser's region; written by Rodeo, unconfirmed in Vectorworks)
- [x] Publish (File > Publish…): chosen sheets and layers as one PDF or a PDF, SVG or DXF each, into a folder; publishing sets per drawing
- [x] Publish saved views (PDF, a page each or a file each: the view's layers and classes as opening it shows them, cropped to its area; kept in publishing sets)
- [x] Page Setup for sheet layers (File > Page Setup…: ARCH, ISO and US sizes or custom, either orientation, one sheet or all): the page drawn as an outline centred on the sheet's origin, and prints and PDF exports put each sheet on its page
- [ ] Page Setup for design layers (where their page sits is not known yet) and print areas
- [ ] References (referencing other .vwx files)

## Edit

- [x] Undo and redo for every edit
- [x] Cut, copy, paste, paste in place (between files through the clipboard)
- [x] Duplicate, Duplicate Array (linear, grid, circular), delete
- [x] Edit > Duplicate Along Path…: copies along a selected line, polyline, polygon, rectangle, arc or circle, by number (spread from a start offset to the end, or round a closed path) or by distance, optionally tangent to the path, in one undo step
- [x] 3D objects move, rotate, mirror, duplicate, copy and paste (also between files, their textures and materials matched by name) and go into symbols like 2D ones: extrudes, sweeps, multiple extrudes and solid additions through their own frame (their profile or operands go with it), 3D polygons and meshes point by point
- [x] Scaling extrudes, multiple extrudes and sweeps (Scale Objects, the selection's handles): the profiles take the scale in the extrude's own plane and its frame keeps its turn; upright extrudes take any scale, ones lying on their side an even one or one along their axes, and sweeps turning about an upright axis an even one. Heights stay, as a 3D polygon's do; the body, which would no longer match, goes, as Vectorworks saves most extrudes
- [x] Moving walls on their own (dragging, nudges, Move, Move 3D): the control line, plan drawing, 3D and the doors and windows in them go along, one undo step
- [x] Walls joined to a wall that moves follow it: dragging, nudging, moving, turning or mirroring a wall (Rotate, Flip), or changing its ends in Object Info, takes the end of a wall mitred to it at a corner of two along, and every corner of a wall that moves, turns or follows is mitred again along the line through where the faces cross; a wall meeting its side at a tee slides its end along its own line, each component as deep in it as it was, one undo step; a copy made in place (Rotate, Mirror and Duplicate Along Path copies, arrays) takes none along. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Walls meeting a wall that moves three or more at a point, at a corner Vectorworks cut its own way, or butted into its end follow it (moving, turning, mirroring, changing its ends): an end at its point goes where its end went, a butted one as the end went, each keeping its cut; walls stacked on it at other heights stay
- [x] Turning, mirroring and duplicating walls (Rotate, Flip, Mirror, Duplicate, the arrays) with the doors and windows in them, and plug-in objects (doors, windows, furniture, tags; a camera keeps its view), one undo step each; a mirrored plug-in's matrix mirrors, as Vectorworks saves mirrored doors and windows (cameras and Heliodons don't mirror)
- [x] Slabs and roof faces move, lift, turn, mirror and duplicate on their own (Move, nudges, Move 3D, Rotate, Flip, Mirror, Duplicate), with their outline, components and 3D, one undo step each; Object Info's Slab section shows their style and thickness
- [x] Walls, slabs, plug-in objects and lights through the clipboard within a drawing (Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste in Place; onto another design layer as high above it as they were), with their doors and windows, components and 3D, a camera keeping its view; plug-in objects and lights also go into symbols (Create Symbol, pasting into an open symbol). Pasted objects' materials are matched by name in another drawing. Untested in Vectorworks
- [ ] Walls, slabs, plug-in objects and lights pasted into another drawing (they and what plug-ins draw name its resources in ways not all known: walls' aux records 110, 116 and 124, plug-in parameters naming materials, textures, symbols and record formats), and walls and slabs in symbols; moving and copying generic solids on their own; scaling sweeps lying down and solid additions, extrudes tilted against the scale, and heights (Scale Objects in z); stretching a polyline's arc vertices exactly (in 2D and in profiles they stay circular, their radius scaled by the mean)
- [x] Select All, Select None, Select Similar (matching on the attributes Select Similar Preferences ticks), Select Connected Objects (lines, open polylines and polygons and partial arcs meeting end to end, followed through)
- [x] Select Coincident Objects… on the drawing's right-click menu over an object (greyed with only one there): the Select dialog lists every object a click there could pick (a group for what is inside it), topmost first, with its place in the stacking order, kind and name; those picked in the list (one or several) are selected on the drawing as they are picked, OK keeps them, Cancel puts the selection back, and a double-click selects one and closes. The Coincident Selection key, as Vectorworks keys it (Connect/Combine moves to ⇧J): J held while the Selection tool clicks the drawing opens the same dialog there, or selects the one object there is. The asterisk Vectorworks adds to the cursor over coincident objects isn't drawn
- [x] Edit > Invert Selection (what Select All would select that isn't selected; on the layer or in the group being edited) and Edit > Previous Selection (the selection before this one, and again further back, through the last 20 of the front drawing; objects gone or in a group not open are passed over, and one on another layer makes it active)
- [x] Create Similar Object (Edit menu and the right-click menu): the tool that draws the selected object, with its attributes and class; a wall's, slab's, door's, window's, plug-in object's or symbol's kind to copy, text's justification and size on paper, a light's kind; it says so when the tool can't copy that object, and refuses groups and 3D objects
- [x] Activate Class and Create Class Using Object Attributes… on the drawing's right-click menu, for the object under the pointer (selected or not; inside a group, the group): Activate Class makes its class the active one (greyed when it is already); Create Class Using Object Attributes asks the new class's name (one a class or layer has refused) with Edit properties after creation to open the class's attributes first, gives the class the object's fill, pen colour, line style, line weight and opacities (a line's fill left as a new class has it) and puts the object in it with those set by class; one undo step. Rodeo's classes have no Use at Creation setting to turn on
- [x] Update Class Attributes from Object… on the drawing's right-click menu, for the object under the pointer (selected or not; inside a group, the group, which has no attributes of its own, so it is greyed): the Update Class dialog's class (the object's own first), the attributes to take of those the object has (fill, line style, pen colour, line weight, opacity; a line has no fill to give) and Assign the object to the class, which puts it in the class with those attributes set by class; one undo step. Use at Creation isn't turned on, as Rodeo's classes have no such setting
- [x] Force Select, Activate Layer and Activate Class on the drawing's right-click menu for an object the layer and class options keep from a click (on another visible design layer, or in a visible class that isn't active): Force Select makes its layer and class active where needed and selects it; Activate Layer and Activate Class make its layer or class the active one; not while editing a group or symbol
- [x] Edit > Repeat Last Command (and both right-click menus) runs again the last command run from a menu, its key or a right-click, greyed when it doesn't apply now; Edit > Recent Commands lists the last ten, latest first, each run again from there. File, Window and Help commands, Undo, Redo and the clipboard's aren't repeated. Kept while the page is open
- [x] Select Objects of Same Symbol on the drawing's right-click menu over a symbol instance (selected or not): every instance of that symbol definition where Select Similar looks (the active layer, or the group or symbol being edited) is selected, the one right-clicked among them; a toast says how many. Select Hoists with the Same Symbol isn't offered: Rodeo's hoists are grouped plain objects, not symbol instances
- [x] Custom Selection (type, class, symbol, fill and pen colour, line weight; is or is not; replace, add to or remove from the selection)
- [x] Find and Modify (Tools): Custom Selection's criteria, then change class, fill or pen colour, line weight, opacity, symbol or layer of every object found, as one undo step
- [x] Text > Find-Replace Text: Action Find Next (selects what holds the text, the outermost group it sits in, on its layer, and zooms to it; the next use goes on from there, round to the first), Replace Next (the next text, field or cell holding it), Replace All Selected (in the selected objects and what they hold) and Replace All; Look in Text Objects, Record Fields (the text fields of records attached to objects) and Worksheets (text cells; not with Replace All Selected); Options Active Layer Only, All Layers or Visible Layers Only; Case-sensitive, and Whole words only as Rodeo adds; each replace one undo step; the settings kept from one use to the next. Not searched: a plug-in object's own parameters and the text it draws (Rodeo can't redraw most plug-ins from them), number and formula cells, database rows, and text in symbol definitions; a replace that would empty a text object leaves it as it was
- [x] Text > Capitalization: UPPER CASE, lower case and Title Caps (each word's first letter capital, the rest small) for the selected texts, one undo step
- [x] Check Spelling (Text menu: selected text, active layer or all layers, British or American English; Ignore, Ignore All, Change, Change All, Add to this browser's word list; skips words in capitals and with numbers; one undo step on Done)
- [x] Edit > Paste as Bitmap: the picture on the system clipboard, placed as an image at 72 dpi on the layer; or else the objects last copied or cut in this window drawn as one picture (as Export SVG draws them, hidden objects left out) on a clear background, their own size, centred in the view as Paste centres them, as sharp as the view shows them or 150 dpi on paper, whichever is finer (8000 pixels a side at most). Rodeo's reading: a Copy is drawn when it's pasted, so an edit made after it asks for the copy again (a Cut is drawn as it's made); copies from other windows paste as a bitmap only through the system clipboard's picture; drawings with no image yet can't take one

## View

- [x] Zoom in, out, to fit, to selection; pan; zoom to cursor on the wheel
- [x] View > Previous View and Next View: back and forward through the plan views shown (a zoom or pan counts once it settles; per drawing), also in the View bar's zoom menu and the drawing's context menu
- [x] View > Zoom > Fit to Objects (⌘6, and in the View bar's zoom menu): the selected objects fill the window, or with nothing selected every visible object does; in the 3D view the selection is framed from the direction it looks, the visible model with none
- [x] View > Fit to Page Area (a sheet's page fills the window; greyed on design layers, whose page isn't placed yet) and Normal Scale (the drawing as large on screen as it prints, at the active layer's scale), also in the View bar's zoom menu; without Vectorworks' ⌘4 and ⌘5, which the browser keeps for its tabs
- [x] View > Zoom Line Thickness: line weights at their size on paper (the layer's or viewport's scale), thickening as the drawing is zoomed in; off, a weight keeps its width on screen at any zoom
- [x] 3D view: Top, Bottom, Front, Back, Left, Right and the eight isometrics (Right, Left, Right Rear and Left Rear Isometric, and their Lower ones from below) (View > Standard Views); Wireframe, Shaded, Textured (View > Rendering); orbit, pan, zoom; click to select
- [x] View > Set 3D View…: viewer position and look-to point typed (x, y, height above the active layer's elevation), starting at the camera or, from the plan, at eye height at the window's foot
- [x] Grid: show and snap
- [x] Layer and class options, all six: Active Only, Gray Others, Gray/Snap Others, Show Others, Show/Snap Others, Show/Snap/Modify Others (others snapped to only under the /Snap ones, selected only under the last); in View > Layer Options and Class Options, the View bar, the Navigation palette and the drawing's right-click menu; keyed ⌥⌘3–8, classes ⇧⌥⌘3–8
- [x] View > Unhide Objects, Show Selected Objects Only, Hide Selected Objects and Object Visibility… (Show, Show Only or Hide the objects that meet Custom Selection's criteria, at once), as Vectorworks 2026 has them: hidden objects aren't drawn, selected (click, box, Select All) or snapped to, and leave the 3D view (a top-level object whole), sheet and design-layer viewports, and every export and printout (PDF, Print, PNG, SVG, DXF, OBJ, glTF, IFC, 3DS, STL, USDZ, shapefiles, Publish); Show Selected Objects Only leaves objects already out of sight (hidden layer or class) as they are; the drawing shows an Unhide Objects button while any are hidden; not while a symbol is edited. Kept while the drawing is open, not yet saved with it (how Vectorworks marks a hidden object isn't known); a group member hidden while the group is edited still shows in 3D and 3D exports; not yet shown ghosted
- [x] Perspective and orthogonal projection switch (View > Projection, the View bar, the Visualization palette)
- [x] View > Projection > Narrow, Normal and Wide Perspective (20°, 35° and 60° from the picture's top to its bottom) and Set Custom Perspective… (any angle from 5° to 120°): the camera stands nearer or further back so the model at the orbit point keeps its size; standard views keep the lens chosen
- [x] View > Projection > 2D Plan: the plan drawing, ticked while it shows (as Top/Plan); a 3D projection picked from the plan opens the 3D view in it, from the last 3D direction rather than from Top as Vectorworks does
- [x] View > Rotate Plan: turns the plan on screen without moving anything in the drawing. Click the center, then click (or press and drag) along the line that will lie level; Shift gives 15° steps, Esc cancels. Or type the angle in the View bar's field beside its button (positive counter-clockwise), 0 to take it off. View > Rotated Top/Plan (⌘5) and Standard Views swap between it and the true Top/Plan; turning it glides. The grid, rulers (blue, from the center), snap to grid, Shift angles, the marquee, the selection's corners and scaling handles, arrow-key nudges and Zoom go with the view, and the View Cube turns with it; Rectangle, Rounded Rectangle, Oval, Text, Symbol and the tools that place a part lay theirs square to the screen. Each open drawing keeps its own rotation for this session; sheet layers and editing a symbol show unrotated. Not offered: saving the rotation in the file (where a file keeps it isn't known), saved views or Previous/Next keeping it, X'/Y' labels, the data bar or Object Info reading along the plan (they read the drawing's), a draggable working plane, turned exports or prints. Other drawing tools lie square to the drawing; a rectangle laid square to a rotated plan is turned in the drawing, so it has scaling handles but no corner grips, and an oval there becomes a polygon. Where Vectorworks' second click and the positive direction aren't spelled out, these are Rodeo's readings of the help
- [x] View > Projection > Two-Point Perspective: a perspective whose eye stays level, so uprights stay upright however the view is turned
- [x] View > Projection > Oblique Cavalier 45, Cavalier 30, Cabinet 45 and Cabinet 30: the view's depth drawn slantwise at 45° or 30°, full length (Cavalier) or half (Cabinet), the front kept true; picking follows the slanted rays
- [x] View > Rotate 3D View…: the view turned by typed angles about the screen's horizontal, the screen's upright and the drawing's z axis, in that order; the last angles kept
- [x] Onscreen View Control (View > Onscreen View Control, and its own menu button): a cube in a corner of the drawing turned as the view is, its faces named; a face, edge or corner clicked goes to that view (the six standard views and the eight isometrics; an edge looks along it), the view shown already to the opposite one; dragged, the 3D view turns about its centre as a flyover does; the wheel over it zooms the view about its centre; X, Y and Z axes beside it; Top/Plan shows its top; the Top face's 2D/3D toggle goes to Top/Plan (2D) or from it back to the 3D view (3D), shown wherever the Top face is; Show Onscreen View Control, Show Axes, Show Cube and Position (any corner) kept as application settings; with several view panes, in the active pane's corner, turned as its view is
- [x] Onscreen View Control's compass ring: in a georeferenced drawing it lies round the cube's foot with N at true north, and turns with the view; it only shows, as Vectorworks' does. Show Compass Ring is in the control's menu and View > Onscreen View Control
- [x] 3D view: extrudes Vectorworks saved without a body (most plug-in objects' 3D parts) drawn from their profile, holes included; plug-in objects' 3D parts, site models and doors and windows in walls placed where the plug-in is; multiple extrudes' and solid additions' bodies drawn in their parent's coordinates, where Vectorworks caches them, and carried along when they move; multiple extrudes saved without a body drawn as a loft through their profiles
- [x] Flyover and walkthrough in the 3D view: orbit about a double-clicked point; W A S D or the arrows walk, Q and E go down and up (Shift: faster)
- [x] Clip cube (View > Clip Cube; the Visualization palette's X, Y and Z ranges cut the model; picking ignores what is cut away)
- [x] Render modes: Hidden Line and Dashed Hidden Line (View > Rendering and the View bar)
- [x] Shadows from the sun, in Shaded and Textured (glass casts none)
- [x] Render modes: Sketch (View > Rendering and the View bar): hidden line on paper, each edge drawn twice in pencil with wobbling ends that stay put as the view turns
- [x] View > Rendering > Shaded Options… (also at the foot of the View bar's Render mode pop-up): Textures (Shaded draws the objects' textures, as Textured does), Colors (off: white faces; plants and image props keep their cut-out pictures), Shadows (the Visualization palette's switch), On ground only (shadows fall on a ground plane a hair under the visible model's lowest point, not on the objects), Draw edges (off: Shaded and Textured draw no outlines) and shadow quality (Low, Medium, High, Very High: the sun's and light objects' shadow maps at a quarter, a half, the usual 4096 px or twice it, as far as the graphics card allows). Not here: Anti-aliasing (always on), Edge thickness (WebGL draws 1 px lines), Crease angle (the model's edges are baked at 20°), environment lighting and reflections, object reflections, Lighting and Background Options, the camera effects and the faceting detail. Settings of the 3D view, as the Visualization palette's, not saved in the file
- [x] View > Rendering > Line Render Options…: Dash Style (the document's dash styles, drawn at the active design layer's scale, the first 8 dashes and gaps of each; Default keeps dashes a share of the model's size) and Dash Shade (0 % the edges' grey to 100 % white; a number, not a slider) for Dashed Hidden Line. Dashed Hidden Line's hidden edges are now really dashed (they drew solid before: three left the indexed edge sets without line distances). Not here: Smoothing Angle (edges baked at 20°), Generate Intersecting Lines, Display Surface Hatches, Display Text and Markers and the sketch options
- [ ] View > Rendering > Wireframe Options (fills of planar objects in 3D views): Rodeo's 3D view shows no planar 2D objects to fill
- [x] View > Renderworks Style (also a submenu of the View bar's Render mode pop-up): Vectorworks' default styles by name, each a render mode and the Render palette's lighting, sky, quality and clay settings at once: Preview Exterior and Interior (Realistic without ambient occlusion, reflections or glow), Realistic Exterior and Interior Fast (Realistic), Final (Path Traced to Exterior's 3 or Interior's 16 bounces, 1024 samples), Exterior Night Fast and Final (a dark sky, no sun, light objects doubled, a stop brighter) and Render Style White Model (the clay look in white). Interior styles open the exposure a stop. The style in effect is checked until its mode or a setting it applied changes; the materials' finishes, fog, lens and grading are kept
- [ ] Renderworks styles: Vectorworks' library styles' own settings (unpublished; these are Rodeo's readings of their names), Realistic Colors White (plain colours white with textures kept: the clay look drops textures), the file's own Renderworks style resources (listed in the Resource Manager, their settings not read), New/Edit Renderworks Style, Artistic and Redshift types, per-layer and per-viewport styles
- [x] Lighting options and the Heliodon: the sun for a latitude, date, solar time and north, or a headlight with the view; ambient light (a setting of the window, not saved in the file)
- [x] View > Set Sun Position…: the site's latitude and longitude, its time zone's meridian (a list of GMT offsets by the half hour, and the three a quarter hour off), page north, the date, the clock time and daylight saving, with the results worked out as they are typed: the azimuth from south (west positive) and from north (clockwise), both before page north turns them, the elevation and the true solar time. OK aims the selected directional lights (ones the drawing places) at the sun, else adds a directional light at the view's centre on the active layer, 2400 mm up, selected; one undo step. Rodeo's readings: page north turns counter-clockwise (Vectorworks doesn't say which way), a sun below the horizon is refused, and a new light copies one the drawing has (a drawing with none says so), as the Light tool's do. What was typed is kept for the session, not saved in the file
- [x] View > Set Lighting Options…: Indirect Lighting (None, 2 Bounces, Exterior 3, Normal 4, Interior 16; Rodeo's path tracer counts the first hit too, so Normal is its 5, and another count it has shows as itself), Ambient Info On and Brightness (the Shaded view's ambient light; Vectorworks' default 35 % is Rodeo's usual, so 70 % doubles it), Ambient Occlusion On and Strength (the Realistic engine's), Emitter Brightness (every light object in Realistic and Path Traced renders); the same settings as the Visualization and Render palettes, kept on this machine, no undo
- [ ] Set Lighting Options: the ambient light's Color, the occlusion's Size, White Color Temperature and Environment Lighting (Realistic and Path Traced are lit by the Render palette's sky), ambient light in Realistic and Path Traced renders, the Renderworks-style and viewport versions of the dialog
- [x] Heliodon objects: Object Info's Sun section sets a Heliodon's latitude, date and solar time, shows the sun's altitude and azimuth and the sunrise and sunset, writes the figures the Heliodon shows as Vectorworks does and turns its lights to the sun (editing those parameters in its record does the same); Light the 3D view puts the 3D view's sun there
- [x] Heliodon objects: the time and date its label prints follow its sun, and the Sun section sets its longitude and daylight saving (the clock then puts the sun elsewhere when it keeps clock time)
- [ ] Heliodon objects: a time zone chosen in the Sun section (Vectorworks' list of zones isn't known; its record takes one as text)
- [x] Standard views and render modes on the plan's own view: a design layer's saved view (Top/Plan, or a 3D view with its projection, render mode and camera) opens with the drawing, and saving stores the 3D view's camera, projection and render mode on the active layer; View > Standard Views > Top/Plan goes back to the plan

## Modify

- [x] Group, Ungroup, Edit Group (⌘[) and Exit Group (⌘])
- [x] Modify > Top Level: leaves every group being edited at once, and the symbol being edited, back to the drawing with the outermost group selected; on the drawing's right-click menu too when it does more than Exit Group or Exit Symbol (a group open inside another, or inside a symbol)
- [x] Exit Group button over the drawing while a group is open (with its name, when it has one), and Window > Edit Group and Object Visibility Options (Vectorworks 2025's name for Exit Group Options): Use Large Buttons or Use Small Buttons for the Exit Group, Exit Symbol and Unhide Objects buttons over the drawing (an icon and its label, or the icon alone with the label as its tooltip; an old Icon Only choice reads as Small), and <Esc><Esc> Exits Group or Does Not Exit Group (the first Esc clears the selection), kept in this browser. The Unhide Objects button sits in the same stack as the edit bars, under the View bar; Vectorworks' Text Only button style is gone, as it is from Vectorworks 2025
- [x] Send to front, forward, backward, to back
- [x] Rotate 90°, Flip horizontal and vertical, Scale Objects, Move (typed values), Rotate… (a typed angle about the selection's centre)
- [x] Modify > Rotate Each Left 90° and Rotate Each Right 90° (and the right-click menu's Rotate and Flip): each selected object turns a quarter about the centre of its own bounds, so each stays where it was; one undo step
- [x] Rounded rectangles turn, mirror and scale as rectangles do, in groups, symbols' contents and extrude profiles too, their corners stretching with the sides they lie along (stretched across the sides of a turned one, it becomes a polygon, as a rectangle does); they copy, duplicate, paste into other drawings and go into symbols
- [x] Modify > Move 3D… (X, Y and Z offsets): extrudes, sweeps, solids, 3D polygons and meshes, and symbol instances and plug-in objects with their z, raised or lowered with what they hold, in one undo step; 2D objects have no height and move in plan only
- [x] Modify > Unrotate 3D Objects (⇧⌘0): the selected extrudes, sweeps, multiple extrudes and solids turned back to the layer's axes, whether turned in plan or tilted in 3D, each about the middle of its 3D box (a cached body in the layer's frame turned with it), and symbol instances holding 3D about their insertion point, their scale kept; one undo step. Mirrored or stretched ones, plug-in objects, doors and windows set in walls and objects inside selected groups are left, and a selection with nothing turned says so. a sweep turns about its own y axis, so one standing as Sweep makes it comes back lying along the layer's y axis. Vectorworks' help doesn't say which point it keeps; Rodeo keeps each in place
- [x] Modify > Rotate 3D… (Vectorworks' Rotate Object in 3D dialog): the selection turned by a typed angle about the X, Y or Z axis of the layer plane or of the active working plane, through the working plane's centre (the layer's origin at its elevation when the layer plane is active), the middle of the box the selection fills in 3D, or the next mouse click; one undo step, the dialog's settings kept for next time. About the layer's Z axis every object turns, as Rotate… turns it; about any other axis the extrudes, sweeps, multiple extrudes, solids, 3D polygons, meshes and symbols holding only 3D turn (those in groups too, a body cached in the layer's frame turning with them), and the rest (2D objects, plug-in objects, walls, hybrid symbols, doors and windows in walls) stay as they are, with a note saying how many.
