Rodeo takes the mouse click in plan, on the layer plane, where Vectorworks takes it in a 3D view too. A turned extrude's plan bounds are its 3D box's corners turned, so they can be looser than what it draws. A positive angle turns counter-clockwise seen from the axis's positive end; Vectorworks' help doesn't say which way. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] Align and Distribute; Align to Grid (each selected object's upper-left corner to the nearest snap grid point, one undo step)
- [x] Modify > Align > Align by Increment…: each selected object moves to the nearest multiple of Round to the nearest along the axes ticked (X, Y and Z, at least one), by its insertion point (symbols and plug-ins, lighting devices among them) or else its centre; Use snap grid takes X and Y to the nearest snap grid point instead; Shift+click (Align immediately using previous settings, or Display this dialog box) says what a Shift-click on the command does; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: Z goes by the middle of what the object shows in 3D, above its layer's elevation, as Rodeo knows insertion points in plan only, and objects with nothing in 3D keep their height; with Use snap grid, Z still rounds to the increment; it takes any objects, not only light plot ones; the settings are kept for the session, starting at 6"
- [x] Modify > Align > Align/Distribute…: horizontally and vertically, each ticked or not, Align or Distribute by left, centre or right (top, centre or bottom), by insertion point (symbols and plug-in objects; others by their centres) or, distributing, by Spacing (equal gaps); both directions one undo step, the dialog's settings kept for next time. A locus in the selection stays put and the others line up with it; without one they line up with the selection's edge, or its middle for centres and insertion points; distributing keeps the outermost two. The right-click menu's Align/Distribute flyout (Align Left, Center, Right, Top, Middle, Bottom, Distribute Horizontally and Vertically (Spacing), Align Insertion Point Horizontally and Vertically, Align/Distribute…) keeps the object right-clicked put. Locked objects aren't kept as the reference (Rodeo can't lock objects yet), and Align/Distribute Leader Lines isn't built
- [x] Modify > Align > Align/Distribute 3D… (⇧⌘=), Vectorworks' 3D Object Align and Distribute dialog: X, Y and Z, each ticked or not, Align or Distribute by Minimum, Center, Insertion Point or Maximum or, distributing, by Spacing (equal gaps), along the layer plane's axes or the active working plane's; each object measured by the box its 3D faces and edges fill in those axes, all three one undo step, the settings kept for next time. 2D objects stay where they are (a message counts them), as Vectorworks leaves them. Insertion points count along the layer plane's X and Y only (Rodeo knows them in plan); elsewhere objects go by their centres. Rodeo has no 3D loci or locked objects, so none is kept as the reference: objects line up with the selection's edge or middle
- [x] Modify > Align > Align/Distribute Leader Lines…, Vectorworks' Align Leader Lines dialog: Align shoulder points, Align label points (with both, every label point also under the topmost's), Move shoulder points (label points alone: the shoulder lines keep their length, or only the label points move), and Vertical Distribution None, Parallel target lines (turned parallel to the topmost's as it ends up) or Equal distance between shoulder lines (the top two's gap); then, for a horizontal alignment, a reference line drawn with two clicks (not level, refused if it is), the leaders shown where they'd go as it's drawn; none ticked moves them at once. One undo step. Rodeo's leaders are the Leader Line tools' three-point open polygons (the shoulder the more level segment, within 30°) and the Callout tool's groups (its note and box go with the label point, its marker stays); data tags, plants, landscape areas, stakes, benchmarks, redlines and the like aren't Rodeo objects with leaders yet. Other selected objects are left, and a selection with no leaders says so
- [x] Add, Clip and Intersect Surface; Compose, Decompose; Convert to Polygons, Convert to Lines (arcs and circles sampled); Convert Copy to Polygons and Convert Copy to Lines (the originals stay, copies just above them convert; one undo step)
- [x] Convert to Polylines: rectangles, polygons, circles, circular arcs and round-cornered rounded rectangles become polylines tracing them exactly (arc vertices for corners and circles, a point-on-arc vertex for an arc's middle), keeping class and attributes, one undo step; ellipses and elliptical corners stay, with a note
- [x] Convert Arc to Radius Polyline on the drawing's right-click menu over a polyline with an arc (fillet) vertex, selected or not, as Vectorworks has it: its arc vertices become radius vertices, which lie on the curve, so it is reshaped by dragging a point of it. Each fillet as drawn becomes a corner where it leaves the edge before, a radius vertex half way round and a corner where it meets the edge after; arcs meeting on one circle become one radius vertex where they meet (as Vectorworks' example shows two fillets becoming one). The curve is the same, its class and attributes kept, selected, one undo step. Rodeo's reading: a fillet next to a Bezier, cubic or radius vertex moves that neighbour's end from the fillet's corner point to where the fillet ends, which can change that neighbour's curve; hidden edges are shown again
- [x] Modify > Poly Smoothing: No Smoothing, Bezier Spline Smoothing, Cubic Spline Smoothing, Arc Smoothing and Radius Smoothing on polygons and polylines (a polyline's own vertices kept, not its flattened curve), named as Vectorworks names them. Radius Smoothing rounds each corner as Arc Smoothing does (as large as fits) and makes each curve a radius vertex, as Convert Arc to Radius Polyline does. Rodeo's reading: how Vectorworks draws radius vertices side by side isn't known, so it keeps corner vertices where the curves meet rather than making every vertex a radius vertex; the Fillet tool's radius isn't used for Arc or Radius Smoothing
- [x] Modify > Join (⌘J) and Join (No Trim) (⇧⌘J): two selected lines that aren't parallel meet where their lines cross, the end of each nearer the crossing moving to it (a line stopping short is extended, one running past is cut back by its shorter part; which part Vectorworks keeps isn't documented, so Rodeo keeps the longer); Join (No Trim) only extends, leaving a line that runs past as it is. Two straight walls meet in a mitred corner; with No Trim a wall running past the crossing stays whole and the other runs into its side (a tee), and walls that both run past are refused. Class and attributes kept, one undo step
- [x] Modify > Join > Join and Fillet (⌥⌘J): four selected lines making two double lines (two parallel pairs, the pairs not parallel to each other) are joined with rounded corners at the fillet radius the Fillet tool uses (the two share it; Fillet Settings asks for it the first time when the Fillet tool hasn't set one). Where both end at their meeting they make an L: the inner lines meet in a fillet of the radius and the outer ones in one larger by the double lines' width, about the same centre. Where one runs on past the other they make a T: each line of the one that ends rounds into the near line of the other, which opens between the two fillets; the far line stays. Double lines that cross are refused, as is a radius the lines are too short for. Class and attributes kept, each arc taking its line's; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the radius is the inner corner's (Vectorworks doesn't say which); the double lines are found from the four lines' geometry, since Rodeo's Double Line tool draws plain lines; lines only, not polylines or the polygons Double Line can draw
- [ ] Modify > Join between a wall and a set of double lines
- [x] Modify > Hatch…: one selected closed object (rectangle, polygon, closed polyline, circle or oval) filled with one of the drawing's hatches, picked from a list beside its preview; click where the hatch starts, then click again to turn it (Shift 15°), the hatch shown over the object as it turns, Esc giving up. The lines are cut to the outline (even-odd, so a notch is skipped) and to their dashes, and grouped just above the object in each line family's colour and weight; the group isn't tied to the object or the hatch afterwards (Vectorworks' non-associative hatch). A hatch sized on the page is drawn at the layer's scale. One undo step. A hatch that would need more than 20,000 lines in the object is refused, circles and ovals are traced with 180 points, and a new blank drawing has no hatch to pick (New Hatch needs one to copy settings from)
- [x] Create Symbol from the selection
- [ ] Lock and unlock objects (needs a Vectorworks-saved file with a known locked object: none of the header flag bits matches locking, FORMAT.md §2)
- [x] Combine into Surface (a polygon of the area the selected pieces enclose; where several areas, the outside of each connected set rather than a clicked one)
- [x] Convert to Group (⌘K): a symbol becomes a group of its contents, placed as it was (3D contents too, raised as the symbol raises them)
- [x] Convert to Group for plug-in objects: a group of the plug-in's drawing as it shows (in its class, with its shadow and opacity, its text the right way up), its 3D parts placed and raised as the plug-in placed them
- [x] Convert to Group for symbols and plug-in objects holding plug-in objects: those stay plug-ins, placed, turned and mirrored with the rest through their matrix, what they draw kept as it is
- [x] Convert to Mesh (Modify > Convert: 3D objects, 3D polygons, symbols and plug-in objects become one mesh each of the flat faces they show in 3D, convex and of at most ten corners, sharing their corners; each face drawn as the part it came from, the object's texture on the mesh; settings from a mesh in the drawing)
- [x] Modify > Simplify Mesh…: the selected meshes, and those inside selected groups, plug-in objects (an IFC entity's, a plant's) and the definitions of selected symbols, drawn with fewer faces: Quality (1–100%) sets about how many each keeps; Original number of faces shows, and Preview works out the simplified number. Edges are collapsed cheapest first by quadric error, so flat parts go first while borders, creases and where differently drawn faces meet keep their shape; nothing is turned more than 60° or pinched. A UV-mapped mesh keeps its map exact: every vertex left is one it had, seams moving only along themselves, so the texture doesn't slide or crack. Each mesh keeps its record (id, name, class, attributes, records, texture); its faces become triangles drawn as the faces they came from (a face's own aux records dropped). One undo step. Faces are counted as triangles (a face of four corners is two). Not yet: Preview showing the simplified meshes (it gives the count only); two values of a UV map's key 6 that Rodeo doesn't know are kept as they were, so Vectorworks may redo or drop such a map. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Modify > Convert > Convert Object to Dimension: each selected line becomes a linear dimension measuring it, its dimension line where the line was, and each circular arc an angular dimension of the angle it spans (clockwise arcs too, past a half turn too), its arc where the arc was; a line becomes a dimension object when the drawing has a linear one to copy, as the Dimension tool's do, and otherwise (always for arcs: Rodeo makes no angular dimension objects) the dimension is drawn as its lines, arc and value, grouped, in the object's place with its class and attributes; one undo step, the dimensions selected. Double lines aren't converted: Rodeo's Double Line tool draws plain lines or polygons
- [x] Convert to 3D Polygons (Modify > Convert: 3D objects, symbols and plug-in objects become groups of closed 3D polygons, one per flat face they show in 3D, each drawn as the part it came from)
- [x] Modify > Convert > Replace with Symbol…: the selected objects become instances of a symbol picked from the drawing's definitions (Choose a Symbol, by folder): a locus's on its point, a symbol instance keeping its placement and taking the new definition, any other object's on the centre of its bounds, upright at full size, in the object's layer and class. Retain the objects' records carries their data records over (plug-in parameters never); Add the symbol definition's records adds the definition's with its values, an object's own record of the same format winning. It asks to confirm, then one undo step, the instances selected
- [ ] Replace with Symbol: guesses to check against Vectorworks: where a non-locus object's instance goes (the centre of its bounds), whose values win when an object and the definition have the same record, and that the instances go on top rather than where each object stacked; the drawing needs a symbol instance to copy settings from, so a new blank drawing can't
- [ ] Convert to NURBS; Convert to Group for plug-ins whose 3D is a body or terrain of their own (site models), and for symbols and plug-ins tilted or stretched in 3D holding 3D objects; 3D polygons keeping a face's texture and holes
- [x] Edit symbol definitions in place: Modify > Edit Symbol, a double-click on an instance or Resources' Edit Symbol opens the definition alone, every tool edits it, every instance follows (bounds too), Exit Symbol returns to the layer and view
- [x] Model > Add Solids and Subtract Solids: two or more extrudes, multiple extrudes, sweeps and solids on one layer or group become one solid adding them or taking the others from the backmost, drawn in 3D from its operands (a subtraction cut), saved without a cached body as Extrude
- [x] Model > Intersect Solids (⌥⌘I): two or more extrudes, multiple extrudes, sweeps and solids on one layer or group replaced by one mesh of the volume they all enclose (BSP booleans, after csg.js, over at most 4,000 triangles between them), in the backmost's class and with its attributes; solids that don't all overlap are refused; one undo step, the mesh selected. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks makes a solid intersection that keeps its operands and can be edited; Rodeo makes a mesh, because none of the demos' 9,000-odd solids (kinds 1, 3, 8, 9, 16, 17, 19 and 26) is an intersection, so the kind Vectorworks saves for one isn't known (kinds 8 and 9 lie inside their second operand but not their first). Volumes match exactly for boxes and to the tessellation for curved extrudes. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] Model > Section Solids… (⌥⌘T): two objects selected, one an extrude, multiple extrude, sweep, solid or mesh to be cut and the other a 3D polygon, extrude, multiple extrude, sweep, solid or mesh to cut it, on one layer or group; the dialog's Cut with (the one that can't be cut, else the frontmost, as Vectorworks offers the newest first), Retain Sectioning Object and Reverse Direction (Object Info's in Vectorworks), the last ticks kept. A flat 3D polygon cuts away the part on the side its normal points to (the right-hand rule round its points; the other side reversed) and must cover the solid seen square to it, as Vectorworks has the sectioning surface larger than the solid; a solid cuts away the part inside it, or reversed keeps only that part. What is left becomes a mesh in the solid's place, class and attributes, the faces the cut made drawn with the sectioning object's attributes (Vectorworks marks them with its colour); the sectioning object is deleted unless retained; one undo step. A polygon that isn't flat or doesn't cover the solid, a cut that leaves all or none of it, solids that don't overlap and more than 4,000 faces are refused. Volumes match for a box cut level both ways, cut through its middle by a polygon tilted 30° and 15° (half), and cut by and intersected with an overlapping box. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks makes a solid section that stays editable (its X/Y/Z Scale, Reverse Direction afterwards); Rodeo makes a plain mesh, as it does for Intersect Solids. NURBS surfaces don't cut (stepped sections), and a mesh is taken as a closed solid. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] Model > 3D Power Pack > Create Helix-Spiral…: the Helix-Spiral Creation dialog's Use Turns (Turns) or Use Pitch (Pitch, the distance between turns), Start Radius and End Radius (a spiral when they differ, growing evenly along the path), Start Angle (from the path's right toward up, looking along it at its start) and Reverse Direction (the coil turns clockwise seen looking along the path, anticlockwise reversed); the last settings kept. Each selected line, arc, rectangle, polygon, polyline, circle, ellipse or 3D polygon becomes an open 3D polygon coiling round it in its place, class and attributes, 48 points a turn, its circles square to the path and each run's stretch reaching the planes that mitre its corners, so the coil goes on unbroken round them; one undo step. Settings out of range, a path turning back too sharply and more than 20,000 points are refused. Checked: a coil round a line keeps its radius to 1e-14 and its length to the helix formula, comes back to its start angle every pitch, a spiral's radius grows exactly, and coils round an L and a circle have no gaps. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks makes a helix-spiral object (ungrouping to a NURBS curve) whose settings stay editable; Rodeo makes a plain 3D polygon, which Extrude Along Path takes as its path. Flatten isn't offered (where its flat spiral lies isn't documented), nor NURBS curves as paths; the start angle's zero and the turning direction are Rodeo's choice. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] Model > 3D Power Pack > Create Planar Caps (⌥⌘Q), Create Surface from Curves (⌥⌘K), Create Drape Surface…, Revolve with Rail and Unfold Surfaces, and Add Solids taking meshes. Create Planar Caps puts a flat mesh, a separate object, over each open end of the selected solids or meshes that lies in a plane (an end that doesn't gets none, and a toast says so) and leaves them selected with the solid; Add Solids with a mesh among its objects now makes one mesh of them, in the backmost's place, the volume they enclose when each is closed and otherwise their faces together, so a solid and its caps close up (Subtract and Intersect Solids still don't take meshes). Create Surface from Curves takes one closed curve (2D shapes count), or open curves meeting end to end: flat when they lie in a plane, a Coons patch for four sides, otherwise stretched across like a soap film. Curves that only cross, or interior curves in a network, aren't taken, as Vectorworks' U and V network is. Create Drape Surface's U Direction, V Direction (more than 3, up to 200 here) and Plane Z Value (lower than the objects' highest point; in the model, not above the layer) give a rectangular mesh over any objects showing 3D faces, reaching one step past their box: a height field from above, so it hangs straight down and doesn't fold under anything as cloth would. Revolve with Rail takes three selected curves as axis, profile and rail in the order they were selected, or else whichever order fits, checking Vectorworks' conditions, rather than clicking them after picking the command; the profile scales so its farthest point follows the rail. Unfold Surfaces gives a polygon per flattened surface on the active layer, in a row beside the selection, in the surface's class and attributes: one piece for an open mesh that flattens whole, otherwise one to each surface between creases over 20°, and it refuses a surface curved two ways (a cone's tip is allowed); a hole is a polygon of its own, not cut from the outline. All results are meshes of flat pieces (polygons for Unfold), not NURBS surfaces, and hold no link to the curves.
- [x] Model > 3D Power Pack > Create Fillet Surface… (⌥⌘Y), Stitch and Trim Surfaces, Extend NURBS… and Rebuild NURBS…, on meshes and 3D polygons, as Rodeo has no NURBS. Create Fillet Surface takes two open meshes (a closed one is refused, pointing at Fillet Edge): the Fillet Radius, the Cross Section Type (Circular, Linear, and Tangent and Curvature Continuous Blends, which are cubic and quintic Bézier curves between the same feet) and Trim Original Surfaces; a rolling ball's centre line is where the meshes moved out along their normals cross, so a surface must reach far enough for that, and two checks reverse each normal to choose the corner, standing in for Object Info's Reverse Normal (a new mesh has no normal to show). The longest such centre line is used, and Trim Type's Edge Tangency, Minimal, Maximal and None aren't offered: the fillet runs where both surfaces reach. Stitch and Trim Surfaces splits surfaces and solids where they cross, peels off every flap with a free edge, and replaces them with one closed mesh per enclosed volume (a sheet through a box gives two), up to 4,000 faces. Extend NURBS… takes one open 3D polygon (End Point or Start Point) or a mesh (an Edge of its rim, between corners of over 30°, or all round a tube's end), Linear or Smooth Extension and Distance; Smooth carries on round the circle through the last three points, so on an irregular mesh it's approximate. Rebuild NURBS… takes 3D polygons (Number of Points, at least 3) and meshes (U and V, at least 4, each up to 100) with Keep Original and a live Maximum Deviation: curves are resampled evenly, which can drop corners; a surface must be four-sided or a tube with two open ends, and its grid is projected onto the old mesh. Create Interpolated Surface isn't here yet, as there's no way to reshape a surface's points.
- [ ] The other solid operations (fillet, chamfer, shell; the kinds Vectorworks saves for them aren't identified)
- [x] Reshape by numbers: Object Info steps through a polygon's or polyline's vertices to type each position, vertex type and arc radius, add or delete one, open or close it
- [x] Select Vertex in Object Info Palette on the drawing's right-click menu over a vertex (within a few pixels) of the polygon or polyline under the pointer, or of a selected one (an arc vertex's handle, off its curve): Object Info shown on its Shape tab with that vertex stepped to, filled on the drawing
- [x] Trace Bitmap: filled outlines (with holes) or centrelines of an image's dark or light parts, at a threshold, simplified, specks dropped
- [x] Modify > Edit Crop… (and Edit Crop… on the drawing's right-click menu over an image): select an image and one rectangle, circle or oval, polygon or closed polyline drawn over it, and the picture keeps only what lies inside, as Vectorworks' crop followed by Delete Outside Crop; a level rectangle cuts it square (a JPEG stays a JPEG), any other shape cuts it to the shape's box and clears what lies outside the outline (a PNG then); the image object shrinks to the kept pixels at its scale, the shape deleted or kept as a frame, one undo step. With the image alone selected, a dialog trims a length off its Left, Right, Top and Bottom
- [x] Edit Image Effects… (an image's right-click menu) and Object Info's Image Effects… button: Vectorworks' Image Effects dialog for the selected images (more than one at once, as Vectorworks allows): a preview of the first that follows the sliders, Exposure, Contrast, Highlights, Shadows, Saturation, Temperature and Blur and Sharpness (−100 to 100), Sepia and Soft Edges (0 to 100), Invert Colors, Auto-Level (Exposure and Contrast set to stretch the picture's lightness) and Reset; Apply works the effects into each picture, a JPEG staying a JPEG unless soft edges need clear pixels, the image where it was at its size, one undo step. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks doesn't publish its formulas, so each slider is Rodeo's reading of the help's words (exposure two stops each way, contrast about middle grey, blur and sharpening sized to the picture); Vectorworks keeps the effects with the image to turn off (Apply Image Effects) or change later, while Rodeo changes the pixels for good (as Edit Crop does); no saved sets; image resources, textures, backgrounds and rendered viewports aren't offered
- [ ] Edit Crop as Vectorworks keeps it: the crop object stored with the image, edited later in a crop mode, shown with Crop Visible, and Object Info's Crop status; Rodeo cuts the pixels for good (Undo brings them back). Cropping images inside symbols or groups without opening them, and cropping PDF pages, aren't covered
- [x] Modify > Drafting Aids > Simplify Polys…: corner vertices taken from the selected polygons, polylines and 3D polygons by maximum deviation (0: collinear vertices) or minimum distance (0: coincident ones), in the document's units; start and end vertices, a polyline's curve vertices and the corners beside them, and a corner between a shown and a hidden edge stay; each object keeps its id, class, attributes and records; one undo step. Not yet: coincident curve vertices (Vectorworks takes those too). Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Modify > Drafting Aids > Arc into Segments…: the selected arcs, circles and ovals drawn again as straight segments of equal length along them, by their number or the longest a segment may be (in the document's units), as one polygon (closed for a whole turn) or a line per segment, in each arc's class and attributes just above it; the arcs stay; the new objects selected; one undo step. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Modify > Drafting Aids > Line into Segments…: the selected lines divided into a number of equal segments, with loci placed between them, the line broken into those segments, or both, the line left intact or not; in its class and attributes; one undo step. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Modify > Drafting Aids > Even Divide…: the selected lines, circular arcs and circles split into a number of equal lines or arcs (more than one), and rectangles into a grid of equal rectangles by divisions in width and height, a turned rectangle's parts turned with it; the original retained or not, a retained one left selected with the parts or not; in its class and attributes; one undo step. Ovals aren't divided (Vectorworks names lines, arcs, circles and rectangles)
- [x] Modify > Drafting Aids > Create Dividing Lines…: with two lines selected, click where the new lines start and where they end, a guide showing through the pointer (a circle round the lines' crossing, or for parallel lines a line across them) and the lines as the second click is sought; then the Dividing Lines Settings dialog's number of divisions (the last kept). The space between the two is divided evenly: lines at an angle get lines running out from the crossing between the two circles clicked, parallel lines get lines alongside them between the two lines across; both clicks on one guide place loci on it instead. In the active class with the attributes for new objects, on the layer, selected; one undo step; Esc gives up. Which of the two angles between crossing lines Vectorworks divides isn't documented: Rodeo divides the one each line runs out into from the crossing
- [x] Modify > Attributes > Make All Attributes by Class (every attribute group from the objects' classes), Remove by Class Settings (what each draws with kept as its own), Set Default Attributes (saves the default set: the one selected object's attributes, which new objects then draw with, or with nothing selected the Attributes palette's settings for new objects, markers and drop shadow too) and Use Default Attributes (the set given to the selected objects, or with nothing selected the palette put back to it); one undo step each; the four are in the Attributes palette's title bar menu too, as in Vectorworks' palette. The default set is kept while the drawing is open, not saved in it (where Vectorworks keeps it isn't known)
- [x] Modify > Guides: Make Guide (the selected objects into the class Guides, made when the drawing has none), Show Guides and Hide Guides (the class's visibility), Select Guides (the shown guides on the active layer or in the group being edited) and Delete All Guides (on every layer); one undo step each
- [x] Poly Smoothing's Cubic Spline Smoothing (a curve through every vertex), and cubic vertices drawn as Vectorworks draws them; Object Info's vertex type offers Cubic

## Basic palette tools

- [x] Selection, Pan, Zoom
- [x] Text
- [x] Line, Double Line
- [x] Rectangle, Circle, Arc, Oval
- [x] Freehand, Polyline, Polygon, Regular Polygon
- [x] Mirror, Rotate (with copy), Offset
- [x] Fillet, Chamfer, Trim, Split, Connect
- [x] Eyedropper
- [x] Rounded Rectangle (corners a quarter of the shorter side)
- [x] Rounded Rectangle preferences (proportional share, or fixed radii across and up)
- [x] Arc by radius, through three points, and Quarter Arc
- [x] Tangent Arc (start, the direction it leaves in, end)
- [x] Spiral
- [x] Double Line Polygon
- [x] Double Line modes: top, centre or bottom control line; create lines, the polygon between, or both
- [x] 2D Locus (click to place; it snaps, doesn't print, and goes to DXF as a POINT)
- [x] Callout (leader with an arrow, dot or no marker; shoulder; note with no box, a box or a rounded box)
- [x] Revision Cloud (round a rectangle or a polygon; arc length in preferences)
- [x] Clip tool (Exclusion, Inclusion, Split by a dragged rectangle)
- [x] Shear and Fixed Point Resize tools (beside Clip): a fixed point, then a drag or click-move-click on the selection; rectangles, rounded rectangles, polygons, circles and ovals (Shear on polylines too), others left as they are with a note. Fixed Point Resize stretches across and up independently about the fixed point (a start level or plumb with it refused, reversed past it, Shift keeps the proportions); Shear skews so the dragged point lands under the pointer (Shift across or up). A rectangle, circle or oval that can't keep its kind becomes a polygon; the factors or angles shown at the pointer; one undo step
- [x] Reshape tool (Move Vertices with a box across objects, Move Edge, Change Vertex to corner, Bezier or arc, Add Vertex, Delete Vertex; Delete removes picked vertices)
- [x] Move by Points (move or copy)
- [x] Attribute Mapping: drag a hatch's, tile's or gradient's origin and axis ends (turn, scale, skew; Shift 15° or square), the fill following the drag
- [x] New Hatch from Locally Mapped Hatch on the drawing's right-click menu over an object whose own hatch (not its class's) a fill mapping lays other than plainly: a new hatch resource, named in a dialog (the old name with -2 offered), its levels' start points, directions and spacings put through the mapping (moved, turned and skewed as drawn), copying the old hatch's ground, colours, weights, page or world sizing and tags, its previews drawn anew; the object takes it and its mapping goes back to the plain one, so it looks as before (lines within half a pixel at 2 px/mm). One undo step. Rodeo's reading: a mapping's axes turn and skew a hatch but don't scale it, as Rodeo draws mapped hatches; whether Vectorworks then clears the mapping or keeps a plain one isn't known (Rodeo keeps a plain one). New Tile from Locally Mapped Tile isn't offered: Rodeo can't make tiles
- [x] Visibility tool: click an object to show, gray or hide its class or its layer (modes 1–5); Option sets every other class or layer; the active layer stays shown; one undo step a click
- [x] Select Similar tool: click an object to select every object like it (Select any Object) or the selected ones like it (Current Selection), Shift adding; Select Similar Preferences: other (type, class, symbol, plug-in), fill, pen, text, wall and slab, and line end marker attributes, with All per group
- [x] Flyover (3D): drag orbits, double-click a point to orbit about it; W A S D, arrows, Q and E walk; the Flyover tool turns about the view's centre, a clicked origin or the selection's middle (see Visualization's Flyover, Translate View and Walkthrough)
- [x] Triangle (sharing the 2D Polygon's place): two clicks or a drag set the first side, Triangle Settings takes the rest by the mode (keys 1–3: Three Sides; Two Sides and Included Angle; Two Angles and Common Side) with Side 1 as clicked, then a click on the side of the first one where it goes, both possible triangles showing until then; Vectorworks' restriction messages when the values can't make a triangle; a polygon. Rodeo's reading: side 2 starts at the second click (the included angle there), Angle 1 is at the first click and Angle 2 at the second
- [x] Quarter Arc tool (sharing the Arc's place): click the start, then click (or release a drag) at the end; the arc leaves the start the way the pointer first moves off it (across, or up and down) and meets the end square to that, circular when the end is as far across as up or down (Shift keeps it so, at 45°), else a quarter ellipse; a polyline either way, as Vectorworks lists quarter arcs (a circular one with an arc vertex, an elliptical one a curve through 13 points on the ellipse). No Push/Pull mode (3D). The Arc tool keeps Rodeo's own Quarter Arc mode (an arc object, Option bulging it the other way)

## Dims/Notes

- [x] Dimension: linear, radial, diameter, angular (drawn as geometry)
- [x] Tape Measure
- [x] Protractor: the angle between two sides from a clicked vertex (the smaller way round, the larger and the sides' directions under it), drawn as an arc; Shift 15° sides; nothing added to the drawing
- [x] Native linear dimension objects: the Dimension tool's linear, chain and baseline modes place Vectorworks dimension objects (copies of a linear one the drawing has, in its standard: spacing, text and markers; a chain or baseline grouped) when the drawing has one to copy, else plain geometry as before. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Editing a linear dimension object in Object Info: the points it measures, the way it runs, its offset and its value (which measures again unless it says something else of its own), each one undo step, its lines and value redrawn in its own standard
- [x] A chain dimension's right-click menu, by where the click is: on a witness line, Split Dimension (two chains meeting there, the second selected; offered but dimmed at an end) and Delete Witness Line (the dimensions either side become one measuring both, its value measured again when it said its length; at an end, the end dimension goes); on a dimension's line, markers or value, Split Dimension (that dimension deleted, the chains either side of it kept), Delete Dimension (that dimension deleted and the ones after it moved back along the chain's line by its length, closing the gap, each keeping its length, value and line) and Edit Dimension (the chain opened as Edit Group opens it, that dimension selected, so Object Info edits it alone). A chain left with one dimension is that dimension out of its group. One undo step each. Add Dimension(s), anywhere on a chain or on a linear dimension: the Add Dimensions tool takes clicks, each where a new witness line ends (its dimension measuring along the chain's line): between two witness lines it splits that dimension there (values measured again), past an end it adds one (a lone linear dimension becomes a chain of it and the new ones), on one already there it's refused with a message; the witness line a click would draw shows as the pointer moves; double-click or Enter ends, Backspace takes back the last, Esc ends; each click one undo step. Rodeo's chains are groups of linear dimension objects (as its Dimension tool makes them when the drawing has a dimension to copy), not Vectorworks' chain dimension objects; a chain drawn as plain lines and text has none of these. Add Dimension(s) groups the chain again with each click (a new group, its own name and attributes not kept), so it's offered only for a chain or dimension on its layer, not in a group; Create smart chain dimension objects isn't a preference (a lone dimension always becomes a chain). Delete Dimension's closing up is Rodeo's reading of the help's "the remaining dimensions adjust", so the dimensions after it no longer stand where they measured
- [x] Associative dimensions, as Vectorworks makes them with Associate dimensions on (its default): a lone linear dimension the Dimension tool places from a vertex of an object to a vertex of the same or another one keeps which vertices they are (the top-most object's, as Auto associate picks), and when an edit moves or reshapes those objects it follows, its line as far from them as before, its value measured again unless it says something of its own; in the same undo step. Moving the dimension by itself, or deleting an object it measures, breaks the link (as Vectorworks does for an angular one); Disassociate on the dimension's right-click menu breaks it too. Dimension objects and dimensions drawn as pieces both follow (the pieces, and dimension objects Rodeo can't redraw in place, drawn anew). Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks ties them with coincident constraints both ways (moving the dimension moves the object); Rodeo keeps the link in a record of its own (Rodeo Associated Dimension) and follows one way, so Vectorworks sees plain dimensions; chains, baselines, ordinates, radial, diameter and angular dimensions, walls and dimensions across layers aren't associated, and there is no Associate dimensions or Auto associate setting
- [ ] Native ordinate, radial, diameter and angular dimension objects; associative dimensions both ways (moving the dimension moving the object), and for chains, baselines and radial, diameter and angular dimensions; changing a dimension's standard; editing dimensions drawn otherwise (extension lines dragged off their points, chains' shared lines, record data attached)
- [x] Chain, baseline, ordinate and constrained dimensions (Dimension tool modes 1–4; ordinates drawn as geometry, as radial, diameter and angular ones are)
- [x] Callout with leader; revision clouds (drawn as plain grouped objects, not the plug-in objects)
- [x] Text styles: Text > Text Style and Object Info apply one of the file's (its font, size and alignment) or unstyle text; a text shows the style it follows
- [x] Creating, editing, duplicating and deleting text styles (Resources: name, font, size, alignment; editing restyles the texts that follow it); a class's text style (Organization > Edit…) and Class Text Style for text (Text > Text Style, Object Info)
- [x] Markers (the Dims/Notes tool set): Section-Elevation Marker (a section line with a bubble past each end looking left or right, or an elevation's bubble looking where dragged), Interior Elevation Marker (four looks, each numbered, turned by a drag), Detail Callout (the detail circled from its centre to its edge, a leader on to its bubble), Drawing Label (bubble, title on a line, the layer's scale under it) and Elevation Benchmark (a quartered circle with the point's Y in the drawing's units over a label); bubbles split with the drawing and sheet numbers, arrows filled, sizes on paper, numbers and titles in Preferences; grouped plain objects
- [x] Dims/Notes: Reference Marker — a bubble with Drawing Number over Sheet Number, a Note under it, Arrow on Marker at an Arrow Angle; Use leader with Leader Type Line, Arc (Leader Radius on paper; too small for the span draws a half circle) or Bézier, an arrowhead at the target, a Shoulder Length on paper, Horizontal Position Auto/Left/Right, Marker Angle and Marker Scale Factor; modes Towards Target Object (the marker, then the target) and Towards Reference Marker (the target, then the marker); without a leader a click places it. Rodeo's reading: VW's styles, Drawing Title and Linked Viewport (Design Suite) aren't there, so the marker doesn't follow a viewport and the layout is fixed (the note under the bubble); the leader's arrowhead is drawn rather than taken from the Attributes palette's marker; grouped plain objects
- [x] Dims/Notes: Grid Line — Parallel (a drag or two clicks; Shift keeps to 15° steps from the last grid line, so parallel or square to it), Radial (the centre, where the line starts, where it ends along the ray), Polyline (corner vertices; a double-click or Enter ends it) and Duplicate (the last grid line drawn copied across, offset as a polyline or turned about a radial line's centre, to the pointer, or by Preferences' Duplicate Offset or Duplicate Angle); Preferences: Next Grid Label, Do Not Include Characters, Shoulder Length at Start/End, Show Bubble at Start/End/Both/None, Bubble Scale Factor; labels step on as VW's Grid label increments page says (the rightmost run of digits or same-case letters; 12.1a→12.1b, 01c.9→01c.10, A1.9→A2.0, 3.09→3.10, Az→Aaa, z→aa, ZZ→AAA; every example there checked), skipping labels holding a character not to include. Rodeo's reading: doubled letters step AA→BB→CC as VW's rule reads (mixed letters carry, AZ→BA); the bubble is 10 mm across on paper; polyline grid lines take corner vertices only (no arc vertices); the label isn't checked against grid lines already in the drawing, which are plain objects grouped, so Duplicate copies only the last one the tool drew this session; no styles, grid sequences, elbows, Grid Line Instances, IFC export or the Building Shell copy of the tool; Grid - Rectangular and Grid - Polar have no help page found and aren't done
- [x] Dims/Notes: Redline — Oval and Rectangular (a drag or two clicks), Regular Polygon (corners; a click on the first, a double-click or Enter closes it) and Freehand Polygon (a press dragged round the change), in Redline Style Cloud, Radius, Radius Dashed, Simple or Simple Dashed, red and unfilled; the Place Redline dialog then takes the Redline Notation, Authorized By and Assigned To, and a leader runs from the outline to a tag up and out past its top corner (Tag Alignment Auto/Left/Right) with the date and, as Show Authorizer and Show Assignee say, "Auth By:" and "Assigned To:" over a line of the Tag Length, the notation wrapped under it, at the Text Size. Rodeo's reading: Auto puts the tag to the right; the dashed styles take the drawing's first dashed line type, and stay solid in a drawing that has none; grouped plain objects in the Notes-Redlines class, named "Redline 3 (date made)", so there's no editing a redline afterwards in Object Info and no Redline Manager
- [x] Text > Redlines — Pick up Redline (the selected open ones turn yellow and show today's date), Restore Redline (open and red again, with the date they were made), Show or Hide Redlines (the Notes-Redlines class, which a new redline turns back on) and Create Redline Status WS (the "Redlines Status Report" worksheet made afresh and opened, Open and Closed Redlines with each one's design layer, date and comment, hidden ones too); Pick up and Restore are on the right-click menu as well. Rodeo's reading: these know the Redline tool's own groups by their names, so redlines drawn before this and Vectorworks' Redline objects in an opened drawing aren't picked up or listed; a redline's state is its pen, so a redline recoloured by hand reads as closed; there's no Picked Up checkbox in Object Info, the date picked up isn't kept once restored, and it's in the browser's date format; a worksheet can only be made by copying one, so a drawing with none shows the report in a dialog instead, and says so
- [x] Dims/Notes: General Notes — a click places a block's top left, then the notes are typed one a line (the last ones offered again); Preferences: Title (underlined), General Notes Display (1, 1., A, A., a, a.), Prefix, Suffix, Overall Width, Note Text Indent (the marks hang left of the wrapped text), Line Spacing (blank lines between notes), # of Columns, Gutter Width, Break Note Text Across Columns, Body Text Alignment Left/Center/Right, Title and Body Text Size, sizes on paper. Rodeo's reading: text is wrapped by an estimate of its width, not the font's; no notes database (Notes Manager, Database Notes), text styles, Note Spacing in other units or editing the block afterwards; grouped plain objects
- [x] Dims/Notes: North Arrow (a needle in a circle with an N, pointing up on a click or at the pointer when dragged), Scale Bar (four segments of a round length for the layer's scale, alternately filled, labelled in the drawing's units, feet steps for imperial drawings), Data Tag (press on an object and drag: a leader to a box with its name, class, area or length) and Graphic Legend (a swatch per class used on the active layer and not hidden, drawn in its class's attributes, with its name); grouped plain objects
- [x] Dims/Notes: Symmetry Label (a drag or two clicks: the axis crossed near each end by two short bars, as ISO 128 marks symmetry; the bars' length on paper in Preferences, a quarter of it apart) and Center Mark (a click on a circle, oval, rectangle or rounded rectangle crosses its centre with two lines along its sides reaching past it by a paper distance set in Preferences, previewed under the pointer; Option/Alt on a rounded rectangle marks its nearest corner's centre); grouped plain lines in the drawing's attributes, not Vectorworks's dimension-standard objects, so the axis isn't a centre line type (blank drawings have no line types) and marks don't follow their objects; arcs and 3D faces aren't marked
- [x] Dims/Notes: the ten Welding Sym- tools, Fillet, Flange, Groove, Misc and Slot-Plug each in AWS (A2.4) and ISO (2553): click the joint, then where the arrow meets the reference line, which runs level away from the arrow. Fillet, Groove and Slot-Plug: Arrow Side, Other Side or Both Sides, each side's own data; AWS's Symmetrical (both sides the arrow side's); ISO's Configuration, Symmetrical or the arrow side below or above the continuous line with the other side on the dashed identification line. Fillet: Weld Size, Number of Welds (ISO), Length of Weld and Pitch/Spacing (AWS 1-3, ISO 10 x 30 (25)), Stagger (AWS moves the other side along, ISO draws its Z). Groove: Weld Symbol (<None>, Bevel, Flare - Bevel, Flare - V, Scarf, Square, J, U, V, Back or Backing Weld, Melt Through, Backing, Backing (Remove), Consumable Insert), Weld Size after the Depth of Bevel in brackets, Root Opening and Groove Angle beyond the symbol. Flange: Edge- or Corner-Flange, Significance Arrow or Other Side, Radius + Flange Size over Weld Size, Root Opening, Complete Joint Penetration's mark. Misc: Spot, Seam, Stud or Surfacing, Significance Arrow Side, Other Side or None (across the line); Weld Size, Length (seam), Spacing or Pitch and Number of Welds. Slot-Plug: Plug (Ø size) or Slot (width, length - spacing), Depth of Filling in the box, Angle of Countersink and Number of Welds beyond. Every side's Finish Contour (None, Flush or Flat, Convex, Concave; along a fillet's slope) with its Finish Method letter (C, G, H, M, R, or Unspecified); and Arrow, Reference with three Ref. Lines in a tail at the line's far end, Weld All Around and Field Weld with its four flag positions; Text size on paper, the symbol as high as its text. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it: the symbols' proportions and where each text goes are read from the help pages' pictures and the standards' symbols; a bare number for an angle gets a degree sign and a number of welds brackets where VW's pictures show them; the arrow's end and the tail's follow the clicks rather than Position fields; the reference line stays level (no Rotation field: Rotate turns it); grouped plain objects where Vectorworks makes plug-in objects, so the arrow segment's leader end and shoulder can't be dragged afterwards, and no text styles
- [x] Dims/Notes: Arc Length Dimension (click a circle or arc where the stretch starts and where it ends, then place a dimension arc about its centre, outside or inside; witness lines Perpendicular to Chord, along radii past a half turn, or Perpendicular to Tangent; Arc Indicator Visible or Invisible), Slope Dimension (a drag or two clicks along the slope: Rise over Run's triangle labelled with the base and rise, its Scale Factor; Ratio, Percent or Degrees over the line; Draw Roof Surface dashed in the drawing's first dashed line type, solid when it has none) and Feature Control Frame (a click places it, a press dragged turns it: fourteen geometric characteristics drawn as lines, circles and arrowheads, Composite Box, Tolerance with Show Diameter Symbol and a Material Condition, Statistical Tolerance, Projected Tolerance Zone with Zone Height in or under the frame, Primary/Secondary/Tertiary Datum References with their material conditions; text size in Preferences); grouped plain lines, arcs and text, not Vectorworks's plug-in or dimension objects, so they don't follow what they measure. Vectorworks's help shows the slope styles and the frame's symbols only as images: Rodeo's four styles and its M, L and S modifiers are its own reading of them, and its frame has one tolerance area (no Tolerance Area #2), no Text Style choice and no leader
- [x] Dims/Notes: Dimension Tape (click its start, click its end, Shift in 15° steps, or drag between them; then click where it lies, parallel to them: Dist. between Hash Marks, Number of subdivisions, Height of hash marker and of subdivision marker, Number in negative direction, Number placement above or below a level tape and right or left of an upright one, Mark from center with the 0 moved along it by an offset; numbers in the drawing's units, read along the tape the right way up, their size on paper in Preferences; 2' between hash marks in a drawing in feet or inches, as Vectorworks' example has them, 1 m otherwise) and Geom Dim and Tol Note (a click places it at its Rotation; a press dragged from where its arrow points to the shoulder adds a leader with an arrowhead, its shoulder off the note's Left or Right at the Shoulder Length: Note Type Round Hole, Square Hole, Counterbore, Countersink, Conical Taper, Flat Taper or Depth, their symbols and the diameter sign drawn as lines; Hole Size/Taper and Depth/Angle each with a None, Single, Double, Limit (1) or Limit (2) tolerance from its Top and Bottom; a depth of 0 or less left out; Leader and Trailer text; Precision to four places; decimal inches without a leading zero in a drawing in feet or inches, as ASME writes them). Grouped plain lines and text, not Vectorworks's plug-in objects. Rodeo's reading: a tape's third click sets where it lies (Vectorworks' help says only that it "establishes the position"), its numbers can crowd when the spacing is short for them, and its specific markers (added from Vectorworks' context menu) aren't offered; a note's text size is on paper rather than a Text Style, its leader is drawn when placed rather than by a control point, a stacked Double tolerance shows Top as + and Bottom taken away (Vectorworks' picture shows both as +.002), and its shoulder length is on paper
- [x] Detailing: Hole - Drilled (seen from the top, or cut through its axis from the side; through, or blind ending in the drill's point at the angle in Preferences; plain, counterbored or countersunk), Hole - Tapped (Metric) and Hole - Tapped (Inch) (ISO coarse M2–M24 and UNC #4–1" threads with their tap drills; drawn as ISO 6410 shows threads: from the top the tap drill's circle and three quarters of the thread's, cut through the thread's lines beside the drill's to the thread depth, closed across there; through or blind) and Slot (a click at each end, then one for its width; half-round ends; a centre line if Preferences say so); a click places a hole, a press dragged turns it; centre lines reach 3 mm on paper past what they cross; grouped plain objects in the drawing's attributes, not plug-in objects, so no custom thread sizes, Detailed thread style, 3D holes to subtract from solids, or editing in Object Info afterwards, and threads aren't thinner lines
- [x] Detailing: Hole Pattern (round, tapped or slotted holes in rows and columns or round a bolt circle, the first at the top, centred on the click, counterbored if Preferences say so, centre lines along the rows and columns or round the circle and out across each hole; at most 400 holes; seen from the top only), Screw Threads (an external thread from the side, ISO metric proportions: simplified with its root lines as ISO 6410 draws them, schematic with a line at each crest and root, or detailed as its profile; chamfered or flat end; the shank past the thread at its full or pitch diameter) and Keyway (a shaft or a bore cut across with its keyway, the key from DIN 6885-1 or ASME B17.1 for the diameter, square or rectangular, or a custom size seated half in each); a click places them, a press dragged turns them; grouped plain objects, not plug-in objects, so no Woodruff keys, thread runout, shoulder fillets, dashed thread lines or 3D
- [x] Machine Components (a tool set of its own, as in Vectorworks): Spur Gear (a click places it, a press dragged turns it; Front View with involute teeth (standard full depth: addendum one module, dedendum 1.25, the first tooth up) or Simplified (outside and pitch circles), or Side View cut through its axis: hub standing out on both sides or one, web recess, rim and root lines; Pitch Diameter, Number of Teeth, Pressure Angle 14.5°, 20° or 25°, Face Width, Draw Recess with Web Thickness and Recess Diameter, Draw Hub with its diameter and projection, Draw Bore with a Square, Rectangular (ASME B17.1 for the bore) or custom Keyway, web holes round a circle, Show Center Lines with the pitch circle; module, diametral pitch, outside and root diameters shown for reference) and Spur Gear Rack (Front View with all teeth or two at each end and the tips' and roots' lines between, Draw Fillet at the roots, Top View, Side View; Module or Diametral Pitch, Pressure Angle, Number of Teeth, Thickness, Face Width, Show Pitch Line); grouped plain objects, not plug-in objects: no undercut on few teeth, no hub or recess corner and fillet radii, no spoked webs, no helix or 3D gears
- [x] Machine Components: Sprocket (a click places it, a press dragged turns it; Front View with its teeth in the ISO 606 tooth form (each size midway between the standard's least and most), a seat to the right, or Side View cut through its axis with a row of teeth for each strand; Std. Chain No. ANSI 25 to 160 or ISO 06B to 24B, Number of Teeth, Number of Rows, Use Standard Width and Spacing or set them, and the gear's recess, hub, bore and keyway, web holes and Draw Center Lines; the pitch diameter shown for reference), Roller Chain - Linear (a click places the run starting there, a press dragged turns it; Std. Chain No., No. of Links, Start with Pin Link) and Roller Chain - Circular (round a sprocket's pitch circle about the click: Standard Chain No., No. of Teeth (Sprocket), Start Angle, Number of Links, Clockwise or Counterclockwise, Start with Pin Link, Place Locus at Center); chains seen from the side as waisted link plates (pin links' outer plates over roller links' inner ones) and pins; grouped plain objects, not plug-in objects: chain sizes from catalogue tables, no offset (half) links within a run (Roller Chain - Offset Link draws one on its own), a circular chain goes round once at most
- [x] Machine Components: Ball Bearing, Roller Bearing and Needle Bearing (a click places one centred on it, a press dragged turns it; Front View down the axis, the balls or rollers shown only between the rings' edges, or Side View cut through the axis; Bearing Type: ball Single Row Radial or Angular, Double Row Radial or Angular (back to back, contact lines drawn); roller Single Row Separable, Single Row, Single Row Self Aligning, Double Row, Double Row Self-Aligning (barrel rollers in a spherical outer ring); needle Machined Ring or Drawn Cup (the types are only pictured in VW's help, so these are Rodeo's reading); Inside and Outside Diameter, Width, Number of Rows and Show Inner Ring for needles, Show Detail (off: ISO 8826-1's simplified outline with an upright cross), Show Center Line); grouped plain objects: rings, grooves, ribs and rolling elements proportioned from the three sizes rather than a maker's catalogue, no cage drawn, no 3D versions (VW's Section and Highlight Rollers are 3D-only)
- [x] Machine Components: Tapered Roller Bearing (Single Row, or Double Row as one outer ring over two inner rings back to back; Inside and Outside Diameter, Width of Inner Ring, Width of Outer Ring, Space Between Rows, Inner Ring Projection past the outer ring at the rollers' large ends, Show Retainers (the cage's side rings in section), Show Detail, Show Center Line; the front view looks at the rollers' large ends) and Thrust Bearing (Single Ball, Double Ball (a shaft washer between two rows), Cylindrical Roller; Raceway Style Grooved or Flat; Inside and Outside Diameter, Thickness, Show Retainer(s), Show Detail, Show Center Line); placed like the other bearings, front or side (keys 1, 2); VW pictures more types than its text names, so only the named ones are here; proportions from the sizes, no 3D versions
- [x] Machine Components: Bearing Lock Nut (Size KM0 to KM20 per DIN 981 catalogue tables, front or side view (keys 1, 2), Show Washer (the MB tab washer's outer tabs round the nut, one bent into each slot; the side view, a section, has none, as VW's help says), Show Center Line; the front view on the chamfered face with its four slots and the thread drawn as ISO 6410 shows a tapped hole); no 3D version
- [x] Machine Components: Pulley (Outside Diameter, Groove Depth, Groove Width, Groove Angle, Groove Radius (0 for a sharp bottom; a round belt's groove is a small angle with the radius half the width), 1 to 10 grooves with Dist. to First Groove and Distance Between Grooves, Face Width for one groove, Draw Recess/Hub/Bore with the gear's settings, an ASME or custom keyway, Web Configuration Plain, Holes (Percent), Holes (Diameter) or Spokes with Number, Size, Center Distance and Hole Diameter, Show Center Lines, Show Root Diameter (front view)); front or side (keys 1, 2); the side view draws a spoked web as solid; no 3D version
- [x] Machine Components: Key (Nom. Size from DIN 6885-1's 16 metric keys, their long edges chamfered by its smallest chamfer, or ASME B17.1's square and rectangular inch keys, or Custom Width and Height; Length) and Woodruff Key (Nom. Size from DIN 6888's 24 keys or ASME B17.2's 41 full-radius keys, the keyseat's depths in the shaft and hub shown in Preferences); front, top or side (keys 1 to 3); the Standard is picked with the size (one list), ASME's flat-bottom Woodruff keys aren't in, and four DIN 6888 sizes' keyseat depths follow the table's t1 + t2 = h + 0.2; no 3D versions
- [x] Machine Components: Compression Spring 1 (Type of Ends Plain, Plain and Ground, Squared (Closed) or Squared and Ground; Wire Diameter, Outside Diameter, Solid Height), Compression Spring 2 (the same with Mean Diameter and Number of Active Coils), Conical Compression Spring (Wire Diameter, OD - Small End, OD - Large End, No. of Active Coils; squared and ground ends) and Die Spring (Fits into Hole, Fits over Shaft, Wire Width, Wire Height, Solid Height; rectangular wire, closed and ground ends); each with Working Length, Spiral Left or Right Hand and Draw Center Line, placed by its end with the axis running right (a press dragged turns it); Display on keys 1 to 5: Solid - Front Coils Only and Solid - Rear Coils Only (named in VW's help), Front and Rear Coils, Section and Simplified (ISO 2162-1) are Rodeo's; each half coil is a straight band of wire, coils behind others cut away where hidden; the working length is set in Preferences, not by a control point; no 3D versions
- [x] Machine Components: Extension Spring - Front (Outside Diameter, Wire Diameter, Free Length, Working Length, End Style, Hook Length, Spiral, View, Set Free Length to Working Length, Show All Coils, Draw Center Line; the compression springs' five displays on keys 1 to 5), close wound at the free length and spread over the working length, a loop or hook at each end facing or edgewise, Show All Coils off drawing three coils at each end with lines across the rest; Extension Spring - End (Wire Diameter, Outside Diameter, Ends at Right Angles, Draw Center Lines), seen down the axis centred on the click; Belleville Spring (Inside Diameter, Outside Diameter, Thickness, Height, Height Is Inside or Outside, Number of Springs, Configuration, Number Down, Number Up, Draw First Spring Up, Draw Center Line), a stack sectioned through its axis (key 1) or one seen down it (key 2). VW shows End Style, View and Configuration's choices only as pictures, so Full Loop / Open Hook, Loops Facing / Edgewise, All Up / All Down and Section / Front are Rodeo's names (Alternate Down and Up is VW's); Height Is Inside read as the cone's rise; the loops are outlined, not cut, in Section; the Belleville section isn't hatched; Extension Spring - End has no Spiral (it doesn't show from the end); no 3D versions
- [x] Machine Components: Pillow Block (Size, Use Custom Dimensions, Bore Size, Center Distance, Housing Width, Base Length, Base Thickness, Hole Centers, Slot Width, Slot Length, Base Width, Collar Width, Collar Diameter, Set Screw Size, Show Set Screws, Show Grease Fitting, Show Center Lines and Hidden Lines; Front, Side and Top on keys 1 to 3), Flanged Bearing - 4 Hole (Nominal Size, Use Custom Dimensions, Bore Size, Collar Diameter, Housing Diameter, Base Size (Sq.), Hole Centers (Sq.), Hole Size, Base Thickness, Housing Thickness, Overall Thickness, Set Screw Size and the three Show options; Front and Side) and Flanged Bearing - 2 Hole (the same with Base Width and Base Length for the oval flange); sizes are FYH's UCP2, UCF2 and UCFL2 units with UC2 inserts, UC204 to UC215 (catalogue 3330), where VW's own size lists aren't published; Collar Diameter, Housing Diameter, the pillow block's Housing Width and its housing's round top aren't in the catalogue and are Rodeo's estimates; the View choices show only as pictures in VW's help, so Front/Side/Top are Rodeo's names; hidden lines are dashed in the drawing's first dashed line type, or cut into dashes in a drawing without one; one set screw is drawn, facing the viewer; no 3D versions
- [x] Machine Components: Roller Chain - Offset Link (Standard Chain No. ANSI 25 to 160 or ISO 06B to 24B, Draw Center Lines; the roller end on the click and the pin end a pitch to the right, a press dragged turns it), from the side (key 1: the plate, the bushing's end, the pin with its cotter) or above (key 2: the plates cranked from the inner link's width to the outer's, the roller and the pin); Show Pins is 3D only in VW, the views and Draw Center Lines are Rodeo's, and the plate thickness is worked out from the chain's strand spacing; no 3D version
- [x] Machine Components: Bevel Gears (Value to Use Diametral Pitch or Module, Shaft Angle, Face Width, and for the gear and the pinion No. of Teeth, Mounting Distance, Hub Diameter, Hub Projection, Bore Diameter, Key Type None, Square or Rectangular (ASME B17.1 for the bore) or Custom with Kwy Width and Kwy Height, Recess and Fillet Radius; Show Pinion at Left, Draw Center Lines; the pitch diameters and cone distance shown for reference) with the pitch apex on the click, a press dragged turns it: Side (Section) (key 1) cuts the pair through their axes, the gear's upright below the apex and the pinion's turned the shaft angle from it, the pitch cones drawn to their heels; Front (key 2) looks down the gear's axis (the pinion's when it's alone) at its heel and toe tips, toe roots, recess and keyed bore. Straight full-depth teeth (addendum a module, dedendum 1.25) tapering to the apex; Configuration (Gear and Pinion, Gear Only, Pinion Only, Miter Gears) is Rodeo's reading of VW's pictured choices; teeth aren't drawn one by one in either view; Show Teeth and Spiral Angle are 3D only in VW; no 3D versions
- [x] Machine Components: Worm (Pitch Diameter, Axial Pitch, Pressure Angle (deg.) 14.5° to 30°, Number of Starts Single to Quad, Face Width, Draw Hub(s), Hub Diameter, Hub Projection (Left/Right) (negative sets the hub into the face), Draw Bore, Bore Diameter, Keyway None, Square or Rectangular (ASME B17.1) or Custom with Keyway Width/Height, Show Center Lines; module, lead, outside and root diameters and lead angle shown for reference), centred on the click with its axis running right, a press dragged turns it; View Front (the threads' outline with each crest's and root's helix wound round the near side), Right Side, Left Side, Side - Section (outside and root circles) and Front - Section (the thread's trapezoids above and below, half a lead apart) on keys 1 to 5, Tooth Profile Detailed or Schematic (the outside with root lines) on keys 6 and 7. Worm Gear (Circular Pitch, Number of Teeth, Pressure Angle (deg.), Number of Starts (Worm), Pitch Dia (Worm), Face Width, Draw Recess with Web Thickness, Recess Diameter, Recess Corner Radius and Recess Fillet Radius, Draw Hub with Hub Diameter, Hub Projection (Left/Right), Hub Corner Radius and Hub Fillet Radius, Draw Bore, Bore Diameter and Keyway, Web Configuration Plain, Holes (Percent), Holes (Diameter) or Spokes with Number, Size, Center Distance and Hole Diameter, Show Center Lines; Pitch Diameter (Ref.), throat and outside diameters and centre distance shown for reference): View Front, Side - Section (the rim hollowed to the throat's arc about the worm's axis, with the root and pitch arcs), Side - Solid, Side - Solid with Hidden Lines and Back, Detailed (involute teeth) or Schematic. The choices' names come from the captions of VW's help pictures. Rodeo's own reading: the threads are right hand, the gear's teeth are drawn at the throat in the front and back views, its outside diameter follows the usual rule (throat plus 0.4775 of the pitch for one or two starts, 0.3183 for three or four), and the worm's Side - Section cuts across the threads as circles; spoked webs draw as solid in the side views; no 3D versions
- [x] Machine Components: Shaft, built in its Configuration dialog (a preview with the current segment in red, < Add / Delete / Add > and < Previous / Next >, up to 50 segments): Segment Type Straight, Tapered or Threaded and Segment Length; Dimensions with each end's Diameter and Style Plain, Chamfer, Flare, Fillet or Radius (Chamfer Length, Chamfer Angle, Fillet Radius); a threaded segment's Thread Type Inch Series (UNC and UNF #4 to 2"), Metric Series (ISO 261 coarse and fine), Custom (Outside Diameter, Pitch, Included Angle, top and bottom Flatness, Number of Starts), Acme (29° or 30° metric trapezoidal) or Bearing Locknut (KM0 to KM20, Thread Relief None, Left or Right), Thread Spiral, and Plain or Chamfered ends; on a straight segment Keyway (Measure From, Distance, Key Standard Imperial or Metric, Position Front, Top, Bottom or Back, a Standard Square or Rectangular keyway sized for the diameter by ASME B17.1 or DIN 6885 with its Length, or a Woodruff Keyway by Nom. Size), Groove (Round, Rectangular or V-Groove; Width, Depth) and Wrench Flats (Single or Double; Length, Width Across Flats); for the whole shaft Show Threads As Simplified (ASME), Simplified (ISO), Schematic or Detailed, Show Center Line and Add Center Drill (Plain or Bell, DIN 332 sizes 1 to 10 mm, Left, Right or Both). The left end goes on the click with the axis running right, a press dragged turns it. Rodeo's reading: Chamfer and Flare both bevel the end's corner and Fillet and Radius both round it (concave or convex as the step makes it), VW's Special Threads dialog is folded into Thread Type, pipe threads and Thread Taper aren't in, a thread relief is two pitches long at the root, and centre drills follow DIN 332 with a pilot as deep as it is wide; one side view; no 3D version
- [x] Machine Components, in 3D: Spur Gear, Spur Gear Rack, Sprocket, Roller Chain - Linear, - Circular and - Offset Link, Pulley, Bevel Gears, Worm, Worm Gear, Key, Woodruff Key, Shaft, Ball, Roller, Needle, Tapered Roller and Thrust Bearing, Bearing Lock Nut, Pillow Block, Flanged Bearing - 2 Hole and - 4 Hole, Compression Spring 1 and 2, Conical Comp Spring, Die Spring, Extension Spring, Torsion Spring and Belleville Spring - 3D: the 2D tool's Preferences and sizes, a click placing it (a press dragged turns it) as a group of one mesh and the 2D view it draws from above, as Fasteners' 3D tools do. Wheels, bearings, nuts and Belleville stacks stand on the layer axis up, with the Front view for plan; flanged bearings stand on the flange; a pillow block stands on its base with the Top view, its shaft axis level; shafts, worms and springs lie along x with the view they run in, and chains lie flat with their pins upright; racks and keys lie with their Top view; bevel gears lie as their Side (Section) view cuts them, its plane level. Spur gears and sprockets have their real tooth outline; a multi-row sprocket has a row of teeth for each strand; a worm's thread is a real helix for each start; springs are the wire swept along their helix, conical and rectangular wire included, ground ends cut flat. Rodeo's reading, since the help doesn't show these objects: each part's orientation, and that a mesh is close enough to Vectorworks' plug-in object. Left out: bevel gears have no teeth (plain cones, so Show Teeth and Spiral Angle do nothing); a worm gear's teeth run straight across a rim that isn't hollowed; keyways, web holes, spokes, set screws, grease fittings, a lock nut's slots and a shaft's threads, keyways and flats aren't modelled, so bores are plain; a bearing's balls and rollers are rings with no cage; an offset link's plates have square ends; an extension spring's end wire is a straight run to its loop, and a torsion leg that crosses the coils bends sharply. A mesh isn't editable from its Preferences after placing. Not opened in Vectorworks. Tests: `machineSolids.check.ts` (volumes, each solid wound outward and on the layer); all 29 placed on a blank drawing in Playwright, seen in 3D and plan, reopened
- [ ] Machine Components still missing: Hub - 3D (it has no page in the 2025 help, and Rodeo has no 2D Hub to build it from)
- [x] Detailing's Bolt and Nut - inch and - mm: a click places a bolt through a joint (a press dragged turns it), the head's bearing face on the click, in Front, Side, Top or Bottom on keys 1-4, as a group of the view and a solid standing head up from the click with the rest below the layer, as Vectorworks' is a hybrid. Bolt Type and Nut Type Hex or Square (inch only; the mm tool's are hex), Washer Type Narrow, Regular or Wide, Bolt Size, Bearing Length, Bolt Length, Thread Style Simplified, Schematic or Detailed, Top Washer, Top Lock Washer, Bottom Washer, Bottom Lock Washer, Nut, Use Standard Lengths, Adjust Bolt Length (the joint, its washers and nut and two threads past it), Show Center Line, off until Preferences as on the 3D tools. Sizes are the Fastener tool's standards: ASME B18.2.1 and B18.2.2, B18.22.1 Type B washers and B18.21.1 lock washers, or ISO 4014, ISO 4032, ISO 7092, 7089 and 7093-1 and ASME B18.21.2M lock washers. Which standards are Rodeo's reading, since the help names none; the mm tool's Narrow, Regular and Wide map to ISO's Small, Normal and Large. No control points to drag the lengths after placing; the solid has no threads and a lock washer is a plain ring
- [x] Fasteners tool set, in 3D: Clevis Pin (Inch), (Metric) and (DIN), Dowel Pin (Inch), Parallel Pin (DIN), Taper Pin (Inch) and (DIN), Large and Small Rivet (Inch), Rivet (DIN), Tubular Rivet (DIN), Acorn Nut (Inch), Hex Cap Nut (DIN) and Knurled Thumb Nut (DIN) and (Inch) - 3D: the 2D tool's Preferences and sizes, a click placing it (a press dragged turns it) as a group of one mesh and the outline it draws from above, since Rodeo's plan shows no meshes and its 3D view no plan drawings. Pins stand on the layer, large end up; rivets and nuts stand on the head's or nut's bearing face, so a rivet's shank runs below the layer, as in their 2D Front. Round parts are turned from the 2D side view in 48 facets, hex bodies cut from that with their chamfers. Rodeo's reading, since the help doesn't show these objects: the standing direction, and that a mesh is close enough to Vectorworks' plug-in object. Not modelled: threads, knurling, a clevis pin's split pin hole, and the threaded holes of acorn and hex cap nuts, which are closed underneath. Preferences that only affect 2D (Show Center Line) still appear, and a Show Center Line adds its lines to the plan
- [x] Fasteners tool set, in 3D, the rest: Cotter Pin (Inch), Wing Nut (DIN) and Type A to D (Inch), Retaining Ring (ASME) and (DIN), Retaining Washer (DIN), Eye, Swing, Swing Eye, T, U and J Bolts, Thumb Screw (Inch), Carriage Bolt, Lag Screw, Wood Screw and Sheet Metal Screw - 3D, each from its 2D tool as the others are: its Preferences and sizes, a click (a press dragged turns it) placing a group of one mesh and its top view (a ring's front). A cotter pin lies on its side, its wire flat instead of half-round. Wing nuts have a turned boss with the thread's hole through it and flat wings; a die cast wing's hollow isn't cut, and a stamped nut's wings are strips bent up from a ring. Rings and washers are flat with their lugs' holes through them. An eye bolt's forged eye is a ring of round section and a bent one the rod itself; swing, swing eye and thumb heads are flat plates as thick as the 2D side view; a J-bolt's nut and washers are on it as solid bodies. Carriage bolts' necks are cut square, lag screws' heads cut to their flats. Not modelled: threads (a thread drawn as teeth turns into rings, not a helix), slots and recesses in screw heads, a thumb screw's dished and rounded head edges. Not opened in Vectorworks.
- [x] Detailing: Hole - Drilled, Hole - Tapped (Metric) and Hole - Tapped (Inch) - 3D: the 2D tool's Preferences, Styles and Views, a click placing a real sweep of the hole, mouth at the layer's level and running down, for Modify > Subtract Solids to take out of a solid as the help says, beside (not grouped with) the 2D drawing for plan: Subtract Solids takes only what's selected at the top, so a group couldn't go, and selecting the plan drawing with it does no harm. The plan drawing stays when the hole's been subtracted, and moves apart from the sweep if only one is moved; a sweep alone draws in plan as one line, as every sweep does in Rodeo. A tapped hole is its tap drill's hole with no thread cut. The hole has to be moved in 3D to the top of a solid that isn't at the layer's level. Rodeo's reading, since the help doesn't show this object: that the mouth goes on the layer's level.
- [x] Fasteners tool set: the pins, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) in a view on keys 1 to 5, side views from the pin's left end with its axis running right. Clevis Pin (Inch) (Size 3/16 to 1 in per ASME B18.8.1, Use Standard Length (ASME's length under the head), Length, Head Style Chamfered or Rounded, Show Center Line), Clevis Pin (Metric) and Clevis Pin (DIN) (Size 3 to 100 mm per ISO 2341 / DIN EN 22341, Pin Type Type A/B or Form A/B without or with split pin holes, Use Standard Length Increments, Length, Head Style Chamfered, Rounded or Headless (ISO 2340, a hole at each end)), each in Top (down the hole), Side, Head End and Body End; Cotter Pin (Inch) (Cotter Pin Type Square Cut or Hammer Lock, Nominal Size 1/32 to 3/4 in per ASME B18.8.1, Grip Length, Length, Bend Angle (Top) and (Bottom)) in Front, Left Side, Right Side, Top and Bottom; Dowel Pin (Inch) (Dowel Pin Type ANSI Hardened Ground Machine, Hardened Ground Production or Unhardened Ground, Nominal Size 1/16 to 1 in, Length, Show Center Line) in Side, Left End and Right End; Parallel Pin (DIN) (Size 0.6 to 50 mm per DIN 7 / ISO 2338, Pin Type Form A or B, Use Standard Length Increments, Length) in Top View and End View; Taper Pin (Inch) (Size 7/0 to 14 per ASME B18.8.2, 1/4 in per foot, Use Standard Length Range, Length) in Side, Left End and Right End; Taper Pin (DIN) (Size 0.6 to 50 mm per DIN 1 / ISO 2339, 1:50, Use Standard Length Range and Increments) in Side View and End View. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it: a cotter pin's eye is twice its head diameter long and the bends show from the front, top and bottom only, a hammer lock's extended prong turns down at its end, production dowel pins' corners are rounded and unhardened ones' chamfered a tenth of the diameter, DIN 7 form A's dome is a sphere as wide as the pin and its chamfers run at 15°, metric clevis pins' end chamfer is half ISO's c max at 45°, inch taper pins' ends are crowned with spheres as wide as each end and Use Standard Length Range keeps them to the 1/4 to 8 in they're sold in (each size's range isn't published freely), ISO 2339's length ranges are the usual supplier tables'; no 3D versions
- [x] Fasteners tool set: the rivets, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) in a view on keys 1 and 2. Large Rivet (Inch) (Diameter 1/2 to 1-3/4 in per ASME B18.1.2, Length under the head or overall for countersunk heads, Head Type #1 Button, High Button, Cone, Pan, Flat Countersunk or Oval Countersunk, Head Type #2 None or any of them, Show Center Line), Small Rivet (Inch) (Diameter 1/16 to 7/16 in per ASME B18.1.1, heads Button, Flat, Pan, Truss or Flat Countersunk) and Rivet (DIN) (Diameter 1 to 8 mm, heads Round per DIN 660, Flat Round per DIN 674, Mushroom per DIN 662 and Flat Countersunk per DIN 661, a size its head's standard doesn't cover refused), each in Front (head up) and Top; Tubular Rivet (DIN) (Size per DIN 7340 tube and wall, Use Standard Length Increments, Length, Form A or B, Show Uncompressed Length, Show Center Line) in Side and Top. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it: inch heads take the standards' proportions of the diameter (the tables' rounding aside), a small pan head's sides draft from its rim to its corner radius, a high button head's sides are arcs tangent to its top radius, a set tubular rivet's far flange takes the flange's volume as tube from its length and DIN 7340 forms A and B differ in square or rounded flanges, Use Standard Length Increments uses ISO's preferred lengths (DIN 7340's own list per size isn't published freely); no 3D versions
- [x] Fasteners tool set: the acorn, hex cap and knurled thumb nuts, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) crown or head up on its bearing face, in a view on keys 1 to 3. Acorn Nut (Inch) (Size #6 to 1-1/4 in per SAE J483, Style Low Crown or High Crown, Show Center Line) and Hex Cap Nut (DIN) (Size M4 to M24 per DIN 1587, high type) in Front (across the corners), Side (across the flats) and Top; Knurled Thumb Nut (DIN) (Size M1 to M10 per DIN 466, high type, Show Detail for the knurl, Show Center Line) and Knurled Thumb Nut (Inch) (#4-40 to 1/2-20, UNC and UNF) in Front and Top. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it: the hex faces' chamfered ends are arcs dropping the corners as a 30° chamfer to 0.95 of the flats would, a DIN 1587 dome is a hemisphere on a band, inch knurled thumb nuts (which no free standard tabulates) take DIN 466's sizes at the same diameter and their Size names the thread series where VW's help mentions a style, the knurl's ridges are 0.8 mm apart round the rim; no 3D versions
- [x] Fasteners tool set: the wing nuts, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) wings up on its bearing face, in Front, Side or Top on keys 1 to 3, with Show Center Line. Wing Nut (DIN) (Size M4 to M24 per DIN 315, rounded wings), Wing Nut - Type A (Inch) (#3 to 3/4 in per ASME B18.6.9, each size in the light, regular or heavy series it comes in), Wing Nut - Type B (Inch) (Style 1 moderate or Style 2 high wings, #5 to 3/4 in per ANSI B18.17), Wing Nut - Type C (Inch) (die cast, hollow wings, Style 1 regular, 2 low, 3 high) and Wing Nut - Type D (Inch) (stamped strip, Style 1 regular, 2 low wings, 3 large base).
