Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it: the wings' lobed outline (a tip round of under half the wing's height on curves from the boss), DIN 315's gap between the wings taken as 0.7 of the boss, the thread drawn hidden in the boss, and Types C and D (whose ASME tables aren't published freely) scaled from Type B Style 1's sizes; no 3D versions
- [x] Fasteners tool set: the retaining rings and washers, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) centred on it with the gap down, in Front, Side-Section or Side-Solid on keys 1 to 3, with Place Locus at Center. Retaining Ring (ASME) (Type Basic Internal Ring (ANSI B27.7 3BM1, bores 8 to 250 mm), Basic External Ring (3AM1, shafts 4 to 100 mm), Heavy Duty External (B27.8M 3DM1, 10 to 50 mm), Basic E-Ring (3CM1, 1 to 25 mm), Reinforced E-Ring (3EM1, 4 to 15 mm) or C-Ring (3FM1, 3 to 55 mm); Size (mm)), Retaining Ring (DIN) (Type Bores, Regular or Heavy (DIN 472, 8 to 300 and 20 to 100 mm) or Shafts, Regular or Heavy (DIN 471, 3 to 300 and 15 to 100 mm); Size (mm)) and Retaining Washer (DIN) (Groove Size 0.8 to 30 mm per DIN 6799). B27.8M's types are sized from Rotor Clip's MSR, MRE and MC tables, the same rings. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it or the standard leaves it out: where the lugs and prongs sit round the gap, the lugs' rounding, the band's taper (DIN gives no narrowest section, so 0.58 of the widest), an E-ring's cut-outs and 3CM1's lower prongs 0.82 of its inside apart, a C-ring's tapered ends, DIN 6799's band (a tenth of the outside), and the side section marking only where the cut through the centre leaves the ring; another Type keeps the nearest size it comes in; no 3D versions
- [x] Fasteners tool set: the bolts, each placed by a click (a press dragged turns it) in Front, Side, Top or Bottom on keys 1 to 4 (a T-bolt in Front, Top and Bottom), on any UNC thread from 1/4 in or ISO coarse thread from M5, its Thread Style Simplified (ASME), Simplified (ISO), Schematic or Detailed, with Show Center Line; lengths left as shown follow a new Size. Eye Bolt (Type Plain, Shoulder or Bent; Eye Inside Dia, Length, Thread Length; inch sizes 1/4 to 2 in per ASME B18.15's forged eye bolts, Style A) stands its bearing face on the click; Swing Bolt (Head Width, Shoulder Length, Head Thickness, Length, Thread Length); Swing Eye Bolt (Eye Inside Dia, Outside Diameter, Length, Thread Length; metric sizes M5 to M36 per DIN 444 form B); T-Bolt (Head Size, Head Thickness, Length, Thread Length); U-Bolt (Inside Distance, Length from the inside of the bend, Thread Length) centred on its bend; J-Bolt (Length, Thread Length, Bend Radius, Anchor Length, Add Nut hex or square, Add Lock Washer, Add Washer narrow, regular or wide, Bearing Length kept as they change, Anchor to the right) hanging from its threaded end; Place Locus at Center on the eye, swing, swing eye and U-bolts. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it or no standard tabulates it: metric eye bolts take ASME's sizes interpolated by diameter and inch swing eye bolts DIN 444's, a bent eye's S-bend rises three rod radii past the loop, swing bolts start with a head twice the thread wide, as thick, its pin hole half its width and its centre 1.3 diameters up, T-bolts a square head twice the thread across and 0.7 of it high, threads ISO 4014's 2d + 6 mm (ASME's 2D + 1/4 in), J-bolt nuts and washers drawn in proportion to the thread (hex nuts 1.5 across the flats, square 1.625, both 0.875 high; washers 2, 2.5 or 3 across, 0.2 thick; lock washers 1.75 across, 0.25 thick), VW's one Add Lock Washer/Washer choice is two fields here and its control point to drag the nut is Bearing Length typed; no 3D versions
- [x] Fasteners tool set: Thumb Screw (Inch), ASME B18.17 (IFI 156): Screw Type A Regular (#6 to 3/8), A Heavy (#10 to 1/2), B Regular (#6 to 1/2) or B Heavy (#10 to 1/2) at the standard's head widths, heights, thicknesses, mid-sections and shoulders (mid-tolerance), Point Type Plain, Cup/Flat, Dog, Oval or Cone, Length under the head with Use Standard Length Increments (ASME B18.6.3's machine screw steps, kept within each size's lengths), Thread Style and Show Center Line; Front, Side and Top on keys 1 to 3, head up on its bearing face at the click, a press dragged turns it. Lock washers: ASME inch Regular, Heavy, Extra Duty, Hi-Collar (B18.21.1); ASME metric Regular and Heavy (B18.21.2M); DIN 127. Size names the thread (3/8-16 UNC, M8 × 1.25) and Show Center Line. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it: the Size list holds every size of the standard and one a type doesn't come in moves to its nearest (VW's follows the type), dimensions are the tables' largest (ISO and DIN flats nominal, washers' holes least and thickness mean), tapped holes the basic minor diameter with a 3/4 circle for the major, chamfers drop the corners to a circle 0.95 of the flats, square nuts show their corners from the front and the side as VW's pictures do, castle nuts always six slots (DIN puts eight above M39), and ASME metric nuts come washer faced only from M20 where B18.2.4 gives the face; ASME Square Flat and Heavy Flat (no such B18.2.2 table) and a Type A Regular washer aren't offered, and ISO, having no spring lock washer standard, takes B18.21.2M's; no 3D versions

Set Screw: Head Type Hex Socket, Spline Socket, Slotted or Square in ASME inch (B18.3 #0 to 2 in, spline to 7/8; B18.6.2 slotted #0 to 3/4 with its crowned end, square head #10 to 1-1/2 with its neck relief), Hex Socket in ASME metric (B18.3.6M, M1.6 to M24), Hex Socket or Slotted in ISO (4026 to 4029, M1.6 to M24; 4766 and 7434 to 7436, M1.2 to M12) and DIN (913 to 916, M1.4 to M24; 551, 553, 417 and 438, M1 to M12); Point Type as VW lists it per standard (ASME Cup/Flat, Full Dog (inch only), Half Dog, Oval, Cone; ISO Dog, Flat, Cup, Cone; DIN Flat, Cup, Cone, Half Dog) at the tables' sizes, cones 90° on long screws and 118° (ASME) or 120° (ISO, DIN) on short ones, short ISO and DIN screws with the shallower socket and short dog, and a point a size doesn't come with refused; Size, Thread Pitch UNC or UNF (inch), Length (overall, a square head's under it), Use Standard Length Increments (B18.3's inch steps, ISO 4026's lengths), Thread Style, Show Center Line. Shoulder Screw: Size the shoulder's diameter (ASME B18.3 1/8 to 2 in; B18.3.3M and ISO 7379 6.5 to 25 mm; DIN as ISO 7379, which DIN publishes as is), Length the shoulder's, Use Standard Length Increments (B18.3's steps, ISO 7379's lengths), Head Style Plain, Chamfered or Rounded (ISO), Draw Underhead Fillet (ASME, DIN), Thread Style, Show Center Line, with the thread's undercut where it meets the shoulder. Rodeo's reading where VW only pictures it or the tables leave it out: a headless end chamfered as deep as its thread, a short ASME inch screw's socket 0.55 of what its point leaves (B18.3 gives depths only from each size's shortest full-engagement length), ASME metric sockets B18.3.6M's key engagement by length, its oval point a 0.75 d radius and its cones 90° from the table's length a as the inch standard's (the source states it the other way round), DIN's L0 above M6 the ISO screw's (the nearest ISO size's for M14, M18 and M22) and DIN 915's short dog above M6 ISO 4028's, a spline socket six splines with roots 0.85 of its width, a square head's crowned top drawn as its chamfer, an ISO shoulder head's chamfer or radius B18.3.3M's c, and VW's single Cup/Flat drawn as a cup; no 3D versions
- [ ] Fasteners tool set: ASME metric Spline Socket set screws (no B18.3.6M spline sizes found), ASME inch Slotted Round and Slotted Fillister Head Cap Screws (no B18.6.2 table found), DIN's and ISO's reduced-shank flange bolts
- [x] Detailing: Stipple (dots in a dragged rectangle at a density per square centimetre on paper, even or thinning across it, the same rectangle giving the same dots; up to 4,000)
- [ ] Markers, data tags and legends as Vectorworks' plug-in objects that follow what they name: a section viewport made from a section line and numbers following the viewport's and its sheet's; tags updating as the tagged object changes; legends as the drawing's classes change

## Building Shell and other tool sets

- [x] Walls read: a wall's control line, thickness, bottom and top, and wall style, shown in Object Info's Wall section; data visualisations by object style take walls' styles
- [x] Wall tool (Building Shell): click the start, then each corner, a straight wall per click in a style the drawing has (a copy of one of its square-ended walls without doors or windows, stretched along the line), one undo step each
- [x] Plain walls: a drawing with no styled walls, a new blank one among them, also offers walls 4", 6", 8" and 1' thick and 8' high (100, 150, 200 and 300 mm and 2700 mm high in metric), for the Wall tool, Create Objects from Shapes, Create Walls from Spaces and a wall's Style in Object Info. They mitre, tee, join, change size, turn into rooms, save and reopen as a copied wall does. Each is Rodeo's own minimal record: 256 bytes where Vectorworks writes over 2,000, a white polygon in plan and a box of edges in its group in 3D, with no components, solid additions or aux data, and it has not been opened in Vectorworks. A wall holding a door isn't possible until a blank drawing has a door to copy
- [x] New record formats in any drawing: Tools > Records > New Record Format…, Import Shapefile's attributes, and the Space Data and Piping Run records work in a drawing with no record format to copy, a new blank one among them; the format starts from Rodeo's own record, as a plug-in format does, not yet opened in Vectorworks
- [x] Walls joined at their corners as the Wall tool draws them: each wall mitred to the one before it along the bisector of the corner, and the last to the first when a run closes on its start
- [x] Joining new walls to walls already drawn: the Wall tool starting or ending on a wall's end mitres the corner (L), and on a wall's side runs to its face, cut along it (T); the wall met isn't cut. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Wall Join tool (Building Shell): T Join runs a wall's end to another's face, cut along it; L Join takes an end of each wall to where their lines cross and mitres the corner; the part clicked stays where a wall crosses the other, and the joined walls follow each other afterwards. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Editing a wall in Object Info: its start and end, its length and its bottom, top and height, its drawing and 3D carried along (mitred and cut ends keep their shape), one undo step each
- [x] Splitting walls (Split tool): Point Split splits a straight wall where the point lies along its line, Line Split every wall the line crosses; the wall keeps the part from its start and a copy (unnamed) the rest, each square there, their far ends keeping their corners and cuts, the walls teeing into it staying with the part they meet; one undo step. Walls with doors, windows or symbols set in them, or drawn with more than their two ends, and a point where another wall meets the side, are refused. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Wall Join tool: X Join, for walls crossing between their ends: the first breaks where they cross and each part runs to the second's face toward it, cut along it, as a tee; one undo step. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] A wall's style and thickness in Object Info: Style offers each style and thickness the drawing has a plain wall in, and choosing one gives the wall that style in its place (a copy on its control line at its own heights, in its class), its mitred corners mitred again to the new faces, its ends cut along a face kept, and walls teeing into it slid onto its new faces, each part as far into it for its width as before; one undo step; the thickness follows the style, as in Vectorworks. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Editing walls with doors, windows and symbols set in them: they, their openings and breaks keep their place from the end that stays put, turning with the wall, and their height above its bottom as its top moves; an end can't pass through one, nor the top come down through one. Untested in Vectorworks
- [ ] Cleaning up the wall a tee meets (Vectorworks breaks its face lines across the joint and notches its components where the other's run in: 47 of the 2023 demo's 53 tees are saved so), and Vectorworks' own join records; a wall in a style the drawing has no plain wall in, and changing the style of one holding doors or windows; curved and round walls; the ends of a wall whose door or window reaches over them, and the heights of one sloping over its doors and windows; component styles
- [ ] Curtain wall
- [x] Slab tool (Building Shell): a rectangular slab between two corners in a style the drawing has (a copy of one of its rectangular slab plug-ins, stretched onto the rectangle), one undo step
- [x] AEC > Create Auto Hybrid and Modify > Edit Auto Hybrid: the selected 3D objects (extrudes, sweeps, meshes, solids, 3D polygons, 3D symbols and plug-in objects; walls and anything with no 3D faces are left out, and the message says how many) held in an auto hybrid in one undo step, which 3D views show as the objects are and Top/Plan as a plan worked out from them. The 2D Appearance dialog (asked first, and from Object Info's 2D Appearance… button) sets the cut plane's Elevation above the layer, Display Cut Plane and its Class (or <Contained Object Class(es)>), Fill (None, the contained objects' own or the class's) and Pen; Display Extents Below Cut Plane with an Infinite Range or a Finite Depth, its Class and Pen, and Dashed Hidden Line with a Dash Style; Display Extents Above Cut Plane with an Infinite Range or a Finite Height, its Class and Pen, and Include Hidden Lines; Show as Reflected Ceiling Plan; Smoothing Angle; and Save Current Settings as Defaults (kept in the browser). The cut shows each object's outline where the plane goes through it, filled round any holes; below and above show the edges of what lies there (rims, folds sharper than the smoothing angle, and where faces turn away from the eye), each split into the parts in sight and those hidden by nearer faces or by the cut, looked at from above or, for a reflected ceiling plan, from below. Hidden lines below are drawn in the dash style when asked for, and those above when Include Hidden Lines is on; an edge right over another in plan is drawn once. Object Info's Cut Plane Elevation draws the plan again. Edit Auto Hybrid (or a double-click, or right-click Edit) opens its objects as a group, and Exit Group makes it again with the same 2D Appearance. Rodeo's reading: it's a Rodeo plug-in object, not Vectorworks' own auto hybrid; below and above are drawn as edges only, so their Fill isn't offered; Generate Intersecting Lines, 3D Conversion Resolution, a story-relative elevation and the design layer cut plane, Dash Shade, and 2D Only display aren't done; what's hidden is found by sampling along each edge, so where an edge goes out of sight can be off by a little; a drawing with no dashed line type (a blank one) leaves hidden lines out and says so.
- [x] AEC > Create Objects from Shapes…: the selected lines, rectangles, polygons and polylines without curves become walls along their sides in a style the drawing has, each mitred to the one before and the last to the first round a closed shape, or a slab over each rectangle square to x and y; Delete Source Shapes takes them away; one undo step, the new objects selected. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Create Polys from Walls…: Gross Area Polys (a polygon round the outside of each run of selected walls that meet) or Net Area (Room) Polys (a polygon inside each room they close), on the active layer in the active class with the attributes new objects are drawn with, selected, one undo step; the walls stay. Each wall is the convex hull of its plan drawing (its lines, polygons and polylines), so a door's or window's gap is filled and its joins' mitres kept; the hulls are joined (polygon-clipping, snapped to a 0.001 mm grid, coarser if that fails) and in-line and doubled points taken out. A toast gives the count and total area; walls that close no room say so. Rodeo's reading: polygons only (Vectorworks may make polylines where walls curve); round walls and walls in symbols aren't taken, and a wall whose plan drawing isn't convex (a jog drawn in it) counts as its hull. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Dimension Exterior Walls… (Spotlight > Architectural in Vectorworks' Spotlight workspace). Every building the walls on the Source Layer (a design layer holding walls, the active one first) make, their areas joined as Create Polys from Walls joins them, is dimensioned on all four sides along its own two directions (its longest side's and square to it, so a turned building is dimensioned along its sides): nearest, a chain through the doors and windows set in the walls facing that side (Dimension to Openings: their Edges, or their Centers, as far as each one's drawing reaches along its wall) between the corners, and with Dimension T-joins in exterior walls the walls inside that meet them (their faces, or centrelines); then a chain through every corner and jog seen from that side; then the overall size. A chain that would say no more than the next one out is left out. Dimension to: Outer Sides (the joined outline) or Centerlines (the walls' control lines' ends). Offsets from Building set each chain's distance out from the building's furthest point on its side (Openings, Walls, Overall; 10, 20 and 30 mm on paper as it comes, each further than the one before). Small Dimensions: a span shorter than the limit is drawn in red (often a wall a little out of line) or not drawn. The dimensions are the Dimension tool's: dimension objects copying one the drawing has, else plain lines, ticks and text grouped; on the source layer in the Dimension class when the drawing has one; each chain grouped, all selected after, in one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the dimensions aren't associative (they don't follow the walls when they move), and there's no Dimension Relative to core components (walls are measured whole); a door's or window's opening is as far as its drawing reaches along the wall, not the rough opening Vectorworks works out from the unit or leaf size, frame and shim gap, and an invisible three-point polygon in a symbol isn't read as its rough opening; the Text Options (Rotation, Location, Above Dim Line) aren't offered, so the text reads along each dimension as the Dimension tool draws it; curved walls and a building's sides at other angles than its two directions are measured only where their corners fall; T-joins are walls square to a side whose end lies on one of its walls; only design layers' walls, all of them rather than a selection; not checked against Vectorworks files
- [x] AEC > Pillar…: each selected rectangle, circle, oval or closed polygon stood up to the Pillar Preferences' Height, and each line, open polyline or arc stood up as a flat screen (a movie screen, a room divider), named Pillar. Rodeo's reading: a pillar is a plain extrude and a screen a plain mesh, not Vectorworks' plug-in object, so walls don't join to one and there's no Edit Pillar (an extrude's height is in Object Info; a screen's shape is fixed once made); a curved polyline's screen follows its flattened outline; open shapes can't be stood up while editing a group, since a mesh is made on the layer
- [x] AEC > Framing > Create Joists…: the selected rectangle, polygon, polyline or other closed 2D shape framed in one undo step. Draw Joists lays joists the Width × Height, their tops at Elevation of Top of Joist above the layer, Center-to-Center Spacing apart and parallel to two clicks made after OK, laid out from the first (Shift keeps the second to 15° steps; the layout is previewed under the pointer, Esc gives up). A joist goes wherever both its sides stay on the floor clear of the perimeter members and the headers, and runs on to the End Condition of Joists at Perimeter (the perimeter members' and headers' Inside Face, Centerline or Outside Face), each end cut along the edge it meets. Draw Perimeter Framing Members runs members of their own size and Elevation of Top of Framing round the inside of the outline, their corners Lapped (members across the joists run through and those alongside butt between them; with no joists the longer side runs through) or Mitered. Other closed shapes selected inside the floor are openings, each ringed outside by headers the joists' size, the members alongside the joists running through. Joists and perimeter members each take a Class, a 2D Display (Solid, Centerline, Width, Width with Centerline) and Show Label/Label Text as the Framing Member tool draws them; members take their class's attributes, or the drawing's in None, and are timber-coloured in 3D there. Delete Source Geometry removes the selected shapes. A Joist Take-Off worksheet (type, width, height, length, count and total length, alike members counted together, and a total row) is made when the drawing already has a worksheet to copy one from; otherwise the message says there's none. Defaults: 2x10s 16" apart in inch and foot drawings, 50 × 225 mm 400 apart otherwise. Rodeo's reading: each member is a group of its plan and a solid, not a framing member object Object Info can edit; Type is a solid beam only (rafter, wood truss, open-web steel joist, cold-formed steel channel, steel section and custom profile aren't drawn), and vertical reference, hangers, the separate Set Joist/Framing Member Attributes fill, pen and thickness choices and textures aren't offered; slabs, floors, roof faces and extrudes aren't read as sources, so Use Slab Component isn't offered; openings that overlap each other or the floor's edge, and joists running past an inward corner of the outline, aren't trimmed to each other.
- [x] AEC > Framing > Wall Framer…: the walls on the design layers ticked framed in one undo step, onto layers of the framing model's own. Model Name names them: 3D Model draws each wall's pieces on a design layer of that name as timber-coloured meshes, one per kind of piece, grouped by wall; 2D Model draws a "<name> Plan" layer (the bottom plates and the uprights but cripples, as plan shows them) and a "<name> Diagram" layer (each wall's elevation, stacked, labelled Frame N with its layer, class and size); Worksheets makes "<name> Frame TakeOff" (each frame's members by kind and length, counted, with a total) and "<name> Frame Wall Info" (each wall's length, height, area less its openings, studs, plates' run and the sheets that cover one face). A model layer that already holds objects is emptied and drawn again once Replace is clicked; a model named as a layer being framed is refused. Each wall class has its own Stud Spacing, Sheet Stock Width and Height, Max Plate Length, lumber (2"x4", 2"x6" or 2"x10", or a size typed), Fire Blocking, and Double End Stud, Top Plate and Bottom Plate. A wall's frame: plates cut at the longest plate and through doors, end studs, studs the spacing apart from its start, and round each door or window set in it (found from what it shows in 3D) king and jack studs, a header the lumber's long side doubled deep, cripples over it and, under a window, a sill with cripples below; fire blocking runs between the studs at the sheet's height, or halfway up a wall no taller. Rodeo's reading: each wall is framed end to end along its own line, so corners overlap rather than being built up; an opening's width and height include its casing; walls that aren't a straight run Rodeo can read are left out; the lumber list takes no new types, there's no New or Delete for framing models, and header depth, cripple and fire blocking placement are Rodeo's reading of the help; worksheets need a worksheet in the drawing to copy one from.
- [x] AEC > Framing > Roof Framer…: the selected roofs framed in one undo step, each member a group of its plan and its solid in the class chosen for its kind, with a Roof Framing Take-Off worksheet (type, width, height, length, count, total length) when the drawing has a worksheet to copy one from. Rafters: Create Rafters, Rafter Width and Height, Minimum and Maximum Spacing (the fewest rafters no more than Maximum apart, evenly along each slope's eave, one at each end; Minimum apart from one end, with the last at the other, when even spacing would come closer than that, as setting both the same gives), Set top of Rafters to Top or Bottom of Roof object/component, and Match Rafters across Ridges/Hips/Valleys (slopes whose eaves run the same way share one set of places, so rafters meet across ridges); rafters run down each slope, jacks cut by the hips and valleys, stopping short of the ridges, hips and valleys framed. Ridges: Create Ridges, size, and Ridge Elevation Flush to Top of Rafters, Notched into rafters (Notch Depth: the ridge's top that far above the rafters' plumb cut), Flush to Bottom of Rafters, Specify AFF or Flush to Top of Hips. Hips/Valleys: Create Hips and Valleys, size, Set top of Hips and Valleys to Top or Bottom of Roof, their sides plumb. Purlins: Create Purlins, size, Elevation of First Purlin (its bottom above the floor, or above the rafters' lower end with Purlins Start at the Lower End of the Rafter), Elevation of Last Purlin (its top that far below the roof's top), Create Purlins by Number of Purlins (evenly between) or Distance Between Purlins, Purlin Position Top or Bottom of Rafter, Rotate Purlins to Roof Slope, and Bearing inset (a level purlin under the rafters let up into them that far). Plates: Create Plates, size, Rafter Connection Flush to Top of Plate or Notched for Plate (Notch Depth, no more than a third of the rafters' height), along each eave under the rafters. Rodeo's reading: a roof is framed from its 3D faces (the faces looking up at a slope, grouped by plane, each with the underside under it), so roof objects and roof faces read from a file, Create Roof's roofs and meshes all frame alike, where Vectorworks takes a roof object only; ridges are level lines where two slopes fall away, hips sloping ones, valleys lines where they rise away; the roof doesn't say where its walls are, so the plates take a Plate Inset from Eave (Rodeo's own field) to their outer face; a notch moves the ridge or plate, the rafters themselves aren't cut; elevations above finished floor are above the roof's layer; the dialog is one list under the tabs' headings; not offered: Collars, Dormer Rafter Size, Use Roof Components, Edit List's sizes, Object Attributes (members take their class's attributes, or the drawing's in None, and a timber colour in 3D), flat roofs (faces under about 3°), and framing member objects (each member is a plain group, so changing the roof means framing it again)
- [x] AEC > Framing > Simple Beam Calculator…: a beam of uniform section in one of twelve configurations (a simple beam under a uniform load, a load at its center, a load at any point or two equal loads placed alike; fixed at both ends under a uniform load, a load at its center or at any point; a propped cantilever, fixed at its left end and supported at its right, under a uniform load or a load at its center; a cantilever under a uniform load, a load at its free end or at any point), drawn small above the fields, in Imperial (ft, lb, lb/ft, psi, in⁴, in³) or Metric (m, kN, kN/m, GPa, cm⁴, cm³) units, solved as the values are typed: the reactions and moments at each end, the greatest shear and bending moment, the bending stress and the greatest deflection with where it lies; the last values kept. Reactions and end moments come from the beam diagrams' formulas; the deflection is the bending moment integrated twice along the beam in 20,000 steps (within 1e-8 of the closed forms for all twelve, where it lies within 0.005%). Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' help lists a Max. Shear Stress where Rodeo gives the bending stress, the greatest moment over the section modulus (a shear stress needs the section's shape); Vectorworks' Select Structural Shape and Material dialog isn't there, so the modulus of elasticity, moment of inertia and section modulus are typed; which configurations Vectorworks offers wasn't compared. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Machine Design > 3D Properties…: the selected 3D objects' surface area, volume, radii of gyration X, Y and Z (√(I/V), about axes through the centre of mass parallel to the drawing's) and centre of mass, together, in the Units chosen, measured from the faces as Volumetric Properties measures them; Calculate Mass Properties… takes a System of Units (English (lb, in) or Metric (kg, m)), a Property to Use (Density or Specific Gravity, against water at 62.428 lb/cu ft or 1000 kg/cu m) and its Value (0.2836 lb/cu in, steel, to start), and adds the mass, the weight (lbf, or N at 9.80665 m/s²) and the mass moments of inertia X, Y and Z to the list; Place locus at center of mass, Place properties on drawing (the list as text at the next click) and Write properties to a file, as Vectorworks has them; the mass settings are remembered. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' help doesn't say which axes the radii and moments are about, or what the lists' lines and units are, so the centroidal axes parallel to x, y and z and the units shown are Rodeo's; English weight is pounds-force, numerically the mass; objects whose faces don't close get a surface area alone and no mass properties
- [x] AEC > Machine Design > Create 3D Object From 2D: each selected 2D machine part becomes its 3D version (the part's "- 3D" tool's group of plan and solid, or an extruded steel section), with the same sizes and settings, on the part's layer, in its class, at its insertion point and turn, the 2D part left as it is; several at once, one undo step, the 3D parts selected after. What Rodeo's Machine Components, Fasteners and Steel Section tools place now keeps the tool, its settings (JSON) and its insertion point and turn in a 'Rodeo Machine Part' record, which the command builds from; a part moved since is followed by its bounds' centre. Objects that can't be converted are deselected and counted, as Vectorworks deselects them. Rodeo's reading: a part turned, mirrored or scaled after placing comes out at its first turn and size; parts drawn before Rodeo kept the record, and Vectorworks' own 2D machine plug-in objects in a drawing (whose parameters Rodeo doesn't map to its tools), have no 3D version; a gear's, sprocket's, worm gear's or pulley's hub is part of its one solid rather than a separate 3D Hub object; Vectorworks' Bulb Flat has no Rodeo tool
- [x] AEC > Machine Design > Spring Calculator…: a round-wire helical compression spring in Inches or Millimeters (the drawing's kind to start), by Method (1 - Outside Dia., Wire Dia., Solid Height; 2 - Mean Dia., Wire Dia., No. of Active Coils; and 3 and 4, the other two pairings) and Type of Ends (Closed and Ground, Open and Ground, Closed, Open), the values the method doesn't take worked out and shown; a Material list setting the shear modulus G (or Other, typed), and the Spring Rate G·d⁴/(8·D³·n) in lb/in or N/mm and the Unit Stress 8·D/(π·d³) in (lb/sq in)/lb or (N/mm²)/N as the values are typed, matching Vectorworks' help's worked example (8.939 and 16.05 lb/in, 3074); with the total coils, spring index and Wahl factor besides. Place 2D spring on drawing or Place 3D spring on drawing hands the spring to the Compression Spring 2 tool or its 3D tool for the next click (keeping the tool's working length unless the spring doesn't fit it, then 1.5 × its solid height); its last values are remembered. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' Method list past 2 isn't in its help, so 3 and 4 are Rodeo's; its Material list and moduli aren't either, so the list is Shigley's spring wire (music wire at 11.85 Mpsi to 0.032", where Vectorworks' example shows 12,000,000), and the modulus is named Shear Modulus (G) where Vectorworks says Mod. of Elasticity; the two Place boxes are one or the other, since one tool places the spring
- [x] AEC > Machine Design: Belt Length Calculator… (an open belt's length round two pulleys of given pitch diameters at a center distance, or the center distance a belt of a given length fits them at; left blank, the least center distance, the pitch circles touching), Chain Length Calculator… (a roller chain of the 17 ANSI and ISO B sizes the Roller Chain tool draws, round sprockets of 6 to 150 teeth: its length and number of pitches at a center distance, or the center distance a chain of a given length fits them at, and both sprockets' pitch diameters; left blank, the least center distance, the sprockets' tips touching), each solved as the values are typed in the drawing's units and able to place its data as text where the drawing is clicked next; Centroid… (one closed 2D object's centroid, with a locus placed there if asked); Conversion Factors… (93 units of 13 quantities: length, area, volume, mass, force, pressure and stress, energy, power, torque, velocity, angle, moment of inertia and density; the factor and the converted amount to six significant figures); Solution of Triangles… (three sides, two sides and the angle between, two angles and the side between, two angles and a side opposite, or two sides and an angle opposite, with both answers where two triangles fit: the sides, angles, area and perimeter). Rodeo's reading: a belt's length is the open-belt formula 2C + π(D + d)/2 + (D − d)²/4C and a chain's the usual pitch-count formula, not rounded to a whole number of pitches; which units Vectorworks' Conversion Factors lists isn't known. Spring Calculator, Control Values for Keys, Shaft Analysis and 3D Properties aren't there yet. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Roads > Station on Polyline…, Place Station Points Along Polyline: with a polyline, polygon or line selected, stakes along it every 100' (30.48 m) from its first point, at the Partial station interval between them (0 for none) and at its end, each labeled with its station (1+50; in a metric drawing, metres past the last full station, 1+15.24). Curve data: Create individual curve labels draws each circular curve's PC and PT ticks, its tangent guide lines to the PI and its data (PC, PT, I, T, R, L, C) inside it, Create circular curve data table lists them in a new Station Worksheet numbered in turn, or No Curve Data. A polyline's lengths are measured along its true arcs. Rodeo's readings: the end stake; a worksheet copies one the drawing already has, so a drawing with none gets the data drawn at its curves, and says so; only circular curves count, not Bézier or cubic ones. Not offered: stakes as Stake plug-in objects (they're the Stake tool's default look, as grouped 2D objects, with no properties dialog), elevations from a site model, or leading on to Create Road from Stakes; at most 2,000 stations on a line
- [x] AEC > Electrical > Conduit Sizing Calc…, the Conduit Sizing Calculator: pick a wire's Type and Size (its C-S Area in sq inches shows), the Number of conductors, Add >> to the Conductors Currently in Conduit list (Remove, Clear All), then the Conduit Type, Number of conduits and Conduit stuff percentage; it reports the Actual Conduit Size required in inches and mm and the Nominal (Trade) size, and the fill at that size. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks' wire specifications database (manufacturers and part numbers) isn't known, so the lists are the NEC's Chapter 9: Table 5 for THHN/THWN-2 and XHHW to 500 kcmil, TW/THW and RHW to 4 AWG, with Other for any wire's area typed; Table 4 for EMT, RMC, IMC, FMC, LFMC and PVC 40 and 80; Table 1's 53%, 31% and 40% suggested by how many wires each conduit holds, typed over at will, a new wire or conduit count suggesting afresh. The wires are shared evenly by area between the conduits. Areas were checked against secondary sources, not the code book, and the conduits past 2" weren't all confirmed; the dialog says to check them against the edition that applies
- [x] AEC > Plants > Vegetation Line…: a line round the outer edge of the selected closed objects and symbols as they overlap, Standard following that edge or Cloud running arcs along it (Minimum and Maximum Radius, Billow Height, with 1 a half circle), with Delete Original. It's one polygon for each mass, in the drawing's attributes and active class, so a solid fill or hiding its class works as in Vectorworks. A symbol, plant or group counts as the ellipse its box holds, where Vectorworks goes by its outlines; each cloud's arcs come from a fixed sequence, so they can't be reshuffled.
- [x] Space Planning's adjacency tools: Space Link (click one space, then the other; Object Info has its Strength from 1 to 18, and the stronger the heavier its line), Adjacency Score (the sum of each link's length × strength, in the drawing's length unit, click to place and click for rotation), Adjacency Matrix (the spaces with their links' strengths in a diamond grid, in the order of each space's Matrix Order, with Rotation, Height Scale and Width Scale) and Stacking Diagram (a row for each layer with spaces, highest elevation on top, its name and area to the left and its spaces as wide as their areas and in their fills, departments together with thin lines inside a department, with Rotation, Height, Width and Gap Scales and Show Totals), and AEC > Space Planning > Import Adjacency Matrix… (a tab- or comma-delimited .txt or .csv, with Header Rows, Row Spacing, Field Delimiter, column numbers and a preview, makes the spaces, their links, a matrix and a stacking diagram). They follow the spaces: after each edit whatever's changed is drawn again in the same undo step, and a link whose space has gone goes with it. A space's Department and Matrix Order are in its Space Data record and Department is in the Space tool's Preferences. Limits: they read Rodeo's spaces, not Vectorworks' own Space objects; a link's length is centre to centre, its line only the part between the boundaries; the score's unit and a file with Row Spacing 0 (read as n columns along being the space n rows below) are this app's reading of help that doesn't say; a matrix leaves cells with no link blank where Vectorworks draws a dash; imported spaces are squares of the area in rows, not a worked-out bubble layout, and a space with no area takes 100 of the area unit; there's no properties dialog the first time (Preferences holds the same settings); and Vectorworks' own adjacency objects aren't read, nor are these checked in Vectorworks.
- [x] Space Planning's Space tool past its first form: Rectangle, Polygon, Inner Boundary (a click in a room the active layer's visible walls close) and Picked Walls (click each wall, Enter makes the space within them), with Preferences for Space Number Type (Incrementing Value counting on through integers or letters, or Label Text), Increment and Next Value, Space Type, Space Name, Occupant Type, Occup. Organization, Occupant Name, Proposed Area, Height, Show 2D Boundary and which of the name, number and area the label shows. AEC > Space Planning > Create Spaces from Polys (closed shapes become spaces in their place), Create Spaces from Walls… (Create Room Spaces or Create Gross Floor Space, Source and Destination Layer, Minimum area, Selected Walls Only; run again after moving walls, spaces already there are reshaped and keep their data), Create Walls from Spaces… (Selected Spaces Only, Source and Destination Layer, the four Place Exterior Walls alignments, Exterior and Interior Wall Style, Combine collinear interior walls; exterior walls run round the outside as mitred loops, interior ones between spaces that touch, teed into what they meet) and Update Spaces… (selected, on the layer, or all; area re-measured in label and record). Not Vectorworks' Space objects: a group named Space … of its boundary polygon and label, with its data in a Space Data record; Area there is a number in the drawing's area unit. The commands read only spaces made this way, not Space objects a drawing has. No room finishes, zones, data tags, area modifiers, net or gross boundary options, wall-bound updating, 3D volume, Pick Up/Apply or space styles. Curved edges come out as straight runs and alignment goes by the whole wall rather than its core; Constrain straight walls isn't offered. Wall styles are the drawing's own, as the Wall tool's are, so Create Walls from Spaces needs a drawing with a straight wall to copy.
- [x] AEC > Electrical > Conductor Sizing Calc…, the Conductor Sizing Calculator: one of Wire Size, Ohms/ft/cmil-ft, Voltage Drop, Phase Factor, Voltage, Ampacity or Length of Run worked out from the rest by VD = PF × K × I × L / CM, as the values are typed, its field greyed: Phase Factor (2 single-phase, 1.732 three-phase), Type (Copper, K 12.9 ohm-cmil/ft and alpha 0.00323 at 75 °C, or Aluminum, 21.2 and 0.0033), Size (18 AWG to 4/0, then 250 to 1000 kcmil, or Other) with its sectional area in cmil, the resistance at 75 °C and alpha with the temperature the K is taken at, Amps, Length (ft.), the Voltage Drop as a Percentage of the voltage or Absolute, and Voltage. A wire size shows its area, the nearest AWG and the next listed size up; the last values are kept. Rodeo's reading: the AWG areas come from the AWG formula (12 AWG 6530 cmil); Ampacity is the current that drops the voltage given, not a rating from an ampacity table; Vectorworks' wire specification database isn't there, and its Calculate button has nothing to do since the answer follows the typing. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Survey Input > 2D Polys to 3D Source Data…: the selected 2D polygons and polylines (lines, rectangles, arcs, circles and ellipses too) as 3D polygons, or 3D loci at their points, at their elevations, for a site model's source data. The first dialog takes the Start Elevation, the Interval, a Segmentation Length (0 keeps curves as drawn) and Create 3D Polygons or Create 3D Loci; Set Elevation then steps through them from the backmost, each selected in turn in a panel at the drawing's corner, its elevation the last one's plus the interval, or the same with Use same height as prev., Up and Dn (or the arrow keys) moving it by the interval; Next goes on, Done stops with the one shown counted, and the originals are then deleted (the 3D polygon takes each one's place) or kept (each 3D polygon just behind its original), in its class with its attributes; the new ones selected, one undo step. Elevations are above the layer. Rodeo's reading: a Segmentation Length only thins curves (a point kept once the outline has run that far, and at every corner), since Rodeo flattens them as it draws them (arcs 7.5° a step, a polyline's 32 points a turn); polygons, rectangles and lines keep every vertex. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Simplify 3D Polygons…, the Simplify Selected 3D Polygons dialog: a Simplification Tolerance and the layer to Put Simplified Result in (the design layers, the active one first chosen). The selected 3D polygons stay as they are; copies of them go to that layer, as high above it as they were above their own, with the vertices that lie within the tolerance of the outline without them taken out (Simplify Polys' maximum deviation, measured in 3D; start and end vertices stay), and are selected when that layer is active; a toast says how many vertices went; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the tolerance is taken as a maximum deviation, as Simplify Polys reads one; 3D polygons in groups aren't taken. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Site Model from Source Data…, Site Model from Boundary…, Send to Surface and Update Site Model, with Site Planning's Site Modifiers tool (Grade Limits and Planar Pad, each drawn as a rectangle or polygon). From Source Data triangulates the selected 3D loci and 3D polygons, going into groups (Delaunay, in plan; a point within a millimetre of another is left out), inside their convex hull; From Boundary makes a flat model inside a closed shape on a grid of the Grid Spacing, starting from its lower left corner rather than a clicked origin, and an Elevation Vectorworks doesn't ask for. The Site Model Settings dialog: Name, Keep Source Data, Maximum and Minimum Elevation, Extend Min/Max when Needed, Datum Elevation, Minor Contour Interval, Major Contour Multiplier, Start Contour Offset, Use Site Modifiers on (All, Visible, Same Layer; no Select Layers), 2D Display and all six 2D Styles, Draw Site Border, Show Contour Labels (with Maximum Labels per Contour and Minimum Distance Between Labels, the line broken under each label, and their tops uphill), Show Flow Arrows, Flow Arrow Spacing, Scaled to Slope, a 2D Cut and Fill Area, 3D Display (No 3D Display, Existing, Proposed, Cut and Fill) and all ten 3D Styles, Show Skirt, Show 3D Contour, 3D Grid Spacing (By Distance only). Object Info: those displays, Geometry Lowest Z (moving the model and its source, not its modifiers), Projected Area, Surface Area, Volume, Cut, Fill, Net and Total, Site Model Settings, Update, Reset to Min/Max of Surface Geometry, and Recreate from Source Data, which lays the source out as a group of 3D loci and takes it back at the next press rather than entering an edit mode. A changed modifier wears a red and white border on the model until Update, in the same undo step; Update also moves the model back to the origin if it was moved, as its contours belong where its points are. A modifier is a closed polygon with a Site Modifier record (Type, Elevation, Slope percent, Slope Direction degrees), reshaped as any polygon and changed in the Data tab: Grade Limits take out the ground inside them and hold their outline at the existing height; a pad takes out the ground inside it and brings its outline to its plane, at its Elevation at the outline's middle, Use Surface Elevation reading that from the proposed surface. Send to Surface (with Choose Surface when there's more than one) lifts 3D loci and anything with 3D faces, by its lowest point at the middle of its box, and remakes polylines and 2D polygons as 3D polygons, and 2D loci as 3D loci, at the surface; what's off the edge stays. Cut and fill volumes are summed on a 256 by 256 grid; heights are above the model's layer. Tested in the browser on synthetic ground, on a layer with an elevation, through a round trip of the file, and 20,000 points (about a second). Left out so far: point clouds as source; Snap to Contour Elevation and the clicked slope direction; Graphic Properties (the pens and analysis colours are fixed, the nearest in the drawing's palette); components and survey points; texture and geoimage; label Units and Precision; mesh smoothing. Modifiers don't show in 3D. Vectorworks' own site models stay as they were drawn (this is a plug-in of Rodeo's own, named Rodeo Site Model, and hasn't been opened in Vectorworks).
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Create Grade Limits from Planar Pad… (Distance or Batter Slope, Max. Offset, Slope Definition, Max. Slope, Use Midpoints, Test Increment), Create Planar Pad from Grade Limits… (Uniform or Per side with Prev/Next and a small drawing of the side, Data display method; the slopes stay with the limit in a Grade Limits Slopes record), Site Model Section… (layer, grid and profile colours, station points at vertices or an interval, scale, vertical magnification, margins, legend, elevations, line weights), Create Site Model Volume List… (the ten columns, which grade limits, unmodified surface or not, as a text file, triangle group or worksheet) and View > Show or Hide Site Modifiers. Limits: grade limits go only around planar pads, not roads, massing models or landscape walls; a limit that would cross itself is refused, not untangled. A pad from limits is level, its sides parallel to the limit's, each side set back by the steepest climb along that side, so the batter is at most the slope you typed and only reaches it at that side's worst point; the balance is worked out on the limit's own outline and then the update's own triangles differ slightly (about 1% in testing). Section: no station profile for roads or stakes as station points (they need Landmark's road stations), and it's plain lines and text that a later change to the model doesn't redraw, as in Vectorworks. Volume list: per triangle of the proposed surface, not the common triangles of both, since the two aren't overlaid; the existing surface is read at a grid of points in each triangle, so totals are close to but not equal to the model's Object Info figures (within 0.5% in testing). Columns can be picked but not reordered; Cut means ground taken away and Fill ground brought in, the way the model's own figures read, where the help's wording for the two columns reads the other way round. A worksheet needs another worksheet in the drawing to copy settings from, so a blank drawing gets the text file; worksheets stop at 5,000 rows and triangle groups at 2,000 triangles. Show or Hide switches the Site-Modifiers class.
- [x] Site Planning: Grade tool (Standard Insertion, Edit Elevation, Update, Preferences) + AEC > Terrain > Create 3D Loci at Grade Points: a Rodeo plug-in object between two elevations, set by any two of Elevation 1, Elevation 2, a grade, a ratio or the change; slope in percent, permille, ratio or angle, lengths, elevations, marker (built-in or a symbol), downhill arrow; shared ends share an elevation and move together (Detach from Grade Network), a new end on a grade splits it; global settings and analysis (grade or elevation out of range, intersections; highlight, select) kept on each object; takes heights from a site model or changes it at Update as a line of known heights, on the existing or proposed surface. Limits: ends move by the object's own Move and Projected Length, not Reshape handles; a grade changing the site is a break line with no grade limits of its own, so it pulls the surface in sharply unless Grade Limits are drawn round it.
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Zone of Visual Influence…: pick the site model and view height (60" by default), click the observation point, click again. Instead of a rendered light, what can be seen is worked out over the proposed surface in a 200 by 200 grid and drawn as a group: dark outlines in plan, a dark mesh lying on the ground in 3D, and the point with a 3D locus at eye height. Limits: only the surface blocks the view, not buildings or plants on it; it doesn't follow later changes, so run it again.
- [x] AEC > Survey Input > Grid Method Entry… (click the upper left corner, then rows, columns, spacing and 3D loci or stakes; Enter Z Values is one table of every point, Tab moves across and a block pasted from a spreadsheet fills it, in place of one dialog per point; up to 2,500 points) and Import Survey File… (Excel .xlsx first sheet, tab or any other delimiter, a space counting however many in a row; PNEZD, PENZD, PNEZ, PENZ, NEZD, ENZD, NEZ, ENZ; feet, US survey feet, inches, yards, metres, centimetres, millimetres; 3D loci or stakes with a label; lines that don't fit are skipped and counted; an option to center far-off coordinates on the internal origin, on by default past 10 km). Stakes from these, the Stake tool and Stake Object from 3D Locus now hold a 3D locus at their height, so a site model reads them as source data and the Grade tool takes their elevation. Limit: Excel .xls isn't read yet.
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Site Modifiers tool's other modifiers, each a polygon with the Site Modifier record. Pad with Retaining Edge makes a pad and a second polygon around it at Retaining Edge Offset, both selected and reshaped apart; the edge follows the ground, or holds one height when it's given an Elevation in the Data tab (one height for the edge, not one per corner). Aligned: each corner of the outline takes the height of a pad, open edge line or grade it touches (within a foot or so), or its own Elevation, and its top runs straight between corners; it doesn't align to walls, roadways, hardscapes or 3D polygons, and there are no profile lines or interpolation settings. Open Edge Line is an open polyline by clicked corners, at its Elevation and Slope. Spoil Pile adds its Elevation as an even thickness (negative takes off), meeting the ground again a little outside it. Texture Bed leaves the terrain alone and lays a mesh over the model's ground in 3D Mesh styles, in the polygon's class with the class's texture when it has one and the polygon's fill or a plain green when it doesn't. Apply To (Proposed or Existing) is new on every modifier, a drawing from before it gains the field at the next modifier made. AEC > Create Objects from Shapes… has a Site Modifier type with these styles (a Retaining Edge on its own; an Open Edge Line from any line). Not done: Vertical sides for inner modifiers, Show the surface in 3D, Simplification Tolerance, a modifier's own Update Calculations, Slope A/B and contour angle.
- [x] Tool Sets: Site Planning's Site Modifiers tool's Pathway Path and Boundary Path, and AEC > Create Objects from Shapes… for them. Pathway: clicked corners and a width in the Site Modifier: Pathway Mode dialog, the sides mitred at the corners (held to three times the width at a sharp one). Boundary: a closed outline, its centre line worked out from it, between the two edges farthest apart with its sides taken at the same share of their lengths. Its start and end elevations come from the site model under it. The longitudinal profile: Start Elevation, End Elevation and Slope; Edit Profile; Realign Elevations; Fit to Surface (on the model's existing surface). Transverse profiles: Add Profile by a click on the path, Previous and Next, Edit Selected Profile, Delete Profile, shading from one to the next along the path, each holding past the last, and stretched with the width. Show the surface in 3D, Apply To, Update Site Model, and profile line, names, slopes, downhill arrow and vertex elevation switches. Reshape moves, adds and deletes corners of its path, stretching what's along it; Edit Path Points gives them as figures. The object can be moved and turned. A profile is edited as a table of figures with a little drawing of it, not in a graphic editing mode; there's no Reshape Longitudinal Profile mode, profile classes or marker styles, Vertical sides for inner modifiers, Simplification Tolerance, Update Calculations or Slopes Grade Def (slopes are percent). A boundary with a sharp bend can pair its sides badly, so its centre line wanders. It's a plug-in of Rodeo's own, named Rodeo Path Modifier, and hasn't been opened in Vectorworks.
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Edit Site Model Crop, Edit Existing Contours, Edit Proposed Contours, Edit Existing Surface and Edit Proposed Surface, also as buttons in the model's Object Info; Modify > Edit Crop… and the right-click Edit Crop… take a selected site model. Edit Crop makes a Site Border modifier round the model (or goes back to the one it has) and takes up Reshape; a border can also come from a shape by Create Objects from Shapes. The surface ends along it at the next Update, its heights unchanged and its source data kept, so deleting the border brings the ground back. Edit Surface is one tool, Surface Sculpting, with Vectorworks' Sculptor, Add Vertex and Swale/Berm as its modes, and the Existing or Proposed surface as another. The Sculptor has Single Element (a vertex, an edge's two ends or a triangle's three corners, by where you press) and Radius, with Soft Selection (Falloff %) or even, Tessellation and Max Distance; a vertical drag gives the distance, shown in the hint, and a click asks for it. A swale or berm is drawn along a line of clicks with a Width, a Depth/Height (negative is a swale), Flat, Rounded or Sharp and Follow Terrain. Edit Contours picks a contour for Reshape or a move, the ground following it at Update and leaving the old line, or Site Model Contour draws a new one at a locked Elevation or the ground's height where it starts. Differences from Vectorworks: these aren't a separate object-editing mode. Each edit stays a site modifier in the drawing (a circle, a line, a locus), with its record, applied again at every Update and undone by deleting it; a sculpt follows its circle if it's moved or reshaped, but its Single Element form stays on the vertices it took. Sculpting is on the Top/Plan drawing with a vertical drag: not on the rendered surface, and with no colour preview of the falloff. A drag's distance depends on the zoom. An edited contour's new height reaches the triangles between it and its neighbours rather than new contours being interpolated between them. Add Vertex takes a point at the ground's height and can't set one. A Site Border applies to every model its scope reaches, and the last one drawn wins. Tests: shape checks plus a Playwright run over a tilted synthetic site, drags, the distance dialog, Single Element, Add Vertex, each Swale/Berm profile, contour pick and draw on either surface, undo and redo, a format from before Options, and a round trip.
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Add Contour Labels and Edit Contour Labels (buttons in the model's Object Info too), and Create a Snapshot in Object Info. Edit Contour Labels gives each label on the drawing a place of its own and a handle; Move Label drags it along its contour, Add Label takes a click on any contour, major or minor, and Delete Label a click on a handle. Add Contour Labels puts one wherever a line of clicks crosses a contour, and the line isn't kept. Place Labels Automatically goes back to the Maximum Labels and Minimum Distance settings. A snapshot is a plug-in over the model on the active layer, showing its 2D and 3D display and styles as they were: it keeps its own copy of the model's values, so it stays as it is when the model changes or is deleted, until Unfreeze (after which it follows each Update, and Freeze stops that). Opacity works on the whole of it, and with Coloured Elevations or Slopes in plan it has a legend with Show Legend, Title Text and Reverse Order. Differences from Vectorworks: these labels are a separate tool rather than two extra Reshape modes, and there's no Custom Label Placement check in the settings. A label moved off its contour by a change of ground goes to the nearest place on a contour of its height, and one whose contour has gone isn't drawn. A label's place is what's kept, not its place relative to the contour. The legend moves by its Legend X and Y in Object Info rather than a control point, its text has no class of its own and Object Info's figures are shown as they were, with no Area Display Type. A snapshot holds a second copy of the source points. Tests: a Playwright run on a synthetic site: moving, adding and deleting labels, undo, labels along a line, the labels through Update and a round trip, labels turned on from None; a snapshot's legend, opacity, freezing, unfreezing and following, and with its model deleted.
- [x] Site Planning > Massing Model: a building shell from a rectangle or polygon outline. Name, Show in Plan (Roof or Footprint), a label of name and gross area, Height to the eaves, Floor Count and Floor Usage Data, pitched (hips and valleys, Square, Vertical or Horizontal eaves, Slope) or flat roof, Overhang, Thickness, Wall and Roof Class, Draw Floors, Slab Thickness, Display Shadow in Plan View, Use Site Modifiers (a pad under it and grade limits round it, at Update), Grade Limits Off., Gross Area, Reshape moves its corners. Also from AEC > Create Objects from Shapes. It stands on the ground at its middle when drawn, and has a Send to Surface button. Not done: Use Custom Roof from Profile with Modify > Edit Massing Model; the FloorUsageData worksheet function (the field can be read as a record); the label moves by typed Label X and Y, not by a control point; the shadow falls one fixed way, with no Shadow Settings. Pitched roofs take what AEC > Create Roof can work out and otherwise draw flat. Walls are always 200 mm thick, which only a section shows.
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Create Retaining Wall Site Modifier: from connected walls or an extrude (as a straight run along its tightest box), in any selection order, going the way the first was drawn. Dialog: Pad, Left and Right Modifier Edge, each Offset Below Wall Top, Offset Above Wall Bottom or Follow Terrain; Include Start and End Modifier Edge. Object Info: Apply To, Width, Pad Offset, the Include toggles, Move (Left, Right, Start or End Edge) with Elevation, previous and next vertex with Vertex Elevation, Send Edges to Surface, Send Pad to Surface; AEC > Terrain > Send to Surface takes its edges. Reshape moves the corners of its centre line, keeping heights, and new corners take theirs from where they fall. Not done: Follow Top and Bottom Wall Peaks (wall peaks' format isn't known); moving its left and right edges apart with Reshape. Rodeo's own curbs and hardscapes are plain drawn groups, so they can't be picked for it. AEC > Create Stepped Wall needs wall peaks too, so it stays blocked.
- [x] AEC > Existing Trees > Import Tree Survey File: Excel (.xlsx, first sheet), tab-delimited or any delimiter; Data Starts at Row Number, Data has column headers, Create custom record with name; a mapping table of Survey Column, Parameter, Unit and Add to Record, with each column's first value and guesses from its name and units; X, Y and Z coordinates, XY or YX pairs in brackets, latitude and longitude (through the drawing's georeferencing, asked for if it has none) and their pairs, and lists of pairs as an area, drawn as a polygon; Create existing tree objects, 2D locus objects or 3D locus objects; saved settings. Not done: a tree here is the Existing Tree tool's plain group, so columns such as Height and Condition stay in the record only; record fields are all text; saved settings are kept in this browser, not the file; old .xls workbooks can't be read.
- [x] AEC > Terrain > Validate 3D Data: the selected 3D polygons, and those in selected groups, checked as a site model's source data. With nothing wrong a box says "No problems were found."; otherwise Problems with Site Model Source Data lists copies of another 3D polygon (the same points, either way round; Delete keeps the backmost), 3D polygons of fewer than 2 points (Delete), points repeated in a row (Remove: Simplify Polys by distance takes them out, start and end kept), places in plan with points one above another and points in more than one place in the data (Select picks the polygons with them), checked again after each correction, each its own undo step. Points are the same within 0.001 mm in each of x, y and z. Rodeo's reading: what Vectorworks counts as each problem and which buttons its dialog has aren't known, and a closing point that repeats the first isn't counted; 3D loci and stakes aren't looked at (Rodeo can't write 3D loci). Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] AEC > Create Roof…: a roof over the selected walls (the outside of each run that meets), polygons or polylines (any closed shape), one for each outline. A hip roof all round, with valleys at inward corners, worked out as the outline's straight skeleton, so L, T, U and skewed plans come out right; clicking an edge in the dialog's plan makes it a gable, with an optional triangular Gable Wall to its underside (Gable Wall Thickness, Show Gable Wall). Eave Profile (Square, Vertical, Horizontal, Double, with Vertical and Horizontal for a double cut, each following the other), Thickness, Bearing Inset, Roof Pitch (degrees or rise:run), Bearing Height, Eave Height and Eave Overhang, with Calculate on those last four as in Vectorworks; changing a value recalculates the Eave Height, and changing that the Bearing Height. Retain Original Objects. Vectorworks' help doesn't say where each level is measured: here the Eave Height is the eave's lowest point and the Bearing Height the underside over the Bearing Inset. Each roof is a group of its plan and one solid, so it can't be changed afterwards; Vectorworks' is a roof object you go on editing. Not modelled: roof styles and components, Dutch hips, dormers and skylights, each edge with its own pitch or bearing height, Hole Cut (nothing cuts it), Layer and Class (it goes on the active ones), Create Accessories, and walls trimmed up to the roof. Not opened in Vectorworks.
- [x] AEC > Roof Face…: the selected closed 2D shape (a polygon, rectangle, circle or closed polyline, holes and all) becomes a roof face, one sloping slab over it. The Create Roof Face dialog takes Vectorworks' Roof Slope (Angle, Rise over Run, or 2nd Click Height), Eave Cut (Vertical, Horizontal, Double with its vertical, Square), Hole Cut (Vertical, Splayed, Square), Axis Z and Thickness; then the axis is clicked, or dragged, across the shape and a third click picks the side that rises (with 2nd Click Height, where the roof is that high), an arrow and the angle showing. It is drawn in plan as its outline in the shape's attributes and in 3D as a closed solid; Object Info edits Angle, Rise and Run (each following the others), Axis Z, Thickness, the cuts and a double cut's Vertical or Horizontal, and shows its area in its slope and in plan; Reshape moves its corners and Clip Surface cuts holes in it or pieces off it, the holes taking the Hole Cut; in one undo step each. Rodeo's reading: Axis Z is the underside at the axis; the eave is every edge whose outside faces down the slope within 45° of square to it, the other edges cut vertically (as Vectorworks' Remove Mitered Edges leaves them); Splayed is horizontal on a hole's upper edges and vertical on the rest. It is a plug-in with Rodeo's own name (Rodeo Roof Face), as Vectorworks' roof face object holds Parasolid data Rodeo can't write, so Vectorworks sees its plan and 3D as drawn; there are no roof styles or components, dormers, skylights, wall clipping or Connect/Combine joins, and Ungroup doesn't give back the 2D shape
- [ ] Slabs with other outlines, and in styles whose edge stands past the outline; roof objects you can edit; roof faces joined by Connect/Combine, with dormers and skylights; editing a slab's outline, thickness and slope
- [x] Door and Window tools (Building Shell): a click on a straight wall sets a copy of a door or window the drawing has set alone in a wall of that style and thickness, its middle at the click, with its opening (the wall redrawn as a copy of that wall laid along its line, its ends cut and its heights, class, name and records its own; the walls joined to it untouched); Preferences picks the door or window by size and wall; a wall of another style or thickness, one holding a door already, sloping, with an end not cut straight or a cleaned-up joint, a door reaching an end or a wall meeting the side, and a top below the opening are refused, saying why; one undo step. Untested in Vectorworks
- [x] Doors, windows and symbols set in walls picked out of their wall with a click (Select tool), and slid along it with their opening by dragging, nudging or Move (the wall's drawing and 3D within 60 mm of the door's drawing go with it; the drag runs along the wall's line); one that would reach the wall's end, another door or window or a wall meeting the side, or whose opening can't be told apart from theirs, is refused, and so are turning, deleting, duplicating and grouping one, saying why; one undo step. Untested in Vectorworks
- [ ] Doors and windows: placed in walls the tools can't redraw so (a style with no door set alone in it, a wall holding doors already, sloping walls, cleaned-up joints), flipped to face the other way, of another size than one the drawing has (a copy keeps the copied one's drawing), dragged off their wall or into another, and deleted from it (the opening closed); their parameters (they draw and their parameters edit today)
- [ ] Stair and Column tools that make a Vectorworks plug-in object from their own settings (Plug-in Insertion places a copy of one the drawing has); Space's own objects too (the Space tool draws grouped objects)
- [ ] Site model editing, plants, hardscape
- [x] Model > Extrude (⌘E): rectangles, rounded rectangles, polygons, polylines, circles and ellipses become extrudes of a given height holding them as their profile (saved without a cached body; Vectorworks rebuilding it is unconfirmed)
- [x] Model > Multiple Extrude (⌥⌘E): two or more of those shapes on one layer or group become one multiple extrude of a given height through them, the backmost at the bottom (saved without a cached body, as Extrude)
- [x] Model > Tapered Extrude… (⌥⌘U): the Create Tapered Extrude dialog's Height (Z) and Taper Angle (a positive angle leans the sides in, a negative one out, 0 straight up; the last taper kept); each selected rectangle, rounded rectangle, polygon, polyline, circle or ellipse becomes a mesh standing on its layer whose top is the profile with each side moved in by the height times the taper's tangent, its corners mitred, in the shape's class and with its attributes; a taper that closes a profile up or folds it over before the top is refused and nothing changes; one undo step for all of them. Volumes match the frustum formula for squares, circles in 48 sides and either winding, and an L's slices to 5e-10. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks makes a Tapered Extrude plug-in object whose height and taper stay editable in Object Info; Rodeo makes a plain mesh (making that plug-in isn't worked out), and a shape's holes, 3D polygons and NURBS curves aren't taken. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] Model > Extrude Along Path… (⌥⌘X): with two objects selected, one a rectangle, rounded rectangle, polygon, polyline, circle or ellipse to be the profile and the other a line, arc, circle, rectangle, polygon, polyline or 3D polygon to be the path, the dialog's Path object (offering the open one, else the larger), Scale None or Uniform Scale with its Scale Factor (the profile grown evenly from 1 at the path's start to the factor at its end), and Lock Profile Plane (the profile's y kept upright, square to each run; unticked, carried round each bend so it may twist); the last settings kept. The profile is centred (its box's middle) on the path's start and square to it, looking along the path its x to the right and its y up, and mitred at each corner; a closed path makes a ring, an open one is capped. Both objects become one mesh on the layer in the profile's class and with its attributes; one undo step. A path that turns back on itself too sharply is refused and nothing changes. Volumes match the profile's area times the path's length for a straight line, an L, a closed rectangle and a quarter arc, a circle along a circle the 48-sided torus, a uniform scale of 2 the frustum formula, and 3D polygons tilted out of plan locked and unlocked. Rodeo's reading: Vectorworks makes an Extrude Along Path object whose profile and path stay editable; Rodeo makes a plain mesh. Exponential Scale and Fix Profile aren't offered; a profile's holes, 3D polygons and NURBS curves aren't taken as profiles, and paths are followed as drawn (arcs in steps of 7.5° at most) rather than as NURBS curves. Not checked against Vectorworks
- [x] Top/Plan draws a mesh that stands alone or in a plain group, from above: its rims, sharp folds and the edges where it turns away, in its pen, and a click on those lines picks it. So what the 3D Modeling tools make shows in plan. A mesh in a symbol or plug-in, or grouped with a plan drawing, leaves plan to that, and a viewport's view of a mesh still shows nothing of it. Vectorworks draws every edge of a mesh here; Rodeo leaves out the smooth ones between faces, as Top/Plan would be a mass of lines otherwise.
- [x] Model > Create Surface Array…: the selected items repeated across a flat object or open surface, also selected, with Next and Prev to choose which is the base (the largest at first, its rim in red), Origin X' and Y' Factor, Pattern Rotation, Repetition Mode (No. of Repetitions fits them to the base, Fixed Distance sets their length), Offset and Shift Factors, Edge Conditions (Overlap, Omit, Trim), Display Base Surface, Scale Z' Value and Switch Z' Direction, the 3D view showing the array's rectangles as they change. Rodeo has no surface array object and doesn't know the format's, so it makes a plain group of the base and the copies as meshes, and the items are used up: there's no Edit Surface Array or changing it in Object Info (undo and make it again, the dialog remembers its values). A surface here has no U and V, so the grid lies on the plane the surface faces most and each point is carried along that plane's normal onto it: cells stretch where it's steep, and one that folds back over itself shows only its top. Solids, flat shapes, groups and symbols go as faces, lines and 3D polygons as lines, 3D loci as loci; text and images are left out. Trim doesn't cut lines, 3D polygons or loci, which stay whole. Omit and Trim go by each item's rectangle, not its outline.
- [x] Tools > Class and Layer Mapping…: a list of the drawing's classes, and with Go To Layers its design layers, each with the name it's to have. A new name renames it, another's name merges it with that one (those proposing a new name together go into the first, which takes it), and a blank deletes it with the objects on it, after saying how many. Reset All, Load and Save read and write Vectorworks' mapping files (the first and last lines and the tab-separated Renamed/Same/Merged lines the help sets out), and Delete empty classes and layers removes those with nothing left. One undo step. Tested with the drawing's own synthetic classes and layers, including swapped names, merging into None and a deleted active layer, through save and reopen; the file format is from the help and no file from Vectorworks has been read. Limits: names in rows stand in for Vectorworks' Existing, Proposed and Merged lists; a merged layer's objects keep their height above the layer, and its own scale and settings go; objects inside symbols and plug-in objects go to None with a deleted class rather than being deleted; a group whose class is deleted goes as a whole, but its parts in that class leave a group behind them; the DXF/DWG import and export mapping isn't connected to these files.
- [x] Model > Create Contours…: planes level with the layer, or square to its x or y axis, from a first plane every interval; each cut through the selected 3D objects, symbols and plug-ins a 3D polygon (closed where it goes round, open where the surface is), drawn as the part it cuts, grouped after its object
- [x] Model > Cut 2D Section: the section line's start and end clicked across the selected 3D objects, symbols and plug-in objects (Shift 15°), then the side to keep; the upright plane through the line cuts them onto a new design layer (Section, at the active layer's scale), seen from the side given up: cut faces as polygons with the attributes of the part cut, holes kept, grouped per object in its class, standing on the line's start; one undo step, Esc cancels (the model beyond the cut is not drawn)
- [x] Model > Cut 3D Section: the same clicks; what of each selected object lies on the kept side, where it was, as a mesh on a new design layer (3D Section) closed by a face over the cut (holes kept), each face drawn as the part it came from, the object's texture on the mesh, in its class; objects wholly on the other side left out; the model stays; one undo step (the drawing needs a mesh to copy settings from, as Convert to Mesh does; Vectorworks keeps solids where Rodeo makes meshes)
- [x] Model > Sweep…: each of those shapes stands up on its layer and turns about the upright through its left edge, through an arc (up to 360°) in segments of a given angle; the 3D view draws every sweep turned from its profile, about the 2D locus it holds where it has one (the bodiless ones in the demos showed nothing before)
- [x] Model > Engineering Properties…: one closed 2D object (a polygon, polyline, rectangle, rounded rectangle, or full circle or ellipse), and a locus when one is selected too: area, perimeter, centroid, Ix, Iy and Ixy about the centroid, the polar and principal moments with the principal axis's angle, the section moduli and the radii of gyration, and with the locus the moments, polar moment and radii about axes through it and the centroid's offset from it; in the document's units, mm, cm, m, in or ft. The dialog can place a locus at the centroid, place the list as text (2.5 mm on paper) where the drawing is clicked next (Esc gives up), and save it as a text file. Curved polyline edges are taken as drawn; circles and ellipses are exact. Which properties Vectorworks lists, and its dialog's wording, weren't compared
- [x] Model > Volumetric Properties…: the surface area, volume and centre of mass of the selected 3D objects together (those on their layer, not inside a group being edited), in the same units, placed as text or saved as a file as Engineering Properties does. Faces are taken as the 3D view draws them (curved ones as their facets); each run of faces joined edge to edge is wound one way, and the volume is given only when every run's faces close (some plug-in objects' faces as Rodeo draws them don't, so they get a surface area alone). Place locus at center of mass puts a 3D locus there (one undo), and a hollow sealed inside a solid counts as solid
- [ ] Sweep's start angle and pitch (their two fields aren't told apart; every demo sweep has 0 in both), Push/Pull and NURBS modelling

## Objects read and drawn

- [x] Lines, rectangles, rounded rectangles, ovals, arcs, polygons, polylines (with curves: bezier, fillet and point-on-arc vertices)
- [x] Text (rotated, justified, sized, in its font and style); images
- [x] Groups, symbols (nested, mirrored), plug-in objects (their cached 2D and 3D)
- [x] 3D objects in Top/Plan: extrudes' profiles placed by their own frame, 3D polygons as their outline seen from above, and every 3D object picked and moved by where it lies
- [x] Design and sheet layers, scales, elevations, stories; classes and their visibility
- [x] Viewports: plan (live, class overrides, data visualisations), section, elevation and detail (cached drawing), crops
- [x] View > Create Viewport…: a plan viewport on a sheet layer or a new one: Viewport Name, Create on Layer, Scale (the active layer's at first), each design layer Visible, Gray or Hidden (as they are now at first; the active one visible), hidden and grayed classes staying so, showing all the visible layers draw or, with a closed shape selected on a design layer, that shape's box. It is then live and edited like any plan viewport. Rodeo's reading: a plan only, with no style, drawing label, drawing title or Clip Cube; the crop is the shape's box, not its outline, and the shape stays on its layer; no chosen view, so a 3D view gives a plan; a drawing with a plan viewport copies that one's settings, otherwise it's built by the file format's notes, and Vectorworks hasn't opened one built so yet
- [x] View > Create Detail Viewport…: a plan viewport of a detail from one selected closed shape (the crop object), drawn round it on a design layer, or on a sheet layer over a plan viewport (the top one under the crop's middle), whose layers and classes the detail then shows (on a design layer: that layer visible and the others as they are, as Create Viewport starts). The dialog: Name viewport as Dwg No./Sheet No. (on: named Drawing Number/sheet layer name; off: Viewport Name, Detail-n at first), Create on Layer (a sheet layer or a new one), Drawing Number (one past the sheet's plan viewports, skipping names in use), Drawing Title, Scale (the crop layer's, or the viewport's under a sheet crop), Detail callout round the crop, Drawing label under the viewport.
