The callout is a leader up and right from the crop's outline to a bubble with the drawing and sheet numbers, grouped with the crop (or alone when the crop is already in a group), 10 mm on paper at the crop layer's scale; the label is a drawing label on the sheet under the viewport with the number, sheet, title and scale. The new viewport sits right of what its sheet already holds, tops level (at the origin of an empty sheet), and the sheet is shown with it selected; all of it one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the viewport is cropped to the crop's box, not its shape (a circle's detail is its square); a crop on a turned sheet viewport is taken back as its box's box; the callout and label are plain lines and texts, not Vectorworks' linked Detail Callout and Drawing Label objects, so they don't follow later renumbering or moves, and the title lives only in the label; a crop inside a viewport's annotations isn't handled (draw it on the sheet over the viewport); the Drawing Number only follows the sheet first offered, not one picked afterwards
- [x] View > Create Section Viewport…: with one Section-Elevation Line selected (Dims/Notes' tool), or a plain line or open polyline, a viewport on a sheet layer of the 3D model cut by an upright plane along it and looked at square to it. The dialog: Name viewport as Dwg No./Sheet No. (on: Drawing Number/sheet layer name; off: Viewport Name, Section-n at first), Create on Layer (a sheet layer or a new one), Drawing Number (a section line's bubble number, else one past the sheet's viewports), Drawing Title, Scale (the line's layer's), Look (left or right of the way the line was drawn; a section line's arrows set it), Display extents beyond cut plane with a limit (0 unlimited; off draws the cut alone), Drawing label under the viewport. The model is the 3D view's: the visible design layers, without hidden objects or hidden classes. What the plane cuts is filled in the colour of the faces cut, with a 14-mil outline (a cut through a hollow keeps its hole, as one outline joined by edges that aren't drawn); edges beyond, creases (faces meeting at more than 25°), open surfaces' borders and curved surfaces' outlines are drawn in 5-mil black where nothing nearer hides them, worked out as lines (hidden-line) and grouped by object. Nothing in front of the plane is drawn, and only what lies across the line's length; faces lying on the plane are neither cut nor drawn, so what only touches it isn't cut. The viewport sits right of what its sheet holds, tops level, its drawing in its annotations; all of it one undo step. A 2.26-million-triangle drawing cut in 1.3 s in Node. Rodeo's reading: the section is drawn as the model is when it's made and isn't updated, and the viewport shows no design layers (Rodeo's viewports draw only plans live); a line with corners is cut straight between its ends, not stepped; it doesn't renumber the section line's bubbles; no render modes, section styles, cut-plane fill by class, hatches, or interior/horizontal sections; surfaces faceted as the 3D view draws them, so a sphere's outline zig-zags along its facets; faces seen exactly edge on hide nothing
- [x] View > Create Interior Elevation Viewport…: with one Interior Elevation Marker selected (Dims/Notes' tool), or a closed shape drawn round the room, up to four section viewports of the room's walls on a sheet layer, one for each way the marker's arrows point, named by compass quarter as Vectorworks does (North, East, South, West, each sweeping 45° either side). The dialog (Create Interior Elevation Viewports): Name viewport as Dwg No./Sheet No. (off: Viewport Name, Interior Elevation-n at first, with the compass name after it), Create on Layer, each look's tick, Number (the marker's number by that arrow, else the next free ones on the sheet) and Title (North Elevation and so on), Scale (the room layer's), Display extents beyond cut plane, Use drawing label. Each look is cut along the wall behind it and runs across from wall to wall and up from floor to ceiling, as Vectorworks finds the nearest walls: here the first faces met straight out from the marker's middle 1 m above its layer (beyond them, the model's extents), up and down for the ceiling and floor; a shape's own sides bound it across instead. Drawn as Create Section Viewport draws (hidden-line, the cut filled), in a row on the sheet right of what it holds, tops level, each with its drawing label; a shape gets an Interior Elevation Marker at its middle with each look's number and the sheet's name; all one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the viewports are drawn once, as the model is, and the marker isn't linked to them (Edit Section Lines, moving the marker, and renumbering don't follow); a marker's numbers aren't rewritten; no viewport styles, detail levels or render modes; walls are found by the faces straight out from the middle, so a column or a piece of furniture in the way stops a look short
- [x] View > Create Horizontal Section Viewport… (with a design layer active): the 3D model cut level at a height above the active layer and looked down on, as a viewport on a sheet layer. The dialog: Name viewport as Dwg No./Sheet No. (off: Viewport Name, Horizontal Section-n at first), Create on Layer, Drawing Number (one past the first sheet's viewports), Drawing Title (the layer's name and Section), Scale (the layer's), Cut Plane (1200 mm above the active layer at first), Display extents below cut plane with a limit (0 unlimited; off draws the cut alone), Use drawing label. Drawn as Create Section Viewport draws: what's cut filled in its colour with a heavier outline, what lies below in thin lines where nothing nearer hides it, at plan's own orientation; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the cut is drawn once and isn't kept up to date; the cut plane is relative to the active layer only (not a story or story level), there's no Cut Plane and Extents dialog, nothing above the cut is drawn (Display extents above cut plane), and 2D planar objects aren't sectioned; no clip-cube or existing-viewport starting points, styles, render modes or hidden-object display
- [x] View > Create Multiple Viewports… (⇧⌘M): the 3D model's Front, Top, Right, Left, Bottom and Back views and an isometric, any of them, each a viewport at one scale. The dialog: Viewport Scale (the active or first visible design layer's), Method (First angle projection, or Third angle when the drawing is in inches, feet, yards or miles), Views (Front, Top and Left checked in first angle, Front, Top and Right in third). Laid out round Front a fixed 20 mm apart, each lined up with Front along the axis they share, and centred on the active sheet layer, or on a new sheet layer when a design layer is active: third angle puts Top above and Right to the right, first angle Top below and Left to the right, Bottom opposite Top and Back at the far right; the isometric is Right Isometric in third angle and Left in first, in the corner the usual three views leave empty. Each viewport is named after its view and drawn hidden-line, of the layers and classes visible now; one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the views are drawn once into each viewport's annotations and aren't kept up to date; there are no viewport styles (Use Style, Edit Style, Create unstyled viewport from selected style), the Views checks don't follow a change of Method in the dialog, where the isometric sits isn't documented, and Vectorworks' fixed gap isn't either
- [x] View > Update Selected Viewports, View > Update All Viewports, and Update on a viewport's right-click menu: the hidden-line viewports Rodeo draws (Create Section, Interior Elevation, Horizontal Section and Multiple Viewports) cut again from the model as it is now, through the same plane and of the same design layers (one deleted since is left out), the classes hidden in the viewport left out (its own class visibility, else the drawing's). The new drawing replaces the old one where that one stands now, the viewport's box fits it again, and the rest of the viewport's annotations (dimensions, notes) stays; a view from outside the model (Multiple Viewports) stays outside however the model grows. One undo step; a toast counts what was updated. Rodeo's reading: plan viewports draw live and those read from Vectorworks files draw the copy their file caches, so both are counted and left alone; there is no out-of-date red border and nothing is updated automatically before printing or exporting; Vectorworks' Update button in Object Info isn't there
- [x] Edit Annotations (a sheet viewport's right-click menu, or a double-click on it): the viewport's annotations open as the active layer, at its scale, the picture staying where it is, over what the viewport shows (a plan's design layers drawn live, a section's or elevation's cached drawing, cropped as on the sheet), with the rest of the sheet grayed when Show Other Objects While in Editing Modes is on; every tool draws, snaps and edits there as on a design layer at that scale, snapping to the viewport's drawing too, and new objects go into the viewport's annotations; a bar names the viewport, and Exit Viewport Annotation (the bar, the right-click menu, ⌘] or choosing a layer) goes back to the sheet. Undo, saving and reopening keep them in the viewport. Rodeo's reading: a double-click opens the annotations straight away, where Vectorworks asks which part to edit (Rodeo has no Edit Crop or Edit Design Layer mode for viewports, nor an Edit Viewport dialog with an Annotations button); snaps to the viewport's drawing ignore its class overrides; the sheet's other viewports aren't drawn behind; annotations stay in their viewport (Object Info doesn't move them to a layer)
- [x] Worksheets on sheets (cached cells)
- [x] Fills: solid, bitmap patterns (known ones), hatches, tiles, gradients; fill mapping
- [x] Line types (dashes), line weights, line markers (arrows, dots, slashes)
- [x] Class styles (fill, pen, line type, weight, opacity by class); materials
- [x] Data visualisations by class, object style, material, layer, story
- [x] Data visualisations in section and detail viewports (their cached drawing, by class and material)
- [x] 3D: walls, slabs, roofs, extrudes, sweeps, solids, symbols, 3D polygons, meshes, site terrain, textures
- [x] Tile mapping: origin, axes and cell size from each object's fill mapping
- [ ] Image fills (no demo file uses one, so how an object names its image is unknown)
- [x] Drop shadows (canvas, SVG and PNG; a container casts one shadow)
- [x] Drop shadows in PDF exports (layered outlines in place of a blur)
- [ ] Viewport class overrides of fill and pen
- [x] Data visualisation by object type: slabs (the one type code the demos use)
- [ ] Data visualisation by other object types
- [x] Rendered viewport images (cached Renderworks renders, under the viewport's line work, at its scale; in PDF, SVG and PNG exports too)
- [ ] Free-form 3D surfaces (Parasolid) and the site model's NURBS drape

## Data and documentation

- [x] Plug-in parameters and attached records: view and edit
- [ ] Plug-ins redrawn from their parameters (needs each plug-in's own code)
- [x] Records: attach (with the format's default values) and detach, in Object Info's Data tab
- [x] Record formats: create and edit them (Tools > Records or a format's Resources tile: fields added, renamed, moved and removed with their defaults; attached records keep their values)
- [x] Tools > Records > Attach Record…: a record of one of the drawing's data record formats, holding its default values, attached to each selected object without one, in one undo step; the dialog says how many have one already and refuses a format they all have; greyed in a drawing with no record formats
- [x] Tools > Records > Detach Record…: the records of a format some selected object has taken off every selected object that has one, with their values, in one undo step
- [x] Tools > Records > Change One Field… and Change All Fields…: a data record's values on symbol definitions, for those in a Symbol Folder (and the folders in it), at the top level (None) or in the whole drawing (All). Change One Field sets one field (Field Name and New Value, a control by the field's kind); Change All Fields opens Change Fields, named for the record, with every field to tick and set (editing a value ticks it; a long list scrolls where Vectorworks pages 16 at a time). Both say how many definitions change and ask first; symbols already placed keep their own values; one undo step. Only definitions with the record attached change (whether Vectorworks attaches it to those without isn't documented); areas and volumes, which are worked out, aren't offered
- [x] Tools > Records > Merge Record Formats… and Split Record Format…: Merge puts one data record format's fields at the end of another's (a name the other has already gets "_" in front), every object and symbol definition with a record of the first holding its values in a record of the second, and deletes the first; Split moves the fields ticked to a new format named in the dialog, every holder of the source format gaining a record of the new one with its values. One undo step each. Pop-up fields are refused for now (their list of choices would stay behind), and Merge refuses a format the drawing names elsewhere as text (plug-in parameters, worksheet formulas, data visualizations), as deleting one does
- [x] Tools > Records > Modify by Record…: a data record's field, read as a number (text holding one too), sets each object's colour, size or height. Color Gradient (between a lower and upper bound, the found values' to start with, along one of the drawing's gradients or a built-in yellow-red-brown one, midpoints honoured), Color Ranges (lower to upper bound and a colour each; three across the found values to start with; Add Range and Delete) and Color Value (each value found, Apply and a colour) set the fill's or pen's background or foreground colour as the nearest of the drawing's colours; Resize scales each object about its middle by value × multiplier; Extrude extrudes it value × multiplier high; Elevate 2D Polys turns each polygon and polyline into a 3D polygon at value × multiplier (both in the drawing's units). Apply to the entire drawing or the selected objects only, with or without the components of groups (groups and symbols themselves are left alone); objects whose field holds no number stay as they are. One undo step; extruded or elevated objects that were selected are replaced by their results in the selection; the last settings, record and field are kept in this browser
- [ ] Modify by Record: saved sets (only the last settings are kept), Include components of symbols, gradients from libraries (the drawing's and a built-in one only), colours outside the drawing's colour table (the nearest is used), the records carried over to elevated 3D polygons, resizing 3D objects
- [x] Worksheet editing: text and number cells, in a grid editor (Tools > Reports, a Resources tile, or a double-click on a worksheet on a sheet)
- [ ] Worksheet formulas and database rows: editing them (shown read-only, as last calculated)
- [x] Duplicate on Layers… (a title block border's right-click menu): Vectorworks' Select Layers dialog, every layer no title block border is on (sheet and design layers) with a tick, Select/Deselect All, at least one needed; a copy of the border clicked on each layer ticked, where it stands, with its records and drawing. On a sheet layer the copy's Sheet Number and Sheet Title become the sheet's, its drawn texts showing them following (as Title Block Manager changes them); one undo step. Rodeo's reading: the sheet data is always coordinated, whatever the border's Use Automatic Drawing Coordination setting; texts showing a value in another form (not exactly) stay as they were until Vectorworks resets the border; it's offered on title block borders read from Vectorworks files, not on the plain groups Rodeo's own Title Block Border tool draws
- [x] File > Title Block Manager…: project data on every title block, sheet data on the title blocks ticked (several at once, values they differ in shown as Varies and kept unless edited; one renumbered at a time), text, number and on/off fields; the title blocks' drawn text following where it shows a changed value exactly (both copies a border draws), and Sheet Number and Sheet Title renaming the sheet layer; one undo step
- [ ] Title block revision and issue data; title blocks redrawn from their layout (a value drawn in another format follows only when Vectorworks resets them); drawing list editing (a drawing list's subrows don't say which title block each shows)
- [ ] Data Manager, IFC data

## Collaboration and scripting

- [x] Live collaboration on cloud drawings (several people editing one drawing at once, presence, follow, version history; docs/PLATFORM.md)
- [x] Project Sharing: layers checked out for one member's editing alone (File > Project Sharing…, a layer's right-click menu; held layers locked in the Layers list; the owner releases anyone's; kept by the server past the holder's connection); edits to a layer someone else holds are refused, naming them
- [ ] Project Sharing: checking out single objects and resources, and working offline in a working file to commit later
- [x] Python scripts (Plug-ins > Run Script…, and Tools > Plug-ins): Python written for Vectorworks run on the open drawing, in Pyodide in a Web Worker, with 238 of the vs calls (drawing objects and polylines, handles and the drawing list, ForEachObject and Count with search criteria, geometry, class, name, attributes, records, selection, moving, turning, scaling, mirroring, duplicating, grouping, layers and classes, the dialogs, layout dialogs, points clicked in the drawing, a plug-in's parameters, object and Object Info widgets), and 11 setup calls of plug-in objects and 6 layout dialog calls (AlignItemEdge, edge and proportional bindings, the dialog's size and position, help strings) accepted and ignored; the script works on a copy of the drawing and what it changed is applied when it ends, as one undo step (nothing when it fails or is stopped); a window that keeps the script, opens and saves .py files, shows what it prints and links a failing line; the dialogs that wait for an answer need a browser that can pause WebAssembly (Chrome, Edge)
- [x] Script palettes (Window > Script Palettes: each palette, and New Script Palette…): named palettes of Python scripts and Vectorscripts in a Script Palettes palette, a section each (click its name to show or hide its scripts); a double-click (or Enter) runs a script on the open drawing as one undo step, an error shown in an alert with its line and what it prints in a toast. A palette's right-click menu: New Script…, Import Script… (a .py, .vs or .px file), Rename Palette…, Delete Palette; a script's: Run, Edit… (the Script Editor: name, Python Script or VectorScript, the code), Rename…, Duplicate, Export…, Delete. Kept in this browser for every drawing, where Vectorworks keeps them in the drawing; the editor's Procedure, Criteria and Tool/Attribute helpers, Compile Script and Find/Replace aren't offered
- [x] Vectorscript scripts in Run Script (a .vs or .px file, or a script that starts with PROCEDURE or FUNCTION): translated to Python in the same worker and run on the same vs calls by their Vectorscript names; nested procedures and functions with VAR parameters and FORWARD, CONST, TYPE and VAR sections, INTEGER, LONGINT, REAL, BOOLEAN, STRING, CHAR, HANDLE, ARRAY (any bounds, several dimensions), RECORD, VECTOR, DYNARRAY with ALLOCATE, IF, CASE, FOR, WHILE, REPEAT, EXIT, IN, lengths written with ' and " (3'6"), the Pascal string and number routines (Concat, Copy, Pos, Delete, Insert, UpperString, Round, Trunc, Sqrt…), search criteria written out (ForEachObject(Label, ((T=RECT) & (C='Walls')))), the vs procedures that hand values back through VAR parameters (GetBBox, HCenter, GetUnits, GetFillBack…), and a layout dialog's handler procedure (its VAR item set to -1 keeps the dialog open); errors are given at the Vectorscript's own lines; {$INCLUDE} files, WITH, GOTO and the vs calls Rodeo lacks are refused by name; set types beyond IN tests aren't
- [x] Vectorworks plug-ins (Plug-ins > Vectorworks Plug-ins): menu commands (.vsm), tools (.vst) and objects (.vso) made with Vectorworks' Plug-in Manager, in Python or Vectorscript, installed in this browser from their files with the Python files they import (chosen together, or a zipped plug-in folder), listed with Manage… to remove them; commands and tools run from that submenu, a tool's GetPt, GetLine and GetRect taking clicks in the drawing (unsnapped; Esc ends the script, keeping what it did); objects placed with the Plug-in Insertion tool (a click, turned by a drag; a drag along a linear one; a drag across a rectangular one) in a parameter record format made for them, their script drawing into them with their parameters (P and the name, dimensions in the drawing's units), GetCustomObjectInfo, GetPluginInfo and IsNewCustomObject, the redraw event from vsoGetEventInfo; Object Info shows their parameters' display names, popup choices, static text and class parameters (a menu of the drawing's classes), and a change draws the object again, one undo step each. An object that lays out Object Info itself (SetObjPropVS's UI override, vsoAddWidget, vsoAddParamWidget, vsoInsertAllParams, vsoWidgetSetIndLvl, run once for its setup event) is shown that way: its parameters in its order, indents and names, dividers, lines of text and buttons; when it says so, its script runs for each object's values to grey and hide widgets (vsoWidgetSetEnable, vsoWidgetSetVisible), and a button runs it for the click, the object then drawn again with the parameters it set (or after ResetObject), one undo step. A dialog a script builds (CreateLayout, then its static text, text, number and length fields, check boxes, three-state check boxes, radio buttons, push buttons, pull-down menus, class and layer menus, list boxes, colour menus, group boxes, check-box and radio group boxes, tab controls and separators, placed with SetFirstLayoutItem, SetRightItem, SetBelowItem and SetFirstGroupItem with their indents and spacing) is shown over the drawing and runs its handler for the setup, each change, each button, OK and Cancel, the controls brought up to what the handler set (choices added, items greyed, hidden or relabelled, OK greyed), the script waiting for it in a browser that can pause WebAssembly (Chrome, Edge). Plug-ins whose script is encrypted, and SDK plug-ins (.vlb), are refused. Untested in Vectorworks: the file doesn't say an object's shape, so it is taken from what its script reads (PLineLength, PBoxWidth and PBoxHeight), a linear object's record holds LineLength and a rectangular one's BoxWidth and BoxHeight, and a rectangular object's origin is the middle of its left side
- [ ] Vectorworks plug-ins: path objects (GetCustomObjectPath, profile groups), control points, objects inserted in walls, the layout dialog controls Rodeo lacks (list browsers, sliders, images and image popups from resource files, which take no room, symbol and texture menus, tree controls), events while a field is typed in (a field's change comes when it's left), resizable dialogs resizing their controls, preference dialogs, plug-in strings from their resource files (GetPluginString gives "", so a button with no other label says "Button"), Object Info widgets other than parameters, dividers, text and buttons (a disclosure is shown as a divider), changes a button's script makes besides the object's parameters (left unapplied), a command's or tool's parameters kept with the drawing, and plug-ins' parameter types Rodeo doesn't read (kept as text)
- [ ] The rest of the vs calls (preferences, object variables, 3D, walls, worksheets, dialogs built with the Dialog Builder calls, events other than an object's redraw); scripts kept in the drawing (script palettes and Resource Manager scripts read from or saved in a .vwx: Rodeo's script palettes are kept in the browser)
- [ ] Marionette
